- Post-renovation sales monitoring vs. projected uplift (W96.10)
- Capex vs. budget tracking and fixed asset capitalization (W96.11)
- Integration with W4 (replenishment — resume/adjust for renovation), W16 (new store — shares setup procedures for IT, planogram, restocking), W21 (capex — renovation funding), W22 (transfers — excess inventory redistribution), W22B (store-to-DC returns — inventory draw-down), W47 (facility maintenance — renovation scope may originate from maintenance assessment), W63 (shelf labels — updated for new layout), W67 (store performance — renovation ROI tracking), W83 (marketing — re-opening campaign), W86 (planogram — updated layout), W97 (sample/demo — reinstall or replace display samples)

### Pain Points / Risks
- Revenue loss during closure — full-store renovations mean 2–8 weeks of zero revenue from that location while fixed costs (lease, utilities, security) continue; payback period must be carefully modeled in the W21 capex request
- Construction delays from contractor performance issues or material supply disruptions in the Philippines, extending the closure period and compounding revenue loss
- Inventory draw-down timing risk — if replenishment is reduced too aggressively before renovation, stores run out of key items before closure, losing final weeks of sales; if too conservative, excess inventory must be emergency-transferred at premium logistics cost
- Post-renovation sales uplift below projections — if the 90-day monitoring shows uplift < 50% of the capex business case, the renovation ROI fails to meet investment criteria, affecting future renovation budget approvals

---

### Time Estimate
2–8 weeks per renovation project depending on scope (light refresh: 2–3 weeks, PHP 2–5M; partial remodel: 4–6 weeks, PHP 5–15M; full renovation: 6–8 weeks, PHP 15–40M). Store Ops Director: 8–16 hours planning + 2 hours/week during construction + 8 hours post-renovation per project. Facilities Manager: ~10 hours/week per active renovation. Post-renovation monitoring: 2 hours/week for 90 days.

### Staffing Implication
- **Store Ops Director**: oversees ~10–15 renovation projects/year; each project requires ~8–16 hours of planning + ~2 hours/week during construction + ~8 hours post-renovation. With staggered projects, peak load is 2–3 concurrent renovations = ~10–15 hours/week during peak. May delegate day-to-day supervision to Facilities Manager for smaller projects.
- **Facilities Manager**: serves as on-site construction supervisor; adds ~10 hours/week per active renovation. With 1–2 concurrent renovations, this is manageable.
- **Visual Merchandiser**: design and layout work adds ~8–16 hours per renovation. Absorbed within existing Marketing team.
- **IT Manager**: adds ~2–4 days per renovation for IT relocation. Absorbed with support from IT team.
- **No incremental headcount.**

## W109. Store-Level Inventory Receiving & Putaway

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | DC delivery truck arrives at store; or DSD vendor delivery; or inter-store transfer arrives; or customer return processed for restocking |
| **Frequency** | 2–3 DC deliveries per store per week; ~5–10 DSD deliveries per store per month; occasional transfers and returns |
| **Volume** | ~6–10 receiving events per store per week; avg 20–50 lines per DC delivery; 5–15 lines per DSD |
| **Owner** | Receiving Clerk (store) |
| **Participants** | Receiving Clerk, Stock Associate, Department Supervisor, Store Manager, DC Driver |

### Background

W3 covers DC receiving in detail — barcode-directed putaway, quality inspection, cross-dock flows, and yard management. However, store-level receiving is fundamentally different: (a) no WMS-directed putaway — stores use handheld RF devices or mobile tablets, not full WMS; (b) putaway means moving stock to the sales floor (shelf replenishment) or backroom (overflow), not to bin locations; (c) stores receive from multiple sources (DC delivery, DSD vendor, inter-store transfer, customer return restocking); (d) receiving happens during store operating hours with customers present, unlike DC receiving which is a warehouse-only activity. This workflow fills the gap between DC outbound dispatch (W106) and store shelf availability.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Pre-arrival preparation**: System alerts Receiving Clerk of scheduled inbound deliveries for the day (from DC delivery schedule per W106, DSD schedule per W18B, or transfer ETA per W22); Clerk reviews expected receiving volume and coordinates with Stock Associates for putaway support | Receiving Clerk | Store Manager | 10 min/morning |
| 2 | **Truck arrival and dock check**: Driver arrives at store receiving area; Receiving Clerk checks delivery manifest against system-scheduled deliveries; if unscheduled delivery (no matching PO/TO): Clerk checks with Store Manager before accepting | Receiving Clerk / Driver | Store Manager | 5 min |
| 3 | **Unloading**: Receiving Clerk and Stock Associates unload truck; stage goods in receiving area sorted by department (lumber, plumbing, tiles, tools, hardware, paint, appliances); catch-weight items (lumber, wire) measured and verified at unload | Receiving Clerk / Stock Associate | Department Supervisor | 20–45 min/delivery |
| 4 | **Scan receiving against Transfer Order / PO**: Receiving Clerk scans each case/tote/item barcode using handheld RF device against the Transfer Order (DC delivery) or PO (DSD); system displays expected SKU and quantity per line; Clerk confirms or enters actual quantity received | Receiving Clerk | Department Supervisor | 15–30 min/delivery |
| 5 | **Discrepancy handling**: (a) **Shortage** (received less than expected): Clerk enters actual quantity; system posts partial receipt; shortage logged; if > 5% variance: Clerk notifies Store Manager and DC Dispatch per W22.9a; (b) **Overage** (received more than expected): Clerk flags in system; Store Manager decides to accept (updates TO/PO quantity) or reject excess; (c) **Wrong item** (SKU doesn't match TO/PO): Clerk rejects item; Driver takes back or DC Dispatch notified; (d) **Damage**: Clerk documents damage with photos per W91 damage report process; routes to disposition (W93 markdown, W88 RTV, or scrap) | Receiving Clerk | Store Manager | 5 min/discrepancy |
| 6 | **Quality spot-check**: For DSD items (fresh delivery from vendor, no DC quality inspection): Department Supervisor spot-checks 3–5 items per delivery for damage, correct labeling, and shelf-life; DC-delivered items already inspected at W3 AQL checkpoint — no additional store inspection unless visibly damaged | Department Supervisor | Store Manager | 5 min/delivery |
| 7 | **Confirm receipt in system**: Receiving Clerk confirms Goods Receipt in handheld; system posts: (a) store inventory increases (perpetual), (b) in-transit inventory clears (for DC deliveries), (c) inventory ownership at store confirmed (Depot Inc.), (d) receiving timestamp and Clerk ID logged | Receiving Clerk / System | Department Supervisor | 2 min |
| 8 | **Putaway to sales floor (primary)**: Stock Associate moves received goods from receiving staging area to sales floor: (a) system provides shelf location guidance on handheld (aisle/bay) based on planogram (W86); (b) Stock Associate places items on shelf in designated location; scan-confirms shelf placement; (c) for promotional items (W57): Stock Associate places in designated promo display area, not regular shelf; (d) for catch-weight items: Stock Associate places in yard or designated cut-to-length area per store layout; (e) putaway priority: promotional items first, then A-items (fast movers), then B/C-items | Stock Associate | Department Supervisor | 30–60 min/delivery |
| 9 | **Putaway to backroom (overflow)**: If sales floor shelf is full (no space for all received quantity): (a) Stock Associate places excess in backroom stock area; (b) system records backroom location (zone-level, not bin-level — stores do not have bin-level location tracking); (c) backroom inventory tracked separately from floor inventory in system; (d) Stock Associates replenish from backroom to floor during daily shelf restocking (W5A step 7) | Stock Associate | Department Supervisor | 10 min/delivery |
| 10 | **Packaging and waste disposal**: Stock Associate breaks down empty cartons, pallets, and packaging materials; cardboard recycled or compacted; packaging waste disposed per store waste management protocol; wooden pallets stacked for DC truck backhaul on next delivery | Stock Associate | Department Supervisor | 10 min/delivery |
| 11 | **Receiving completion**: Receiving Clerk closes receiving session in handheld; system generates receiving confirmation with: TO/PO number, total lines received, discrepancies noted, receiving completion timestamp; Driver signs delivery receipt (proof of delivery for DC) and departs | Receiving Clerk / Driver | Store Manager | 5 min |

**Total time per DC delivery**: ~1.5–2.5 hours (unloading + scanning + putaway)
**Total time per DSD delivery**: ~30–60 minutes

### Receiving by Source

| Source | Receiving Document | Scan Against | Discrepancy Route | Putaway Priority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **DC delivery** (W4, W106) | Transfer Order | TO lines | W22.9a (transfer discrepancy) | Promo → A-items → B/C-items |
| **DSD vendor** (W18) | Purchase Order | PO lines | W18 discrepancy process | Direct to shelf/yard |
| **Inter-store transfer** (W22) | Transfer Order | TO lines | W22.9a | Per dept priority |
| **Customer return restock** (W12) | Return transaction | Return line items | N/A — already processed | Direct to shelf |
| **RTV backhaul from vendor** (W88) | RTV shipment | RTV record | W88 vendor dispute | To RTV holding area |

### System Touchpoints
- Daily inbound delivery schedule from W106 DC dispatch, W18B DSD schedule, and W22 transfers (W109.1)
- Handheld RF barcode scanning against Transfer Order or Purchase Order with line-level quantity confirmation (W109.4)
- Partial receipt posting with discrepancy logging and auto-notification (W109.5)
- Shelf location guidance from planogram integration (W86) on handheld (W109.8)
- Dual inventory tracking: sales floor vs. backroom at zone level (W109.9)
- Receiving confirmation with proof of delivery for DC truck (W109.11)
- Catch-weight item measurement capture at receiving (W109.3)
- Integration with W4 (replenishment — the orders being received), W18 (DSD — vendor delivery receiving), W22 (transfers — inter-store receiving), W42 (physical inventory — receiving affects count accuracy), W47 (facility — receiving dock maintenance), W86 (planogram — shelf placement guidance), W91 (damage handling), W106 (DC dispatch — proof of delivery loop), W63 (shelf labels — new items may need labels)

### Pain Points / Risks
- Receiving dock bottleneck — with 2–3 DC deliveries and 2–3 DSD deliveries per week plus occasional inter-store transfers, the single receiving dock creates scheduling conflicts and driver wait times
- Scan accuracy under time pressure — Receiving Clerks rushing through barcode scanning during high-volume deliveries may confirm quantities without actually scanning each item, leading to receiving discrepancies that propagate into inventory inaccuracy
- Putaway priority conflicts — Stock Associates must prioritize promotional items, then A-items, but urgent BOPIS picking requests and customer service needs compete for the same Stock Associate labor during receiving windows
- Backroom inventory visibility — zone-level (not bin-level) backroom tracking means Stock Associates cannot quickly locate specific items in the backroom, reducing replenishment speed and increasing time-to-shelf

---

### Time Estimate
~1.5–2.5 hours per DC delivery (unloading + scanning + putaway) and ~30–60 min per DSD delivery. At 6–10 receiving events/store/week across 200 stores, total chain-wide receiving labor is ~1,800–5,000 hours/week. Pre-arrival preparation adds 10 min/morning per store. Receiving Clerk and Stock Associate time is the largest single labor component in store operations.

### Staffing Implication
- **2 Receiving Clerks per store**: already in staffing model (30/store). Each handles ~3–5 deliveries/week × 2 hours = ~6–10 hours/week. Remainder of time on backroom management, BOPIS staging, and other duties.
- **4 Stock Associates per store**: putaway adds ~1–1.5 hours per delivery event × ~6–10 events/week = ~6–15 hours/week. Balanced with daily shelf replenishment (W5A.7), cycle counting (W6), and damage handling (W91).
- **Staffed at 4 per store** to accommodate continuous replenishment and omnichannel fulfillment.

## W111. Store Energy & Utility Consumption Management

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Monthly utility bill received; quarterly energy review calendar; or ad-hoc triggered by abnormal consumption alert |
| **Frequency** | Monthly bill processing; quarterly consumption review; annual energy budget |
| **Volume** | 200 stores × 3–4 utility types (electricity, water, internet, waste disposal) = ~600–800 utility bills/month; 4 DCs × 3–4 utility types = ~15–20 DC bills/month |
| **Owner** | Store Manager (store-level); DC Manager (DC-level); Facilities Manager (chain-wide) |
| **Participants** | Store Manager, DC Manager, Facilities Manager, AP Clerk, Finance Manager, VP Operations |

### Background

BuildRight operates 200 stores (8,000–15,000 sqm each) and 4 DCs (25,000–40,000 sqm each) across the Philippines. Utility costs — particularly electricity for lighting, HVAC, and equipment — are a significant operating expense. At an estimated PHP 80–150 per sqm per month for electricity in Philippine big-box retail, annual electricity cost alone is ~PHP 1.5–2.7B (3–4% of revenue). W7C processes utility bills as non-PO invoices, but there is no workflow for monitoring consumption trends, benchmarking across stores/DCs, identifying energy waste, and tracking sustainability KPIs. This workflow creates that operational layer.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Utility bill processing**: AP Clerk receives utility bill (electricity, water, internet, waste disposal) per W7C non-PO invoice process; system captures: utility provider, meter reading (if available), consumption units (kWh, cubic meters), total amount, billing period, and store/DC location code; AP Clerk enters consumption data in utility tracking module alongside financial data | AP Clerk | AP Supervisor | Per W7C + 5 min/bill for consumption entry |
| 2 | **Consumption normalization**: System calculates normalized consumption metrics per location: (a) kWh per sqm per month, (b) kWh per PHP revenue per month, (c) water consumption per sqm per month, (d) total utility cost as % of store revenue; normalizes for billing period length (some bills span 28–35 days) | System | — | Automated |
| 3 | **Automated anomaly detection**: System compares each location's current month consumption to: (a) same month prior year (seasonal comparison), (b) trailing 3-month average (trend comparison), (c) peer group average (stores of similar size and format in same region); flags locations where consumption exceeds comparison by > 20%; generates anomaly alert to Store Manager and Facilities Manager | System | — | Automated |
| 4 | **Store Manager anomaly response**: Upon receiving anomaly alert, Store Manager investigates: (a) check for operational causes — extended HVAC use during heat wave, new equipment added, construction/renovation activity (W96), increased lighting for promotional displays; (b) check for maintenance issues — HVAC running inefficiently, water leak, lighting left on during non-business hours; (c) if maintenance issue identified: Store Manager creates facility maintenance work order per W47; (d) response documented in system with root cause and corrective action | Store Manager | Regional Manager | 30 min/alert |
| 5 | **Monthly utility dashboard**: Facilities Manager reviews monthly utility dashboard: (a) total utility spend by type and trend, (b) top 20 highest-consuming stores/DCs, (c) top 20 stores with highest utility cost as % of revenue, (d) anomaly alerts and resolution status, (e) comparison of actual utility spend to budget (W26); dashboard available to VP Operations, CFO, and Store Managers (own store only) | Facilities Manager | VP Operations | 1 hour/month |
| 6 | **Quarterly energy review**: Facilities Manager conducts quarterly energy review with Store Ops Director and VP Operations: (a) chain-wide utility consumption trend and cost trajectory, (b) store-level benchmarking — best and worst performers by kWh/sqm and utility cost/revenue, (c) seasonal patterns and preparation for peak consumption periods (summer HVAC, holiday lighting), (d) energy efficiency project pipeline — proposed initiatives (LED lighting retrofit, HVAC upgrades, motion-sensor lighting in backroom, solar panel feasibility for select stores) with cost-benefit analysis, (e) review of approved energy efficiency capex projects (per W21) — actual vs. projected savings | Facilities Manager | VP Operations | 2 hours/quarter |
| 7 | **Annual energy budget**: As part of W26 annual budget preparation, Facilities Manager prepares utility budget per location based on: (a) historical consumption by month (3-year trend), (b) planned changes (new store openings W16, renovations W96, equipment replacements), (c) known rate increases from utility providers (Meralco, Davao Light, VECO rate adjustments), (d) projected savings from energy efficiency initiatives; submits per-location utility budget to Finance Manager for consolidation into W26 | Facilities Manager / Finance Manager | VP Operations / CFO | Annual (8 hours) |
| 8 | **DC utility management**: DC Manager monitors DC utility consumption with focus on: (a) refrigeration and cold-chain equipment (if applicable to paint/chemical storage), (b) warehouse lighting and ventilation, (c) charging stations for electric forklifts and handheld devices, (d) yard lighting for outdoor lumber/building materials area; DC utility benchmarking uses DC-specific metrics (kWh per sqm, kWh per TEU processed) | DC Manager | Supply Chain Manager | 30 min/month |

### System Touchpoints
- Utility bill processing with consumption data capture (kWh, cubic meters) alongside financial data per W7C (W111.1)
- Automated consumption normalization: kWh/sqm, kWh/revenue, cost/revenue (W111.2)
- Automated anomaly detection with configurable thresholds (> 20% deviation from seasonal, trend, or peer benchmarks) (W111.3)
- Monthly utility dashboard with multi-dimensional benchmarking (W111.5)
- Quarterly energy review with efficiency project pipeline and capex integration (W111.6)
- Annual utility budget per location integrated with W26 budget module (W111.7)
- Integration with W7C (utility bill processing), W16 (new store — initial utility setup), W21 (energy efficiency capex), W26 (annual budget), W47 (facility maintenance — HVAC/lighting repair), W67 (store performance — utility cost as P&L line), W86 (planogram — lighting for displays), W96 (renovation — utility impact)

### Pain Points / Risks
- Utility bill lag and estimation — Meralco and other Philippine utilities sometimes issue estimated bills rather than actual readings, distorting consumption tracking and anomaly detection accuracy
- Anomaly investigation burden on Store Managers — a 20% consumption spike alert may be caused by legitimate factors (summer HVAC, renovation activity) but still requires 30 min of documentation, creating alert fatigue over time
- Energy efficiency capex payback uncertainty — projected savings from LED retrofits or HVAC upgrades may not materialize if store operating hours or equipment usage patterns change after installation
- Utility provider rate volatility — Philippine electricity rates fluctuate significantly (generation charge, transmission charge, VAT components), making annual utility budgeting (W26) unreliable and complicating store-level P&L forecasting

---

### Time Estimate
AP Clerk: ~5 min additional per utility bill for consumption data entry (800+ bills/month = ~65 hours/month). Facilities Manager: 1 hour/month dashboard review + 2 hours/quarter energy review + 8 hours annual budget preparation. Store Manager: ~30 min per anomaly alert (1–2 per quarter). DC Manager: 30 min/month DC utility review.

### Staffing Implication
- **1 Facilities Manager** (within Store Operations team of ~23 at HQ): manages chain-wide energy management, utility vendor relationships, and energy efficiency projects. This role is already implied by W47 (facility maintenance coordination) but formalized here.
- **Store Managers**: 30 min per anomaly alert (estimated 1–2 alerts/quarter/store). Absorbed.
- **AP Clerks**: 5 min additional per utility bill for consumption data entry = ~60–80 hours/month for 800+ bills. Absorbed within existing AP team.
- **No incremental headcount.**

## W170. Senior Citizen & PWD Discount Compliance (PH Legal)

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer presents Senior Citizen (SC) or Person with Disability (PWD) ID at POS |
| **Frequency** | High; ~5–10% of retail transactions in the Philippines |
| **Volume** | ~70,000–140,000 transactions/month chain-wide |
| **Owner** | Lead Cashier |
| **Participants** | Cashier, SC/PWD Customer, Store Manager (for high-value overrides) |

### Background

Philippine law (RA 9994 and RA 10754) mandates a 20% discount and 12% VAT exemption on specific "basic necessities and prime commodities" for SCs and PWDs. For hardware stores, this applies to specific items like light bulbs, batteries, and certain construction materials used for home repair, subject to DTI/DA quantity limits.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Identification**: Customer presents OSCA (Senior) or PWD ID card + Purchase Book | Cashier | — | 1 min |
| 2 | **Validation**: Cashier verifies ID authenticity and matches name to the customer; checks Purchase Book for prior purchases of the same items to ensure within weekly quantity limits (e.g., PHP 1,300/week for basic goods) | Cashier | — | 2 min |
| 3 | **POS Entry**: Cashier selects "SC/PWD Discount" in POS; system prompts for ID number and Name; system automatically filters items eligible for discount/VAT-exempt status per BIR/DTI rules | Cashier | — | 1 min |
| 4 | **Transaction**: System applies 20% discount on eligible items and removes 12% VAT; receipt prints with mandatory SC/PWD disclosure (Name, ID#, Gross Sale, Discount, VAT Exempt Sales) | System | — | Automated |
| 5 | **Logbook Entry**: Cashier manually records transaction in the mandatory BIR/OSCA Logbook (Legal requirement for audit) | Cashier | Lead Cashier | 2 min |
| 6 | **Filing**: Monthly: Finance consolidates SC/PWD sales for BIR Form 2550M (VAT) and 1702 (Income Tax) reporting, claiming the discount as a deductible expense | Finance | Controller | 4 hours |

### System Touchpoints
- POS SC/PWD discount module with ID number capture and automatic eligible-item filtering per BIR/DTI rules (W170.3)
- BIR-compliant receipt printing with mandatory SC/PWD disclosure fields (W170.4)
- BIR/OSCA electronic logbook integration or manual logbook template (W170.5)
- GL posting segregation for SC/PWD discount expense and VAT-exempt sales for BIR Form 2550M reporting (W170.6)
- Integration with W5B (POS selling), W9A (BIR VAT reporting), W54 (LGU compliance)

### Time Estimate
~6 min per SC/PWD transaction at POS (1 min ID verification + 2 min Purchase Book check + 1 min POS entry + 2 min logbook entry). At 70,000–140,000 transactions/month chain-wide, total cashier time is ~6,000–14,000 hours/month. Finance monthly consolidation: 4 hours.

### Pain Points / Risks
- Manual BIR/OSCA logbook entry is time-consuming and error-prone — cashiers handwriting entries during peak hours creates queues and risks illegible or incomplete records that fail BIR audit
- Eligible item determination complexity — BIR/DTI rules on which hardware items qualify for SC/PWD discount are ambiguous (e.g., is cement a "basic necessity"?), leading to inconsistent application across stores and customer disputes
- Purchase Book tracking limitation — cashiers cannot easily verify a customer's cumulative weekly purchases across multiple BuildRight stores, allowing customers to exceed quantity limits by splitting purchases between locations
- BIR audit exposure — failure to maintain complete SC/PWD logbook records or incorrectly applying discount/VAT-exempt calculations can result in BIR penalties and VAT assessment during tax examination

### Staffing Implication
- Each cashier handles 10–20 SC/PWD transactions/day. No incremental headcount; training is critical.
- Finance adds 4 hours/month for BIR Form 2550M consolidation of SC/PWD sales. Absorbed within existing Finance team.
- Cashier training on eligible-item rules and logbook compliance is essential — recommend quarterly refresher sessions (30 min each) to reduce BIR audit exposure.

---

## W171. Store Physical Security & Yard Patrol Routine

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Hourly patrol requirement; or high-risk alert (storm, local unrest) |
| **Frequency** | Hourly (24/7) |
| **Volume** | Covers all 200 stores and 4 DCs |
| **Owner** | Store Security Lead |
| **Participants** | Security Guard, Store Manager |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Patrol Start**: Guard initiates patrol on handheld device/app; scans NFC tag at the Guard Post | Security Guard | — | 1 min |
| 2 | **Perimeter Check**: Walk exterior walls; check for structural breaches, fence tampering, or unauthorized loitering | Security Guard | — | 10 min |
| 3 | **Lumber Yard/Outdoor Inventory**: Verify seals on outdoor containers; check that bulky stock (cement, steel) is covered/secured; scan NFC tags at yard corners | Security Guard | — | 15 min |
| 4 | **Utility & Fire Check**: Inspect fire pump room, generator set (check fuel/oil levels), and electrical sub-station for any leaks or abnormal noise | Security Guard | — | 10 min |
| 5 | **Backroom/Receiving**: Verify that the receiving dock is locked and no staff are in restricted areas after-hours | Security Guard | — | 5 min |
| 6 | **Digital Log**: Guard submits patrol report via app with photos of any "Alert" items; Store Manager receives instant notification for "Critical" issues | Security Guard | Store Mgr | 2 min |

### System Touchpoints
- Guard patrol app with NFC tag scanning at checkpoint locations (W171.1, W171.3)
- Digital patrol report submission with photo attachment capability (W171.6)
- Real-time critical issue notification to Store Manager via mobile app
- Integration with W47 (facility maintenance work orders for structural/fire issues), W71 (physical security & alarm management), W37 (loss prevention — outdoor inventory theft detection)

### Time Estimate
~45 min per patrol round (1 min start + 10 min perimeter + 15 min yard inventory + 10 min utility/fire + 5 min backroom + 2 min reporting). Hourly patrols × 24 hours = ~18 hours/day of guard patrol time per store. Each guard conducts 8–12 patrols per 8-hour shift.

### Pain Points / Risks
- Guard complacency during routine patrols — after weeks of uneventful rounds, guards may conduct superficial walkthroughs without thorough inspection, missing early signs of fence tampering or inventory pilferage
- NFC tag vandalism — outdoor checkpoint tags can be damaged or removed by unauthorized persons, allowing guards to skip sections of the patrol without detection
- Weather-related patrol gaps — during typhoons or heavy rain, guards suspend exterior patrols, leaving outdoor lumber yard and building materials inventory unmonitored for extended periods
- Critical issue response delay if Store Manager is unreachable after hours — the notification system depends on the Store Manager receiving and acting on alerts; if they are asleep or in a dead-signal area, response is delayed until the Facilities Coordinator backup is contacted

### Staffing Implication
- Guard force: 2 guards per store (day + night shift) as part of contracted security service (W71). Patrol duties absorbed into existing guard deployment.
- No incremental BuildRight headcount. Guard patrol app and NFC infrastructure are one-time setup costs per store.
- Facilities Coordinator reviews patrol reports as part of weekly security oversight — adds ~30 min/week.

## W173. Store-Level Solar Energy Monitoring

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Daily performance tracking; or "Inverter Failure" alert from IoT system |
| **Frequency** | Daily monitoring; Quarterly cleaning |
| **Volume** | Applicable to ~80 stores with rooftop solar installations |
| **Owner** | Facilities Coordinator |
| **Participants** | Store Maintenance, Solar Vendor (External) |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Generation Tracking**: System pulls data from Solar Inverters; compares "Actual Generation" vs. "Expected" (based on weather/irradiance) | System | Facilities Coord | Automated |
| 2 | **Anomaly Detection**: If generation drops > 15% below expected: system creates a W47 maintenance ticket; Facilities Coordinator reviews CCTV/Drone footage for panel obstructions (dust, debris) | System | Facilities Coord | 15 min |
| 3 | **Cleaning Routine**: Quarterly: Store Maintenance or Vendor performs panel cleaning to remove soot/dust (critical in high-traffic/industrial areas) | Maintenance | Store Mgr | 4 hours |
| 4 | **Savings Verification**: Monthly: Finance compares Solar Generation (Self-Consumption) against Meralco/Utility bill reduction to verify ROI | Finance | Controller | 1 hour |

### System Touchpoints
- IoT solar inverter integration with automated generation data pull and expected vs. actual comparison (W173.1)
- Automated anomaly detection with W47 maintenance ticket auto-creation for generation drops > 15% (W173.2)
- Utility bill comparison module for savings verification (W173.4)
- Integration with W47 (facility maintenance — solar panel repair/cleaning), W111 (energy & utility consumption management — solar offsets utility cost)

### Time Estimate
Facilities Coordinator: ~15 min per anomaly alert investigation (estimated 2–4 per month across 80 solar-equipped stores). Finance: 1 hour/month for savings verification. Quarterly panel cleaning: 4 hours per store per cleaning event. Total chain-wide monitoring: ~8–10 hours/month for Facilities Coordinator across all 80 solar stores.

### Pain Points / Risks
- Panel degradation underestimation — Philippine tropical conditions (humidity, salt air near coastal stores, volcanic ash) accelerate panel degradation beyond manufacturer projections, reducing actual ROI vs. business case assumptions
- Inverter failure response lag — if an inverter fails and the Facilities Coordinator does not investigate the anomaly alert promptly, the store loses days or weeks of solar generation, directly impacting utility cost savings
- Cleaning vendor quality inconsistency — quarterly panel cleaning by store maintenance staff may be inadequate; professional cleaning vendors add cost but are necessary for stores in industrial or high-dust areas
- Net metering application delays — Meralco net metering applications for excess solar generation can take 6–12 months for approval, during which excess energy is exported to the grid without compensation

### Staffing Implication
- Facilities Coordinator: ~8–10 hours/month for solar monitoring across 80 stores. Absorbed within existing role.
- Store Maintenance: 4 hours/quarter per store for panel cleaning. Absorbed within existing Maintenance/Utility staff duties at solar-equipped stores.
- Finance: 1 hour/month for savings verification. No incremental headcount.
- Solar vendor (external) handles major repairs and inverter replacements under service contract.

## W176. Store-to-DC Reverse Logistics (Consolidation)

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Accumulation of customer returns, overstock, or defective items at store level requiring vendor return |
| **Frequency** | Bi-weekly per store |
| **Volume** | ~2–5 pallets per shipment |
| **Owner** | Store Receiving Clerk |
| **Participants** | Store Receiving Clerk, DC Receiving Clerk, Logistics (Fleet) |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Consolidation**: Store Receiving Clerk identifies items in "RTV Staging Area" (from W12 returns or W91 damage); groups by Vendor and Reason (Defective, Overstock) | Store Clerk | Asst Store Mgr | 1 hour |
| 2 | **Transfer Creation**: Clerk creates a "Reverse Stock Transfer Order (RSTO)" in ERP from Store to Hub DC; system ensures items are non-saleable in store inventory | Store Clerk | — | 15 min |
| 3 | **Palletization**: Items are boxed and palletized; "Return Shipment" labels generated from ERP | Store Clerk | — | 30 min |
| 4 | **Backhaul Pickup**: Logistics truck (after making a store delivery, W4) picks up the return pallets (Backhaul) | Driver | — | 15 min |
| 5 | **DC Receipt**: DC Receiving Clerk receives pallets; scan-verifies against RSTO; moves to DC RTV Zone for consolidated shipment to Vendor (W88) | DC Clerk | DC Supervisor | 30 min |

### System Touchpoints
- Reverse Stock Transfer Order (RSTO) creation
- Backhaul load planning (Logistics integration)
- Consolidated RTV tracking (Store -> DC -> Vendor)

### Time Estimate
~2 hours per store bi-weekly consolidation event (1 hour item identification/grouping + 15 min RSTO creation + 30 min palletization + 15 min handoff to driver). At 200 stores × bi-weekly = ~400 hours/2 weeks chain-wide. DC receipt adds 30 min per incoming return shipment.

### Pain Points / Risks
- RSTO accuracy — store Receiving Clerks may incorrectly classify items (e.g., marking damaged goods as overstock), causing downstream processing errors at the DC RTV zone and vendor rejection of returns
- Backhaul scheduling dependency — reverse logistics pallets are picked up only when a DC delivery truck is already making a store drop-off; if DC delivery frequency changes, return pallets accumulate at the store, consuming backroom space
- Fragile item damage during palletization — returned appliances and fixtures improperly packed for transport arrive at DC in worse condition, reducing vendor credit recovery value
- Vendor credit note delays — even after items reach DC and are shipped to vendor, credit notes may take 30–60 days, leaving the original store carrying the COGS impact on its P&L during the interim

### Staffing Implication
- Receiving Clerk: ~2 hours bi-weekly per store for consolidation and palletization. Absorbed within existing 2 Receiving Clerks per store.
- DC Receiving Clerk: ~30 min per incoming return shipment. Absorbed within existing DC receiving team.
- No incremental headcount. Bi-weekly cadence ensures manageable workload per event.

---

## W177. Vending & Concessionaire Management

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | New concessionaire application; monthly rent/commission calculation |
| **Frequency** | ~1–3 new concessionaire onboardings per store per year; monthly billing cycle |
| **Volume** | ~200–600 active concessionaire agreements chain-wide (1–3 per store across 200 stores) |
| **Owner** | Store Manager |
| **Participants** | Concessionaire, Finance (AR), Store Operations |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Onboarding**: Store Manager reviews application for in-store cafe, snack bar, or ATM space; validates compliance with safety/health standards | Store Mgr | — | 2 hours |
| 2 | **Contract Setup**: Legal/Finance setup lease in ERP (W117); defines Rent or % Commission on Sales | Finance | — | 1 hour |
| 3 | **Sales Tracking**: If commission-based: Concessionaire sales are rung through a dedicated "Non-Inventory" SKU at POS; system tracks total monthly throughput | Cashier | — | Continuous |
| 4 | **Billing**: Monthly: Finance generates invoice for Rent + Utilities + Commission; offsets any payments collected on behalf of concessionaire | AR Clerk | Controller | 30 min |

### System Touchpoints
- Lease management integration (W117)
- Non-inventory POS SKU for concessionaire sales tracking
- Automated monthly billing

### Time Estimate
~2 hours per new concessionaire onboarding + 1 hour contract setup. Ongoing: ~30 min/month for Finance billing and reconciliation per concessionaire. Store Manager oversight: ~15 min/month per concessionaire. With an estimated 1–3 concessionaires per store across 200 stores, total ongoing management is ~100–300 hours/month chain-wide.

### Pain Points / Risks
- Concessionaire sales underreporting — commission-based concessionaires may under-ring sales or process transactions outside the dedicated POS SKU, reducing BuildRight's commission revenue
- Health and safety liability — food concessionaires (cafes, snack bars) must comply with LGU health permits and food safety standards; any violation reflects on BuildRight's store reputation and may trigger LGU inspection of the entire premises
- Utility cost allocation disputes — concessionaires consuming disproportionate electricity or water relative to their leased space, with no sub-metering to accurately allocate costs
- Concessionaire abandonment — operator ceases business but leaves equipment and contractual obligations unresolved, requiring legal intervention per W62 contract termination

### Staffing Implication
- Store Manager: ~15 min/month per concessionaire for oversight and coordination. With 1–3 per store, this is ~15–45 min/month. Absorbed.
- Finance (AR Clerk): ~30 min/month per concessionaire for billing and reconciliation. Across 200–600 agreements chain-wide, this is ~100–300 hours/month. Absorbed within existing AR team.
- No incremental headcount. Onboarding events (1–3 per store per year) handled by existing Store Manager and Legal/Finance teams.

## W182. Gift / Home Registry Lifecycle

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer (Home Builder/Renovator) creates a "Project Wishlist" or "Registry" |
| **Frequency** | ~50–100 new registries per month |
| **Volume** | Average registry value PHP 20,000–100,000; 10–30 items per registry |
| **Owner** | Customer Service Rep (CSR) |
| **Participants** | Customer, Sales Associate, Gift Givers |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Creation**: Customer creates Registry via Website or In-store Tablet; specifies "Housewarming" or "Renovation Project" | Customer | — | 15 min |
| 2 | **Item Selection**: Customer walks store with Sales Associate; scans items into Registry using Mobile App | Customer / Assoc | — | 1 hour |
| 3 | **Sharing**: Customer shares Registry link with family/friends; or provides "Registry ID" for in-store lookup | Customer | — | — |
| 4 | **Purchase**: Giver buys item from Registry (In-store or Online); system marks item as "Purchased" to prevent duplicates | Cashier / System | — | 2 min |
| 5 | **Fulfillment**: Items can be picked up by Giver, or held at CSR counter for "Consolidated Delivery" to the Registry Owner | CSR | Store Mgr | 10 min |
| 6 | **Completion**: Registry Owner receives "Completion Discount" (e.g., 10% off remaining items) after the event date | System | — | Automated |

### System Touchpoints
- Registry master data (Customer + Items + Event Date)
- Real-time "Purchased" status sync across channels
- Completion discount logic (Promo engine integration)

### Time Estimate
~1 hour 15 min per registry creation (15 min setup + 1 hour item selection walk). Ongoing per-registry maintenance: ~10 min. At 50–100 new registries/month chain-wide, total CSR and Sales Associate time is ~60–125 hours/month. Gift giver purchases are standard POS transactions (~3 min each).

### Pain Points / Risks
- Low registry completion rate — Philippine hardware/home improvement registries are a nascent concept; most gift givers prefer cash gifts, leading to registries with few purchased items and disappointed registry owners
- Inventory availability changes during the registry lifecycle — items selected into the registry may go out of stock or be discontinued (W68) before gift givers purchase them, requiring CSR outreach to update the registry
- Consolidated delivery logistics complexity — holding purchased items at the CSR counter for a consolidated delivery to the registry owner requires dedicated staging space and coordination, competing with layaway and BOPIS staging areas
- Completion discount margin erosion — offering 10% off remaining items after the event may cannibalize full-margin sales if customers create registries primarily to access the completion discount rather than for genuine gift coordination

### Staffing Implication
- CSR: ~1 hour 15 min per registry creation. At ~0.25–0.5 new registries per store per month, total CSR time is ~30–60 min/store/month. Absorbed.
- Sales Associate: 1 hour per registry item selection walk. Rotational duty — absorbed within existing floor staff.
- No incremental headcount. Registry volume is low enough to be handled as an additional CSR service alongside returns and layaway.

## W205. Employee Purchase Program & Internal Staff Sales

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Employee wishes to purchase merchandise for personal use |
| **Frequency** | Daily; ~1,000 transactions/month across group |
| **Volume** | Covers all 6,715+ employees |
| **Owner** | Store Manager / HR Manager |
| **Participants** | Employee (Buyer), Cashier, HR (for eligibility) |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Eligibility Check**: Employee presents ID at POS; System verifies active employment status and "Monthly Purchase Limit" (e.g., PHP 20,000/month) | Cashier / System | HR Manager | 1 min |
| 2 | **Discount Application**: System applies "Staff Discount" (e.g., Cost + 5% or 15% off SRP) based on product category rules | System | — | Real-time |
| 3 | **Payment**: Employee pays via Cash, Card, or "Payroll Deduction" (W10/W76) | Employee | Cashier | 2 min |
| 4 | **Verification**: Manager signs receipt; confirms items are for personal use (not resale) | Store Manager | — | 1 min |
| 5 | **Audit**: LP Team reviews high-frequency staff purchases for potential abuse (W37) | LP Analyst | VP Compliance | Monthly |

### System Touchpoints
- POS Employee Purchase tender type with employee ID verification and active employment status check via HR integration (W205.1)
- Automated staff discount application with configurable category-level rules (cost + 5% or 15% off SRP) (W205.2)
- Monthly purchase limit enforcement per employee (e.g., PHP 20,000/month) with running total tracking (W205.1)
- Payroll deduction integration for employee purchases via salary deduction (W10/W76) (W205.3)
- LP audit dashboard for high-frequency and high-value employee purchase pattern detection (W37) (W205.5)
- Integration with W5B (POS selling — employee purchase workflow), W10 (payroll — salary deduction), W37 (loss prevention — abuse detection), W72 (HR — employee records)

### Time Estimate
~5 min per employee purchase transaction (1 min eligibility check + 2 min payment + 1 min manager sign-off + 1 min receipt). At ~1,000 transactions/month across 6,715+ employees, total POS time is ~80 hours/month. LP monthly audit: 2–4 hours. HR eligibility verification is automated.

### Pain Points / Risks
- Resale abuse — employees purchasing items at staff discount for resale to third parties (friends, sari-sari stores, online marketplace) at profit, violating the "personal use only" policy and eroding margin on discounted items
- Manager sign-off bypass — Store Managers rubber-stamping employee purchase receipts without verifying items are for personal use, especially during busy periods when the 1-min sign-off is rushed
- Payroll deduction disputes — employees disputing salary deductions for purchases they claim were unauthorized or for different amounts than expected; resolution requires cross-referencing POS transaction logs with payroll records
- Policy inconsistency across stores — different Store Managers applying different interpretations of what constitutes "personal use" (e.g., purchasing materials for a family member's home renovation), creating fairness complaints and potential HR grievances

### Staffing Implication
- ~5 employee purchases per store per day × ~5 min each = ~25 min/store/day of POS time. Absorbed by existing Cashiers.
- Store Manager sign-off: ~1 min per transaction. LP monthly audit: 2–4 hours chain-wide. No incremental headcount.
- HR handles payroll deduction disputes as part of standard HR operations — estimated 5–10 disputes/month chain-wide.

## W212. Automated Store Cash Management & Smart Safe Integration

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Shift end; or cash drawer limit reached |
| **Frequency** | 2–3 times per day per cashier |
| **Volume** | Covers all 200 stores; ~600 POS terminals |
| **Owner** | Store Cashier Supervisor |
| **Participants** | Cashier, Supervisor, CIT (Cash-in-Transit) Partner |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Drawer Limit Alert**: System triggers alert when POS cash drawer exceeds threshold (e.g., PHP 50,000) | System | Cashier | Real-time |
| 2 | **Cash Skimming**: Cashier counts excess cash; Supervisor verifies; records "Skim" in POS | Cashier | Supervisor | 5 min |
| 3 | **Smart Safe Deposit**: Cashier inserts bills into the in-store Smart Safe; safe validates bills and counts in real-time | Cashier | — | 3 min |
| 4 | **Credit Recognition**: Smart Safe API sends deposit confirmation to ERP/Bank; Bank provides "Same-Day Credit" to BuildRight's account | System | Finance | Automated |
| 5 | **EOD Reconciliation**: At EOD, system reconciles POS Sales vs. Smart Safe Deposits vs. Remaining Drawer Cash; flags variances | System | Supervisor | Automated |
| 6 | **CIT Pickup**: CIT partner (e.g., G4S/Securicor) empties safe when full; system logs pickup; safe resets for next cycle | CIT Partner | — | 20 min |

### System Touchpoints
- Smart Safe API integration with ERP and Bank (W30)
- POS cash limit alerts and skimming workflow
- Automated daily bank reconciliation for cash deposits
- CIT pickup tracking and safe capacity monitoring

### Time Estimate
~8 min per skim event (5 min count/verify + 3 min smart safe deposit). At 2–3 times per cashier per day × 5 terminals = 10–15 skim events per store per day = ~80–120 min/store/day. EOD reconciliation: automated. CIT pickup: 20 min per visit (typically 1–2 times/week). Chain-wide daily skim labor: ~270–400 hours/day.

### Pain Points / Risks
- Smart Safe hardware failure — if the safe's bill validator jams or the API connection to the bank fails, cash cannot be deposited, and same-day credit is lost, forcing manual cash handling until repair
- Skimming count discrepancies — cashier and supervisor counts disagree on the excess amount, causing delays and disputes that require Store Manager intervention and CCTV review per W272
- CIT pickup scheduling rigidity — armored car partners operate on fixed schedules; if a store's safe reaches capacity between scheduled pickups, cash must be held overnight in the manual safe, increasing security risk
- Bank credit timing dependency — same-day credit relies on the smart safe deposit being processed before the bank's cutoff time (typically 3:00 PM for Philippine banks); late-day deposits receive next-day credit, affecting daily cash position reporting for Treasury

### Staffing Implication
- Cashier Supervisor handles 10–15 skim events per store per day (~80–120 min). Absorbed into existing supervisory duties.
- CIT partner visits 1–2 times/week per store (20 min each). No incremental BuildRight headcount.
- Smart Safe hardware is a per-store capex investment (~PHP 150K–300K per unit). Justified by reduced cash handling risk and faster bank credit.

---

## W206. Mobile POS (mPOS) & Queue-Busting Operations

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | High foot traffic / long queues at main checkout; or customer needing checkout in Yard/Materials area |
| **Frequency** | Peak hours (weekends, holidays); and for bulky outdoor sales |
| **Volume** | ~5–10% of total store transactions |
| **Owner** | Store Manager |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate (mPOS Operator), Cashier (for cash handling if needed), Customer |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Deployment**: Store Manager monitors queues; dispatches Sales Associates with tablets/mPOS devices to the floor | Store Manager | — | 2 min |
| 2 | **Scanning**: Associate scans items in customer's cart or in the Yard area; system applies real-time pricing and promos | Associate | — | 3–5 min |
| 3 | **Customer Lookup**: Associate scans Loyalty Card or looks up customer via phone/email (W17) | Associate | — | 1 min |
| 4 | **Tendering**: (a) If Card/E-wallet: Associate processes payment on the mPOS device; (b) If Cash: Associate generates "Pre-order Barcode" and directs customer to "Express Cash" lane | Associate | — | 2 min |
| 5 | **Receipt**: System generates Digital Receipt (Email/SMS) or prints via Bluetooth belt printer | System | — | 30 sec |
| 6 | **Fulfillment**: If items are in Yard: Associate marks items as "Released" in system; guard verifies digital receipt at gate | Associate | — | 2 min |
| 7 | **Sync**: Transactions sync instantly with central ERP inventory and Finance; no manual EOD re-entry required | System | — | Automated |

### System Touchpoints
- mPOS tablet application with full SKU/Price/Loyalty integration
- Bluetooth printer / Digital receipt integration
- Yard-gate verification dashboard
- Real-time inventory deduction (same as fixed POS)

### Time Estimate
~10–12 min per mPOS transaction (3–5 min scanning + 1 min customer lookup + 2 min tendering + 30 sec receipt + 2 min yard release if applicable). Deployment decision: ~2 min Store Manager assessment. At ~5–10% of total store transactions (~23–47 mPOS transactions/store/day), mPOS handles ~4,600–9,400 transactions/day chain-wide.

### Pain Points / Risks
- WiFi coverage dead zones in lumber yard and outdoor materials areas — mPOS tablets lose connectivity, forcing associates to walk the customer back to a fixed POS terminal, defeating the queue-busting purpose
- Cash handling vulnerability — mPOS "Express Cash" lane introduces a second cash handling point without the same dual-control safeguards as fixed terminals, increasing cash variance risk
- Battery life limitations during peak hours — tablet batteries drain under continuous use; if charging stations are not readily available, mPOS devices become unavailable during the busiest periods
- Yard gate verification bottleneck — associates marking items as "Released" in the yard require guard verification at exit; if the guard is occupied, customers wait with large items, creating a new bottleneck outside the store

### Staffing Implication
- Sales Associates serve as mPOS operators during peak hours. No incremental headcount — redeployed from floor selling during queue-busting periods.
- Each store needs 2–3 mPOS tablets and trained operators. Training is brief (~30 min per associate) and absorbed into onboarding.
- Peak-hour deployment consumes ~2–3 Sales Associate hours/store/day during weekends and holidays. Offset by reduced queue abandonment.

## W248. Store Inventory Variance & LP Investigation

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | High-value inventory variance flagged in cycle counts (W6) or annual physical count (W42) |
| **Frequency** | Ad-hoc (weekly reviews) |
| **Volume** | ~5–10 investigations per store/month |
| **Owner** | Store Loss Prevention Officer |
| **Participants** | Store Manager, LP Auditor, Cashier Supervisor, WMS/ERP IT Specialist |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Alert Generation**: System auto-flags high-value SKU discrepancies (> PHP 10,000 variance or > 5% of bin stock) during count post-reconciliation (W6/W42). | System | — | Automated |
| 2 | **Audit Trail Review**: LP Officer extracts transactional audit logs for the flagged SKU: reviews receiving logs (W3/W18), transfer records (W22), POS sales details, and manual adjustment histories. | LP Officer | Store Manager | 1 hour |
| 3 | **CCTV Timeline Correlation**: LP Officer maps count timestamp against CCTV footage using smart cameras with POS transaction overlay (W207); audits cash drawers for cashier scan errors. | LP Officer | — | 2 hours |
| 4 | **Stock Verification**: LP team conducts a physical floor sweep and structural review of the adjacent bin locations to eliminate human putaway errors (W109). | LP Auditor | LP Officer | 30 min |
| 5 | **Investigation Ruling**: LP Officer classifies the root cause: (a) Internal Theft / Shoplifting → triggers police/legal escalation; (b) Process Error (wrong SKU scanned at POS/GR) → triggers cashier/receiving retraining; (c) System Glitch → escalates to IT. | LP Officer | Store Manager | 1 hour |
| 6 | **Adjustment Regularization**: Store Manager authorizes stock correction journal in ERP per W92 adjustment guidelines to restore inventory count accuracy. | Store Manager | — | 10 min |

### System Touchpoints
- Real-time inventory discrepancy reporting dashboard
- Transactional audit logs with cashier ID and timestamp overlays
- Integration with W6 (Cycle counts), W42 (Physical audit), W92 (Stock adjustment), W207 (CCTV Loss Prevention)

### Time Estimate
~4.5–5 hours per investigation (automated alert + 1 hour audit trail review + 2 hours CCTV correlation + 30 min floor sweep + 1 hour investigation ruling + 10 min adjustment). At 5–10 investigations per store per month across 200 stores, total LP labor is ~4,500–10,000 hours/month chain-wide.

### Pain Points / Risks
- Investigation backlog — with 5–10 investigations per store per month and limited LP Officer capacity, lower-value variances may be deprioritized or closed without thorough investigation, allowing systematic theft patterns to persist
- CCTV footage quality and coverage gaps — not all store areas have camera coverage, and footage from older analog cameras may be insufficient for positive identification; the 30-day retention window also limits historical investigation capability
- Internal theft investigation sensitivity — when investigation points to employee theft, the LP Officer must balance evidence gathering with employee rights under Philippine Labor Code; premature accusation or insufficient documentation can lead to illegal dismissal cases filed with NLRC
- Stock adjustment authorization abuse — Store Managers authorizing stock correction journals to clear variances without completing full investigation, masking ongoing shrinkage and undermining LP data integrity

### Staffing Implication
- LP Officers handle 5–10 investigations per store per month at ~5 hours each = ~25–50 hours/store/month. With regional LP Officers covering multiple stores, workload is heavy but manageable with prioritization.
- Store Manager involvement: ~10 min per investigation for stock adjustment authorization. Absorbed.
- **Regional LP Officers**: At ~4,500–10,000 hours/month chain-wide, the LP team is staffed with 20 LP Officers (1 per 10 stores) for effective variance investigation coverage across the 200-store chain.

## W272. Cashier Over/Short Dispute & Deduction Resolution

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | End-of-day cash count reveals a variance (shortage or overage) beyond the allowable threshold. |
| **Frequency** | Daily |
| **Volume** | Varies (1-5 per store per week) |
| **Owner** | Store Manager |
| **Participants** | Cashier, Store Cashier Supervisor, HR, Finance |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | System flags variance during W5F (Store Closing): Z-report shows cash count vs. system total differ by > PHP 200 | System | Cashier Supervisor | 5 min |
| 2 | Cashier and Supervisor recount the till together; both sign a variance incident report with count details | Cashier / Supervisor | Store Manager | 15 min |
| 3 | Store Manager reviews CCTV footage, POS transaction log, and void/refund history (W37) for the cashier's shift | Store Manager | Regional Manager | 30 min |
| 4 | If shortage is confirmed due to cashier error (not system or customer cause), Store Manager routes incident to HR with supporting evidence | Store Manager | HR Manager | 10 min |
| 5 | HR generates salary deduction authorization per PH Labor Code Art. 113 (max 1 week's pay per incident; written consent required); cashier signs | HR Manager | Store Manager | 30 min |
| 6 | Payroll deducts approved amount from next semi-monthly run; Finance reconciles cash clearing account (W10) | Payroll Officer | Finance Manager | Automated |

### System Touchpoints
- POS EOD Variance Reporting (W5F Z-report)
- CCTV / LP Exception Dashboard (W37)
- HR Incident and Deduction Module (W72)
- Payroll Deduction Processing (W10)
- Cash Clearing Account in GL (W9A)
- Regional Manager Variance Alert Dashboard

### Pain Points / Risks
- DOLE disputes if salary deductions lack written authorization (Art. 113, Labor Code: max 1 week's pay, must not bring below minimum wage)
- Cashier morale and turnover risk from frequent investigations, especially in high-volume stores
- "Covering up" variances — cashiers personally covering shortages to avoid reports, masking systemic issues
- Union grievances if deduction policy is applied inconsistently across stores
- CCTV footage retention limitations (typically 30 days) delaying investigations for variances discovered late

### Staffing Implication
1-5 variances per store per week × 200 stores = 200-1,000 incidents/week chain-wide. Each investigation ~1-2 hours. Absorbed by Store Managers (200) with escalation to Regional Managers and HR for confirmed cases. No incremental headcount; workload is a known store management responsibility.

### Time Estimate
1-2 hours per incident (30 min initial investigation + 30-60 min for confirmed cases with HR processing).
Breakdown: 5 min Z-report variance flag + 15 min dual recount + 30 min CCTV/POS log review + 10 min HR routing if confirmed + 30 min HR deduction processing.
At 200-1,000 incidents/week chain-wide, total Store Manager investigation time is ~200-1,000 hours/week.

---

## W273. In-Store Endless Aisle & Vendor Direct-to-Customer Delivery

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer wants to purchase an out-of-stock or non-ranged bulky item while physically in the store. |
| **Frequency** | Moderate |
| **Volume** | 10-20 per store per day |
| **Owner** | Store Selling Staff |
| **Participants** | Customer, Cashier, Vendor, Ecommerce Fulfillment |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Sales Associate helps customer browse the extended catalog (Endless Aisle) on in-store tablet or POS kiosk, checking vendor real-time inventory (ECOM-001) | Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 15 min |
| 2 | Associate creates a Drop-Ship Sales Order in the system, linked to the customer's profile (W156) and the selected vendor/SKU | Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 5 min |
| 3 | Customer pays at the physical POS — cashier processes as "Endless Aisle / Special Order" tender type | Cashier | Store Manager | 5 min |
| 4 | System auto-generates a Drop-Ship Purchase Order (W38) to the vendor with customer delivery address and expected delivery date | System | Purchasing Dept | Automated |
| 5 | Vendor picks, packs, and ships directly to the customer's home; system sends tracking SMS/email to customer | Vendor / 3PL | Logistics | 1-5 days |
| 6 | Upon delivery, customer signs POD (Proof of Delivery); vendor uploads to BuildRight portal | Vendor | AP Clerk | 10 min |
| 7 | System 3-way matches: POS payment ↔ Drop-Ship PO ↔ Vendor invoice + POD; settles vendor payment per W7 | System | AP Clerk | Automated |

### System Touchpoints
- POS Endless Aisle module (catalog browsing, special order tender)
- Ecommerce extended catalog / PIM (W50) for vendor product data
- Procurement auto-PO generation (W38 special order fulfillment)
- AP 3-way match for drop-ship settlement (W7)
- CRM customer profile linkage (W156)
- Customer notification engine (SMS/email tracking)

### Pain Points / Risks
- Vendor inventory inaccuracy — item shown as available online but out of stock at vendor, causing order cancellation and customer frustration
- Delivery delays reflecting poorly on BuildRight's brand even though vendor is responsible
- Drop-ship PO and POS payment reconciliation complexity (timing mismatch between customer payment at POS and vendor invoice receipt)
- Vendor inability to deliver to provincial/remote addresses (Philippine logistics coverage gaps)
- No physical quality check before delivery — defective items shipped directly to customer
- Commission/attribution tracking for sales associates on endless aisle orders

### Staffing Implication
10-20 orders per store per day × 200 stores = 2,000-4,000 orders/day chain-wide. Each order takes ~25 min of store staff time (browsing + order creation + POS). Absorbed by existing Sales Associates and Cashiers. AP reconciliation adds ~1-2 FTE at shared services for the drop-ship invoice matching volume.

### Time Estimate
~25 min per transaction (in-store); 1-5 days for vendor delivery; automated settlement.
Breakdown: 15 min catalog browsing with customer + 5 min drop-ship order creation + 5 min POS payment processing.
AP reconciliation of drop-ship invoices adds ~1-2 FTE at shared services for the 2,000-4,000 daily orders chain-wide.


---

## W274. Third-Party Customer Financing / Equipment Leasing

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer opts to finance a high-value purchase using a third-party financing partner (e.g., Home Credit) in-store. |
| **Frequency** | Daily |
| **Volume** | 5-15 per store per day |
| **Owner** | Cashier |
| **Participants** | Customer, Financing Rep, Store Cashier, Treasury |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Customer selects high-value goods (e.g., appliances, power tools) and applies for financing with the in-store financing rep (e.g., Home Credit, CGCC) | Financing Rep | — | 15 min |
| 2 | Financing partner runs credit check and approves the loan; issues a reference code/voucher valid for 24 hours | Financing Partner | — | 10 min |
| 3 | Cashier rings up the sale on POS, selects "Third-Party Financing" as tender type, and inputs the reference code | Cashier | Store Manager | 5 min |
| 4 | Customer takes the goods; system records a receivable from the financing partner (not from the customer) | Customer | — | — |
| 5 | Treasury reconciles the batch of financed sales against the remittance from the financing partner (minus MDR of 3-7%) within T+3 business days (W261) | Treasury Analyst | Finance Manager | 2 hours/batch |
| 6 | Finance posts any MDR variance or unreconciled reference codes to a suspense account for follow-up | Finance Analyst | Controller | 30 min |

### System Touchpoints
- POS Tender Type configuration (Third-Party Financing with reference code validation)
- Financing Partner API integration (real-time credit approval)
- AR module (partner receivable, not customer receivable)
- Treasury reconciliation dashboard (W261 settlement matching)
- GL suspense account for unreconciled financing transactions
- Reporting: financing volume by partner, by store, by SKU category

### Pain Points / Risks
- Reference code mismatches between POS entry and partner system causing reconciliation failures
- Delayed remittances from financing partners (T+3 often becomes T+5+ in Philippine banking)
- MDR (Merchant Discount Rate) of 3-7% eroding margins on high-value items
- Financing partner system outages halting the entire checkout flow for financed purchases
- Customer disputes on financing terms (interest rates, hidden fees) blamed on BuildRight staff
- BSP regulations on consumer financing disclosures
- Risk of fraud — fictitious financing applications using stolen IDs (RA 10173 implications)

### Staffing Implication
5-15 financing transactions per store per day × 200 stores = 1,000-3,000/day chain-wide. POS processing ~5 min per transaction — absorbed by existing Cashiers. Treasury reconciliation ~2 hours/day for batch processing — 1 Treasury Analyst dedicated to financing partner reconciliation. Financing reps are employed by the partner, not BuildRight.

### Time Estimate
30 min per application (customer-facing); 5 min at POS; 2 hours daily for Treasury batch reconciliation.
Breakdown: 15 min customer credit application with financing rep + 10 min credit approval + 5 min POS tender processing.
Treasury batch reconciliation: 2 hours/day for 1,000-3,000 daily transactions across 200 stores. MDR variance follow-up: 30 min/day.

## W278. Mid-Day Cash Skimming / Till Sweeps

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | A POS till exceeds the maximum allowable cash holding threshold (e.g., PHP 50,000) during trading hours. |
| **Frequency** | Daily, multiple times per store |
| **Volume** | 2-5 skims per POS terminal per day |
| **Owner** | Store Cashier Supervisor |
| **Participants** | Cashier, Vault Custodian / Supervisor |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | POS system alerts Cashier and Supervisor that the till has exceeded the PHP 50,000 cash holding threshold | System | Cashier Supervisor | 5 min |
| 2 | Supervisor initiates a "Cash Drop / Skim" transaction on the POS terminal without closing the active shift | Cashier Supervisor | Store Manager | 2 min |
| 3 | Cashier counts out the excess large bills (PHP 500 and PHP 1,000 denominations) under dual-witness with Supervisor | Cashier | Cashier Supervisor | 3 min |
| 4 | POS prints a skim receipt showing amount, cashier ID, supervisor ID, and timestamp; both sign the physical receipt | System | Cashier Supervisor | 2 min |
| 5 | Supervisor immediately walks to back-office and deposits the skimmed cash into the Smart Safe (W212); Safe logs the deposit with unique transaction ID | Cashier Supervisor | Store Manager | 5 min |
| 6 | At EOD (W5F), the system calculates Total Cash = (Current Till + Sum of All Skims) for final Z-report balancing | System | Cashier Supervisor | Automated |

### System Touchpoints
- POS Skim / Cash Drop function (real-time threshold alerting)
- Smart Safe integration (W212 — deposit logging, denomination counting)
- EOD Z-report module (W5F — skim-inclusive cash reconciliation)
- LP Exception Dashboard (flagging stores with unusual skim frequency or amounts)
- Retail Store Backend (audit trail of all skim transactions)

### Pain Points / Risks
- Security risk of holding excessive cash in the till during peak hours (robbery / holdup risk in PH retail)
- Skim recording errors (wrong amount entered, receipt not signed) causing EOD reconciliation panic
- Supervisor unavailable during peak hours — cashier waiting with excess cash creates bottlenecks
- Smart Safe malfunctions forcing manual cash handling and delayed deposits
- LP risk: collusive skimming between cashier and supervisor (mitigated by dual-sign and CCTV)
- Discrepancy between POS skim record and Smart Safe deposit amount (requires investigation per W272)

### Staffing Implication
2-5 skims per POS terminal per day × ~5 terminals per store × 200 stores = 2,000-5,000 skims/day chain-wide. Each skim ~5-7 minutes of Supervisor time. Absorbed by existing Cashier Supervisors as a standard operational duty. No incremental headcount.

### Time Estimate
5-7 min per skim (17-35 min/day per terminal at 2-5 skims/day).
Breakdown: 2 min POS skim transaction initiation + 3 min dual-witness cash count + 2 min smart safe deposit walk + 1 min receipt verification.
At 2,000-5,000 skims/day chain-wide across 200 stores, total Supervisor skim labor is ~170-580 hours/day. EOD reconciliation is automated via smart safe integration.

## W281. Self-Checkout (SCO) Exception & Intervention Management

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer triggers a system exception at a self-checkout kiosk (e.g., weight mismatch, age-restricted item, voided item). |
| **Frequency** | Very High (Multiple times per hour) |
| **Volume** | Dozens per store per day |
| **Owner** | SCO Attendant |
| **Participants** | Customer, SCO Attendant, Loss Prevention |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Customer scans an item at the SCO kiosk; system detects an exception (weight mismatch in bagging area, age-restricted item, barcode duplication, or unexpected item removal) | Customer | — | — |
| 2 | SCO terminal locks the transaction, flashes an amber light above the kiosk, and displays a "Please Wait for Assistance" message | System | SCO Attendant | Real-time |
| 3 | SCO Attendant receives a mobile alert on their handheld device detailing the exception type, SKU, and kiosk number | System | SCO Attendant | < 10 sec |
| 4 | Attendant physically approaches the kiosk, verifies the customer and item (checks ID for age-restricted items per RA 7610, verifies product match for weight mismatches), and scans their employee badge to unlock the terminal | SCO Attendant | SCO Supervisor | 30-60 sec |
| 5 | Attendant clears the exception (approve, void item, or override price) and the transaction resumes; if fraud suspected, Attendant discreetly alerts LP via mobile app | SCO Attendant | LP Officer | 15-30 sec |
| 6 | System logs the override with: Attendant ID, exception type, SKU, timestamp, action taken, and kiosk number; feeds data to LP analytics dashboard (W37) | System | LP Officer | Automated |

### System Touchpoints
- POS SCO Module (weight-scale integration, age-restriction logic, barcode validation)
- SCO kiosk hardware (amber light indicator, customer-facing display)
- Mobile Staff App (real-time exception alerts to attendant handheld)
- LP Exception Dashboard (W37 — override frequency analytics, pattern detection)
- Age-Restricted Product Master (POS-018 integration)
- Employee Badge Scanner at SCO terminal

### Pain Points / Risks
- High intervention rates (>15% of transactions) lead to customer frustration and SCO abandonment, negating labor savings
- "Sweethearting" — attendants routinely clearing exceptions without proper verification, increasing shrinkage
- Weight-scale calibration drift causing false exceptions and unnecessary interventions
- Age-restricted items (solvents, cutting tools per POS-018) requiring manual ID verification slows the self-checkout experience
- LP risk: customers "skip-scanning" items during attendant distraction at another kiosk
- RA 7610 compliance — penalties for selling age-restricted products to minors without verification
- Equipment reliability — kiosk hardware failures (scanner, scale, card reader) requiring frequent IT dispatch (W265)

### Staffing Implication
Dozens of interventions per store per day × 200 stores = thousands of daily interventions chain-wide. Each intervention ~1-2 min. 1-2 SCO Attendants per store (200-400 FTE) dedicated to monitoring 4-6 SCO kiosks. SCO Supervisor oversees 2-3 Attendants. Significant staffing commitment but offset by reduced traditional cashier FTE.

### Time Estimate
30-90 seconds per intervention; SCO Attendant role is continuous monitoring during store hours.
Breakdown: 10 sec mobile alert receipt + 30-60 sec physical approach, verify, and badge scan + 15-30 sec exception clearance and transaction resumption.
Dozens of interventions per store per day × 1-2 min each = continuous Attendant workload during peak hours. 1-2 SCO Attendants per store (200-400 FTE chain-wide) offset by reduced traditional cashier FTE.
