## W330. In-Store Emergency Response Protocol

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Any emergency event occurring within the store premises that threatens the safety of customers, staff, or the public — including fire, medical emergency, structural failure (e.g., racking collapse, roof leak), bomb threat, civil disturbance, chemical spill (in-store), or severe weather while customers are present |
| **Frequency** | Rare; hopefully < 1 actual emergency per store per year; drills conducted quarterly per W158 |
| **Volume** | 200 stores × ~467 customers/day + 30 staff = ~18,000+ people at risk at any given time during operating hours |
| **Owner** | Store Manager (immediate response); Facilities Coordinator (post-incident coordination) |
| **Participants** | Store Manager, all store staff, Security Guard, Facilities Coordinator, Loss Prevention Officer, HR, Legal, local emergency services (PNP, BFP, EMS) |

### Background

W140 covers occupational health and safety incidents affecting employees (worker injury, workplace accident). W49 covers natural disaster/typhoon business continuity at the organizational level. Neither addresses in-store emergencies affecting a mixed population of customers and employees during business hours. BuildRight stores are large-format buildings (8,000–15,000 sqm) containing hazardous materials (paint, solvents, chemicals, lumber), heavy equipment displays, and outdoor yard areas. A structured emergency response protocol is essential for legal compliance (RA 11058, DOLE DO 198-18) and duty of care to customers.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Emergency detection and assessment**: First staff member to identify the emergency announces the situation to Store Manager (via radio or PA system); Store Manager classifies: (a) **Minor** — localized, contained, no evacuation needed (e.g., small spill, minor medical issue), (b) **Major** — threatens multiple areas, requires evacuation (e.g., fire, structural failure, bomb threat, chemical release), (c) **Life-threatening** — immediate danger to life, requires immediate evacuation and emergency services (e.g., large fire, active threat) | Staff Member / Store Manager | Store Manager | < 1 min |
| 2 | **Minor emergency response**: Store Manager dispatches trained first responder (at least 2 staff per store hold first aid/CPR certification): (a) Medical: first aid administered, customer/staff offered ambulance transport if needed, incident logged per W140 adapted for customer context, (b) Spill: hazmat spill kit deployed, area cordoned, cleanup per W238 adapted for retail quantities, (c) Structural: area evacuated internally, Facilities Coordinator contacted for W47 emergency work order | Trained Staff / Store Manager | Store Manager | 15–60 min |
| 3 | **Major/Life-threatening — Evacuation**: Store Manager activates store PA evacuation announcement (pre-recorded in English and Filipino); assigns staff to evacuation duties: (a) Floor marshals guide customers to nearest emergency exit (minimum 2 exits per store per BFP Fire Code), (b) Receiving dock staff secures dock doors and assists backroom evacuation, (c) Security guard manages main entrance — prevents new customers from entering, assists mobility-impaired customers, (d) Lumber yard/outdoor area staff guide customers to outdoor assembly point; headcount conducted at assembly point | All Staff | Store Manager | 5–10 min |
| 4 | **Emergency services notification**: Store Manager (or designated backup) contacts: (a) BFP (Bureau of Fire Protection) for fire/structural: 911 or local fire station, (b) EMS for medical emergency: local hospital ambulance, (c) PNP for security threat/civil disturbance: nearest police station; Store Manager provides: store address, nature of emergency, estimated number of persons, known injuries, hazardous materials present (paint, solvents) | Store Manager / Security Guard | Store Manager | 2–3 min |
| 5 | **Headcount and missing persons**: At assembly point, Department Supervisors conduct staff headcount against shift schedule (W34); Store Manager accounts for all on-duty staff; if anyone missing: (a) do NOT re-enter building, (b) inform arriving emergency services of missing person's last known location, (c) emergency services conduct search and rescue; for customers: Store Manager makes PA announcement asking customers to check their companions | Dept. Supervisors / Store Manager | Store Manager | 5–10 min |
| 6 | **Incident command**: Store Manager serves as Incident Commander until emergency services arrive; upon arrival, transfers command to senior emergency services officer; Store Manager provides: building layout, hazmat locations, utility shutoff locations, number of occupants, known injuries | Store Manager | Store Manager | Until relieved |
| 7 | **All-clear and re-entry**: Only emergency services officer gives all-clear; Store Manager: (a) inspects store for safety before allowing re-entry, (b) if store is unsafe: closes store per W45 emergency closure provisions, (c) if safe: allows staff and customers to return, resumes operations | Emergency Services / Store Manager | Store Manager | Varies |
| 8 | **Incident reporting**: Within 2 hours of incident: Store Manager files incident report in system: (a) time, location, nature of emergency, (b) number of persons affected, injuries, property damage, (c) response actions taken, (d) root cause (if known); Facilities Coordinator and HR notified; within 24 hours: Facilities Coordinator files regulatory report to BFP (if fire), DOLE (if employee injury per W140), LGU (if public safety incident) | Store Manager / Facilities Coordinator | Store Ops Director | 1–2 hours |
| 9 | **Post-incident actions**: (a) Facilities Coordinator arranges cleanup, repair, or temporary closure per W47, (b) HR initiates Critical Incident Stress Debriefing for affected staff, (c) Legal reviews incident for liability exposure and insurance claim per W59/W285, (d) Loss Prevention Officer investigates cause for prevention (W37), (e) Store Ops Director issues lessons-learned bulletin to all stores within 1 week | Facilities Coordinator / HR / Legal / LPO | Store Ops Director | 1–2 weeks |
| 10 | **Quarterly drill**: Facilities Coordinator schedules and coordinates quarterly emergency evacuation drill per W158 (BC drill); drill includes: evacuation timing target (< 5 minutes for full store evacuation), headcount accuracy verification, emergency equipment testing (fire extinguishers, alarms, emergency lighting, first aid kits), post-drill debrief with improvement actions | Facilities Coordinator / Store Manager | Store Ops Director | 1 hour/quarter/store |

### System Touchpoints
- Emergency incident reporting form in ERP/operations system with classification, severity, response actions, and regulatory notification tracking (W330.8)
- Staff headcount verification against shift schedule (W34) via handheld/tablet at assembly point (W330.5)
- Incident reporting integrated with W47 (facility maintenance — emergency work orders), W59 (insurance — incident claims), W140 (OHS — employee injury component), W285 (public liability — customer injury claims), W37 (loss prevention — investigation) (W330.8–9)
- Drill scheduling and compliance tracking integrated with W158 (BC drill and DR testing) (W330.10)
- Emergency equipment maintenance tracking: fire extinguisher inspection dates, alarm test dates, first aid kit contents checks (W47 integration) (W330.10)
- LGU regulatory reporting templates pre-configured per jurisdiction (W54/LGU integration)
- Integration with W5E (store opening delay — emergency before opening), W5F (early closure — emergency during operating hours), W49 (typhoon BC — natural disaster escalation), W71 (physical security — security threat response), W140 (OHS — worker injury component), W158 (BC drill — emergency preparedness), W238 (hazmat spill response — in-store hazmat incidents)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Panic and crowd management** — 467+ daily customers in a large-format store during an emergency creates stampede risk, especially at limited exit points; elderly and disabled customers require assistance that may not be immediately available
- **Hazardous material compounding** — fire involving paint, solvents, or adhesives produces toxic fumes requiring specialized fire suppression that municipal BFP units may not be equipped for
- **Multi-story yard evacuation** — outdoor lumber yards with elevated storage racks create fall risk during emergency evacuation
- **Communication during power outage** — PA systems and radios may fail during fire or structural emergency; backup battery-powered megaphone and whistle protocols needed
- **Drill complacency** — quarterly drills can become perfunctory; if staff treat drills as routine, actual emergency response degrades

### Time Estimate
Minor emergency: 15–60 minutes. Major emergency (evacuation): 2–8 hours including emergency services response and incident reporting. Post-incident actions: 1–2 weeks. Quarterly drill: 1 hour per store per quarter.

### Staffing Implication
- **Store Manager**: primary incident commander; ~1–2 hours per actual incident for coordination and reporting. Quarterly drill: 1 hour. Absorbed.
- **All Store Staff**: 1 hour/quarter for emergency drill. During actual emergency: all staff mobilized for evacuation duties. No incremental headcount.
- **Facilities Coordinator**: ~2–4 hours per incident for post-incident coordination + ~2 hours/quarter for drill scheduling and compliance tracking across 200 stores. Absorbed within existing Facilities team.
- **First aid certified staff**: minimum 2 per store (costs ~PHP 2,000–3,000 per certification, renewed every 2 years). Absorbed within existing staff training budget.

## W420. AI Shelf Monitoring & Real-time Replenishment Alerting

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Computer Vision (CV) system detects Out-of-Stock (OOS) on shelf or Planogram (POG) non-compliance |
| **Frequency** | Real-time / Continuous |
| **Volume** | ~50–100 alerts per store/day |
| **Owner** | Department Supervisor |
| **Participants** | Stock Associate, IT Systems (AI Engine), Store Manager |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Detection**: CV cameras/drones scan shelves; AI engine identifies gaps (OOS) or incorrect SKU placement vs. active Planogram (W86) | System | — | Continuous |
| 2 | **Alert Trigger**: System verifies OOS against ERP "On-Hand" (SOH); if SOH > 0, system triggers a "Shelf Gap" alert to the Department Supervisor's mobile device | System | IT Ops | < 1 min |
| 3 | **Validation**: Supervisor reviews alert on handheld; confirms if gap is actual OOS or if stock is hidden behind other items | Dept Supervisor | Store Manager | 2 min |
| 4 | **Stock Pull**: If OOS confirmed and backroom stock exists: system creates an "Urgent Stock Pull" task for the nearest Stock Associate | System / Supervisor | Dept Supervisor | Automated |
| 5 | **Replenishment**: Stock Associate pulls item from backroom/overstock and replenishes shelf; scans "Task Complete" on handheld | Stock Associate | Dept Supervisor | 10–15 min |
| 6 | **Audit**: AI engine re-scans shelf to confirm gap is closed; system logs "Time-to-Replenish" metric for Store KPIs | System | Store Manager | < 5 min |
| 7 | **Ghost Inventory Investigation**: If SOH > 0 but shelf is empty and backroom is empty: Supervisor triggers an "Inventory Variance Investigation" (W248) to find the "Ghost Inventory" (potential shrinkage) | Dept Supervisor | Store Manager | 15 min |

### System Touchpoints
- Computer Vision (CV) camera integration with AI processing engine (W203)
- Real-time mobile alerting via mPOS / Staff App (W206)
- Integration with W4 (Store Replenishment), W86 (Planogram Compliance), W208 (Retail Analytics), and W248 (LP Investigation)
- Task management dashboard for tracking shelf replenishment velocity

### Time Estimate
Total cycle from alert to shelf replenishment: 15–25 minutes (Detection to Pull: < 5 min; Execution: 10–20 min). Supervisor validation: 2 min. Stock Associate labor: 15 min per alert.

### Pain Points / Risks
- **False Positives**: AI misidentifying a "turned" box or a price tag as a gap, leading to "alert fatigue" for store staff and ignoring real OOS events.
- **WIFI/Network Latency**: In-store dead zones preventing real-time alerts from reaching staff handhelds, defeating the "real-time" benefit of the system.
- **Ghost Inventory**: System thinks 10 units are on hand, so it sends a "replenish" alert, but the physical stock is missing (theft/damage); leads to repetitive fruitless pull tasks.
- **Lighting/Obstruction**: Large displays or customers blocking camera views prevent accurate shelf scanning during peak hours when replenishment is most critical.

### Staffing Implication
- **Department Supervisors**: Handle ~50–100 alerts/day; validation takes ~2 hours/day cumulative. No incremental headcount — replaces manual shelf-walking.
- **Stock Associates**: Replenishment tasks (~10–15 min each) are part of existing daily replenishment duties; AI prioritization makes them more efficient.
- **IT Support**: 1 hour/week per store for CV camera calibration and AI logic tuning. Absorbed by regional IT.

---

## W428. Community Disaster Relief & Emergency Response

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Natural disaster (Typhoon, Earthquake, Flood) affecting a store region; or LGU/NGO request for emergency supply allocation |
| **Frequency** | Stochastically, ~5–10 times per year during monsoon/typhoon season |
| **Volume** | Variable (batch allocation of hundreds of SKUs) |
| **Owner** | Store Operations Director |
| **Participants** | COO, Store Manager, Supply Planner, LGU Representatives, NGO Partners |

### Background

Hardware retailers in the Philippines are designated as "Critical Infrastructure" during disasters. BuildRight stores often serve as the primary source of rebuilding materials (lumber, tarps, nails) and emergency supplies (generators, batteries, flashlights) for local communities. This workflow bypasses standard B2B/B2C fulfillment rules to prioritize humanitarian relief and government emergency procurement.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Emergency Declaration**: COO declares "Disaster Response Mode" for a specific region; system activates "Disaster Priority" flag in ERP | COO | CEO | 30 min |
| 2 | **Inventory Ring-fencing**: Store Manager identifies "Relief SKUs" (generators, tarps, plywood, cement, flashlights); system reserves 50% of on-hand stock for "Priority Relief Allocation" (blocks ecommerce and standard POS sales for these quantities) | Store Manager | System | Automated |
| 3 | **Relief Allocation Intake**: Store Manager receives requests from LGU (City/Municipal Govt) or authorized NGOs (Red Cross, etc.); creates "Relief Sales Order" in system | Store Manager | Store Ops Director | 1 hour |
| 4 | **Pricing & Payment**: Relief orders applied with pre-approved "Humanitarian Discount"; payment terms set to "Deferred Relief Term" (Net 90) for LGU accounts to expedite release | Finance Mgr | CFO | 30 min |
| 5 | **Priority Fulfillment**: Stock Associates prioritize picking and staging for relief trucks; bypasses standard BOPIS and home delivery queues | Stock Associate | Dept Supervisor | 2 hours |
| 6 | **Emergency Replenishment**: Supply Planner triggers immediate "Push" transfers from safe DCs to the disaster-hit region, bypassing standard reorder points (W31) | Supply Planner | Supply Chain Mgr | 1 hour |
| 7 | **Audit & Tracking**: System tracks all "Relief Mode" transactions separately for ESG reporting (W114) and post-disaster tax claims | Finance | Controller | 1 day (post-event) |

### System Touchpoints

- Disaster Priority Flag: global system state that modifies inventory reservation and allocation rules (W428.1)
- Relief Order Type: specialized SO type with humanitarian pricing and deferred terms (W428.3)
- ESG Dashboard: auto-aggregates value of goods and discounts provided during disasters (W428.7)
- Integration with W49 (Business Continuity) and W114 (ESG Reporting)

### Pain Points / Risks

- **Inventory Cannibalization**: Balancing the needs of regular trade customers with humanitarian relief; releasing too much stock to NGOs can leave local contractors without materials to perform repairs
- **LGU Payment Delays**: Government emergency procurement is often slow to pay despite the urgency of the initial release; requires careful credit monitoring (W108)
- **Security during Release**: High-demand relief goods (especially generators) create a security risk at the receiving dock; requires guard force coordination (W171)

### Staffing Implication

Effort is absorbed by existing Store Ops and Supply Chain teams during emergencies. No incremental headcount.

### Time Estimate

**Total**: Declaration — 30 min; Intake & Fulfillment — 4–6 hours per relief wave; Replenishment — 1 hour; **End-to-end: Continuous during the disaster recovery period (~1–2 weeks)**

## W432. Solo Parent Discount Compliance (RA 11861)

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer presents Solo Parent ID at POS |
| **Frequency** | High; ~2–4% of retail transactions |
| **Volume** | ~30,000–60,000 transactions/month chain-wide |
| **Owner** | Lead Cashier |
| **Participants** | Cashier, Solo Parent Customer, Store Manager |

### Background

The **Expanded Solo Parents Welfare Act (RA 11861)** mandates a 10% discount and 12% VAT exemption on specific "basic necessities and prime commodities" for solo parents whose income is below a certain threshold. For BuildRight, this applies to specific DIY and home repair items categorized by the DTI as basic necessities (e.g., light bulbs, select hardware tools).

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Identification**: Customer presents valid Solo Parent ID (issued by LGU/DSWD) and Solo Parent Booklet | Cashier | — | 1 min |
| 2 | **Validation**: Cashier verifies ID validity and checks the Booklet for the monthly purchase limit (PHP 2,500 per month for basic goods per RA 11861) | Cashier | — | 2 min |
| 3 | **POS Selection**: Cashier selects "Solo Parent" discount type; system prompts for ID number and name | Cashier | — | 1 min |
| 4 | **Item Filtering**: System automatically applies 10% discount and VAT-exemption ONLY to eligible SKUs (mapped in Item Master) | System | — | Automated |
| 5 | **Transaction**: Receipt prints with mandatory Solo Parent disclosure (Name, ID#, Gross Sale, Discount, VAT Exempt Sales) | System | — | Automated |
| 6 | **Manual Log**: Cashier records transaction in the physical Solo Parent Logbook as required by the LGU | Cashier | Lead Cashier | 2 min |

### System Touchpoints
- POS Solo Parent Discount Module (RA 11861 logic)
- Item Master "Solo Parent Eligible" flag (W252)
- BIR-compliant receipt printing with Solo Parent fields
- Monthly tax reconciliation for VAT-exempt sales (W90)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Booklet Verification**: Checking physical booklets for the PHP 2,500 limit is slow and prone to error, especially during peak hours.
- **Audit Risk**: Improperly applying the discount to non-eligible hardware items leads to BIR penalties and VAT assessments.
- **Identity Fraud**: Use of expired or borrowed Solo Parent IDs.

## W438. Yard Dispatch & Customer Vehicle Loading Operations

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer completes purchase of bulky items (Lumber, Cement, Steel, Sand) at POS |
| **Frequency** | High; ~100–300 loading events per store per day |
| **Volume** | Variable (from 5 bags of cement to full truckloads of lumber) |
| **Owner** | Yard Supervisor |
| **Participants** | Customer, Yard Associate (Porter), Forklift Operator, Security Guard |

### Background

Bulk building materials are stored in the outdoor yard or heavy-loading zone. Unlike standard retail checkout, the customer must drive their vehicle to the loading bay after payment. Ensuring the correct quantity is loaded and safety protocols are followed is critical for both loss prevention and public safety.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Dispatch Notification**: Upon payment at POS, system sends "Loading Instruction" to the Yard Supervisor's tablet | System | Yard Supervisor | Automated |
| 2 | **Vehicle Arrival**: Customer drives to the Loading Bay; presents "Paid" receipt or "Loading Slip" to the Guard | Customer / Guard | Yard Supervisor | 2 min |
| 3 | **Order Verification**: Yard Associate scans the Loading Slip barcode; system displays items and quantities to be loaded | Yard Associate | Yard Supervisor | 1 min |
| 4 | **Loading**: Associate or Forklift Operator loads materials into the customer's vehicle; follows safety guidelines (e.g., no overloading, proper weight distribution) | Yard Associate / Forklift Op | — | 5–15 min |
| 5 | **Validation & Signature**: Associate confirms quantity loaded on tablet; customer signs digitally to acknowledge receipt of goods in good condition | Yard Associate | Customer | 1 min |
| 6 | **Exit Clearance**: Guard checks vehicle interior/bed against the "Cleared Loading Slip"; verifies exit timestamp; releases vehicle | Security Guard | — | 2 min |

### System Touchpoints
- Yard Dispatch Queue (Real-time feed from POS)
- Mobile Loading App (Quantity confirmation & Digital Signature)
- Gate Pass Integration (Security exit clearance)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Inaccurate Loading**: Loading more units than paid for (Loss Prevention risk) or wrong SKU (e.g., wrong grade of plywood).
- **Vehicle Overloading**: Safety risk to the customer and liability for BuildRight if the vehicle fails on public roads due to improper loading.
- **Congestion**: Slow loading times lead to vehicle queues blocking store entry/exit points.

### Staffing Implication
- Yard Associates: 2–4 per store (depending on volume).
- Forklift Operators: 1–2 per store.
- Security Guards (Gate): 1 per exit point.

## W445. Display & Demo Infrastructure Maintenance

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Scheduled preventive maintenance (PM) or report of non-functional store display (e.g., dead lightbulb, leaking demo faucet) |
| **Frequency** | Weekly (Inspections); Daily (Minor repairs) |
| **Volume** | ~50–100 active "live" demos per store |
| **Owner** | Store Manager |
| **Participants** | Maintenance / Utility, Department Supervisor, Vendor Rep |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Daily Walkthrough**: Supervisor inspects all "Live Demos" (Lighting, Plumbing, Power Tools) for safety and functionality | Dept Supervisor | Store Manager | 30 min |
| 2 | **Fault Logging**: Non-functional displays logged as a "Maintenance Request" in the Store Operations App | Dept Supervisor | — | 5 min |
| 3 | **Safety Isolation**: If a fault is electrical or involves water (leaks), Maintenance immediately isolates the power/water source | Maintenance | — | 10 min |
| 4 | **Repair/Component Replacement**: Maintenance replaces consumables (bulbs, batteries) or fixes minor hardware issues | Maintenance | — | 30–60 min |
| 5 | **Vendor Coordination**: For complex/branded displays (e.g., custom kitchen modules), Supervisor notifies the Vendor Rep for specialized repair | Dept Supervisor | — | 10 min |
| 6 | **Completion & Verification**: Display tested for functionality; "Live Demo" status restored in the system | Dept Supervisor | — | 5 min |

### System Touchpoints
- Facility Maintenance module (W47) for display-specific work orders
- Vendor portal for reporting issues with branded display units

### Pain Points / Risks
- Customer safety: Exposed wiring or wet floors from leaking demo units.
- Brand image: Burnt-out bulbs in the lighting gallery make the store look poorly managed.
- Slow vendor response for high-tech or proprietary display repairs.

### Staffing Implication
Absorbed by existing Maintenance/Utility and Department Supervisors.

## W463. Catch-Weight & Cut-to-Length Processing

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer selects a variable-unit item (Lumber, Pipe, Wire, Bulk Nails) |
| **Frequency** | High; ~20–30% of big-box hardware transactions |
| **Volume** | Variable (per meter, per board foot, per kg) |
| **Owner** | Department Supervisor |
| **Participants** | Customer, Sales Associate, Cashier |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Selection**: Customer identifies material; Sales Associate assists in measuring/cutting | Sales Associate | — | 2-5 min |
| 2 | **Measurement**: Associate uses calibrated tool (tape measure, scale, counter) to determine quantity | Sales Associate | Dept Supervisor | 1 min |
| 3 | **Tag Generation**: Associate enters quantity into the Department Tablet; system generates a "Measurement Slip" or temporary barcode | Sales Associate | — | 1 min |
| 4 | **POS Scan**: Cashier scans the slip at POS; system retrieves the price based on Unit of Measure (UOM) (e.g., PHP 150/meter) | Cashier | — | 30 sec |
| 5 | **Inventory Update**: System reduces inventory in base UOM (e.g., reduces total roll length by 5 meters) | System | — | Automated |
| 6 | **Reconciliation**: Weekly, Department Supervisor performs a "Remnant Audit" to check for unaccounted waste/off-cuts | Dept Supervisor | Store Manager | 1 hour/week |

### System Touchpoints
- Dual UOM Support (Sales UOM vs. Inventory UOM)
- POS Integration with Department Measurement Slips
- Waste/Off-cut adjustment module (W92)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Measurement Errors**: Manual measurement at the floor level leads to shrinkage or customer overcharging.
- **Remnant Management**: Short off-cuts (e.g., 0.5m pipe) are hard to sell and often discarded without tracking.

## W470. Store-Level Rotational Brownout / Power Outage Management Protocol

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Advance notification from electric utility of scheduled rotational brownout; or sudden unscheduled power outage |
| **Frequency** | Mindanao (60 stores): ~2–4 events/month per store during dry season (March–May); Visayas (40 stores): ~1–2 events/month; Luzon (100 stores): occasional, primarily during typhoon season; total chain: ~300–500 store-outage events/year |
| **Volume** | 1–30 stores affected per event (rotational brownouts affect specific feeder areas); typical duration: 2–8 hours |
| **Owner** | Store Manager |
| **Participants** | Store Manager, Maintenance / Utility, Cashier, Department Supervisor, Security Guard |

### Background

Mindanao — where BuildRight has **60 stores** and its corporate HQ — experiences regular rotational brownouts due to power generation deficits, especially during the dry season (March–May) when hydroelectric output drops. These are **predictable, recurring operational events** distinct from typhoon-related disasters (W49) or one-time opening delays (W5E). Some electric cooperatives publish rotational schedules; others are unscheduled. Luzon and Visayas stores experience brownouts less frequently but are not immune, especially during typhoon season when grid infrastructure is damaged.

This workflow covers the full lifecycle of managing a **planned or routine** power outage at a store — from preparation through reduced-operations mode to power restoration and reconciliation. It differs from W330 (In-Store Emergency Response) which covers fire, structural, and medical emergencies, and from W5G (Offline POS Recovery) which covers the POS-specific technical recovery.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Brownout schedule monitoring**: Maintenance / Utility staff monitors electric utility advisories (social media, SMS alerts, utility website) for scheduled rotational brownouts in the store's area; for utilities that publish schedules, Maintenance receives schedule weekly and shares with Store Manager; for unscheduled outages, trigger is the power loss itself (step 5) | Maintenance / Utility | Store Manager | 15 min/week during brownout season |
| 2 | **Pre-outage preparation** (for scheduled brownouts): (a) Store Manager confirms generator fuel level ≥ 75%; if below, arranges emergency fuel delivery (maintain relationship with local fuel supplier); (b) Maintenance verifies generator startup (test run for 5 minutes if last test > 2 weeks); (c) Maintenance confirms UPS battery charge for POS terminals (each terminal has ~15 min battery backup for graceful shutdown); (d) Store Manager briefs staff on shift: approximate outage window, which operations will be reduced | Maintenance / Store Manager | Store Manager | 30 min/prep |
| 3 | **Customer pre-notification** (for scheduled brownouts): (a) Store Manager posts "Scheduled Power Maintenance" notice at store entrance; (b) reduced lighting areas marked; (c) PA announcement at start of outage: "We are experiencing a temporary power interruption. Our store remains open. Some areas may have reduced lighting. Thank you for your patience." | Store Manager | — | 15 min |
| 4 | **Load priority plan execution**: Store Manager activates pre-defined load priority for generator power: **Priority 1 (always powered)**: (a) 2 of 3 POS terminals (1 terminal shut down to reduce load), (b) security system and CCTV (non-negotiable — W71 requirement), (c) emergency lighting (fire exits, main aisles), (d) fire alarm and suppression monitoring; **Priority 2 (powered if generator capacity allows)**: (e) customer-facing display lighting (main aisles only — gallery and appliance sections deprioritized), (f) paint mixing station (high customer demand), (g) receiving dock lighting (if trucks scheduled); **Priority 3 (not powered during outage)**: (h) backroom/stockroom lighting (use handheld flashlights), (i) office area (computers shut down), (j) air conditioning (customer discomfort is tolerable for 2–4 hours; staff use portable fans), (k) outdoor yard lighting (unless customer traffic is heavy), (l) non-essential signage | Maintenance / Store Manager | Store Manager | 10 min |
| 5 | **Unscheduled outage response**: When power drops without warning: (a) UPS provides ~15 min battery for POS terminals — Cashier completes current transaction and logs out; (b) Maintenance starts generator (target: within 3 minutes of outage); (c) Store Manager makes PA announcement; (d) Security Guard activates handheld flashlight protocol for dark areas; (e) Department Supervisors assist customers in darker sections (tiles gallery, plumbing aisles) toward better-lit areas; (f) if generator fails to start within 10 minutes, escalate to W5E (Store Opening Delay Procedure) — consider partial store closure | Maintenance / Cashier / Store Manager | Store Manager | 5–10 min |
| 6 | **Sensitive goods protection**: (a) **Paint and chemicals**: Maintenance verifies ventilation continues (exhaust fans may be on generator backup); if ambient temperature in paint mixing area exceeds 35°C, temporarily suspend paint mixing operations; (b) **Appliances**: Demo units on display powered down to prevent voltage surge damage; (c) **Refrigerated items** (if any — limited in hardware retail): check temperature log; if outage exceeds 4 hours, disposition per food safety guidelines; (d) **Lumber yard**: no special action required — outdoor inventory is not power-dependent | Maintenance / Department Supervisor | Store Manager | 15 min |
| 7 | **Extended outage escalation** (beyond 4 hours): (a) Store Manager contacts Regional Manager: report generator fuel status, estimated remaining runtime, store revenue impact; (b) if generator fuel is running low (< 25%): Store Manager arranges emergency fuel delivery or coordinates with neighboring store for fuel sharing; (c) if outage will exceed generator capacity (> 6–8 hours): Store Manager considers early closure per abbreviated W5F procedure; cash secured in safe, POS shut down, building secured; (d) Regional Manager reports extended outage stores to Store Ops Director for pattern analysis | Store Manager / Regional Manager | Store Ops Director | 15 min |
| 8 | **Power restoration protocol**: When utility power is restored: (a) Maintenance switches from generator to utility power (do NOT run both simultaneously — risk of backfeed); (b) generator cooldown period (5 min idle before shutdown per manufacturer spec); (c) IT systems restart sequence: (1) network switch and WiFi access point, (2) POS terminals boot and reconnect, (3) POS sync with central server — validates offline transaction queue, (4) handheld devices reconnect, (5) CCTV system verifies recording resumed; (d) Department Supervisors verify lighting and displays restored; (e) Maintenance logs outage event: start time, end time, duration, generator runtime, fuel consumed, any issues | Maintenance / IT (via phone support) | Store Manager | 15–30 min |
| 9 | **Post-outage reconciliation**: (a) POS system processes offline transaction queue (W5G offline recovery procedure); (b) Store Manager reviews offline transaction report for completeness; (c) Finance: no manual journal entry required if POS sync completes successfully; (d) Maintenance logs generator runtime and fuel consumption for facility cost tracking (W111 utility management); (e) if any data discrepancies from offline POS period: Store Manager flags to IT helpdesk (W48) for investigation | Store Manager / Maintenance | Store Manager | 15 min |
| 10 | **Monthly pattern reporting**: Regional Manager aggregates outage reports from all stores in region: (a) total outage hours per store per month, (b) generator fuel cost per store per month, (c) revenue impact estimate (compare sales during outage hours to same day/hour historical average); (d) stores with chronic outage issues (> 8 hours/month) flagged for capex investment (permanent generator, solar panels per W173, or redundant utility feed); (e) report submitted to Store Ops Director monthly | Regional Manager | Store Ops Director | 1 hour/month |

### System Touchpoints
- POS offline capability and transaction queue (W5G) — transactions during outage stored locally and synced upon reconnection (W470.8c, W470.9a)
- Generator runtime and fuel consumption tracking via facility maintenance module (W47) (W470.8e)
- Utility cost tracking integration with W111 (Store Energy & Utility Consumption Management) for generator fuel as utility offset (W470.9d)
- Store closure/reopening status tracking per W5F (abbreviated closure for extended outages) (W470.7c)
- IT helpdesk (W48) for post-outage POS reconciliation issues (W470.9e)
- POS terminal count reduction management — 2 of 3 terminals active during outage; queue management (W470.4a)
- Integration with W5E (opening delay — if outage occurs before opening), W5G (offline POS — technical recovery), W47 (facility maintenance — generator PM), W173 (solar monitoring — solar continues during daytime outages), W330 (in-store emergency — if outage causes a safety incident), W49 (typhoon BC — extended disaster-related outages handled by W49, not this workflow)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Generator maintenance neglect**: Generators at 200 stores require quarterly PM (oil change, filter replacement, load bank testing per W188); if PM is skipped, generators fail to start during outages — the worst possible time; Store Manager must treat generator PM as a W47 priority, not a discretionary expense
- **Mindanao dry season cumulative impact**: During March–May, a single Mindanao store may experience 8–12 brownout days/month (2–4 hours each); cumulative lost revenue and generator fuel cost (~PHP 500–1,000/hour for a 100kVA generator) can reach PHP 50,000–100,000/store/month — PHP 3–6M/month across 60 Mindanao stores
- **Fuel supply chain during widespread outages**: During regional brownouts, all businesses in the area run generators simultaneously; local fuel stations may be unable to meet surge demand or may also be affected by the outage (no electric pumps); Store Managers should maintain minimum 75% fuel level at all times during brownout season
- **POS queue management with reduced terminals**: Reducing from 3 to 2 POS terminals during outage increases average checkout time by ~50%; during peak hours (weekends, sale events), customer queues may become unacceptable — Store Manager may authorize mobile POS (W206) as queue-busting during outage periods
- **Customer safety in reduced lighting**: Darker aisles (tiles gallery, plumbing, electrical) increase slip/trip/fall risk; if a customer injury occurs during a brownout, liability is heightened — W285 (public liability claims) risk increases

### Staffing Implication
- **Maintenance / Utility** (1 per store): Absorbs generator operation, fuel management, and utility monitoring. Brownout response is a core duty during dry season. No incremental headcount.
- **Store Manager**: Decision-making on closure, load priority, and escalation. ~30 min/event. Absorbed into existing duties.
- **Regional Manager**: Monthly pattern reporting. ~1 hour/month. Absorbed into existing reporting duties.

---

## W471. Store-Level Security Incident & Police/Barangay Reporting Protocol

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Security incident at a store that requires law enforcement involvement or formal documentation: shoplifting apprehension, break-in/burglary, vandalism, customer altercation, employee theft discovery, vehicle break-in in parking area, armed robbery |
| **Frequency** | ~2–5 incidents per store per year requiring formal reporting; ~400–1,000 total incidents/year chain-wide |
| **Volume** | 1 incident per event; some stores in high-crime areas may have 1–2 incidents/month |
| **Owner** | Store Manager |
| **Participants** | Store Manager, Security Guard, LPO, Regional Manager, Facilities Coordinator, HR, Legal, Finance (Insurance) |

### Background

With 200 stores across the Philippines, security incidents requiring police or barangay documentation are a daily operational reality across the chain. W71 covers physical security infrastructure (alarms, CCTV, access control) and W37 covers loss prevention exception reporting and investigation. However, neither covers the post-incident workflow of **documenting and filing reports with Philippine law enforcement** — the Philippine National Police (PNP) and/or the barangay. This is a distinct process because: (a) Philippine law requires police reports for insurance claims and criminal prosecution; (b) barangay blotter entries are required for community-level disputes; (c) DTI may request police reports during consumer complaint investigations (W469); (d) employee discipline cases involving theft require police documentation for due process.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Incident classification**: Store Manager or Security Guard classifies the incident on first report: **Category A — Criminal (requires police)**: (a) armed robbery, (b) burglary/break-in (after hours), (c) shoplifting with apprehension and suspect detention, (d) employee theft discovered (per W37 investigation), (e) assault or violent customer altercation, (f) vehicle break-in in parking lot with property damage; **Category B — Community dispute (barangay)**: (a) non-violent customer altercation, (b) minor property damage dispute, (c) noise or nuisance complaint from neighboring establishment; **Category C — Internal documentation only**: (a) attempted shoplifting (suspect fled, not apprehended), (b) minor vandalism (< PHP 5,000 damage), (c) false alarm activation | Store Manager / Security Guard | Store Manager | 5 min |
| 2 | **Immediate response**: (a) **Category A**: Security Guard secures the scene; if suspect detained, held in store office with CCTV coverage — do not use physical restraint beyond minimum necessary; Store Manager contacts PNP station immediately; if violence or weapon involved, staff safety is priority — do not confront; (b) **Category B**: Security Guard separates parties; Store Manager attempts de-escalation; if parties cannot resolve, proceed to barangay reporting; (c) **Category C**: Store Manager documents incident for internal records; LPO includes in daily exception report (W37) | Security Guard / Store Manager | Store Manager | 5–15 min |
| 3 | **Evidence preservation**: (a) Security Guard ensures CCTV recording of the incident is preserved — marks footage with timestamp, camera ID, and incident description; (b) if physical evidence exists (stolen merchandise recovered, damaged property, weapon), photographed in place before moved; (c) Store Manager identifies and separates witnesses (staff and customers); (d) for employee theft cases: do not tip off the employee until LPO is consulted per W37 investigation protocol; (e) all physical evidence tagged and stored in secure location (store manager's office) | Security Guard / Store Manager | Store Manager | 15–30 min |
| 4 | **Police report filing (Category A)**: (a) Store Manager or designated Security Guard goes to the nearest PNP station to file an incident report (commonly called "blotter entry" at PNP); (b) bring: Store Manager's ID, store business permit (W54), CCTV footage on USB (selected clips only — do not provide full recording without formal request), recovered merchandise/evidence (if applicable), list of witnesses with contact information; (c) PNP officer records the incident in the police blotter — Store Manager requests a copy of the blotter entry (signed and stamped by investigating officer); (d) if suspect is detained: PNP takes custody of suspect and processes per Philippine criminal procedure; BuildRight staff should not detain suspects beyond reasonable time (~30 min max before PNP arrival) | Store Manager / Security Guard | Store Manager | 1–2 hours |
| 5 | **Barangay blotter entry (Category B)**: (a) Store Manager or Security Guard goes to the barangay hall to file a blotter entry for community disputes; (b) barangay tanod records the incident; Store Manager requests a certified copy of the blotter entry; (c) barangay may schedule a conciliation meeting (Katarungang Pambarangay) — mandatory first step for minor disputes before court filing; (d) Store Manager attends conciliation meeting with documentation; settlement recorded by barangay | Store Manager / Security Guard | Store Manager | 30 min–1 hour |
| 6 | **Insurance claim initiation** (if property damage, theft, or robbery): (a) for incidents with estimated loss > PHP 10,000: Store Manager notifies Facilities Coordinator and Finance (Treasury) within 24 hours; (b) Finance initiates insurance claim per W59 (Insurance Policy Lifecycle Management) — requires police report copy, photos, inventory loss valuation, and CCTV footage; (c) for robbery or burglary: insurance adjuster may visit the store for inspection — Store Manager coordinates access and preserves the scene until adjuster arrives (if practical) | Store Manager / Finance | Regional Manager | 30 min + insurance process |
| 7 | **LP investigation handoff**: Store Manager provides all incident documentation to LPO within 24 hours: (a) police/barangay blotter copy, (b) CCTV footage reference (camera ID and timestamp range), (c) witness contact list, (d) physical evidence inventory, (e) incident narrative; LPO incorporates into loss prevention investigation per W37 and enters into investigation case management (W37.10) | Store Manager | LPO / Internal Audit | 15 min |
| 8 | **Employee-related incidents** (if employee involvement): (a) for employee theft or fraud: HR is notified per W37 investigation protocol; employee placed on preventive suspension per Philippine Labor Code (due process); (b) for employee injury during incident: HR initiates incident report per W140 (OHS Incident Management) and coordinates with SSS for emergency medical benefit; (c) for employee as witness: HR documents witness statement; employee's shift is adjusted for police/barangay attendance; (d) all employee-related actions comply with DOLE due process requirements — consult HR before any disciplinary action | Store Manager / HR | HR Head | 30 min |
| 9 | **Incident closure and reporting**: (a) Store Manager closes incident in store incident register with: final classification, police/barangay report reference number, evidence disposition, estimated loss value, insurance claim status (if applicable), employee action (if applicable); (b) Monthly: Store Manager includes security incident summary in monthly store performance review (W67) — count by category, loss value, police/barangay report references; (c) Quarterly: Regional Manager aggregates security incident data across stores; identifies high-incident locations for LP intervention (increased patrols per W171, CCTV upgrades per W207, guard force changes); (d) Annual: LPO includes security incident trends in annual shrinkage report (W37.7) | Store Manager / Regional Manager | Store Ops Director / Internal Audit | 15 min/case + monthly aggregation |

### System Touchpoints
- Store incident register (W471.9a): classified incident log with police/barangay report reference numbers, linked to inventory adjustments (W92), insurance claims (W59), and LP investigations (W37)
- CCTV system (W71): footage preservation with incident tagging and timestamp bookmarking (W471.3a)
- Inventory adjustment / shrinkage tracking (W92): stolen or damaged inventory written off with incident reference (W471.6b)
- Insurance claim module (W59): claim initiation with police report and evidence attachment (W471.6)
- LP investigation case management (W37.10): case creation from security incident with all evidence linked (W471.7)
- OHS incident module (W140): employee injury tracking during security incidents (W471.8b)
- Monthly store performance review (W67): security incident KPI included in store scorecard (W471.9b)
- Integration with W71 (physical security — infrastructure), W37 (LP — investigation), W59 (insurance — claims), W140 (OHS — employee injury), W171 (yard patrol — outdoor security), W207 (CCTV audit — footage availability), W285 (public liability — customer injury during incident), W466 (LPAP — asset protection operations)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **PNP response time variability**: PNP response times in provincial areas range from 15 minutes to 2+ hours; during peak hours or in areas with limited police presence, Store Manager may need to manage a detained suspect for an extended period, creating legal risk (unlawful detention claims) and staff safety risk
- **CCTV footage admissibility**: Philippine courts require clear chain-of-custody documentation for CCTV footage; if Store Manager transfers footage to USB without proper logging (who accessed, when, what camera, what time range), the footage may be inadmissible in court or DTI proceedings (W469)
- **Barangay conciliation delays**: Katarungang Pambarangay conciliation meetings may be scheduled weeks after the incident; Store Manager or designated representative must attend during store operating hours, pulling management away from store operations
- **Employee theft due process**: Philippine Labor Code requires due process (twin notice rule: notice of charge + notice of decision with opportunity to be heard) before terminating an employee for theft; premature termination without following due process — even with CCTV evidence — can result in illegal dismissal cases at NLRC (National Labor Relations Commission) with full backpay awards
- **Underreporting of minor incidents**: Store Managers may classify incidents as Category C (internal only) to avoid the time burden of police/barangay reporting, even when incidents warrant Category A or B; this leads to underreporting in the security incident register and inaccurate shrinkage attribution in W37

### Staffing Implication
- **Store Manager**: Incident management, police/barangay filing, and reporting. ~2–4 hours/incident for Category A; ~1 hour for Category B. Across 2–5 incidents/year per store, this is ~8–20 hours/year. Absorbed into existing duties.
- **Security Guard**: Scene securing, evidence preservation, and often accompanies Store Manager to PNP/barangay. Absorbed into existing duties (guard force is already on payroll).
- **LPO**: Investigation handoff and incorporation into LP case management. ~30 min/incident. Absorbed into W37 duties.
- **Facilities Coordinator**: Insurance claim coordination for property damage incidents. Absorbed into W59 duties.

---

## W497. PWD Accessibility Compliance & Store Facilities Audit

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Annual store facilities audit cycle; new store opening (W16); store renovation (W96); customer PWD complaint; DOLE/NCDA inspection advisory |
| **Frequency** | Annual (comprehensive audit); event-driven (complaint-triggered or new store) |
| **Volume** | 200 stores; annual audit of all locations; event-driven audits as needed |
| **Owner** | Store Operations — Facilities Standards Manager |
| **Participants** | Store Manager, Engineering & Construction (W223), VP Store Ops, Legal Head, NCDA (National Council on Disability Affairs) |

### Background

Republic Act 7277 (Magna Carta for Disabled Persons) and its implementing rules, Batas Pambansa 344 (Accessibility Law), and the National Building Code (PD 1096) require Philippine commercial establishments to provide barrier-free accessibility for persons with disabilities (PWDs). With 200 stores across the Philippines, BuildRight must ensure: (a) accessible parking spaces (nearest to entrance, wider stalls); (b) ramps at store entrances (gradient not exceeding 1:12); (c) accessible restrooms (grab bars, wider doors, lower fixtures); (d) accessible checkout counters (at least one lowered counter per store); (e) clear signage with tactile/Braille elements where required; (f) unobstructed pathways through merchandise aisles (minimum 1.2m clearance); (g) elevator or lift access where stores have multiple levels. Additionally, RA 10754 (expanded PWD benefits) and RA 9994 (Senior Citizens Act) require stores to provide discount privileges (covered by W170 and W432), but the physical accessibility of the store is a prerequisite. This workflow covers the accessibility compliance audit and remediation lifecycle. W47 covers general facility maintenance and W96 covers store renovation, but neither specifically addresses PWD accessibility compliance.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Accessibility Standards Checklist Preparation**: Facilities Standards Manager maintains the PWD Accessibility Standards Checklist aligned with BP 344 and RA 7277 implementing rules: (a) **Parking**: designated PWD spaces per lot size, signage, pavement markings, ramp from parking to entrance; (b) **Entrance**: ramp gradient (max 1:12), handrails, level threshold, automatic or easy-open doors; (c) **Pathways**: minimum 1.2m aisle clearance throughout store, no protruding obstacles above 0.7m height, slip-resistant flooring; (d) **Checkout**: at least one lowered counter (max 0.8m height), accessible queue lane; (e) **Restroom**: at least one accessible toilet (wider door, grab bars, lowered sink, emergency call button); (f) **Signage**: PWD-accessible route signage, tactile floor indicators for blind customers at key decision points; (g) **Service counter**: at least one lowered section at customer service counter; (h) **Emergency egress**: accessible evacuation route, visual fire alarms (for hearing-impaired), designated assembly point accessible by ramp | Facilities Standards Mgr | VP Store Ops | 2 days/annual update |
| 2 | **Annual Store Accessibility Audit**: Facilities Standards Manager conducts annual audit of all 200 stores (can be delegated to Regional Managers with standardized checklist): (a) physical inspection of each store against checklist; (b) photograph and document non-compliance items; (c) rate each store: **Compliant** (all mandatory items met), **Minor Non-Compliance** (remediable within 30 days, no immediate safety risk), **Major Non-Compliance** (requires capital improvement, potential safety risk, or regulatory citation risk); (d) compile audit results into Store Accessibility Compliance Dashboard | Facilities Standards Mgr / Regional Manager | VP Store Ops | 2–3 hours/store × 200 stores = ~400–600 hours total (spread across team) |
| 3 | **New Store Accessibility Design Review**: During new store design (W223): (a) Engineering & Construction incorporates PWD accessibility into store design standards; (b) Facilities Standards Manager reviews design plans for BP 344 compliance before construction; (c) verify accessibility in store layout planogram (W314) — aisle widths, checkout counter placement; (d) commissioning inspection (W227) includes accessibility verification; (e) obtain LGU building permit clearance for accessibility compliance (W54) | Facilities Standards Mgr / Engineering Director | VP Store Ops | Per W223 |
| 4 | **Minor Non-Compliance Remediation**: For stores with minor non-compliance: (a) Store Manager initiates corrective action within 30 days: clear obstructed pathways, install missing signage, adjust counter heights (if feasible without construction), re-stripe PWD parking spaces; (b) Facilities Standards Manager verifies remediation completion; (c) update compliance dashboard | Store Manager | Regional Manager | 1–30 days/item |
| 5 | **Major Non-Compliance Capital Remediation**: For stores with major non-compliance requiring construction: (a) Facilities Standards Manager submits capital improvement request (W21 — capex request); (b) scope: ramp installation, restroom modification, elevator/lift installation, parking lot reconfiguration; (c) Engineering & Construction manages remediation project (W226 — store renovation); (d) target: complete within 6–12 months; prioritize by regulatory citation risk and customer complaint history | Facilities Standards Mgr / Engineering Director | VP Store Ops / CFO | Per W21/W226 |
| 6 | **Customer Complaint Response**: If a PWD customer files a complaint (W41) regarding accessibility: (a) Customer Service logs complaint with accessibility tag; (b) Facilities Standards Manager investigates complaint at the specific store; (c) assess whether complaint reveals non-compliance; (d) if non-compliance confirmed: initiate remediation (W497.4 or W497.5); (e) respond to customer with corrective action timeline; (f) if complaint escalates to DTI (W469) or NCDA: coordinate with Legal Head for regulatory response | Facilities Standards Mgr / Legal Head | VP Legal | Per W41/W469 |
| 7 | **Staff Training**: Store staff receive accessibility awareness training: (a) include PWD customer service module in new hire orientation (W15); (b) annual refresher: proper interaction with PWD customers, awareness of accessibility features in store, how to assist PWD customers with merchandise; (c) include in Store Manager training (W51) on accessibility compliance obligations and LGU inspection readiness | HR Training (W51) / Facilities Standards Mgr | CHRO | Per W15/W51 |
| 8 | **Regulatory Inspection Coordination**: If NCDA, DOLE, or LGU conducts a PWD accessibility inspection: (a) Facilities Standards Manager coordinates with Store Manager for inspection preparation; (b) provide accessibility audit reports and remediation evidence; (c) Store Manager hosts inspection visit; (d) Facilities Standards Manager coordinates response to any inspection findings; (e) archive inspection results in document management (W255) and compliance dashboard | Facilities Standards Mgr / Legal Head | VP Legal | Event-driven |

### System Touchpoints
- Store Facilities Management Module: accessibility audit checklist, remediation tracking
- Compliance Dashboard: store-level accessibility compliance status
- Capex Module (W21): capital improvement requests for major remediation
- Store Design Standards (W223): accessibility design requirements
- Customer Complaint System (W41): PWD complaint tracking
- Document Management (W255): audit reports, inspection results, remediation evidence
- Integration with W47 (facility maintenance), W96 (store renovation), W223 (store design), W227 (commissioning), W54 (LGU permits), W170 (PWD discount compliance), W432 (solo parent discount), W41 (complaints), W469 (DTI escalation), W15 (onboarding), W51 (training)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Older store non-compliance**: Stores built before BP 344 enforcement (pre-1983) or in provinces with historically lax building code enforcement may have significant accessibility gaps (no ramps, inaccessible restrooms, narrow aisles) that require expensive structural modifications; full remediation across all legacy stores could cost tens of millions of pesos
- **Big-box format aisle density challenge**: BuildRight's curated 35,000 SKU assortment in 8,000–15,000 sqm stores creates high merchandise density; maintaining 1.2m aisle clearance for PWD accessibility while maximizing selling space requires careful planogram design (W314) and store discipline
- **Parking lot constraints**: Many BuildRight stores are in leased commercial spaces where the parking lot is controlled by the landlord (BuildRight Property Management Inc. for owned sites, third-party landlords for leased sites); PWD parking compliance in shared parking lots requires coordination with property management (W117)
- **Provincial LGU enforcement variability**: PWD accessibility enforcement varies significantly across Philippine LGUs; Metro Manila and major city LGUs may conduct regular inspections, while provincial LGUs may not enforce accessibility requirements, creating inconsistent compliance pressure across the 200-store chain
- **Customer discrimination liability**: Failure to provide PWD accessibility can result in discrimination complaints under RA 7277, with penalties including fines, imprisonment of responsible officers (rare but possible), and mandatory corrective action orders

### Time Estimate
- Annual accessibility standards update: 2 days
- Annual store audit: 2–3 hours per store (delegated to Regional Managers)
- Minor remediation follow-up: ~30 min per item
- Major remediation project management: per W21/W226
- Customer complaint response: 2–4 hours per complaint
- **Total annual**: ~200–300 hours for Facilities Standards Manager

### Staffing Implication
- **Facilities Standards Manager**: ~200–300 hours/year on accessibility compliance. Absorbed within existing Facilities Standards role.
- **Regional Managers**: ~40–60 hours/year for store audit participation. Absorbed into existing store visit schedule.
- **Capital remediation budget**: Estimated ~PHP 5–15M/year for accessibility improvements across the chain (included in annual capex budget W26).

## W501. Store-Level First Aid & Medical Emergency Response

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer or employee medical emergency in store; or scheduled first aid kit audit |
| **Frequency** | Ad hoc (emergencies: ~20–30 incidents/month across 200 stores); quarterly kit audits |
| **Volume** | ~1 emergency per store per 6–9 months; 200 stores × 4 quarterly audits = 800 audits/year |
| **Owner** | Store Manager |
| **Participants** | Certified First Aider, Store Manager, HR Coordinator, Safety Officer |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Certified First Aider responds to medical emergency; assesses situation (conscious/unconscious, breathing, bleeding, seizure, cardiac, allergic reaction); activates store emergency protocol if severe | First Aider | Store Manager | 5 min initial |
| 2 | If severe emergency: First Aider directs colleague to call local emergency services (911 or local hospital); assigns colleague to meet ambulance at store entrance and clear path | First Aider | Store Manager | 2 min |
| 3 | First Aider administers first aid per training protocol: CPR/AED if cardiac, pressure for bleeding, recovery position for unconscious breathing patient, epinephrine auto-injector if anaphylaxis (employee only, if prescribed) | First Aider | Store Manager | Variable |
| 3a | If AED required: retrieve AED from designated wall-mounted location; follow voice prompts; document AED usage for post-incident report | First Aider | Store Manager | 5 min |
| 4 | Store Manager notifies HR Coordinator of employee medical emergency; for customer emergencies, Store Manager contacts insurance coordinator if liability claim may arise (W285) | Store Manager | Store Manager | 10 min |
| 5 | First Aider completes first aid incident report in system: date/time, location in store, individual type (customer/employee), nature of emergency, first aid administered, outcome, whether ambulance called | First Aider | Store Manager | 15 min |
| 6 | System routes incident report: employee incidents → HR for DOLE reporting (W436); customer incidents → Legal/Insurance for potential liability (W285); all incidents → Safety Officer for trend analysis | System | Safety Officer | Automated |
| 7 | Monthly: Safety Officer reviews first aid incident register across stores; identifies patterns (specific zones, times, causes); recommends preventive measures | Safety Officer | VP Operations | 2 hours/month |
| 8 | Quarterly: First Aider conducts first aid kit audit per DOLE-prescribed contents checklist; records stock levels; submits restocking request through system | First Aider | Store Manager | 30 min/store |
| 9 | System generates restocking order for items below minimum threshold; routes to procurement (W136) for replenishment | System | — | Automated |

### System Touchpoints
- First aid incident report with location and nature of emergency (GOV-043)
- AED device inventory and maintenance tracking per store (GOV-043)
- Certified first aider roster with training expiry alerts (W51, HR-019)
- First aid kit inventory per store with DOLE-prescribed content checklist (GOV-043)
- Automated restocking request via indirect procurement (W136)
- Incident trend dashboard by store, zone, and cause (GOV-043)
- Integration with DOLE OHS reporting (W436, W140)

### Time Estimate
Emergencies: ~30 minutes per incident (response + documentation). At ~300/year = ~150 staff-hours/year. Kit audits: 30 min × 200 stores × 4/year = ~400 staff-hours/year. All absorbed within Store Manager and First Aider FTE.

### Pain Points / Risks
- First aider not on shift during emergency — mitigated by requiring ≥2 certified first aiders per store at all times (minimum from 29-person staff)
- AED malfunction discovered during emergency — mitigated by quarterly AED test and battery check as part of kit audit
- Customer sues for inadequate first aid response — mitigated by proper incident documentation and trained first aiders

### Staffing Implication
- Each store must have ≥2 certified first aiders among 29 staff; training cost absorbed by training budget (W51).
- First aid certification refresher every 2 years adds ~16 hours per first aider per cycle.
- Quarterly kit audits consume ~30 min per store per quarter = ~400 staff-hours/year; absorbed by existing staff.

## W502. Store-Level Non-Hazardous Waste Management

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Daily waste accumulation at store; scheduled waste hauler pickup; monthly waste volume reporting |
| **Frequency** | Daily waste handling per store; scheduled hauler pickup (2–3x/week); monthly reporting |
| **Volume** | ~200–400 kg/store/week (cardboard ~50%, plastic packaging ~15%, wood scrap ~10%, general waste ~25%); 200 stores × ~15,000 kg/week total |
| **Owner** | Store Manager |
| **Participants** | Maintenance/Utility Staff, Waste Hauler Vendor, Facilities Coordinator |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Maintenance/Utility Staff segregates waste into designated bins per waste stream: cardboard (baled), plastic packaging, wood/lumber scrap, general waste, organic waste (garden centers) | Maintenance Staff | Store Manager | 30 min/day |
| 2 | For cardboard: Maintenance Staff operates cardboard baler; bales are stored for hauler pickup; system tracks bale count and estimated weight per bale | Maintenance Staff | Store Manager | 15 min/day |
| 3 | Waste hauler arrives per schedule (2–3x/week); Maintenance Staff records waste collected by stream: estimated weight per type, hauler truck plate, driver name | Maintenance Staff | Store Manager | 15 min/pickup |
| 4 | System captures waste collection data: auto-calculates monthly waste volume per store by stream; compares against waste budget allocation | System | — | Automated |
| 5 | For recyclable streams (cardboard, plastic): system calculates recycling revenue based on prevailing market rate per kg; revenue posted to store P&L as waste recycling income | System | — | Automated |
| 6 | Monthly: Store Manager reviews waste volume report; identifies stores with waste volume exceeding baseline by >20%; initiates root cause investigation (over-packaging from vendor, receiving practices) | Store Manager | Regional Manager | 30 min/month |
| 7 | Quarterly: Facilities Coordinator reviews waste hauler vendor performance (pickup reliability, contamination rejection rate, cost per kg); feeds into vendor review (W242) | Facilities Coordinator | VP Operations | 2 hours/quarter |
| 8 | Annually: Facilities Coordinator sets waste reduction targets per store; cardboard recycling target ≥90%; overall waste-to-landfill reduction target; tracks against ESG metrics (W192) | Facilities Coordinator | VP Operations | 4 hours/year |

### System Touchpoints
- Waste stream classification per store with volume tracking (GOV-044)
- Cardboard bale count and weight tracking with recycling revenue calculation (GOV-044)
- Waste hauler vendor scheduling and pickup confirmation (GOV-044, W62B)
- Monthly waste volume report per store with baseline comparison (GOV-044)
- Recycling revenue posting to store P&L (FIN-040, W489)
- Waste reduction target tracking integrated with ESG metrics (W192, W193)
- Integration with store recycling program (W167) and circular economy (W193)

### Time Estimate
Daily waste handling: ~45 min/store/day (segregation + baling + recording). At 200 stores × 365 days = ~54,750 staff-hours/year. Absorbed by Maintenance/Utility Staff (1 per store) as part of daily duties.

### Pain Points / Risks
- Waste hauler missed pickup causing waste accumulation — mitigated by backup hauler arrangement and hauler SLA in vendor contract
- Cardboard bale storage space insufficient during peak season (Christmas, major promos) — mitigated by increasing pickup frequency during peak periods
- Cross-contamination of waste streams reducing recycling revenue — mitigated by proper bin signage and staff training

### Staffing Implication
- Fully absorbed by existing Maintenance/Utility Staff (1 per store) within daily duties.
- Waste hauler vendor management absorbed by Facilities Coordinator at HQ.

## W503. Store-Level Pest Control & Sanitation Management

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Scheduled pest control service visit; pest incident reported by staff; monthly sanitation audit |
| **Frequency** | Monthly pest control service per store; ad hoc pest incidents; monthly sanitation self-audit; quarterly deep sanitation |
| **Volume** | 200 stores × 12 monthly visits = 2,400 visits/year; ~50 pest incidents/month chain-wide |
| **Owner** | Store Manager |
| **Participants** | Maintenance/Utility Staff, Pest Control Vendor Technician, Facilities Coordinator, Safety Officer |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Pest control vendor technician arrives per monthly schedule; conducts inspection of store zones (sales floor, receiving dock, lumber yard, garden center, break room, storage areas) | Vendor Technician | Store Manager | 45 min |
| 2 | Technician documents findings per zone: pest type (rodent, cockroach, ant, termite, bird, fly), severity (none/low/medium/high), treatment applied, bait station refill, trap count | Vendor Technician | Store Manager | 15 min |
| 3 | Technician enters service report into system via vendor portal or tablet: zone-by-zone findings, chemicals applied (for MSDS tracking), next visit recommendations, corrective actions required by store | Vendor Technician | Store Manager | 10 min |
| 4 | If pest incident reported between visits: staff logs incident in system (pest type, location, photo); system auto-escalates to pest control vendor for emergency visit within 48 hours SLA | Maintenance Staff | Store Manager | 10 min |
| 5 | Monthly: Store Manager completes sanitation self-audit checklist per zone: cleanliness score, spill/drainage issues, food/organic waste storage compliance, loading dock cleanliness, break room hygiene | Store Manager | Regional Manager | 30 min |
| 6 | System aggregates pest control and sanitation scores per store; flag stores scoring below threshold (<70% sanitation or >3 high-severity pest findings) for Regional Manager review | System | — | Automated |
| 7 | Quarterly: pest control vendor conducts deep treatment (fogging, drain treatment, termite inspection for lumber yards); separate work order with extended preparation requirements | Vendor Technician | Facilities Coordinator | 2 hours |
| 8 | Annually: Facilities Coordinator reviews pest control vendor performance (response time, incident reduction rate, audit scores); renews or rebids contract; feeds into vendor review (W242) | Facilities Coordinator | VP Operations | 4 hours/year |

### System Touchpoints
- Pest control service scheduling with vendor calendar integration (GOV-045)
- Zone-by-zone pest findings with severity classification (GOV-045)
- Pest incident reporting with emergency vendor dispatch (GOV-045)
- Monthly sanitation self-audit checklist per zone (GOV-045)
- Chemical application tracking for MSDS compliance (W237)
- Store-level pest and sanitation scorecard dashboard (GOV-045)
- Integration with LGU sanitary permits (W448) and DOLE OHS reporting (W436)
- Integration with food-safety SKU management for garden center seasonal food items

### Time Estimate
Monthly pest control visits: ~1 hour/store × 12 months × 200 stores = ~2,400 staff-hours/year. Sanitation self-audits: 30 min × 12 × 200 = ~1,200 staff-hours/year. All absorbed by Store Manager and Maintenance Staff.

### Pain Points / Risks
- Termite infestation in lumber yard causing significant inventory damage — mitigated by quarterly deep treatment and annual termite inspection
- Pest control chemicals contamination of merchandise — mitigated by scheduling treatments outside store hours and covering merchandise
- Negative customer perception if pest sighting occurs on sales floor — mitigated by proactive monthly treatments and rapid incident response

### Staffing Implication
- Fully absorbed by existing Store Manager and Maintenance/Utility Staff FTE.
- Pest control vendor costs: ~PHP 5,000–8,000/store/month = ~PHP 12–19M/year chain-wide.

## W504. Store-Level Digital Signage & Content Management

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Content schedule update from HQ marketing; new promotional campaign; ad-hoc content change request from store |
| **Frequency** | Weekly content updates; campaign-based content changes (~6 major campaigns/year); ad-hoc changes as needed |
| **Volume** | ~10–15 content updates/month across 200 stores; ~50 screens per store × 200 stores = ~10,000 managed endpoints |
| **Owner** | Marketing Content Manager |
| **Participants** | Creative Team, Store Manager, IT Support |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Marketing Content Manager creates digital signage content schedule: assigns content to screen zones (entrance, aisle endcaps, checkout area, lumber yard, garden center, paint section, service desk) and time slots | Content Manager | CMO | 2 hours/week |
| 2 | Creative Team produces zone-specific content: promotional videos, product spotlights, price highlights, brand ads, seasonal themes, safety reminders, store hours | Creative Team | Content Manager | Per campaign |
| 3 | Content Manager uploads content to digital signage content management system (CMS); assigns to store clusters, zones, and schedule | Content Manager | — | 1 hour/week |
| 4 | System validates content: resolution compliance per screen type, duration limits, brand guideline compliance (colors, fonts, logos), DOH/DTI-required health/safety notice inclusion | System | — | Automated |
| 5 | System distributes content to store endpoints during off-peak hours (overnight); confirms download and activation per screen per store | System | — | Automated |
| 6 | System captures automated screenshots of each screen per store at random intervals (3x/day); flags screens showing error, offline, or incorrect content for IT Support follow-up | System | IT Support | Automated |
| 7 | Store Manager submits ad-hoc content change request (e.g., local event promotion, store-specific announcement); request routed to Content Manager for approval and production | Store Manager | Content Manager | 15 min/request |
| 8 | Monthly: Content Manager reviews playback compliance dashboard: content uptime per screen, content rotation adherence, error rate by store; identifies chronic problem screens for IT maintenance (W265) | Content Manager | CMO | 2 hours/month |
| 9 | Quarterly: Content Manager reviews content effectiveness with campaign team: dwell time at endcap screens, campaign performance lift at digital signage stores vs. non-digital | Content Manager | VP Marketing | 2 hours/quarter |

### System Touchpoints
- Centralized CMS with zone-based content scheduling and store cluster targeting (GOV-046)
- Automated content validation (resolution, brand guidelines, mandatory notices) (GOV-046)
- Remote content distribution to 10,000+ endpoints with download confirmation (GOV-046, NFR-012)
- Automated screenshot-based compliance monitoring (GOV-046)
- Ad-hoc content request workflow with approval routing (GOV-046)
- Playback compliance dashboard with uptime and error metrics (GOV-046)
- Integration with promotional setup workflow (W262) and campaign planning (W83)
- Integration with creative production management (W190) for content source

### Time Estimate
Content scheduling and management: ~3 hours/week for Content Manager. Campaign-based production: absorbed by Creative Team. Compliance monitoring: ~2 hours/month. Total: ~200 staff-hours/year. Absorbed by Marketing and IT FTEs.

### Pain Points / Risks
- Screen hardware failures leaving zones with no content — mitigated by playback compliance monitoring and POS hardware maintenance workflow (W265)
- Content scheduling errors displaying wrong promotional pricing — mitigated by content validation and integration with pricing master (W289)
- Network bandwidth at provincial stores causing delayed content download — mitigated by overnight distribution during off-peak and content pre-caching

### Staffing Implication
- 1 Marketing Content Manager role within existing Marketing team (23 FTE) to manage digital signage.
- IT Support for screen maintenance absorbed by existing IT Helpdesk (W48) and POS hardware workflow (W265).
- Creative content production absorbed by in-house creative team (W190).
