## W516. Self-Checkout (SCO) Daily Operations

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Store opening (SCO station activation) or customer approaches SCO station |
| **Frequency** | Daily during store operating hours |
| **Volume** | ~2 SCO stations per store × ~46 transactions/station/day = ~92 SCO transactions/store/day (estimated 10–15% of total store throughput at stores with SCO); rolling out to 100 stores initially |
| **Owner** | SCO Attendant (dedicated role or Lead Cashier doubling) |
| **Participants** | SCO Attendant, Customer, Store Manager (escalation), Loss Prevention (analytics) |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | At store opening (W5A), SCO Attendant powers on SCO stations; system runs self-diagnostic (scanner, scale, payment terminal, receipt printer, coin dispenser); stations report "Ready" or flag hardware issues to IT Helpdesk (W48) | SCO Attendant | Store Manager | 10 min |
| 2 | System loads daily price file, promotional rules, age-restricted SKU list, and product master to SCO stations (same data set as staffed POS terminals per W5A.5) | System | — | Automated |
| 3 | Customer initiates SCO session: scans first item; customer-facing screen displays item name, price, and running total; system validates scanned barcode against product master | Customer / System | — | Per item |
| 4 | **Weight verification**: after customer places scanned item in bagging area, system compares item weight against master weight tolerance (±5%); if mismatch: system pauses and alerts SCO Attendant with visual/audible signal | System / SCO Attendant | SCO Attendant | 15 sec if alert |
| 5 | **Skip-scan detection**: system monitors bagging area weight against cumulative expected weight of scanned items; if weight exceeds expected by tolerance threshold (indicating unscanned item in bagging area): system pauses transaction and alerts SCO Attendant | System / SCO Attendant | SCO Attendant | 15 sec if alert |
| 6 | If customer scans age-restricted SKU (per POS-029 age-restricted list): system pauses transaction; SCO Attendant approaches customer, requests government-issued photo ID, verifies age on SCO Attendant terminal (separate from SCO customer screen); SCO Attendant enters approval or denial on Attendant terminal — customer screen shows "Age verification in progress"; if denied: system removes item from transaction and displays denial message per W520 | SCO Attendant | SCO Attendant | 1 min |
| 7 | Customer completes scanning all items; system displays total with promotional pricing auto-applied (same promo engine as W5B.6); customer selects payment method on screen | Customer / System | — | 30 sec |
| 8 | **Payment processing**: cash — system accepts bills/coins via cash recycler, calculates and dispenses change; card — customer inserts/taps on PIN pad, payment processed per standard card integration (W5B.7); e-wallet — customer scans QR code on SCO screen or taps phone on NFC reader; split tender — customer selects "Split Payment" and chooses tender combination | Customer / System | — | 30–90 sec |
| 9 | System prints receipt; offers e-receipt option per W528; customer collects receipt and bags | System / Customer | — | 15 sec |
| 10 | **Exception / intervention escalation**: if SCO station encounters any unresolvable exception (coupon validation failure, loyalty card read error, catch-weight item, trade account pricing, void request, price dispute, suspected fraudulent scanning): SCO Attendant converts SCO transaction to assisted checkout at the SCO station or redirects customer to nearest staffed POS terminal; SCO Attendant can recall SCO transaction on their terminal and complete as assisted checkout per W5B | SCO Attendant | Store Manager | 2–5 min per event |
| 11 | At store closing (W5F), SCO Attendant closes SCO stations: system generates SCO-specific Z-report (transaction count, revenue by tender, void count, exception count, weight-alert count); SCO Attendant reconciles cash recycler contents against Z-report; secures cash per W5F.5–5b standard procedures | SCO Attendant | Store Manager | 15 min |
| 12 | Weekly: Store Manager reviews SCO analytics dashboard — SCO transaction volume as % of total store throughput, SCO exception rate, SCO void rate, weight-alert frequency, skip-scan alert frequency, average SCO transaction time vs. staffed POS, SCO customer satisfaction (from receipt QR survey W65); identifies SCO stations or stores with high exception rates for LP review or retraining | Store Manager | Regional Manager | 30 min/week |

### System Touchpoints
- SCO station self-diagnostic at startup: scanner, scale, payment terminal, receipt printer, coin dispenser health check (W516.1)
- Weight verification system: master item weight with ±5% tolerance; bagging area weight sensor comparison per scan (W516.4)
- Skip-scan detection: cumulative weight monitoring against expected weight from scanned items (W516.5)
- Age-restricted product prompting at SCO with Attendant terminal approval workflow (W516.6, POS-029, W520)
- Multi-tender payment at SCO: cash recycler, card PIN pad, e-wallet QR/NFC (W516.8, POS-004)
- SCO-to-assisted checkout conversion: transaction recall on Attendant terminal (W516.10)
- SCO-specific Z-report and cash recycler reconciliation (W516.11, POS-009)
- SCO analytics dashboard: throughput, exception rate, void rate, weight-alert frequency, skip-scan frequency, avg transaction time (W516.12)
- Integration with W5A (store opening — SCO startup), W5B (in-store selling — SCO as parallel channel), W5F (store closing — SCO Z-report), W206 (mPOS — complementary queue-busting), W281 (SCO exception management — detailed exception types), W37 (loss prevention — SCO analytics feed), W48 (helpdesk — SCO hardware issues), W265 (POS hardware maintenance — SCO peripheral management)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Shrinkage risk** — SCO has inherently higher shrinkage than staffed POS (industry average 2–4× higher); skip-scan detection mitigates but does not eliminate; LP must monitor SCO shrinkage rates via inventory variance correlation (W248)
- **Customer frustration with weight alerts** — false-positive weight alerts (item packaging variation, customer rearranging bags, leaning on bagging area) interrupt workflow and degrade SCO experience; requiring frequent Attendant intervention defeats self-service purpose
- **Cash recycler reliability** — cash recyclers in SCO stations jam more frequently than staffed POS cash drawers (coins, crumpled bills); a jammed recycler takes the entire SCO station offline until IT or maintenance resolves
- **SCO adoption rates** — in Philippine hardware retail (contractors, tradespeople buying in bulk), SCO may see lower adoption than grocery or general merchandise; high-bulk, catch-weight, and trade account transactions cannot be processed at SCO, limiting applicability
- **Exception flooding during peak hours** — multiple SCO stations triggering exceptions simultaneously overwhelms a single SCO Attendant, creating customer wait times and negative experience

### Time Estimate
SCO startup: ~10 min/store/day. SCO closing: ~15 min/store/day. SCO Attendant monitoring: continuous during operating hours (1 Attendant per 2–4 SCO stations). Weekly analytics review: ~30 min/week. Total incremental labor per SCO-equipped store: ~8–10 hours/day for Attendant coverage + 10 min opening + 15 min closing + 30 min/week analytics = ~55–60 staff-hours/week per store.

### Staffing Implication
- **1 SCO Attendant per store** (dedicated role) for stores with 2+ SCO stations; absorbed by Lead Cashier at stores with only 1 SCO station. Estimated 100 SCO-equipped stores × 1 FTE = 100 incremental FTEs for SCO operations.
- Alternative: SCO Attendant role rotates from existing cashier pool (no incremental FTE) but reduces staffed POS coverage by 1 cashier per shift.
- Training time: SCO Attendant requires standard cashier certification (W518) plus 4-hour SCO-specific training module.

## W517. POS Cashier Shift Handover & Drawer Accountability

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Shift change (typically mid-day, ~2:00 PM at stores with 2 shifts) |
| **Frequency** | Daily per POS terminal with shift-based staffing |
| **Volume** | ~3 terminals × 200 stores = ~600 handovers/day (terminals staffed across 2 shifts); remaining 2 terminals operate single-shift and only require EOD per W5F |
| **Owner** | Outgoing Cashier / Incoming Cashier |
| **Participants** | Outgoing Cashier, Incoming Cashier, Store Manager (variance escalation) |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Outgoing Cashier completes or suspends any in-progress transaction per W521; system enforces no pending transactions before handover can proceed | Outgoing Cashier | — | 1 min |
| 2 | System generates X-report (mid-day sales summary by tender type for this terminal since opening or last handover) — distinct from Z-report which is end-of-day | System | — | Automated |
| 3 | Outgoing Cashier counts physical cash in drawer; compares to X-report expected cash | Outgoing Cashier | — | 5 min |
| 4 | If cash variance > PHP 200: Outgoing Cashier documents variance reason in system; system alerts Store Manager; Store Manager investigates per W272 (cashier over/short dispute resolution) | Outgoing Cashier / Store Manager | Store Manager | 10 min if variance |
| 5 | Outgoing Cashier records drawer count in system: cash by denomination, total cash, credit/debit card slips count, e-wallet transaction count, total tender amount per X-report, variance (if any) | Outgoing Cashier | — | 5 min |
| 6 | Outgoing Cashier removes cash drawer (till); places in tamper-evident bag with handwritten or printed count slip; secures in backroom safe | Outgoing Cashier | Store Manager | 5 min |
| 7 | Store Manager or Lead Cashier issues sealed replacement drawer with standard float (PHP 5,000 per W5A.4) from safe to Incoming Cashier | Store Manager / Lead Cashier | Store Manager | 2 min |
| 8 | Incoming Cashier logs into POS terminal; system displays handover screen showing X-report totals from Outgoing Cashier; Incoming Cashier confirms receipt of sealed drawer and acknowledges handover in system | Incoming Cashier | — | 3 min |
| 9 | System records handover event: outgoing cashier ID, incoming cashier ID, terminal ID, X-report totals, drawer count, variance (if any), handover timestamp; links to W272 if variance escalated | System | — | Automated |
| 10 | If no replacement cashier available (sick call, no-show): Store Manager assigns float cashier from available staff; if no staff available, Store Manager operates terminal directly or closes terminal for shift (reducing store checkout capacity) | Store Manager | — | 5 min |

### System Touchpoints
- X-report generation per terminal per shift (W517.2) — distinct from EOD Z-report (W5F.2)
- System block on handover if pending transactions exist (W517.1, W521)
- Drawer count entry with denomination breakdown and variance flagging (W517.3–5)
- Handover event logging with outgoing/incoming cashier IDs, terminal, X-report, variance (W517.9)
- Automatic W272 escalation for variances > PHP 200 at handover (W517.4)
- Integration with W5A (opening — initial float), W5F (closing — EOD Z-report), W272 (cashier over/short), W278 (mid-day cash skimming), W212 (Smart Safe — sealed drawer storage)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Handover queue delays** — customers waiting in line during handover (peak 2 PM shift change coincides with afternoon rush); mitigated by staggering handover across terminals (not all at once)
- **Cash count disputes** — outgoing and incoming cashiers disagreeing on drawer count, especially when tamper-evident bag is not used and drawer is transferred open; system-enforced count-before-swap mitigates
- **Float availability** — insufficient pre-prepared sealed drawers in safe (Store Manager needs 3 sealed drawers ready for shift change); if float preparation is skipped, handover takes longer while drawer is counted and re-floated
- **Variance pattern undetected** — recurring small variances (< PHP 200) at handover that don't trigger alerts but accumulate over weeks; Store Manager should review weekly variance trends per cashier
- **Cashier login sharing** — incoming cashier using outgoing cashier's login if handover screen is skipped; system should enforce logout/login cycle at shift change

### Time Estimate
~20–25 min per handover (1 min finalize + automated X-report + 5 min count + 5 min record + 5 min drawer swap + 3 min login/confirm). Variance investigation adds 10 min. Across 600 handovers/day chain-wide, total labor is ~200–250 hours/day.

### Staffing Implication
- No incremental headcount — handover is a standard cashier and Store Manager responsibility absorbed within shift structure.
- Store Manager must prepare 3 sealed float drawers before shift change (part of mid-day routine, ~15 min).
- Across 200 stores with 3 handovers/day, total chain-wide handover labor is ~200 hours/day, fully absorbed by existing cashier and Store Manager FTEs.

## W518. Cashier Onboarding, POS Training & Competency Certification

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | New cashier hire completes HR onboarding (W15) or existing employee transferred to cashier role |
| **Frequency** | As needed — estimated 200–300 new cashiers/year across 200 stores (turnover replacement + new store hires + seasonal temp conversion) |
| **Volume** | ~4–6 new cashiers/month across the chain; ~1 per store per year on average |
| **Owner** | Store Manager (training oversight); Lead Cashier (training delivery) |
| **Participants** | New Cashier, Lead Cashier (trainer), Store Manager, HR Training Coordinator (W51) |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | New cashier completes HR onboarding (W15) including employee master creation (W292) and IT provisioning (W152); Store Manager assigns cashier role in POS system with "Trainee" status | Store Manager / HR | Store Manager | Part of W15 |
| 2 | Lead Cashier conducts POS system orientation: terminal layout, login/logout, barcode scanner operation, customer display, cash drawer mechanism, receipt printer loading, basic transaction flow (scan → tender → receipt) | Lead Cashier | Store Manager | 4 hours |
| 3 | New cashier operates in **training mode**: system simulates transactions (simulated barcode scans, tender, receipt) without posting to GL or deducting inventory; training mode receipts print "TRAINING — NOT VALID" watermark; training transactions logged separately | New Cashier | Lead Cashier | 4 hours |
| 4 | **Supervised transactions**: new cashier processes live customer transactions under Lead Cashier supervision; Lead Cashier observes and corrects in real-time; system tracks supervised transaction count against certification milestone (minimum 50 supervised transactions) | New Cashier / Lead Cashier | Store Manager | 2–3 days (~50 transactions) |
| 5 | **Tender type training**: new cashier processes each tender type under supervision — cash (counting change, detecting counterfeit per W37), credit/debit card (PIN pad, signature), e-wallet (QR scan, NFC), split tender, trade account (W5B.4c) | New Cashier / Lead Cashier | Lead Cashier | 1 day |
| 6 | **Special procedure training**: price override with authorization (W5B.4a), void transaction (W5B.10), return/exchange processing (W12A), gift card/store credit (W28), catch-weight items (W5B.2), paint mixing custom SKU (W5B.3), employee purchase (W5B.12), age-restricted products (W520) | New Cashier / Lead Cashier | Lead Cashier | 1 day |
| 7 | **Department product knowledge**: new cashier trains on product identification by department (Lumber, Plumbing/Electrical, Tiles, Tools/Hardware) — recognizing common SKUs, understanding unit-of-measure, identifying catch-weight vs. standard items, hazardous material indicators | New Cashier / Dept. Supervisor | Store Manager | 2 hours |
| 8 | **Certification assessment**: Store Manager observes new cashier processing 10 consecutive live transactions without error; Store Manager signs off on cashier competency checklist; system updates cashier status from "Trainee" to "Certified" — certified cashiers can operate terminals unsupervised | Store Manager / New Cashier | Store Manager | 1 hour |
| 9 | System records cashier certification in employee training log (W51) with: certification date, certified-by manager ID, terminal types qualified (standard POS, SCO, mPOS), competency checklist score; certification linked to employee master (W292) | System / Store Manager | — | Automated |
| 10 | **Annual recertification**: each January, Store Manager reviews all certified cashiers' performance metrics (transaction speed, void rate, variance history from W522); cashiers with performance below threshold (e.g., > 3× average void rate or > PHP 1,000 cumulative annual variance) undergo refresher training (repeat steps 5–8 abbreviated) | Store Manager | Regional Manager | 1 hour/cashier |

### System Touchpoints
- POS "Trainee" role with restricted permissions: training mode, no void authority, no price override authority (W518.1)
- Training mode: simulated transactions with "TRAINING — NOT VALID" receipt watermark; separate transaction log (W518.3)
- Supervised transaction counter: system tracks number of supervised live transactions per trainee against 50-transaction milestone (W518.4)
- Cashier certification status in employee master (W292) and training log (W51): Trainee → Certified date, certified-by, qualified terminal types (W518.9)
- Annual recertification: cashier performance metrics pulled from W522 daily review data (W518.10)
- Integration with W15 (HR onboarding — trigger), W152 (IT provisioning — POS access), W51 (training management — certification log), W292 (employee master — status tracking), W34 (shift scheduling — trainee scheduling constraints)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Training mode gaps** — training mode cannot perfectly simulate all edge cases (network latency, offline mode W5G, 3PL delivery device errors); new cashiers encounter real-world scenarios only during supervised phase
- **Supervision burden** — Lead Cashier must shadow trainee for 50+ transactions (2–3 days), reducing Lead Cashier's own productivity during peak training periods
- **Turnover waste** — high cashier turnover in Philippine retail means significant training investment is lost when newly certified cashiers leave within 3–6 months; Store Manager must continuously train replacements
- **Inconsistent training quality** — without standardized training materials and assessment criteria, training quality varies by Lead Cashier and store; some stores produce better-prepared cashiers than others
- **Trainee errors during supervised phase** — errors by trainees processing live transactions (wrong change, incorrect void, missed discount) impact customer experience and may require W272 variance resolution

### Time Estimate
Total training duration: ~5–7 working days per new cashier (4 hours orientation + 4 hours training mode + 2–3 days supervised ~50 transactions + 1 day tender training + 1 day special procedures + 2 hours product knowledge + 1 hour certification). Annual recertification: ~1 hour per cashier. Across 200–300 new cashiers/year, total training labor is ~1,200–1,800 staff-hours/year (trainer time + trainee time).

### Staffing Implication
- No incremental headcount for training — absorbed by Lead Cashier (trainer) and Store Manager (assessor) within existing FTEs.
- Training reduces Lead Cashier productive capacity by ~30% during supervised phase (2–3 days per trainee); at stores with high turnover, Lead Cashier may spend 10–20% of time on training.
- Standardized POS training materials developed by HR Training Coordinator (W51) as one-time investment.

## W519. POS Suspicious Transaction & AML Compliance Reporting

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Cash transaction ≥ PHP 500,000 (single or cumulative within 24 hours for same customer) OR system detects suspicious structuring pattern OR cashier/manager identifies suspicious customer behavior |
| **Frequency** | Rare — estimated 5–10 flagged transactions per year chain-wide for hardware retail; more likely at stores near construction hubs serving contractors |
| **Volume** | ~5–10 suspicious transaction reports (STRs) filed annually with AMLC |
| **Owner** | Compliance Officer (filing); Store Manager (initial documentation) |
| **Participants** | Cashier, Store Manager, Compliance Officer, AMLC (external) |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Threshold monitoring**: system automatically monitors all cash transactions at POS; if single cash transaction ≥ PHP 500,000: system flags transaction and alerts Store Manager via terminal notification; if same customer (identified via loyalty card, name, or vehicle plate) accumulates cash transactions ≥ PHP 500,000 within 24 hours across multiple transactions: system alerts Store Manager with structuring detection alert | System | — | Automated |
| 2 | **Transaction hold**: for flagged transactions, system allows transaction completion but places cash in temporary hold status; system prompts cashier to collect customer identification (government-issued photo ID) and record customer details (full name, ID type, ID number, address, contact number, nature of transaction/purpose of purchase) | Cashier | Store Manager | 5 min |
| 3 | Cashier completes customer identification form in system (or system auto-populates from loyalty member data if customer is enrolled); if customer refuses to provide identification: Store Manager is notified; transaction is completed but flagged as "Customer Refused ID" — Compliance Officer reviews within 24 hours | Cashier / Customer | Store Manager | 3 min |
| 4 | Store Manager reviews flagged transaction details: customer identity, transaction amount, items purchased, payment method, any prior flagged transactions for same customer; Store Manager assesses whether transaction appears legitimate (e.g., contractor bulk-buying materials for a project) or suspicious (e.g., purchasing high-value easily-resalable items with large cash, customer appears nervous or evasive) | Store Manager | — | 15 min |
| 5 | If Store Manager assesses transaction as **legitimate**: documents assessment with justification (e.g., "Contractor purchasing cement, rebar, and tiles for government project — presented purchase order from agency"); system records legitimacy determination with manager ID; no STR filed; transaction hold released; documentation retained for 5 years per AMLA record retention | Store Manager | — | 10 min |
| 6 | If Store Manager assesses transaction as **suspicious** or is unsure: Store Manager escalates to Compliance Officer within 24 hours; Compliance Officer reviews transaction details, customer history, and Store Manager's assessment | Store Manager / Compliance Officer | Compliance Officer | 30 min |
| 7 | Compliance Officer determines if Suspicious Transaction Report (STR) is warranted per AMLC guidelines; if yes: Compliance Officer prepares STR using AMLC online portal with required details (covered person information, customer information, transaction details, suspicious indicators, supporting documents) | Compliance Officer | CFO | 2 hours |
| 8 | Compliance Officer files STR with AMLC via online portal; system logs STR filing with AMLC reference number, filing date, and linked POS transaction(s) | Compliance Officer | CFO | 30 min |
| 9 | **Tipping-off prohibition**: system restricts visibility of STR filing — cashier and Store Manager who reported are NOT informed of STR filing status; only Compliance Officer and CFO have access to STR records; per AMLA Section 9, no person shall disclose to any customer that a STR has been or will be filed | System / Compliance Officer | CFO | Ongoing |
| 10 | Compliance Officer maintains STR register: all filed STRs with AMLC reference numbers, filing dates, transaction references, and status; register subject to AMLC examination during periodic compliance audits | Compliance Officer | CFO | Quarterly review |
| 11 | Annual: Compliance Officer conducts AML awareness training for all Store Managers and Lead Cashiers (integrated with W51 training program); training covers AMLA requirements, red flags for suspicious transactions, customer identification procedures, tipping-off prohibition, and STR filing process | Compliance Officer | CFO | 2 hours/year |

### System Touchpoints
- Automatic cash transaction threshold monitoring: single transaction ≥ PHP 500,000 and cumulative 24-hour monitoring per customer (W519.1)
- Structuring detection: multiple transactions below threshold by same customer within 24 hours (W519.1)
- Customer identification form in POS with auto-populate from loyalty member data (W519.2–3)
- Transaction hold status for flagged transactions (W519.2)
- Legitimacy assessment documentation with manager ID and justification (W519.5)
- STR filing log with AMLC reference number and restricted visibility per tipping-off prohibition (W519.8–9)
- STR register for AMLC compliance audits (W519.10)
- Integration with W5B (in-store selling — transaction source), W17 (loyalty — customer identification), W37 (loss prevention — suspicious behavior escalation), W51 (training — AML awareness), W292 (employee master — training completion tracking), W354 (B2B AML screening — parallel process for trade accounts)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **False positives** — in hardware retail, PHP 500,000+ cash purchases by contractors are legitimate business transactions; excessive flagging wastes Store Manager and Compliance Officer time; system should learn to reduce false positives over time (e.g., whitelisting known trade account customers with verified business registration)
- **Customer friction** — requiring government ID for large cash purchases may irritate legitimate contractor customers who are time-sensitive; Store Manager must handle diplomatically
- **Tipping-off risk** — if Store Manager informs customer that their transaction was "flagged" or "reported," this constitutes a criminal offense under AMLA Section 9; training must emphasize prohibition
- **Structuring detection limitations** — customers structuring across multiple terminals within same store or across multiple stores within 24 hours may evade detection if system does not aggregate across terminals and locations in real-time
- **Low volume but high stakes** — with only 5–10 reports/year, institutional knowledge is limited; process must be well-documented so any Store Manager can execute correctly

### Time Estimate
Legitimate transaction assessment: ~25 min (15 min review + 10 min documentation). Suspicious transaction escalation: ~1 hour for Store Manager (initial documentation + escalation call). STR preparation and filing: ~2.5 hours for Compliance Officer. Annual AML awareness training: 2 hours/session × ~200 Store Managers and Lead Cashiers (delivered via e-learning + in-person regional sessions).

### Staffing Implication
- **No incremental store-level headcount** — suspicious transaction handling is a Store Manager responsibility (estimated 1–2 hours/year per store at most).
- **Compliance Officer** — existing Compliance Officer within Finance/Legal team handles STR filing; estimated 25–50 hours/year for AML POS-related activity (STR filing, register maintenance, training delivery).
- Annual AML awareness training absorbed by W51 training program (2 hours within annual training calendar).

---

## W520. Age-Restricted Product Verification & Compliance at POS

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Barcode scan of age-restricted SKU at POS or SCO |
| **Frequency** | Multiple daily per store — estimated 5–10 age-restricted transactions per store per day (solvents, thinners, spray paints, cutting tools, pesticides) |
| **Volume** | ~1,000–2,000 age-restricted transactions/day chain-wide |
| **Owner** | Cashier (verification); Store Manager (compliance oversight) |
| **Participants** | Cashier, Customer, Store Manager (escalation), Compliance Team (audit) |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Customer brings age-restricted items to checkout; Cashier scans barcode; system identifies SKU as age-restricted from item master flag (per DENR/FPA-regulated chemicals, cutting tools, solvents list maintained in W252 item master) | Cashier / System | — | Per scan |
| 2 | System interrupts transaction flow: POS terminal displays full-screen age-verification prompt showing item name, minimum age requirement (18 for solvents, cutting tools, pesticides per FPA regulations; configurable per SKU), and "Request Government-Issued Photo ID" instruction to cashier | System | — | Automated |
| 3 | Customer-facing display shows "Age Verification Required" message (does not display specific product or reason to protect customer privacy) | System | — | Automated |
| 4 | Cashier verbally requests customer's government-issued photo ID (acceptable forms: Philippine driver's license, passport, PhilHealth ID, SSS ID, GSIS ID, COMELEC voter's ID, national ID, postal ID with photo) | Cashier | — | 30 sec |
| 5 | Cashier selects ID type from dropdown on POS terminal; enters customer date of birth from ID or scans ID barcode (if machine-readable) | Cashier | — | 30 sec |
| 6 | System calculates customer age from date of birth; if age ≥ minimum age requirement: system displays "VERIFIED — Age Confirmed" on cashier screen; customer display returns to transaction total; transaction proceeds | System | — | Automated |
| 7 | If age < minimum age requirement: system displays "DENIED — Customer Below Required Age" on cashier screen; customer-facing display shows "Transaction Restricted"; system removes age-restricted item(s) from transaction; remaining non-restricted items continue to checkout; Cashier informs customer that specific item(s) cannot be sold due to age restriction | Cashier / System | Store Manager | 30 sec |
| 8 | If customer cannot produce valid ID: system denies sale of age-restricted item(s) per same step 7 procedure; customer can still purchase non-restricted items in transaction | Cashier | — | 30 sec |
| 9 | System logs age verification event: transaction ID, SKU(s) verified, customer age or "No ID" status, cashier ID, verification result (verified/denied/no ID), timestamp; log available for DENR/FPA compliance audit | System | — | Automated |
| 10 | Monthly: Compliance Team runs age-restricted product compliance report — verification rate (% of age-restricted transactions where verification was completed), denial rate, "No ID" rate by store; stores with verification rate < 95% flagged for retraining (W518) | Compliance Team | Legal/Compliance Head | 1 hour/month |
| 11 | Quarterly: Store Manager conducts spot-audit: observes 5 random age-restricted transactions at checkout (or reviews CCTV W71 footage of age-restricted scans) to verify cashiers are following ID request and verification procedure; documents observations in compliance log | Store Manager | Regional Manager | 30 min/quarter |

### System Touchpoints
- Age-restricted SKU flag in item master (W252) with configurable minimum age per SKU or category (W520.1)
- Full-screen age verification prompt interrupting transaction flow at scan (W520.2)
- Customer-facing display "Age Verification Required" message (W520.3)
- ID type dropdown with acceptable Philippine government-issued ID types (W520.5)
- Age calculation from date of birth with automated verified/denied determination (W520.6–7)
- Age verification compliance log with transaction, SKU, customer age/no ID, cashier, result (W520.9)
- Monthly compliance report: verification rate, denial rate, "No ID" rate by store (W520.10)
- Integration with W5B (in-store selling — verification within checkout flow), W252 (item master — age-restricted flag), W37 (loss prevention — compliance log review), W518 (cashier training — age-restricted procedure training), W71 (CCTV — spot-audit footage), W501 (first aid — solvent abuse incident response)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Cashier bypass risk** — cashiers may skip ID verification to avoid customer confrontation or speed up checkout, especially during peak hours; automated prompt mitigates but cashier can enter false DOB; quarterly spot-audits and verification rate monitoring deter bypass
- **Customer confrontation** — customers (especially older adults) may be offended when asked for ID; cashiers must be trained to explain calmly and consistently ("Ma'am/Sir, we are required by law to verify age for these products")
- **Scope of restricted items unclear** — not all Philippine retailers enforce age restrictions consistently; defining the exact SKU list that triggers verification requires legal review and regular updates as regulations change
- **SCO age verification** — self-checkout stations require SCO Attendant intervention for age verification (W516.6), adding to attendant workload; risk that attendant approves without proper ID check
- **ID fraud** — customers may present fake or borrowed IDs; cashiers are not trained forensic document examiners; system accepts DOB entry in good faith

### Time Estimate
~30–60 seconds per age-restricted transaction (30 sec ID request + 30 sec ID entry and system verification). Adds ~15–30 min/day to cashier workload per store at 5–10 age-restricted transactions/day. Monthly compliance report: 1 hour. Quarterly spot-audit: 30 min/store.

### Staffing Implication
- No incremental headcount — age verification adds ~30 sec per affected transaction, absorbed within cashier and Store Manager duties.
- Compliance Team time for monthly reporting: ~1 hour/month (absorbed by existing Compliance FTE).
- Quarterly spot-audit: ~30 min/quarter per Store Manager (100 stores × 30 min = 50 hours/quarter, absorbed within existing FTEs).

---

## W521. POS Transaction Suspend, Park & Recall

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Cashier needs to pause a transaction mid-sale (customer forgot wallet, needs price check, returns to floor for another item, customer asks to add gift wrapping, queue management) |
| **Frequency** | Multiple daily per store — estimated 5–15 suspensions per store per day |
| **Volume** | ~1,000–3,000 suspended transactions/day chain-wide |
| **Owner** | Cashier |
| **Participants** | Cashier, Customer, Store Manager (override for cross-terminal recall) |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Mid-transaction, Cashier or Customer identifies need to suspend: customer forgot payment method, wants to get another item, requests price check, or customer needs assistance (elderly, bulky item carry) | Cashier / Customer | — | — |
| 2 | Cashier selects "Suspend Transaction" on POS; system prompts for reason code (Customer Forgot Payment / Customer Returning to Floor / Price Check Needed / Customer Needs Assistance / Other); Cashier selects reason code | Cashier | — | 10 sec |
| 3 | System generates unique suspend ID (displayed on cashier screen and printed on suspend slip); system parks transaction in suspended transaction queue; all scanned items, applied promotions, and customer loyalty (if scanned) are preserved | System | — | Automated |
| 4 | System temporarily locks cash drawer — no other transaction can start on this terminal until suspended transaction is recalled OR Cashier explicitly starts a new transaction (new transaction clears the suspend lock) | System | — | Automated |
| 5 | Cashier prints suspend slip (small receipt with suspend ID, item count, running total, and reason code); hands to customer or keeps at terminal | Cashier / System | — | 15 sec |
| 6 | Customer returns to same terminal; Cashier selects "Recall Suspended" and scans or enters suspend ID (or selects from suspended transaction list on screen); system restores all items, promotions, and loyalty data; transaction resumes from point of suspension | Cashier / Customer | — | 30 sec |
| 7 | If customer returns to a **different terminal**: original Cashier's suspended transaction is visible on all terminals at the store; any Cashier at any terminal can recall by entering suspend ID; however, if payment-related data was partially entered (e.g., partial card swipe), system clears partial payment data for security and re-prompts at recall | Cashier | Store Manager | 30 sec |
| 8 | **Auto-cancel timeout**: if suspended transaction is not recalled within configurable timeout (default 30 minutes): system auto-cancels the transaction; generates auto-cancel audit log; any loyalty points pre-earned on suspended items are reversed; Store Manager receives list of auto-cancelled transactions for review | System | — | Automated |
| 9 | **Maximum suspended transactions limit**: system enforces maximum of 5 suspended transactions per terminal and 20 per store at any time; if limit reached, Cashier must recall or cancel oldest suspended transaction before suspending new one | System | — | Automated |
| 10 | Store Manager terminal dashboard shows all currently suspended transactions across all store terminals: suspend ID, terminal, cashier, suspend time, item count, running total, reason code, time elapsed; color-coded (green < 15 min, yellow 15–25 min, red > 25 min approaching auto-cancel) | System | Store Manager | Real-time |

### System Touchpoints
- Transaction suspend with reason code selection and unique suspend ID generation (W521.2–3)
- Suspended transaction preservation: items, promotions, loyalty data, discounts (W521.3)
- Cash drawer lock during suspend (cleared if new transaction started) (W521.4)
- Suspend slip printing with suspend ID, item count, running total (W521.5)
- Cross-terminal recall within same store with suspend ID (W521.7)
- Configurable auto-cancel timeout (default 30 min) with audit log and loyalty reversal (W521.8)
- Maximum suspended transaction limits per terminal (5) and per store (20) (W521.9)
- Store Manager dashboard: real-time suspended transaction monitoring with color-coded time elapsed (W521.10)
- Integration with W5B (in-store selling — suspend within checkout), W517 (shift handover — pending transaction transfer), W5F (store closing — auto-cancel all remaining suspended at EOD), W265 (POS hardware — printer slip), W37 (loss prevention — suspicious suspend patterns)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Suspend fraud** — cashier suspends a transaction after scanning items, then recalls on a different terminal with a lower-priced override or without scanning some items; mitigated by full audit trail and cross-terminal visibility
- **Customer abandonment** — suspended transactions that auto-cancel represent lost sales; Store Manager should track auto-cancel rate and address root causes (e.g., store layout requiring customers to walk far for forgotten items)
- **Terminal congestion** — too many suspended transactions consuming terminal capacity; 20-per-store limit mitigates
- **Drawer lock confusion** — Cashier forgets a transaction is suspended, starts new transaction, and cannot access original suspended transaction on same terminal; must recall from different terminal
- **Peak hour abuse** — customers "reserving" checkout position by suspending and returning later, gaming the queue system; auto-cancel timeout mitigates

### Time Estimate
Suspend: ~25 sec (10 sec reason code + automated park + 15 sec slip print). Recall: ~30 sec. Net impact on checkout throughput: ~1 min per suspend/recall cycle. At 5–15 suspensions per store per day, total chain-wide impact is ~100–300 min/day of additional checkout time.

### Staffing Implication
- No incremental headcount — suspend/recall is a standard POS capability absorbed within cashier workflow.
- Suspended transaction monitoring adds ~5 min/day to Store Manager dashboard review (part of W522 daily review).

---

## W522. POS Daily Transaction Review & Cashier Performance Audit

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Daily — Store Manager morning routine (before store opening or within first hour) |
| **Frequency** | Daily per store |
| **Volume** | 200 stores × 365 days = 73,000 reviews/year |
| **Owner** | Store Manager |
| **Participants** | Store Manager, Lead Cashier (follow-up), Regional Manager (weekly summary) |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | System automatically generates **Cashier Performance Dashboard** overnight from prior day's transaction data: per-cashier metrics — transaction count, average transaction value, average transaction time (scan-to-receipt), items per transaction, void count and void value, refund count and refund value, price override count and override value, discount count and discount value, loyalty scan rate (% of transactions with loyalty card), tender type distribution, cash variance (from W5F/W517 handover) | System | — | Automated |
| 2 | Store Manager opens Cashier Performance Dashboard on terminal or tablet during morning routine; reviews each cashier's metrics against store average and chain benchmark (provided by Finance/Operations team) | Store Manager | — | 15 min |
| 3 | System highlights **exception alerts** for cashiers with metrics outside normal bands: void rate > 2× store average, average transaction value deviation > ±30% from store average, cash variance > PHP 200, price override rate > 2× store average, refund rate > 2× store average, loyalty scan rate < 50% (potential missed enrollment opportunity), transaction speed < 50th percentile (slow cashier — training candidate) | System | — | Automated |
| 4 | Store Manager reviews **high-value transactions** from prior day (transactions > PHP 50,000): spot-checks tender type, customer identification (if per W530), price overrides, and item composition for reasonableness | Store Manager | — | 10 min |
| 5 | Store Manager reviews **void and refund detail**: for each void/refund, system displays original transaction details, void/refund reason code, authorizing manager ID, and item list; Store Manager verifies authorization was appropriate | Store Manager | — | 10 min |
| 6 | Store Manager reviews **discount and promotional application**: system shows discount distribution by type (employee, senior citizen, PWD, price override, promotional, competitor match); flags unusual patterns (e.g., same customer receiving multiple discounts in one day, employee discount applied to non-employee) | Store Manager | — | 5 min |
| 7 | If any exception alerts or anomalies are identified: Store Manager documents findings and assigns follow-up actions — retraining for cashier (W518), LP investigation for suspected fraud (W37/W466), cashier performance discussion (W72) | Store Manager | — | 10 min if any |
| 8 | Weekly: Store Manager compiles cashier performance ranking for weekly team meeting; recognizes top-performing cashiers; identifies bottom performers for targeted coaching; submits weekly cashier summary to Regional Manager | Store Manager | Regional Manager | 30 min/week |
| 9 | Monthly: Regional Manager reviews cashier performance data across all stores in region; identifies stores with consistently high void rates, low loyalty scan rates, or high cash variances for Store Manager coaching and process improvement | Regional Manager | VP Store Operations | 2 hours/month |

### System Touchpoints
- Automated overnight Cashier Performance Dashboard generation from prior day transaction data (W522.1)
- Per-cashier metrics: transaction count, avg value, avg time, items/txn, voids, refunds, overrides, discounts, loyalty scan rate, tender distribution, cash variance (W522.1)
- Configurable exception alert thresholds per metric (W522.3)
- High-value transaction spot-check view (W522.4)
- Void/refund detail review with original transaction, reason code, authorizer (W522.5)
- Discount distribution analysis by type with pattern flagging (W522.6)
- Integration with W5B (in-store selling — transaction data source), W5F (store closing — cash variance data), W517 (shift handover — handover variance), W529 (void/refund authorization — authorization data source), W37 (loss prevention — fraud investigation escalation), W72 (employee performance — performance feed), W518 (cashier training — retraining trigger), W170 (SC/PWD discount — discount verification), W205 (employee purchase — employee discount verification)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Dashboard fatigue** — Store Manager reviewing the same dashboard daily without incident may become complacent; exception-based alerting mitigates by focusing attention on anomalies
- **Metric gaming** — cashiers aware of monitored metrics may adjust behavior to game scores (e.g., scanning loyalty card for non-loyalty customers to boost scan rate, avoiding legitimate voids to keep void rate low); metrics must be balanced to prevent gaming
- **Time pressure** — 30+ min daily review adds to Store Manager's already heavy morning workload; some Store Managers may rush or skip the review
- **Cross-store benchmarking limitations** — stores in different locations (urban vs. rural, mall vs. standalone) have inherently different transaction profiles; chain benchmarks must account for store type

### Time Estimate
Daily review: ~20–30 min (15 min dashboard + 10 min high-value + 5 min discounts + exception follow-up if any). Weekly summary: ~30 min. Monthly regional review: ~2 hours. Total per store: ~175 hours/year for daily review.

### Staffing Implication
- No incremental headcount — daily review is a Store Manager responsibility.
- Daily review adds ~25 min to Store Manager's morning routine; absorbed within existing Store Manager FTE (~10–12 hour workday).
- Regional Manager monthly review: ~2 hours/month for ~20 stores per region = ~40 hours/month; absorbed by existing Regional Manager FTE.

## W523. POS Promotional Terminal Setup & Pre-Live Verification

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | New promotion launching (promo start date = tomorrow or today); Store Manager or Lead Cashier receives promotional setup brief from Merchandising team (W262) |
| **Frequency** | ~2–3 new promotions starting per week per store (promotional calendar from W13) |
| **Volume** | ~400–600 promo setups/week chain-wide; ~20–30 per store per month |
| **Owner** | Lead Cashier (terminal verification); Department Supervisor (floor setup per W262) |
| **Participants** | Lead Cashier, Department Supervisor, Store Manager (go/no-go), Merchandising Coordinator (W262) |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Lead Cashier receives promotional setup notification from Merchandising Coordinator: promotion name, start/end dates, participating SKUs, promotional pricing, promo type (discount %, buy-one-take-one, bundle deal, gift-with-purchase, bank-BIN promo), and promotional barcode numbers (if applicable) | Lead Cashier | — | 5 min (read brief) |
| 2 | Lead Cashier opens POS terminal promotional preview mode: system displays list of upcoming promotions loaded for this terminal with effective prices per SKU | Lead Cashier | — | 5 min |
| 3 | **Test scan verification**: Lead Cashier scans each promotional SKU's barcode at terminal; system displays promotional price on cashier screen and customer display; Lead Cashier verifies displayed price matches promo brief | Lead Cashier | — | 2 min/SKU |
| 4 | **Promotional quick-key verification**: if promotion has dedicated quick-keys on POS screen (e.g., "BOGO Cement," "20% Off Tiles"), Lead Cashier presses each quick-key; system adds promotional item at correct price; Lead Cashier verifies quick-key label, price, and item description match promo brief | Lead Cashier | — | 1 min/quick-key |
| 5 | **Customer display verification**: Lead Cashier confirms promotional pricing displays correctly on customer-facing screen — promo price, original price (strikethrough), savings amount, promotional messaging (e.g., "Buy 1 Take 1!") | Lead Cashier | — | 2 min |
| 6 | **Bank-BIN promo verification** (if applicable): Lead Cashier processes test transaction with test bank card for partner bank promos (W149); system identifies card BIN and applies bank-specific promotional pricing or terms; Lead Cashier verifies correct bank promo applied | Lead Cashier | — | 3 min if bank promo |
| 7 | **Bundle/BOGO verification** (if applicable): Lead Cashier scans bundle components or BOGO trigger items; system auto-applies bundle pricing or free-item logic; Lead Cashier verifies correct quantity triggers and pricing | Lead Cashier | — | 3 min if bundle |
| 8 | Lead Cashier documents verification results per terminal in POS system: verification status (Pass/Fail per SKU/quick-key), any discrepancies found; if all pass: terminal marked "Promo Ready"; if any fail: system flags discrepancy to Merchandising Coordinator and IT Helpdesk (W48) for resolution before promo go-live | Lead Cashier | Store Manager | 5 min (documentation) |
| 9 | Store Manager reviews terminal verification status dashboard: confirms all terminals at store are "Promo Ready"; if any terminal shows "Fail": Store Manager contacts IT Helpdesk (W48) for resolution; if unresolved by promo start time: Store Manager posts manual price list at affected terminal per W5E degraded mode procedure | Store Manager | — | 5 min |
| 10 | Post-promo: on promotion end date, system automatically deactivates promotional pricing, removes quick-keys, and reverts to standard pricing; Lead Cashier verifies deactivation by test scanning former promo SKU (should show regular price) | System / Lead Cashier | Store Manager | 5 min/post-promo |

### System Touchpoints
- POS promotional preview mode: upcoming promotions with effective prices per SKU per terminal (W523.2)
- Test scan verification: scan promotional SKU → system displays promo price on cashier and customer screens (W523.3)
- Promotional quick-key configuration and verification (W523.4)
- Customer display promotional formatting: strikethrough original price, savings amount, promo messaging (W523.5)
- Bank-BIN promo test: card BIN identification and bank-specific pricing application (W523.6)
- Bundle/BOGO test: quantity trigger and pricing logic verification (W523.7)
- Terminal verification status dashboard: Pass/Fail per terminal with discrepancy flagging (W523.8–9)
- Automatic promo deactivation on end date with post-promo verification (W523.10)
- Integration with W13 (promotions — promo calendar and pricing source), W262 (store promotional setup — physical display setup), W40 (price changes — price file source), W289 (pricing master — promo pricing rules), W149 (bank partnership — bank-BIN promo config), W300 (promotional rules master — promo rule engine), W48 (helpdesk — terminal issue resolution), W5E (opening delay — degraded mode fallback)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Promo brief timing** — if Merchandising sends promo briefs late (day-of instead of day-before), Lead Cashier has insufficient time for verification; undetected promo pricing errors result in customer disputes at checkout
- **SKU count overwhelm** — large promotions (e.g., 100+ SKUs on discount) make test-scanning every SKU impractical; Lead Cashier must prioritize high-volume and high-value SKUs for test scan, accepting risk on low-volume items
- **Quick-key label errors** — quick-keys created by Merchandising team with wrong item codes or prices; test verification catches most errors but some may slip through if quick-key name does not clearly indicate which item it represents
- **Bank promo complexity** — bank-BIN promos requiring real-time card verification may not test correctly with dummy cards; live testing with real bank cards has audit implications
- **Deactivation failures** — promotional pricing not deactivated on end date (system bug or config error) causes incorrect pricing on day after promo ends; post-promo verification mitigates

### Time Estimate
Per promotion: ~15–30 min per terminal for test scanning and verification (depends on SKU count in promo). Per store with 3 active terminals: ~45–90 min/promo. At 2–3 promos/week/store: ~90–270 min/week per store for promo terminal verification. Chain-wide: ~600–1,800 hours/week.

### Staffing Implication
- No incremental headcount — promotional terminal verification is a Lead Cashier responsibility absorbed within shift duties.
- At ~2–3 promos/week × 30 min average per terminal × 3 terminals = ~3–4.5 hours/week per store for verification. This is a significant time investment; large stores may need to stagger verification across shifts.
- Merchandising Coordinator (W262) provides promo briefs 24 hours in advance to enable verification.

## W524. Demo / Display Unit Selling at POS

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer inquires about purchasing a demo or display unit from the store floor; or Store Manager decides to sell aged display unit to free floor space |
| **Frequency** | Occasional — ~5–10 display unit sales per store per month |
| **Volume** | ~1,000–2,000 display unit sales/year chain-wide |
| **Owner** | Sales Associate (condition assessment); Store Manager (pricing approval and POS processing) |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate, Store Manager, Customer, Merchandising Coordinator (replacement order) |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Customer expresses interest in purchasing floor display or demo unit (e.g., power tool on display, faucet fixture, appliance demo unit, tile display panel); or Store Manager identifies aged display unit for sale (display unit on floor > 6 months per Merchandising guidelines) | Customer / Store Manager | — | — |
| 2 | Sales Associate inspects display unit condition: cosmetic condition (scratches, dents, discoloration), completeness (all accessories, manuals, remote, hoses, attachments present), functionality (power on test for power tools/appliances, mechanical test for fixtures); documents condition with photos on tablet | Sales Associate | Dept. Supervisor | 15 min |
| 3 | Sales Associate enters condition assessment in system: selects display unit SKU, condition grade (A — like new, minor cosmetic; B — visible wear, fully functional; C — significant wear, minor functional limitation; D — functional but poor cosmetic); system records assessment with photos | Sales Associate | — | 5 min |
| 4 | System calculates suggested selling price based on condition grade and display duration: Condition A — 85% of SRP; Condition B — 70% of SRP; Condition C — 55% of SRP; Condition D — 40% of SRP; configurable depreciation schedule per category in item master (W252) | System | — | Automated |
| 5 | Store Manager reviews condition assessment and suggested price; can adjust price within ±10% of system-suggested price without further approval; price adjustments > 10% require Regional Manager approval via W5B.4a price override process | Store Manager | Regional Manager (if > 10%) | 5 min |
| 6 | Store Manager informs customer of display unit status, condition, and price; discloses that item is a display/demo unit with visible wear; provides modified warranty terms (standard warranty duration reduced by 50% for display units, or manufacturer warranty if still valid); customer verbally agrees | Store Manager / Customer | — | 5 min |
| 7 | Customer agrees to purchase; Store Manager processes sale at POS: scans display unit barcode (system recognizes display unit flag); selects "Display Unit Sale" transaction type; system applies approved price; system prints customer disclosure on receipt: "DISPLAY UNIT — Sold As-Is with [X]-month warranty"; system sets return policy for this transaction to "No Returns — Display Unit Final Sale" per W532 | Store Manager | — | 5 min |
| 8 | Sales Associate removes display unit from floor; safely disconnects power/water/gas if applicable; packages item or provides carry-out assistance per W531; cleans display area and stages for replacement unit | Sales Associate | Dept. Supervisor | 20 min |
| 9 | System automatically creates replenishment request for replacement display/demo unit: if item is current assortment → auto-generates internal transfer request (W22) or purchase suggestion (W2A) to restock display; if item is being discontinued → no replacement; system updates planogram compliance status (W86) to show empty display position | System | Merchandising Coordinator | Automated |
| 10 | Monthly: Merchandising Coordinator reviews display unit sales report — units sold, condition grades, average discount from SRP, replacement order status; identifies categories with high display unit turnover for potential display strategy adjustment | Merchandising Coordinator | VP Merchandising | 1 hour/month |

### System Touchpoints
- Display unit condition assessment with photo capture and condition grading (A/B/C/D) (W524.2–3)
- Automated suggested selling price based on condition grade and configurable depreciation schedule per category (W524.4)
- "Display Unit Sale" transaction type with customer disclosure on receipt and modified warranty/return terms (W524.7)
- Display unit flag on item master linking display inventory to saleable inventory (W524.7)
- Automatic replenishment request for replacement display unit (W524.9)
- Planogram compliance update showing empty display position (W524.9, W86)
- Display unit sales analytics: units sold, condition grades, avg discount, replacement status (W524.10)
- Integration with W5B (in-store selling — POS transaction), W97 (sample/demo inventory — display unit tracking), W445 (display infrastructure maintenance — display condition feed), W86 (planogram compliance — display position tracking), W22 (transfers — replacement sourcing), W2A (auto-replenishment — replacement PO trigger), W252 (item master — display unit flag and depreciation schedule), W33 (warranty — modified warranty registration), W532 (clearance/final sale — return policy override)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Customer disputes on condition** — customer claims display unit was represented as better condition than received; photo documentation at assessment mitigates
- **Warranty claim complexity** — display units with modified warranty terms create customer confusion when filing claims; system must clearly track warranty terms per display sale transaction
- **Pricing authority abuse** — Store Manager repeatedly pricing display units at maximum discount (40% SRP for Grade D) for accomplice purchases; LP monitoring through W522 daily review mitigates
- **Replacement delays** — display unit sold but replacement takes weeks to arrive, leaving empty display position that degrades store appearance and planogram compliance
- **Safety liability** — selling display units (especially power tools, electrical fixtures) without thorough safety testing creates product liability risk; condition assessment must include functional safety check

### Time Estimate
~55 min per display unit sale (15 min condition assessment + 5 min system entry + 5 min pricing review + 5 min customer disclosure + 5 min POS processing + 20 min removal and cleanup). At 5–10 per store per month, total per store is ~275–550 min/month (~5–9 hours/month). Chain-wide: ~1,000–2,000 hours/month.

### Staffing Implication
- No incremental headcount — display unit selling is absorbed by Sales Associate (assessment and removal) and Store Manager (pricing and POS).
- Monthly Merchandising Coordinator review: ~1 hour/month absorbed by existing Merchandising FTE.
- Total store-level labor for display unit sales: ~5–9 hours/month per store, spread across ~5–10 transactions.

## W525. POS Continuous Near-Real-Time Sync & Nightly Reconciliation

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Continuous: POS transactions stream to ERP in near-real-time (< 30 sec per event via W533 event bus). Nightly: scheduled reconciliation batch (typically 1:00–4:00 AM, after all stores closed per NFR-015) |
| **Frequency** | Continuous (every transaction) + nightly reconciliation batch (daily) |
| **Volume** | ~93,333 POS transactions/day streamed continuously + 600 terminals × nightly reconciliation cycle |
| **Owner** | IT Operations (reconciliation monitoring); System (automated execution) |
| **Participants** | IT Operations, System, Store Manager (alert recipient if reconciliation failure) |

### Background

POS transactions are no longer uploaded in a nightly batch. Instead, each transaction is published as an event to the ERP message bus within 30 seconds of occurrence (per W533). This continuous streaming ensures that inventory, GL, loyalty, and ecommerce ATP are always current. However, a nightly reconciliation batch still runs to validate completeness and catch any edge cases (events lost in transit, offline transactions from W5G, partial failures). The nightly batch is a **validation and reconciliation** layer, not the primary data transport mechanism.

### Continuous Sync (Real-Time)

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Latency |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| CS-1 | POS terminal publishes each completed transaction (sale, void, return, refund) as a discrete event to the ERP message bus within 30 seconds of completion; event includes complete transaction detail (line items, quantities, prices, discounts, tender, customer, timestamps, void status) | System | — | < 30 sec per event |
| CS-2 | ERP message bus acknowledges receipt; event is persisted to durable queue with guaranteed delivery | System | — | < 5 sec |
| CS-3 | ERP inventory module consumes event; deducts sold quantities from store location inventory; updates ATP for ecommerce, BOPIS, and BI dashboards | System | — | < 60 sec from sale |
| CS-4 | ERP GL module consumes event; posts revenue, VAT, COGS, and tender journal entries | System | — | < 60 sec from sale |
| CS-5 | ERP loyalty module consumes event; posts points earned/redeemed to customer account | System | — | < 60 sec from sale |
| CS-6 | ERP pushes price changes, new items, discontinued flags, and promotional rule updates to online POS terminals continuously throughout the day (within 60 seconds of central activation per POS-047) | System | — | < 60 sec per change |

### Nightly Reconciliation Batch

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| NR-1 | System initiates nightly reconciliation batch after all stores report "Closed" status (W5F completion) or after configurable cutoff time (default 12:00 AM); batch validates completeness of continuous sync and catches edge cases: (a) transaction count verification, (b) inventory adjustment reconciliation, (c) void/return log synchronization, (d) loyalty points reconciliation, (e) full price file push to POS, (f) new item push to POS, (g) discontinued item flag push to POS | System | IT Operations | 1–3 hours (automated) |
| NR-2 | **Transaction count verification**: system compares continuous event stream record count per terminal against terminal Z-report transaction count; flags any discrepancy (missing events or duplicate events) | System | — | Automated |
| NR-3 | **Inventory adjustment reconciliation**: system compares POS deduction log (from continuous events) with ERP on-hand quantity changes including offline transactions (W5G); flags discrepancies where POS deducted but ERP on-hand does not match; generates reconciliation exceptions for IT review | System | — | Automated |
| NR-4 | **Void/return log reconciliation**: void and return transactions from continuous events are cross-checked against ERP void/return register; system verifies void authorization chain (cashier → authorizer) is complete for every void, including offline voids from W5G | System | — | Automated |
| NR-5 | **Loyalty points reconciliation**: loyalty points earned and redeemed via continuous events are reconciled with central loyalty engine; flags discrepancies (e.g., points earned offline but same customer redeemed online simultaneously per W5G.5) | System | — | Automated |
| NR-6 | **Full price file push**: ERP pushes complete price file (base prices, promotional prices effective tomorrow, new quick-key configurations) to all POS terminals as a full refresh (supplementing the continuous incremental pushes from CS-6); terminals acknowledge receipt and update local data store | System | — | Automated |
| NR-7 | **New item push**: newly created SKUs (from W252 item master governance) with full product data (barcode, description, price, UOM, category, age-restricted flag, catch-weight flag) are pushed to POS terminals as a full refresh; POS terminal acknowledges receipt | System | — | Automated |
| NR-8 | **Discontinued item flag push**: items transitioned to "Discontinued" status (W68) are flagged at POS; POS terminals will display "Item Discontinued — No Longer Available" if scanned after effective date | System | — | Automated |
| NR-9 | **Reconciliation report**: system generates consolidated reconciliation report: terminals verified (count), verification failures (count with terminal ID and store), transaction count streamed vs. Z-report expected, inventory discrepancies found (count and value), loyalty reconciliation exceptions, price file update confirmation per terminal | System | — | Automated |
| NR-10 | **Failure detection and alerting**: if any terminal fails reconciliation (network failure, hardware issue, count mismatch): system sends alert to IT Operations dashboard and Store Manager (email/SMS) with terminal ID, store, failure type, and recommended action; failed terminals attempt retry up to 3 times at 15-min intervals; if all retries fail: terminal flagged for manual intervention at store opening (W5A) | System / IT Operations | IT Operations | Automated |
| NR-11 | Morning: IT Operations reviews reconciliation report; investigates and resolves any outstanding failures before store opening; confirms all terminals have current price file and data store before W5A store opening | IT Operations | CIO | 30 min/day |

### System Touchpoints
- Continuous event bus: POS publishes transaction events in near-real-time; ERP message bus with guaranteed delivery (at-least-once with deduplication) (W525.CS-1–2, W533)
- Real-time inventory deduction from continuous events (W525.CS-3)
- Real-time GL posting from continuous events (W525.CS-4)
- Real-time loyalty posting from continuous events (W525.CS-5)
- Continuous price/item/promo push to POS terminals (W525.CS-6, POS-047)
- Nightly reconciliation orchestrator: validates continuous sync completeness with dependency management (W525.NR-1)
- Transaction count verification: continuous event stream vs. Z-report (W525.NR-2)
- Inventory adjustment reconciliation including offline transactions (W525.NR-3)
- Void/return log reconciliation with authorization chain verification (W525.NR-4)
- Loyalty points reconciliation (W525.NR-5)
- Full price file push to POS terminals with acknowledgment tracking (W525.NR-6)
- New item push with full product data and terminal acknowledgment (W525.NR-7)
- Discontinued item flag push with POS display message configuration (W525.NR-8)
- Consolidated reconciliation report with terminal-level detail (W525.NR-9)
- Failure detection with 3× retry, Store Manager alert, and IT Operations escalation (W525.NR-10)
- Integration with W533 (real-time event streaming — continuous transport), W535 (offline capability — local data store refresh), W5A (store opening — price file and new item availability check), W5F (store closing — Z-report as reconciliation input), W5G (offline POS — offline transaction reconciliation), W252 (item master — new/discontinued item feed), W40 (price changes — price update feed), W13 (promotions — promotional rule updates), W289 (pricing master — price file source), W48 (helpdesk — failure escalation), W37 (loss prevention — inventory discrepancy investigation), W17 (loyalty — points reconciliation)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Continuous event delivery gaps** — a network blip between store and ERP can cause event delivery to be delayed (events queued locally on terminal) or duplicated (retry on timeout); nightly reconciliation catches these gaps, but real-time consumers (ecommerce ATP, BI dashboards) may show stale data for minutes during the gap
- **Reconciliation false positives** — legitimate inventory movements (DSD receiving W18, cycle count adjustments W6, transfer receipts W22) occurring during reconciliation window create discrepancies; reconciliation logic must account for in-flight transactions
- **Offline transaction reconciliation load** — after extended offline period (W5G), a large batch of offline transactions must be reconciled; reconciliation logic must sequence offline events correctly and resolve inventory conflicts
- **Reconciliation failure during promotional changeover** — if nightly reconciliation fails on the night before a major promotion starts, terminals may not have verified correct promotional pricing; however, continuous price push (CS-6) reduces this risk since most price updates are already applied during the day
- **Message bus throughput** — 600 terminals generating events at peak (weekend sale event) can create ~2,000 events/minute across the chain; message bus must handle this throughput without backpressure

### Time Estimate
Continuous sync: fully automated, no human time. Nightly reconciliation: automated 1–3 hours. IT Operations morning review: ~30 min/day. Store Manager intervention for failed terminals: ~15 min (if needed, ~2–4 times/store/year). Total human time: ~30–45 min/day centralized + ~15 min/store on rare failures.

### Staffing Implication
- **IT Operations**: 1 staff member reviews nightly reconciliation report daily (~30 min); absorbed by existing IT Operations team.
- **Store Manager**: ~15 min of additional work at store opening when their terminal had a reconciliation failure; occurs ~2–4 times/store/year (infrequent).
- No incremental headcount — continuous sync is fully automated; nightly reconciliation is automated with human review for exceptions only.

## W526. POS Customer Queue Management & Express Lane Operations

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Real-time queue length exceeds configurable threshold (default: > 5 customers waiting at any terminal) |
| **Frequency** | Daily during peak hours (10 AM–12 PM and 4–6 PM) |
| **Volume** | ~400 queue threshold events/day chain-wide (2 peak periods × 200 stores, though not all stores trigger every period) |
| **Owner** | Lead Cashier (queue response); Store Manager (escalation) |
| **Participants** | Lead Cashier, Store Manager, Cashiers, Customer |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | System monitors real-time queue length per terminal: calculates queue depth from POS transaction start timestamps (time between first scan and tender completion) and estimated wait time based on current transaction speed; system also tracks queue abandonment rate (customers leaving line before reaching terminal) via entrance/exit analytics if available | System | — | Continuous |
| 2 | When queue length at any terminal exceeds threshold (default 5 customers): system sends visual alert to Store Manager dashboard and audible alert to Lead Cashier; dashboard displays queue heat map — red (above threshold), yellow (approaching), green (normal) per terminal | System | — | Automated |
| 3 | Lead Cashier initiates queue response: opens backup terminal (if available and staffed); assigns available cashier from float/backup pool to terminal; if no backup cashier available: Lead Cashier or Department Supervisor mans backup terminal | Lead Cashier | Store Manager | 2 min |
| 4 | **Express lane activation** (if applicable): if store has designated express lane terminal, system enables express lane criteria — maximum 10 items, no catch-weight items, no trade account pricing, no price overrides; system enforces item count at checkout; if customer exceeds 10 items: cashier offers to split into two transactions or directs to regular lane | Lead Cashier / System | — | 2 min |
| 5 | **mPOS queue busting** (W206): if all fixed terminals are occupied and queue persists: Lead Cashier deploys mPOS-equipped Sales Associates to process customers in queue using mobile devices; mPOS handles standard barcode-scan items, cashless payment, and e-receipts | Lead Cashier / Sales Associate | Store Manager | 5 min |
| 6 | Lead Cashier communicates with customers in queue: provides estimated wait time, directs to shortest line, offers mPOS alternative; manages customer expectations during peak | Lead Cashier | — | Ongoing |
| 7 | When queue subsides below threshold for 15 consecutive minutes: system sends "Queue Normal" notification; Lead Cashier stands down backup cashier or closes backup terminal; mPOS operators return to floor duties | System / Lead Cashier | — | 2 min |
| 8 | Weekly: Store Manager reviews queue analytics — average wait time by day and hour, queue threshold trigger frequency, average queue duration, queue abandonment rate, express lane utilization rate; identifies recurring peak patterns for staffing adjustment (W34) | Store Manager | Regional Manager | 30 min/week |

### System Touchpoints
- Real-time queue length monitoring per terminal using POS transaction timestamps (W526.1)
- Configurable queue threshold alerting (default 5 customers) with dashboard notification (W526.2)
- Queue heat map on Store Manager dashboard: red/yellow/green per terminal (W526.2)
- Express lane criteria enforcement: max items, no catch-weight, no trade account, no override (W526.4)
- Integration with W206 (mPOS — queue busting deployment), W34 (shift scheduling — staffing adjustment based on queue patterns), W5B (in-store selling — standard checkout), W516 (SCO — complementary queue reduction), W247 (smart locker — BOPIS pickup without queuing), W35 (management reporting — queue analytics in daily flash)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Queue monitoring accuracy** — POS transaction timestamps only capture checkout time, not time spent in line before reaching terminal; queue length estimation may be inaccurate without dedicated queue sensors or CCTV analytics
- **Staff availability** — backup cashier or mPOS operator may not be available during peak if all staff are already occupied with floor duties, DSD receiving, or BOPIS picking; peak staffing (W34) must account for queue management needs
- **Express lane customer disputes** — customers with 11–12 items arguing with cashier about express lane item limit; cashiers may allow exceptions to avoid confrontation, undermining express lane purpose
- **mPOS limitations** — mPOS cannot process catch-weight items, custom SKUs, or trade account pricing; customers redirected from mPOS to regular terminal after waiting, causing frustration
- **Queue abandonment unmeasured** — customers who leave without purchasing are difficult to detect and quantify; lost revenue from abandonment is invisible without dedicated analytics

### Time Estimate
Queue response initiation: ~2 min. Express lane activation: ~2 min. mPOS deployment: ~5 min. Queue normalization stand-down: ~2 min. Weekly analytics review: ~30 min. Total incremental per queue event: ~10 min. At ~2 events/store/day during peak periods, total per store is ~20 min/day.

### Staffing Implication
- No incremental headcount — queue management is a Lead Cashier and Store Manager responsibility.
- Express lane operation: typically absorbed by existing cashier float/backup rotation.
- mPOS queue busting: pulls Sales Associates from floor duties temporarily; reduces floor coverage during peak (acceptable trade-off for customer wait time reduction).
- Peak staffing adjustments from queue analytics: may drive incremental cashier shifts per W34 scheduling optimization.
