## W527. POS Tax Exemption Processing (Government / PEZA / Institutional)

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer presents tax exemption certificate at POS (government agency purchase order, PEZA-registered entity ID, diplomatic exemption letter, BIR tax exemption ruling) |
| **Frequency** | Occasional — varies significantly by store location; ~10–20 per store per month for stores near government centers, PEZA zones, or diplomatic areas; ~2–5 for other stores |
| **Volume** | ~2,000–4,000 tax-exempt transactions/month chain-wide |
| **Owner** | Cashier (processing); Store Manager (verification and approval) |
| **Participants** | Cashier, Store Manager, Customer, Finance Team (compliance review) |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Customer presents tax exemption documentation at checkout: government agency purchase order with VAT exemption certification, PEZA-registered entity ID card, diplomatic exemption letter, or BIR tax exemption ruling with TIN | Customer | — | — |
| 2 | Cashier identifies customer in system: looks up customer account (if existing B2B account per W24) or creates temporary tax-exempt customer record (for one-time government purchases) | Cashier | — | 2 min |
| 3 | Store Manager verifies tax exemption documentation: confirms document validity (government letterhead, PEZA registration number, BIR ruling reference), checks expiry (if applicable), verifies customer identity matches document; approves tax exemption in system with manager ID | Store Manager | — | 5 min |
| 4 | System applies zero output VAT to the transaction: all eligible items recalculated with 0% VAT (net of VAT from SRP); customer display shows "TAX EXEMPT TRANSACTION" with exemption type; receipt prints tax exemption details (exemption type, certificate/reference number, verifying manager ID) | System | — | Automated |
| 5 | System posts transaction to tax-exempt revenue GL account (separate from standard VATable sales); records in tax-exempt transaction register for BIR reporting (W9A.16 VAT return — exempt/zero-rated sales schedule); zero output VAT entry posted alongside standard Dr. Cash / Cr. Revenue | System | — | Automated |
| 6 | If only some items are tax-exempt (partial exemption — e.g., PEZA-registered entity purchasing mixed merchandise where only certain categories qualify): Cashier manually flags exempt items at line level; system applies VAT only to non-exempt items | Cashier / System | Store Manager | 2 min |
| 7 | System logs tax exemption transaction: transaction ID, customer account, exemption type, certificate/reference number, verifying Store Manager ID, total exempt amount, items exempted; log available for Finance Team BIR audit support | System | — | Automated |
| 8 | Monthly: Finance Team reviews tax-exempt transaction register — total exempt sales by exemption type, by store, by customer; verifies documentation completeness for all exempt transactions; flags transactions missing documentation for Store Manager follow-up | Finance Team | Controller | 2 hours/month |

### System Touchpoints
- Tax-exempt customer identification: existing B2B account lookup or temporary tax-exempt customer record (W527.2)
- Tax exemption verification with Store Manager approval in system (W527.3)
- Zero output VAT application at line or transaction level with GL posting to exempt revenue account (W527.4–5)
- Partial exemption: line-level VAT flagging for mixed merchandise (W527.6)
- Tax-exempt transaction register with full documentation for BIR audit support (W527.7)
- Integration with W5B (in-store selling — tax exemption within checkout), W8 (AR processing — B2B account billing), W24 (credit application — customer VAT treatment classification), W253 (customer master — VAT registration status), W9A.16 (VAT return — exempt/zero-rated sales schedule), W54A (BIR CAS — compliant receipt format), W78 (government procurement — PHILGEPS integration)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Document fraud** — forged tax exemption documents presented at POS; Store Manager is not a legal expert in document verification; system should maintain whitelist of verified tax-exempt entities from Finance Team
- **Partial exemption complexity** — PEZA and government exemptions may apply only to specific categories (e.g., capital equipment exempt, consumables taxable); Cashier must know which items qualify — requires product knowledge training
- **BIR audit exposure** — if tax-exempt transactions lack proper documentation or are incorrectly processed, BIR may disallow exemption during audit, resulting in VAT assessment + penalties + interest; Finance Team monthly review mitigates
- **Customer wait time** — tax exemption processing adds ~7 min to checkout (2 min customer lookup + 5 min manager verification); government agency buyers making large purchases accept this but institutional buyers making small purchases may be frustrated
- **Store Manager availability** — every tax-exempt transaction requires Store Manager verification; during peak hours, Store Manager may be unavailable, delaying checkout

### Time Estimate
~7 min per tax-exempt transaction (2 min customer lookup + 5 min verification and approval). Processing and posting: automated. Monthly Finance review: ~2 hours. At 10–20 transactions/store/month, total store-level labor is ~70–140 min/month per store.

### Staffing Implication
- No incremental headcount — tax exemption processing is a Cashier and Store Manager responsibility.
- Finance Team monthly review: ~2 hours/month absorbed by existing Finance FTE.
- Higher-volume stores (near government centers, PEZA zones) may see ~20 tax-exempt transactions/month adding ~140 min to Cashier/Manager workload.

## W528. POS Digital Receipt / E-Receipt Delivery

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer opts for e-receipt at point of sale (instead of or in addition to paper receipt) |
| **Frequency** | Per transaction — increasing adoption as e-receipt awareness grows; estimated 20–30% of transactions opt for e-receipt |
| **Volume** | ~560,000–840,000 e-receipts/month chain-wide (20–30% of 2.8M monthly transactions) |
| **Owner** | Cashier (offer); System (delivery) |
| **Participants** | Cashier, Customer, System, Marketing Team (analytics) |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | After tender completion (W5B.8), system prompts Cashier: "Would customer like a digital receipt?" with options: Paper Only / Email / SMS / Both (Paper + Digital) | System | — | Automated prompt |
| 2 | Cashier asks customer preference; if customer selects Email or SMS: Cashier selects delivery method on POS terminal; if customer is loyalty member and email/mobile is on file: system auto-populates from customer record; if not loyalty member: Cashier enters customer email address or mobile number on POS screen | Cashier / Customer | — | 30 sec |
| 3 | System generates BIR-compliant e-receipt with all required elements: TIN, registered invoice/official receipt number (from CAS permit per W54A), CAS permit number, business name and address, transaction date/time, itemized line items with VAT, VAT amount, total, tender type, cashier ID; e-receipt includes "E-RECEIPT" watermark and digital delivery confirmation reference | System | — | Automated |
| 4 | System transmits e-receipt: Email — sent via transactional email service with receipt as HTML body and PDF attachment; SMS — sent as SMS with link to web-hosted receipt (receipt too long for SMS body); system logs delivery attempt with timestamp | System | — | Automated |
| 5 | System tracks delivery status: Sent → Delivered (email open / SMS link clicked) → Failed (bounce, invalid number, timeout); if delivery fails within 5 minutes: system falls back to printing paper receipt; Cashier hands paper receipt to customer with "Digital receipt delivery failed — here is your printed receipt" | System / Cashier | — | Automated + 15 sec if fallback |
| 6 | **BIR compliance**: e-receipt is in addition to (not replacement for) the POS terminal's internal transaction record; the CAS-registered transaction record stored on the POS terminal (and uploaded via W525 nightly batch) serves as the BIR-registered original; e-receipt is a copy delivery mechanism; BIR requires that the POS terminal retains the registered transaction regardless of receipt format | System | — | Ongoing |
| 7 | Customer receives e-receipt; e-receipt includes link to rate shopping experience (W65 CSAT/NPS survey QR code) and link to loyalty program enrollment (W263) if not already a member | System | — | Automated |
| 8 | Monthly: Marketing Team reviews e-receipt adoption analytics — adoption rate by store, delivery method preference (email vs. SMS), delivery success rate, survey click-through rate from e-receipt, enrollment conversion rate from e-receipt link; identifies stores with low adoption for POS signage promoting e-receipt option | Marketing Team | VP Marketing | 1 hour/month |

### System Touchpoints
- E-receipt delivery method prompt at POS after tender (W528.1)
- Auto-populate email/mobile from loyalty member customer record (W528.2)
- BIR-compliant e-receipt generation with CAS permit number and registered invoice number (W528.3)
- Transactional email/SMS delivery with delivery status tracking and paper fallback (W528.4–5)
- CAS-registered transaction record retention on POS terminal (BIR original) separate from e-receipt copy (W528.6)
- E-receipt analytics: adoption rate, delivery method, success rate, survey CTR, enrollment conversion (W528.8)
- Integration with W5B (in-store selling — receipt generation), W12 (returns — e-receipt as return reference), W54A (BIR CAS — registered receipt format), W17 (loyalty — customer email/mobile from record), W263 (enrollment — enrollment link in e-receipt), W65 (CSAT/NPS — survey link in e-receipt), W253 (customer master — email/mobile data source), W265 (POS hardware — receipt printer fallback)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **BIR compliance uncertainty** — Philippine BIR CAS permits authorize specific receipt formats; adding e-receipt delivery may require CAS permit amendment or clarification that e-receipt is a copy, not the registered original; Legal/Compliance team must confirm with BIR before rollout
- **Email deliverability** — e-receipt emails landing in spam/junk folders; transactional email reputation must be maintained; customer may not receive receipt
- **Data privacy** — collecting customer email/mobile at POS for e-receipt delivery is subject to RA 10173 Data Privacy Act; customer must consent to data collection and use; system must include consent capture (opt-in checkbox on POS screen)
- **Slower checkout for non-loyalty customers** — entering email address or mobile number on POS terminal adds ~30 sec per transaction for customers not in loyalty system; may increase queue times during peak
- **Paper receipt still required by many customers** — Philippine customers, especially contractors needing receipts for reimbursement, still prefer paper; e-receipt adoption may be lower than expected

### Time Estimate
~30 sec additional per e-receipt transaction (customer preference selection + email/mobile entry if not on file). Automated delivery: no cashier time. Paper fallback: ~15 sec. Monthly analytics: ~1 hour. At 20–30% adoption rate, impact is ~30 sec on 20–30% of transactions = ~6–9 sec average increase per transaction.

### Staffing Implication
- No incremental headcount — e-receipt delivery is a system capability with minimal cashier interaction (~30 sec per transaction).
- Marketing Team analytics review: ~1 hour/month absorbed by existing Marketing FTE.
- Email/SMS delivery costs: estimated PHP 0.50–1.00 per e-receipt × ~700K e-receipts/month = ~PHP 350K–700K/month (~PHP 4.2–8.4M/year) in delivery costs; offset by thermal paper savings (~PHP 0.30/receipt × 700K = PHP 210K/month saved).

## W529. POS Void & Refund Tiered Authorization

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Cashier initiates void (pre- or post-tender) or refund at POS |
| **Frequency** | Multiple daily per store — estimated 2–5 voids/refunds per store per day |
| **Volume** | ~400–1,000 voids/refunds/day chain-wide |
| **Owner** | Cashier (initiation); Authorizing Manager (approval per tier) |
| **Participants** | Cashier, Department Supervisor, Assistant Store Manager, Store Manager, Regional Manager (by tier) |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Cashier identifies need for void or refund: customer request, pricing error, wrong item scanned, duplicate scan, post-purchase return; Cashier selects "Void" or "Refund" on POS terminal | Cashier | — | 15 sec |
| 2 | System determines authorization tier based on void/refund amount: **Tier 1 — Department Supervisor**: ≤ PHP 5,000 (covers most common voids — wrong item, duplicate scan, minor pricing error); **Tier 2 — Assistant Store Manager**: > PHP 5,000 and ≤ PHP 20,000; **Tier 3 — Store Manager**: > PHP 20,000 and ≤ PHP 100,000; **Tier 4 — Regional Manager**: > PHP 100,000 (rare — large trade account returns, high-value project material returns) | System | — | Automated |
| 3 | System prompts Cashier for void/refund reason code (Wrong Item Scanned / Duplicate Scan / Pricing Error / Customer Changed Mind / Customer Dissatisfied / Damaged Item / Other); Cashier selects reason code | Cashier | — | 15 sec |
| 4 | System displays required authorizer name and role on cashier screen (e.g., "Authorization Required: Dept. Supervisor — Maria Santos"); Cashier calls or signals the required authorizer to terminal | System / Cashier | — | 1 min |
| 5 | Authorizer arrives at terminal; swipes/scan employee ID badge or enters credentials; system verifies authorizer has required role (Department Supervisor / Asst. Manager / Store Manager / Regional Manager); system displays void/refund details (original transaction, amount, reason code, items) for authorizer review | Authorizer | — | 1 min |
| 6 | Authorizer reviews void/refund request: verifies amount is reasonable for stated reason, checks for patterns (same cashier repeatedly voiding, same customer returning frequently), confirms item(s) are present for refund (if applicable); Authorizer approves or rejects | Authorizer | — | 1–2 min |
| 7 | If approved: system processes void (reverses inventory, payment, loyalty points, promo usage per W5B.10) or refund (processes refund to original tender per W12A.6); system records authorization with: authorizer ID, authorizer role, approval timestamp, void/refund amount, reason code, original transaction reference | System | — | Automated |
| 8 | If rejected: system cancels void/refund request; Cashier informs customer of denial and alternative options (e.g., exchange instead of refund, escalate to Store Manager if Department Supervisor denied); system logs rejected request with reason | System / Cashier | — | 30 sec |
| 9 | **No available authorizer**: if required-tier authorizer is not physically available in store (e.g., Store Manager is the only Tier 3 authorizer and is off-site): Cashier can request remote authorization — system sends authorization request to authorizer's mobile device (push notification with transaction details); authorizer reviews and approves/rejects on mobile; all remote authorizations are logged with GPS location and timestamp of authorization device | System / Authorizer | — | 5 min |
| 10 | Daily: system generates void/refund summary report for Store Manager — count and total value by authorization tier, by reason code, by cashier, by authorizer; Store Manager reviews as part of W522 daily transaction review; Regional Manager receives daily summary of all Tier 4 authorizations (> PHP 100,000) | System | — | Automated |

### System Touchpoints
- Configurable tiered authorization matrix by void/refund amount with role-based access control (W529.2)
- Void/refund reason code selection with configurable reason code list (W529.3)
- Authorizer identification via employee ID badge/credentials with role verification (W529.5)
- Full authorization audit trail: authorizer ID, role, timestamp, amount, reason, original transaction (W529.7)
- Remote authorization capability via mobile push notification with GPS tracking (W529.9)
- Daily void/refund summary by tier, reason, cashier, authorizer (W529.10)
- Integration with W5B.10 (void transaction — reversal logic), W12A (in-store returns — refund processing), W101 (customer refund & credit — credit memo), W272 (cashier over/short — variance feed), W37 (loss prevention — void/refund pattern analysis), W522 (daily transaction review — void/refund summary), W152 (IT provisioning — role-based access), W292 (employee master — authorization role assignment)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Authorizer bottleneck** — at stores where only Store Manager holds Tier 3 authority, voids/refunds > PHP 20,000 wait for Store Manager availability; customer wait time increases; remote authorization mitigates but requires mobile infrastructure
- **Tier circumvention** — Cashier and Supervisor colluding to split a large void into multiple smaller voids below PHP 5,000 to avoid higher-tier authorization; system should flag multiple same-customer voids within a session
- **Remote authorization security** — mobile push authorization could be compromised if authorizer's device is lost or stolen; GPS tracking and biometric device lock mitigate
- **Customer perception** — customer waiting at terminal while authorizer is called feels like being suspected of fraud; Cashier should communicate naturally ("Let me get my supervisor to authorize this for you — standard procedure for this amount")
- **Weekend/after-hours coverage** — stores open on weekends or late evening may not have all tier authorizers on shift; weekend scheduling (W34) must ensure at least one Tier 3 authorizer is always present

### Time Estimate
~3–5 min per void/refund (15 sec initiation + automated tier check + 15 sec reason code + 1 min authorizer call + 1–2 min review + automated processing). Remote authorization adds ~5 min. Daily summary review: part of W522 (~5 min of the daily review covers voids/refunds). Chain-wide at 400–1,000/day: ~20–80 hours/day for void/refund processing.

### Staffing Implication
- No incremental headcount — void/refund authorization is absorbed by existing management structure (Supervisors, Asst. Manager, Store Manager).
- Remote authorization availability requires Store Manager or Asst. Manager to have mobile device with push notification capability during all hours store is open.
- Weekend scheduling must ensure at least one Tier 3 authorizer (Store Manager or Asst. Manager) is always on shift.

## W530. POS High-Value Transaction Documentation & Customer ID

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Transaction total exceeds configurable threshold (default: PHP 50,000) |
| **Frequency** | Several per store per day — hardware retail has inherently high basket values (contractors, project buyers); estimated 10–15 high-value transactions per store per day |
| **Volume** | ~2,000–3,000 high-value transactions/day chain-wide |
| **Owner** | Cashier (processing); Store Manager (oversight) |
| **Participants** | Cashier, Customer, Store Manager, Loss Prevention (analytics) |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Transaction total exceeds PHP 50,000 (configurable per store or chain-wide); system displays "High-Value Transaction — Customer Identification Required" prompt on cashier screen | System | — | Automated |
| 2 | Customer-facing display shows "Transaction requires ID verification — please have your government ID ready" | System | — | Automated |
| 3 | Cashier verbally requests customer's government-issued photo ID; acceptable ID types: Philippine driver's license, passport, national ID (PhilSys), SSS ID, GSIS ID, COMELEC voter's ID, postal ID with photo, PRC ID, PhilHealth ID | Cashier | — | 30 sec |
| 4 | Cashier selects ID type from dropdown on POS terminal; enters ID number (or scans ID barcode if machine-readable); enters customer full name from ID | Cashier | — | 1 min |
| 5 | System records customer identification details against transaction: ID type, ID number (encrypted in audit log, last 4 digits visible), customer name, cashier ID, transaction ID, transaction amount; identification details printed on merchant copy of receipt (not on customer copy for privacy) | System | — | Automated |
| 6 | Transaction proceeds to tender per standard W5B.7; if payment is cash and amount ≥ PHP 500,000: W519 AML reporting process is triggered in addition to high-value documentation | Cashier / System | — | Per W5B |
| 7 | For loyalty members: system auto-populates customer name from loyalty record; Cashier only needs to verify and scan/enter ID — reduces data entry to ~30 sec | System / Cashier | — | 30 sec |
| 8 | Daily: high-value transaction register included in W522 daily review; Store Manager spot-checks high-value transactions for reasonableness (customer name matches purchase type, e.g., contractor buying construction materials) | Store Manager | — | Part of W522 |
| 9 | Weekly: Loss Prevention reviews high-value transaction register for anomalies: same customer name with different IDs, same ID at multiple stores, high-value transactions followed by high-value returns (W12), high-value cash transactions (W519 trigger correlation) | LP Analyst | LP Director | 1 hour/week |

### System Touchpoints
- Configurable high-value transaction threshold (default PHP 50,000) with automatic prompt (W530.1)
- Customer-facing display notification for high-value ID requirement (W530.2)
- ID type dropdown with Philippine government-issued ID types (W530.4)
- Customer identification recording: ID type, encrypted ID number, customer name linked to transaction (W530.5)
- Loyalty member auto-populate for customer name (W530.7)
- High-value transaction register with encrypted ID data for LP review (W530.8–9)
- Integration with W5B (in-store selling — high-value within checkout), W519 (AML — PHP 500K+ cash trigger), W522 (daily review — high-value spot-check), W37 (loss prevention — anomaly analysis), W17 (loyalty — customer auto-populate), W253 (customer master — customer data source), W152 (IT provisioning — ID data encryption)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Customer friction** — requesting ID for PHP 50K+ purchases may irritate regular contractor customers; Cashier should frame as "For your protection" rather than "Store policy requires"
- **Data privacy** — collecting and storing government ID numbers is subject to RA 10173 Data Privacy Act; ID numbers must be encrypted, access restricted, and retention period defined (7 years per BIR retention, then purged); NPC registration (W434) must cover this data
- **ID entry errors** — Cashier mistyping ID number or customer name creates data quality issues; OCR/scanning for machine-readable IDs mitigates
- **Threshold calibration** — PHP 50K threshold may be too low for hardware retail (many contractor purchases exceed PHP 50K); too many triggers dilute the control's effectiveness; Store Manager should review threshold adequacy quarterly
- **Privacy on receipt** — customer ID details must NOT appear on customer receipt copy; only on merchant copy; system must enforce separate receipt formats

### Time Estimate
~1.5 min additional per high-value transaction (30 sec ID request + 1 min ID entry). For loyalty members: ~30 sec additional. At 10–15 high-value transactions per store per day: ~15–23 min/day additional cashier time per store. LP weekly review: ~1 hour.

### Staffing Implication
- No incremental headcount — high-value documentation is a Cashier responsibility (~15–23 min/day per store).
- LP Analyst weekly review: ~1 hour/week absorbed by existing LP team.
- Data privacy compliance: existing DPO (W434) oversight covers ID data handling.

---

## W531. POS Bagging, Carry-Out & Bag Fee Compliance

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Transaction completed; customer requires bags or carry-out assistance for purchased items |
| **Frequency** | Per transaction — nearly all transactions require bagging or carry-out in hardware retail |
| **Volume** | ~2.8M transactions/month chain-wide (majority requiring some form of bagging or carry-out) |
| **Owner** | Cashier (bag fee processing); Sales Associate / Utility Staff (carry-out) |
| **Participants** | Cashier, Sales Associate, Customer, Store Manager (ordinance compliance) |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | After tender completion (W5B.8) and receipt printing (W5B.8), Cashier asks customer: "Would you like a bag?" and offers bag options: small paper bag (if available), large plastic bag, reusable bag (for purchase), or no bag | Cashier | — | 15 sec |
| 2 | System automatically determines bag fee applicability based on store location: system reads store location from terminal ID → matches to LGU jurisdiction in location master (W254) → applies LGU-specific bag fee ordinance (e.g., Makati City: PHP 2/bag; Quezon City: PHP 5/bag for plastic; Pasig: PHP 5/plastic bag; Muntinlupa: plastic bag ban — no plastic bags allowed at all; stores in municipalities without ordinance: no bag fee) | System | — | Automated |
| 3 | If bag fee applies: system adds bag fee line item to transaction (already completed — bag fee added as post-tender adjustment OR presented as pre-tender line item before final total); system selects bag fee amount per LGU ordinance and bag type; receipt itemizes bag fee separately ("LGU Plastic Bag Fee: PHP 5.00") | System / Cashier | — | Automated |
| 4 | If customer brings reusable bag: system applies bag fee waiver (no charge); some LGUs provide incentive (e.g., PHP 1 discount per reusable bag used) — system applies incentive if configured for store location | System / Cashier | — | 15 sec |
| 5 | Cashier bags items or customer self-bags; oversized items (lumber, cement bags, large tools, appliances) that cannot fit in bags are staged at bagging area for carry-out | Cashier / Customer | — | 1–2 min |
| 6 | For bulky/heavy items requiring carry-out: Cashier calls Sales Associate or Utility Staff for carry-out assistance; Staff member assists customer to vehicle loading area (W438 yard dispatch for lumber/building materials) or store exit for smaller bulky items; customer signs or acknowledges receipt of items | Sales Associate / Utility Staff | Store Manager | 5–10 min |
| 7 | **Bag supply replenishment**: Cashier monitors bag supply at terminal; when supply runs low: Cashier notifies Stock Associate to replenish from backroom bag inventory; bag inventory tracked as non-merchandise supply item per W136 (indirect procurement) | Cashier / Stock Associate | Store Manager | 5 min (replenishment) |
| 8 | Monthly: Store Manager reviews bag fee revenue and compliance — bag fees collected vs. expected based on transaction volume per LGU ordinance; identifies stores with low bag fee collection (potential non-compliance with LGU ordinance); bag supply consumption report for W136 procurement planning | Store Manager | Regional Manager | 30 min/month |

### System Touchpoints
- LGU-specific bag fee rules by store location from location master (W254) with automatic applicability determination (W531.2)
- Bag fee line item on transaction with LGU ordinance reference (W531.3)
- Reusable bag incentive (discount per bag used) per LGU incentive program (W531.4)
- Bag fee revenue posting to separate GL account per LGU (for LGU reporting if required) (W531.3)
- Bag supply inventory tracking as non-merchandise supply item (W531.7)
- Integration with W5B (in-store selling — bag fee within checkout), W254 (location master — LGU jurisdiction), W136 (indirect procurement — bag supply purchasing), W438 (yard dispatch — carry-out for bulky items), W489 (store budget — bag supply cost allocation)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **LGU ordinance complexity** — Philippine LGUs have different bag fee amounts, bag type restrictions (some ban plastic entirely, some allow plastic with fee), and incentive structures; maintaining current rules per location requires ongoing legal monitoring; incorrect fee application risks LGU fine
- **Customer resistance to bag fees** — customers in municipalities newly implementing bag fees may resist paying; Cashier must explain calmly that fee is mandated by LGU ordinance
- **Bag supply stockouts** — running out of bags during peak hours creates customer frustration and checkout delays; bag replenishment must be prioritized during peak
- **Carry-out liability** — Staff member assisting customer to vehicle may injure themselves (heavy items, parking lot traffic); OHS incident per W140 if injury occurs
- **Plastic bag ban compliance** — stores in municipalities with full plastic bag bans (Muntinlupa) must ensure NO plastic bags are available at checkout; accidental availability risks LGU penalty and public complaint

### Time Estimate
Bag fee transaction: ~15 sec additional per transaction (bag offer + automated fee calculation). Carry-out assistance: ~5–10 min per bulky item carry-out. Bag replenishment: ~5 min per replenishment event. Monthly compliance review: ~30 min.

### Staffing Implication
- No incremental headcount — bagging is a standard Cashier responsibility; carry-out assistance is absorbed by Sales Associates and Utility Staff.
- Carry-out requests during peak hours pull Sales Associates from floor duties; ~5–10 min per carry-out, ~10–20 carry-outs/store/day during peak construction season.
- Bag supply procurement absorbed by W136 indirect procurement process.

---

## W532. POS Clearance & "Final Sale" Item Processing

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Cashier scans an item flagged as "Clearance / Final Sale" in the product master |
| **Frequency** | Variable — higher during seasonal transitions (January post-Christmas, June back-to-school, October pre-holiday clearance) and markdown events (W93) |
| **Volume** | Estimated 5–15% of transactions during active clearance periods; ~2–5% during normal periods |
| **Owner** | Cashier (processing); Store Manager (clearance authorization) |
| **Participants** | Cashier, Customer, Store Manager, Merchandising Team (clearance pricing per W93) |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Cashier scans item barcode; system identifies item as "Clearance" status from item master lifecycle status (W315 — product lifecycle status) and "Final Sale" flag from markdown configuration (W93) | System | — | Automated |
| 2 | System applies clearance price (already configured per W93 markdown process); customer display shows item name, original price (strikethrough), clearance price, and "CLEARANCE — FINAL SALE" label | System | — | Automated |
| 3 | POS terminal displays prominent notification to Cashier: "CLEARANCE ITEM — No Returns. Verbal confirmation required."; Cashier verbally informs customer: "This item is on clearance — it's a final sale with no returns or exchanges" | System / Cashier | — | 15 sec |
| 4 | Cashier confirms verbal disclosure by pressing "Acknowledge" on POS terminal; if Cashier does not acknowledge within 10 seconds: system re-prompts; acknowledgment is required before transaction can proceed past this scan | Cashier | — | 5 sec |
| 5 | Customer-facing display shows clearance item details with "FINAL SALE — No Returns or Exchanges" notice in prominent text; customer can see this before completing purchase | System | — | Automated |
| 6 | Transaction proceeds per standard W5B; receipt prints "FINAL SALE" notation on each clearance line item with "No returns or exchanges" statement at receipt bottom (if any clearance items in transaction) | System / Cashier | — | Per W5B |
| 7 | **Return attempt block**: if customer later attempts to return a final-sale item (W12): system displays "This item was sold as FINAL SALE — returns and exchanges are not accepted"; Cashier cannot process return for final-sale items; Store Manager override required for any exception (Store Manager documents exception reason per W5B.4a) | System / Cashier | Store Manager | 2 min if return attempted |
| 8 | Weekly: Merchandising Team reviews clearance sell-through report — clearance items sold, remaining clearance inventory, sell-through rate by category, days since markdown; identifies clearance items with low sell-through for further markdown (W93) or write-off (W220) | Merchandising Team | VP Merchandising | 1 hour/week |

### System Touchpoints
- Clearance/Final Sale item identification from item master lifecycle status (W315) and markdown configuration (W93) at scan (W532.1)
- Clearance pricing applied from W93 markdown rules with original price strikethrough on customer display (W532.2)
- Cashier verbal disclosure prompt with mandatory acknowledgment before transaction proceeds (W532.3–4)
- Customer display "FINAL SALE" notice per clearance item (W532.5)
- Receipt "FINAL SALE" notation on clearance line items with no-return statement (W532.6)
- Return block for final-sale items with Store Manager override only (W532.7)
- Clearance sell-through report for Merchandising review (W532.8)
- Integration with W5B (in-store selling — clearance within checkout), W93 (markdown & clearance pricing — clearance price source), W12 (returns — final-sale return block), W220 (SLOB provisioning — unsold clearance write-off), W315 (product lifecycle status — clearance status flag), W252 (item master — clearance item data), W40 (price changes — clearance price configuration), W289 (pricing master — clearance pricing rules)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Customer disputes on final-sale policy** — customers attempting to return clearance items despite verbal and written disclosure; "I didn't hear the cashier say it" or "I didn't notice the receipt"; system block at return (W12) prevents override except by Store Manager
- **Cashier skipping verbal disclosure** — Cashier acknowledging the prompt without actually informing customer (pressing "Acknowledge" while customer is distracted); CCTV spot-audit (W71) and customer display mitigate but do not eliminate
- **Clearance barcode confusion** — clearance items with special clearance barcodes (different from regular barcode) may not scan correctly if not loaded to POS terminal; Cashier must manually look up item, creating checkout delay
- **Inconsistent final-sale policy** — if some clearance items allow returns (e.g., manufacturer defect exception) but others do not, Cashier and customer confusion results; policy must be binary — final sale or not
- **Markdown timing** — items marked clearance too late (after significant value erosion) or too early (unnecessary margin sacrifice); W93 markdown governance mitigates

### Time Estimate
~20 sec additional per clearance transaction (automated identification + 15 sec verbal disclosure + 5 sec acknowledgment). Return attempt (rare): ~2 min if customer tries. Weekly Merchandising review: ~1 hour. Total additional cashier time during clearance periods: ~20 sec × 5–15% of transactions = ~2–5 sec average per transaction during clearance.

### Staffing Implication
- No incremental headcount — clearance processing is a standard Cashier and Merchandising responsibility.
- Merchandising Team weekly clearance review: ~1 hour/week absorbed by existing Merchandising FTE.
- Return desk (CSR) may spend additional ~2 min per denied return attempt during clearance periods; estimated 5–10 denied attempts per store per month during active clearance.

---

## W533. POS Real-Time Event Streaming & Continuous Sync

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Every completed POS transaction (sale, void, return, refund, layaway payment, gift card issuance/redemption) |
| **Frequency** | Continuous — every transaction generates an event; ~93,333 events/day across 600 terminals |
| **Volume** | Peak: ~2,000 events/minute during weekend sale events; average: ~65 events/minute |
| **Owner** | IT Operations (event bus infrastructure); System (automated processing) |
| **Participants** | POS Terminal, ERP Message Bus, ERP Modules (Inventory, GL, Loyalty, BI), IT Operations |

### Background

The POS system operates on an event-driven architecture where every transaction is a discrete, immutable event published to the ERP message bus in near-real-time. This replaces the legacy nightly-batch approach and ensures that inventory, financial, loyalty, and ecommerce systems are always current. The event bus decouples POS terminals from downstream processing — terminals publish events and continue selling regardless of whether downstream systems process immediately or with slight delay.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Latency |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Cashier completes transaction at POS terminal (sale, void, return, refund per W5B); terminal generates complete event payload: transaction ID, timestamp, terminal ID, store ID, cashier ID, line items (SKU, quantity, price, discounts, catch-weight details), tender breakdown, customer ID (if loyalty/trade), authorization details (if void/override), event sequence number | System | — | < 1 sec |
| 2 | Terminal publishes event to ERP message bus via HTTPS/REST; event is encrypted in transit; message bus acknowledges receipt with sequence-confirmed response; if acknowledgment not received within 30 seconds: event queued locally for retry (offline mode per W535) | System | — | < 30 sec |
| 3 | Message bus persists event to durable storage; assigns internal event ID; event is now immutable and tamper-evident (hash chain) | System | — | < 1 sec |
| 4 | **Inventory consumer**: ERP inventory module consumes event; deducts quantities from store location on-hand; updates available-to-promise (ATP) for all channels (ecommerce, BOPIS, marketplace); publishes inventory update event for downstream consumers | System | — | < 60 sec from sale |
| 5 | **GL consumer**: ERP financial module consumes event; posts journal entries (Dr. Cash/AR / Cr. Revenue, Dr. COGS / Cr. Inventory, VAT entries); entries posted per transaction (not batched) | System | — | < 60 sec from sale |
| 6 | **Loyalty consumer**: ERP loyalty module consumes event; posts points earned to customer account; processes points redeemed (if applicable); updates customer tier progress | System | — | < 60 sec from sale |
| 7 | **Ecommerce ATP consumer**: ecommerce module consumes inventory update event; updates per-store and per-DC availability on website/app within 5 minutes; prevents overselling | System | — | < 5 min from sale |
| 8 | **BI consumer**: analytics module consumes event; updates real-time sales dashboards, store performance, category sales velocity | System | — | < 5 min from sale |
| 9 | **Continuous price push (inbound)**: throughout the day, ERP publishes price change events, new item events, discontinued item events, and promotional rule events to the message bus; POS terminals subscribe and apply updates to local data store within 60 seconds of publication (per POS-047); enables same-day promo launches and emergency price freezes (POS-024) without waiting for nightly batch | System | — | < 60 sec per change |
| 10 | **Offline event replay**: when POS terminal reconnects after offline period (per W5G), terminal replays all queued offline events from last-acknowledged sequence number; ERP deduplicates and processes in original sequence order; zero data loss guaranteed | System | — | 2–5 min per offline shift |

### System Touchpoints
- Event-driven POS architecture: each transaction is a discrete, immutable event published to message bus (W533.1–2)
- Durable message bus with at-least-once delivery and idempotent consumer deduplication (W533.3)
- Multi-consumer event processing: inventory, GL, loyalty, ecommerce ATP, BI (W533.4–8)
- Continuous price/item/promo push from ERP to POS terminals (W533.9, POS-047)
- Offline event replay with sequence tracking and zero data loss (W533.10, W5G)
- Event hash chain for tamper-evident audit trail (W533.3)
- Integration with W525 (nightly reconciliation — validates continuous sync completeness), W5G (offline recovery — event replay), W535 (offline capability — local event queue), W11/W19 (ecommerce — ATP consumer), W17 (loyalty — loyalty consumer), W35 (BI — analytics consumer), W40 (price changes — price push source), W13 (promotions — promo push source), W252 (item master — new item push source)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Message bus availability** — if the message bus is unavailable, POS terminals queue events locally and continue selling; but downstream systems (ecommerce ATP, BI dashboards) show stale data until bus is restored; message bus must have high-availability clustering with automatic failover
- **Event ordering across terminals** — events from Terminal 1 and Terminal 2 at the same store are processed independently; if both terminals sell the same last item simultaneously, the second event creates a negative stock situation; resolved by store-level inventory allocation (each terminal decrements from a shared store-level ATP) or terminal-to-terminal LAN sync (W535)
- **Consumer processing lag during peak** — during sale events (~2,000 events/minute), if the GL or loyalty consumer falls behind, there is a temporary lag between the sale and the GL posting; acceptable as long as lag stays under 5 minutes; horizontal scaling of consumers mitigates
- **Event payload size for catch-weight transactions** — catch-weight items (lumber, wire) include additional metadata (length, weight, custom pricing) that increases event payload; not significant at individual event level but contributes to aggregate bus throughput during peak

### Time Estimate
- Event publication latency: < 30 seconds from transaction completion to message bus receipt
- Downstream consumer processing: < 60 seconds for inventory, GL, loyalty; < 5 minutes for ecommerce ATP and BI
- Continuous price push latency: < 60 seconds from ERP change to terminal update
- Offline event replay: 2–5 minutes per offline shift of transactions
- No human time required — fully automated continuous processing

### Staffing Implication
- **IT Operations**: event bus infrastructure monitoring is part of existing IT Operations duties (~15 min/day reviewing event bus health dashboard); no incremental headcount
- Message bus infrastructure is a standard ERP component (not a custom-built system); vendor provides HA, monitoring, and alerting

---

## W534. Multi-Origin / Mixed-Basket Fulfillment Orchestration

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer at POS (or online) places an order containing items fulfilled from multiple origins |
| **Frequency** | ~5–10% of in-store transactions and ~15–20% of ecommerce orders involve multi-origin fulfillment; ~3,000–5,000 mixed-basket orders/day chain-wide |
| **Volume** | Average 2–3 fulfillment origins per mixed-basket order |
| **Owner** | Sales Associate (in-store intake); Unified Order Management engine (routing); DC Dispatch / Store Manager (fulfillment execution) |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate, Cashier, Customer, Unified Order Management engine, Stock Associate (store pick), DC Picker (DC pick), Drop-Ship Vendor, 3PL Carrier |

### Background

Modern omnichannel retail requires that a single customer order can contain items fulfilled from multiple origins: items the customer carries out of the store, items shipped from the DC for home delivery, items transferred from another store, or items drop-shipped from a vendor. The POS system supports this "mixed basket" natively — the customer receives a unified receipt and the system orchestrates multiple fulfillment orders behind the scenes while presenting a seamless customer experience.

### Steps (In-Store Mixed Basket)

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Customer selects items in store; some items are in stock on shelf (immediate pickup), some items are not in stock at this store but available at DC or another store, some items are special-order/drop-ship only; Sales Associate uses "Endless Aisle" terminal or POS to check availability across all fulfillment sources | Sales Associate | Dept. Supervisor | 5 min |
| 2 | Sales Associate creates unified order at POS: scans items customer is taking now (in-store fulfillment), selects items for home delivery from DC (per W19), selects items for inter-store transfer (per W22), selects items for drop-ship from vendor (per W246); system shows estimated availability date per fulfillment source | Sales Associate / Cashier | Store Manager | 5 min |
| 3 | System creates unified sales order in ERP with line items tagged by fulfillment origin: (a) each origin generates a separate fulfillment order (store pick, DC pick, vendor ship) linked to the parent sales order; (b) financial posting is unified at the sales order level (single revenue recognition, single VAT computation, single loyalty points calculation); (c) fulfillment execution is separate per origin with independent tracking | System | — | Automated |
| 4 | System checks ATP per fulfillment origin: store-level ATP for in-store items, DC ATP for delivery items, vendor ATP (if available) for drop-ship items; if ATP unavailable for any line: system offers substitution (W98.1), backorder (W56), or cancellation | System | — | Automated |
| 5 | Customer pays for all items at POS (in-store + delivery items + special orders) as a single transaction; multi-tender supported per POS-004; delivery fees calculated per delivery origin (separate fee per delivery shipment); system collects payment for full order amount | Cashier / Customer | Store Manager | 2 min |
| 6 | System prints unified receipt listing all items with per-item fulfillment origin and estimated availability: "Item A — carry out today", "Item B — delivery from DC, ETA 3–5 business days", "Item C — special order from vendor, ETA 7–10 business days"; customer receives tracking link for delivery items via SMS/email | System | — | Automated |
| 7 | **In-store items**: customer carries out immediately; inventory deducted at sale per W533 continuous sync | Customer | — | Immediate |
| 8 | **DC delivery items**: system routes delivery order to nearest DC with ATP per W19; DC picks, packs, and ships per standard W19 process; customer receives per-item tracking notification | System / DC | DC Supervisor | Per W19 SLA |
| 9 | **Inter-store transfer items**: system creates inter-store transfer order per W22; Stock Associate at source store picks and stages for next scheduled inter-store transfer; customer picks up at originating store when available | System / Stock Associate | Store Manager | 2–5 days |
| 10 | **Drop-ship vendor items**: system creates back-to-back PO per W246; vendor ships directly to customer or store; customer notified per W246 tracking | System / Vendor | Ecommerce Mgr | 3–7 days |
| 11 | **Return handling for mixed-basket orders**: customer may return any item from any origin to any BuildRight store per W12; system identifies original fulfillment origin and routes inventory return to appropriate location; refund processed per standard W101 regardless of original fulfillment source | CSR / System | Store Manager | Per W12 |

### Steps (Ecommerce Mixed Basket)

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 12 | Customer places order on website/app containing items stocked at different locations; system evaluates fulfillment options per W536 unified order management: (a) single-location fulfillment preferred (ship all from one DC), (b) split fulfillment if no single location has ATP for all items, (c) ship-from-store (W19B) if DC out of stock but nearby store has ATP, (d) drop-ship (W246) for vendor-direct items, (e) BOPIS (W11) for store-pickup eligible items | System | — | Automated |
| 13 | System creates unified sales order with per-line fulfillment routing; customer sees single order confirmation with per-item delivery estimates and tracking | System | — | Automated |
| 14 | Each fulfillment origin executes independently per its standard workflow (W11, W19, W19B, W246); unified order management tracks overall order completion | System | — | Per origin workflow |

### System Touchpoints
- Mixed-basket POS order creation with per-item fulfillment origin tagging (W534.2–3)
- Unified financial posting at sales order level with split fulfillment execution (W534.3)
- ATP check across all fulfillment origins (store, DC, vendor) (W534.4)
- Unified receipt with per-item fulfillment origin and estimated availability (W534.6)
- Fulfillment routing to DC (W19), inter-store transfer (W22), drop-ship vendor (W246) (W534.8–10)
- Cross-origin return processing with unified refund (W534.11)
- Ecommerce mixed-basket order routing per W536 unified order management (W534.12–14)
- Integration with W536 (unified order management — orchestration engine), W19 (home delivery — DC fulfillment), W19B (ship-from-store), W22 (inter-store transfer), W246 (drop-ship vendor), W11 (BOPIS), W273 (endless aisle), W5B (in-store selling — carry-out items), W12 (returns — cross-origin returns), W101 (refund processing), W98 (order exceptions — fulfillment failure handling)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Customer expectation management** — customers expect all items from a single order to arrive together; mixed-basket orders arriving in multiple shipments at different times generate "where is the rest of my order?" contacts; clear per-item ETA communication at POS and via tracking notifications is critical
- **Fulfillment origin failure** — if one fulfillment origin fails (DC out of stock after order placed, vendor cannot ship), the unified order is partially fulfilled; customer must be notified and offered options (wait, substitute, cancel partial) per W98
- **Delivery fee fairness** — if a mixed-basket order requires two separate deliveries (one from DC, one from vendor), customer may be charged two delivery fees; transparent fee disclosure at order creation is essential
- **Inventory accuracy dependency** — mixed-basket fulfillment relies on accurate ATP across all locations; phantom inventory at a DC or store causes fulfillment failures after the customer has already paid

### Time Estimate
- In-store mixed-basket order creation: ~10 min (5 min item selection + 5 min unified order entry)
- Payment processing: same as standard W5B (~2 min)
- System routing and fulfillment order creation: automated (< 30 sec)
- Per-origin fulfillment: per respective workflow SLA (W19: 2–5 days, W22: 2–5 days, W246: 3–7 days)
- Store staff additional time: ~10 min per mixed-basket order (vs. ~3 min for standard transaction)
- Estimated ~15–25 mixed-basket orders per store per month = ~2.5–4 additional staff-hours/store/month

### Staffing Implication
- **Sales Associates**: absorb mixed-basket order creation (~10 min each) as part of floor selling duties; estimated 15–25 per store per month = ~2.5–4 hours/store/month; absorbed within existing Sales Associate FTEs
- **Cashiers**: no incremental time — payment processing is the same as standard transaction
- **Store Manager**: approves mixed-basket orders above threshold; estimated 5 min per approval; absorbed
- No incremental headcount — mixed-basket orders are an extension of existing workflows (W273, W5D, W19B)

## W535. POS Offline Capability Scope & Local Operations

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | POS terminal detects loss of WAN connectivity to ERP (network outage, ISP failure, ERP maintenance) |
| **Frequency** | Occasional — estimated 2–4 significant offline events per store per year (Philippine internet reliability); brief interruptions (< 5 min) more frequent (~10–15/year per store) |
| **Volume** | Variable — worst case 8+ hours of transactions (~390 transactions per terminal) |
| **Owner** | Store Manager (offline operations); IT Operations (infrastructure) |
| **Participants** | Cashier, Store Manager, IT Helpdesk |

### Background

POS terminals are designed to operate fully offline for ≥ 8 hours per NFR-011. This is not a degraded "emergency mode" but a fully designed operational state. Each terminal maintains a local embedded data store with the complete active SKU catalog, customer records, and pricing — enabling accurate selling without any central system connection. Terminal-to-terminal LAN sync ensures that all terminals in the same store maintain a consistent view of store-level inventory during offline periods.

### Offline Capability Matrix

| POS Function | Offline Status | Behavior |
|---|---|---|
| Barcode scanning | ✅ Full | Scans all 1D/2D barcodes against local SKU catalog |
| Price lookup / calculation | ✅ Full | Uses local price file including promotional rules active at time of last sync |
| Promotional pricing auto-apply | ✅ Full | Promos active at last sync continue to apply; new promos activated during outage not applied |
| Cash tender | ✅ Full | Cash accepted, change given, cash drawer managed |
| Offline card authorization | ✅ Full | Store-and-forward card authorization (terminal stores encrypted card data for later clearing); floor limit configurable per store |
| Receipt printing (BIR format) | ✅ Full | Thermal receipt with BIR-compliant format; offline indicator printed on receipt |
| Void with manager auth | ✅ Full | Manager swipes/authorizes locally; void logged with full audit trail |
| Returns / exchanges | ✅ Full | Return processed against local transaction history; refund issued as cash or store credit per W12 |
| Catch-weight selling | ✅ Full | Weight/length measurement and pricing per local catch-weight rules |
| Age-restricted prompts | ✅ Full | Mandatory prompts per local age-restricted SKU list |
| Price override with auth | ✅ Full | Manager authorization per standard override rules |
| Loyalty points earning | ⚠️ Degraded | Points earned queued locally; customer's current balance not displayable; points posted on reconnection |
| Loyalty points redemption | ⚠️ Degraded | Available only if customer's cached balance supports redemption; cannot verify real-time balance; floor limit per customer tier |
| Gift card balance inquiry | ⚠️ Degraded | Cached balance only (from last sync); balance may not reflect recent online transactions |
| Trade account charge | ⚠️ Degraded | Customer's credit limit not verifiable in real-time; floor limit per account tier (configurable); charges queued for verification on reconnection |
| Layaway payment | ✅ Full | Payment collected and queued; layaway balance updated on reconnection |
| Customer lookup | ⚠️ Degraded | Local cache of loyalty members (top 80% by transaction frequency) and all trade accounts; new customer enrollment unavailable |
| New customer enrollment | ❌ Unavailable | Requires ERP customer master creation; customer advised to enroll at customer service counter when system restored |
| E-wallet payments (GCash, Maya) | ❌ Unavailable | Requires online payment gateway; customer advised to use cash or card instead |
| Digital receipt delivery | ❌ Unavailable | Receipt printed on paper; e-receipt queued for delivery on reconnection |
| Tax exemption verification | ❌ Unavailable | Government/PEZA tax exemption certificates require online verification; full VAT charged; adjustment processed on reconnection |
| BIR e-invoice transmission | ⚠️ Queued | Sales invoices queued locally; transmitted to BIR EIS on reconnection per FIN-034 (3-day BIR tolerance window) |

### Steps (Offline Transition)

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | POS terminal detects loss of WAN connectivity to ERP (heartbeat timeout after configurable interval, default 30 seconds); terminal transitions to offline mode; displays "OFFLINE MODE" indicator on cashier screen and customer display | System | — | Automated |
| 2 | Terminal activates local event queue; all subsequent transactions are stored as encrypted events in local durable storage with sequence numbering; events will be replayed on reconnection per W533.10 | System | — | Automated |
| 3 | Terminal activates store LAN sync (if other terminals are online via separate WAN link): broadcasts inventory deduction events to other terminals on store LAN; receives inventory deductions from other terminals; maintains consistent store-level ATP across all terminals per POS-046 | System | — | Automated |
| 4 | If all terminals in store are offline (store WAN down, not just terminal): terminals continue operating independently using local data store; no terminal-to-terminal sync (each terminal has full local data); inventory overselling risk managed by per-terminal allocation from store safety stock buffer | System / Cashier | Store Manager | Automated |
| 5 | Cashier continues processing transactions per standard W5B with offline capability matrix constraints; POS terminal displays offline-appropriate prompts (e.g., "Cash or card only — e-wallet unavailable during offline mode") | Cashier | — | Per W5B |
| 6 | Store Manager receives offline notification (SMS/email from IT monitoring); monitors offline duration; if offline exceeds 4 hours: Store Manager contacts IT Helpdesk for status update; if offline exceeds 8 hours: Store Manager activates stale-price protocol (W5G.8) — review transactions for price changes that occurred during outage | Store Manager | Regional Manager | 10 min |
| 7 | Quarterly: Store Manager conducts offline readiness drill — simulate WAN disconnection for 30 minutes during low-traffic period; verify all offline capabilities function correctly; document any issues; report to IT Operations | Store Manager | IT Operations | 30 min/quarter |

### Steps (Online Recovery)

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 8 | WAN connectivity restored; POS terminal detects restored heartbeat; terminal begins uploading queued offline events to ERP message bus per W533.10 | System | — | 2–5 min |
| 9 | Terminal receives any queued price/item/promo updates that were published during outage; updates local data store before processing new transactions | System | — | Automated |
| 10 | ERP processes offline events: inventory deductions applied, GL entries posted, loyalty points posted; system performs conflict detection per W5G (inventory conflicts, loyalty conflicts, stale pricing) | System | Store Manager | 15–30 min for exception review |
| 11 | Store Manager reviews and resolves any flagged exceptions per W5G; confirms reconciliation complete | Store Manager | — | Per W5G |
| 12 | Terminal transitions to online mode; "OFFLINE MODE" indicator removed; all POS functions restored to full capability | System | — | Automated |

### Local Data Store Contents

| Data Element | Scope | Refresh Mechanism |
|---|---|---|
| Active SKU catalog (barcode, description, price, UOM) | All 35,000 active SKUs | Continuous push (W533.9) + nightly full refresh (W525.NR-6–7) |
| Promotional rules (active and upcoming) | All active promos + promos effective within 7 days | Continuous push + nightly refresh |
| Loyalty member records | Top 80% by transaction frequency (~480,000 members) + all members who transacted at this store in last 90 days | Nightly refresh with incremental updates |
| Trade account records | All 5,000 trade accounts with pricing tier and credit limit | Continuous push + nightly refresh |
| Terminal configuration | Lane assignment, receipt format, offline parameters | Managed centrally via MDM (per W404) |
| Age-restricted SKU list | All age-restricted SKUs per POS-029 | Nightly refresh |
| Catch-weight rules | All catch-weight SKU pricing rules per UOM | Continuous push + nightly refresh |
| Quick-key / hot-key configuration | Store-specific quick-key layout | Nightly refresh |
| Store-level inventory shadow | Current on-hand quantities for this store location | Continuous deduction from events; nightly full reconciliation |

### System Touchpoints
- Offline mode detection with automatic transition and visual indicators (W535.1)
- Local encrypted event queue with tamper-proof audit trail and sequence numbering (W535.2)
- Terminal-to-terminal store LAN sync for consistent store-level ATP during offline (W535.3–4, POS-046)
- Offline capability matrix: configurable per function and per store (W535 capability table)
- Local embedded data store with continuous push and nightly full refresh (W535 local data store table, POS-043)
- Offline-to-online recovery with event replay and conflict resolution (W535.8–12, W5G)
- Quarterly offline readiness drill (W535.7)
- Integration with W533 (event streaming — offline event replay), W525 (nightly reconciliation — local data refresh), W5G (offline recovery — conflict resolution), W404 (POS hardware master — terminal configuration), W5B (in-store selling — offline capability within checkout), W48 (helpdesk — offline notification), W49 (disaster recovery — extended offline)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Customer dissatisfaction with unavailable functions** — e-wallet customers (15% of transactions) cannot pay during offline; cash-only requirement may cause transaction abandonment; mitigated by offline card store-and-forward capability
- **Loyalty balance inaccuracy** — points earned offline are not visible to customer until reconnection; customer may believe points were lost; mitigated by receipt printing "Points pending — will be posted to your account within 24 hours"
- **Trade account credit limit risk** — offline charges cannot be verified against real-time credit limit; floor limit per account tier caps exposure; estimated risk: ~PHP 50K–100K/month chain-wide in unverified charges (vs. ~PHP 5B monthly revenue = < 0.002%)
- **Local data store staleness** — after 8+ hours offline, local data (especially promotional rules) becomes stale; stale price detection per W5G.8 flags affected transactions for review
- **Terminal hardware storage** — local data store + 1,500 transaction buffer requires adequate storage on each terminal; ~500 MB–1 GB per terminal for full SKU catalog + customer cache + transaction buffer

### Time Estimate
- Offline transition: automated (instant); Store Manager notification review: ~5 min
- Offline operations: no additional time per transaction vs. online mode (per capability matrix)
- Online recovery: automated event replay 2–5 min + Store Manager exception review 15–30 min (only if conflicts detected)
- Quarterly offline drill: ~30 min per store per quarter
- **Total offline readiness overhead**: ~2 hours/store/year (4 drills × 30 min + 2–4 recoveries × 30 min)

### Staffing Implication
- No incremental headcount — offline capability is a standard POS terminal feature designed into the architecture
- Store Manager offline readiness responsibility: ~2 hours/year absorbed into existing duties
- IT Operations: monitoring and alerting for offline events absorbed into existing infrastructure monitoring (~15 min/day reviewing store connectivity dashboard)

---

## W536. Unified Order Management & Cross-Channel Fulfillment Routing

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Order placed from any channel (POS, ecommerce, marketplace, mobile app, trade counter/pro desk) |
| **Frequency** | Continuous — ~138,000 orders/day across all channels (93,333 POS + 42,900 ecommerce + marketplace) |
| **Volume** | Peak: 3× normal during sale events (~400,000 orders/day) |
| **Owner** | Unified Order Management engine (system); Ecommerce Operations Manager (monitoring); Store Manager (store-origin fulfillment) |
| **Participants** | Customer, System (order routing), Sales Associate (POS), Stock Associate (store pick), DC Picker (DC pick), Drop-Ship Vendor, 3PL Carrier, CSR |

### Background

BuildRight operates across multiple selling channels (in-store POS, ecommerce website/app, marketplace Lazada/Shopee, trade counter/pro desk, mobile app) and multiple fulfillment origins (200 store shelves, 4 DCs, drop-ship vendors, dark stores/fulfillment hubs). The Unified Order Management (UOM) engine is the central orchestration layer that accepts orders from all channels, evaluates ATP across all fulfillment origins, determines optimal routing, and manages the complete order lifecycle across all channels with unified financial posting and cross-channel return processing.

### Steps (Order Lifecycle)

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Order intake**: order received from any channel: (a) in-store POS (W5B, W534 mixed basket), (b) ecommerce website/app (W11, W19), (c) marketplace Lazada/Shopee (W180), (d) trade counter/pro desk (W112), (e) mobile app; system normalizes order to unified format regardless of channel | System | — | < 5 sec |
| 2 | **ATP evaluation**: system checks available-to-promise inventory across all fulfillment origins for each line item: (a) store-level ATP (on-hand − allocated − safety stock) for 200 stores, (b) DC-level ATP for 4 DCs, (c) vendor ATP (if available via vendor portal) for drop-ship items, (d) dark store ATP for fulfillment hubs (W210); evaluates inventory allocation per channel rules (INV-019 multi-channel allocation governance) | System | — | < 10 sec |
| 3 | **Fulfillment routing decision**: system determines optimal fulfillment origin(s) per line item based on configurable routing rules: (a) single-origin preferred (lowest cost, fastest delivery), (b) proximity to customer address (for delivery orders), (c) store with highest ATP for BOPIS, (d) DC with best carrier coverage for home delivery, (e) vendor direct for drop-ship-only items, (f) cost optimization (compare store pick labor vs. DC pick + shipping vs. drop-ship margin), (g) split fulfillment when no single origin has ATP for all items | System | — | < 10 sec |
| 4 | **Fulfillment order creation**: system creates fulfillment order(s) per routing decision: (a) one fulfillment order per origin (store pick, DC pick, vendor ship), (b) all fulfillment orders linked to parent sales order, (c) each fulfillment order gets its own tracking number, carrier assignment, and SLA, (d) customer receives unified order confirmation with per-origin delivery estimates | System | — | Automated |
| 5 | **Fulfillment execution**: each fulfillment origin executes per its standard workflow: (a) store pick (W11, W19B), (b) DC pick/pack/ship (W4, W19), (c) vendor direct ship (W246), (d) dark store fulfillment (W210); fulfillment status updates published to UOM engine in real-time | Stock Associate / DC Picker / Vendor | Store Manager / DC Supervisor | Per origin workflow SLA |
| 6 | **Order status aggregation**: UOM engine aggregates fulfillment status from all origins into unified order status: (a) all items pending → "Processing", (b) some items shipped → "Partially Shipped", (c) all items shipped/delivered → "Completed", (d) any item failed → "Partial Exception" with per-item detail; customer sees unified status via channel of origin (POS receipt tracking link, website order page, marketplace) | System | — | Continuous |
| 7 | **Exception handling**: if any fulfillment origin fails (item not found at pick, carrier failure, vendor delay): UOM engine initiates exception workflow per W98; evaluates re-routing options (alternative origin, substitution, backorder, cancellation); customer notified with options | System / CSR | Store Manager / DC Supervisor | Per W98 |
| 8 | **Cross-channel returns**: customer initiates return via any channel (in-store W12, online W12B, marketplace W180); UOM engine identifies original sales order and fulfillment origin; return processed at receiving location (any store or DC); inventory returned to receiving location; refund processed from original sales order (same payment method, same GL posting reversal); cross-location inventory adjustment if return location ≠ original fulfillment location | CSR / System | Store Manager | Per W12 |

### Fulfillment Routing Rules (Configurable)

| Rule | Priority | Description |
|---|---|---|
| Channel-specific ATP reservation | 1 | INV-019 allocation rules reserve inventory per channel; BOPIS reserves store stock, home delivery reserves DC stock, marketplace reserves DC stock |
| Customer proximity | 2 | For delivery orders, route to nearest fulfillment origin with ATP |
| Cost optimization | 3 | Compare store pick labor cost vs. DC pick + shipping cost vs. drop-ship margin; choose lowest total cost |
| SLA compliance | 4 | If routing decision impacts promised delivery SLA, escalate to Ecommerce Operations Manager |
| Store capacity | 5 | Store-origin fulfillment capped at configurable limit to prevent disruption to in-store operations |
| Dark store priority | 6 | Dark store/fulfillment hub (W210) preferred for high-density urban ecommerce orders |

### System Touchpoints
- Unified order intake from all channels with normalized format (W536.1)
- Multi-origin ATP evaluation across stores, DCs, vendors, dark stores (W536.2)
- Configurable fulfillment routing engine with priority rules (W536.3, routing rules table)
- Multi-origin fulfillment order creation linked to parent sales order (W536.4)
- Fulfillment execution per origin: store (W11, W19B), DC (W4, W19), vendor (W246), dark store (W210) (W536.5)
- Unified order status aggregation from all origins (W536.6)
- Exception handling with re-routing per W98 (W536.7)
- Cross-channel returns with unified refund processing (W536.8)
- Integration with W534 (mixed-basket POS — order creation source), W11 (BOPIS — store pick), W19 (home delivery — DC pick), W19B (ship-from-store), W180 (marketplace), W210 (dark store), W246 (drop-ship), W22 (inter-store transfer), W98 (order exceptions), W12/W12B (returns), W101 (refund), W105 (multi-channel allocation), INV-019 (channel ATP reservation)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Routing complexity** — with 200 stores, 4 DCs, vendors, and dark stores as fulfillment origins, the routing engine must evaluate thousands of ATP combinations per order; performance must stay under 10 seconds per routing decision even during peak
- **Channel conflict on inventory** — a high-demand item available at a store could be allocated to an ecommerce home delivery order (via ship-from-store) while a walk-in customer wants to buy it; channel-specific ATP reservation rules (INV-019) must be carefully calibrated
- **Cross-channel return inventory imbalance** — when an item originally fulfilled from DC is returned to a store, the store gains inventory it may not need while the DC is depleted; proactive rebalancing (W154) must account for cross-channel return patterns
- **Customer expectation vs. fulfillment reality** — unified order management presents a seamless experience, but if one fulfillment origin fails, the customer's unified order is disrupted; clear communication about per-item status is critical

### Time Estimate
- Order intake and routing: automated (< 15 sec per order)
- Fulfillment execution: per origin workflow SLA (W11: 4 hours, W19: 2–5 days, W246: 3–7 days)
- Exception handling: ~10 min per exception (per W98)
- Cross-channel returns: per W12 (~10 min per return)
- Ecommerce Operations Manager monitoring: ~1–2 hours/day reviewing routing exceptions and fulfillment performance
- No store-level incremental time — routing is system-driven

### Staffing Implication
- **Ecommerce Operations Manager**: monitors unified order management dashboard, reviews routing exceptions, adjusts routing rules; ~1–2 hours/day absorbed within existing Ecommerce Operations Manager role
- **Store staff**: no incremental headcount — store-origin fulfillment (W11, W19B) uses existing Stock Associates
- **DC staff**: no incremental headcount — DC-origin fulfillment uses existing pick/pack teams
- **IT Operations**: routing engine infrastructure monitoring absorbed within existing IT Operations duties

---

## W537. POS Card Terminal & Acquirer Settlement Operations

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | End of cashier shift (batch close) or end-of-day store close (W5F); also triggered by card reader error or settlement exception |
| **Frequency** | Daily — per shift per terminal (2 shifts × 3 terminals × 200 stores = ~1,200 batch closes/day) |
| **Volume** | ~1.0M card transactions/month (36% of 2.8M POS transactions); ~5,000 card transactions/day/store |
| **Owner** | Store Manager (settlement review); IT Operations (terminal troubleshooting) |
| **Participants** | Cashier (batch close), Store Manager (exception review), IT Helpdesk (terminal issues), Bank Acquirer (settlement processing) |

### Background

Approximately 36% of BuildRight's POS transactions are paid via credit or debit card (EMV chip, contactless/NFC, or manual key-entry). Each POS terminal has an integrated PIN pad that communicates with the bank acquirer for authorization. At end-of-shift and end-of-day, card transactions must be batch-closed and settled with the acquirer. This workflow covers the daily card terminal operations lifecycle: authorization processing, batch close, settlement reconciliation, exception handling, and PIN pad troubleshooting.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Card authorization at POS**: during checkout (W5B.7), customer presents card; cashier or customer taps/inserts/swipes on PIN pad; POS system sends authorization request to acquirer via integrated payment gateway; system receives approval/decline with authorization code; transaction completed with card tender line on receipt | Cashier / System | — | 5–15 sec per transaction |
| 2 | **Contactless / QR payment**: customer taps contactless card or scans e-wallet QR on PIN pad for amounts below contactless limit (PHP 2,000); system processes without PIN; amounts above limit require PIN entry; e-wallet payments (GCash, Maya) processed via same PIN pad terminal using QR display | Cashier / System | — | 3–10 sec |
| 3 | **Shift-end card batch close**: at shift handover (W517), cashier or Store Manager initiates card batch close on each terminal; system transmits all authorized card transactions since last batch close to acquirer for settlement; acquirer confirms batch total (count + amount); batch close receipt printed and retained | Cashier / Store Manager | Store Manager | 2–5 min per terminal |
| 4 | **Batch total verification**: Store Manager compares card batch total on close receipt to POS card tender total from X-report (mid-shift) or Z-report (end-of-day); verifies transaction count and total amount match; flags variance > PHP 100 for investigation | Store Manager | — | 5 min |
| 5 | **Settlement exception handling**: if batch close fails (communication error, acquirer timeout, total mismatch), system queues batch for retry; Store Manager contacts acquirer support if retry fails after 3 attempts; unsettled transactions held in terminal memory and retransmitted on next successful connection; exception logged per W48 (helpdesk ticket if IT assistance needed) | Store Manager / IT Helpdesk | Store Manager | 10–30 min per exception |
| 6 | **PIN pad error troubleshooting**: if PIN pad malfunctions during transaction (offline, printer error, communication failure), cashier follows troubleshooting protocol: (a) restart PIN pad, (b) test with service card, (c) if persistent, log IT ticket per W265; cashier redirects card customers to adjacent terminal; if all terminals down, cash-only mode per W5E | Cashier / IT Helpdesk | Store Manager | 5–15 min |
| 7 | **Daily settlement reconciliation**: next-business-day, Finance receives settlement file from acquirer per W99 (payment settlement reconciliation); settlement file matched to POS batch totals; discrepancies (MDR fees, chargebacks, rejected transactions) flagged for investigation per FIN-037 | Finance / System | Finance Manager | Per W99 |
| 8 | **Monthly terminal inventory & firmware audit**: IT Operations verifies all 600 PIN pads are online, running current firmware, and PCI-DSS compliant; firmware updates pushed during off-hours; non-compliant terminals flagged for replacement per W265 | IT Operations | IT Manager | Quarterly |

### System Touchpoints
- Integrated card payment gateway on each POS PIN pad (W537.1–2)
- Card batch close integration with acquirer settlement platform (W537.3–4)
- Settlement file matching with ERP payment reconciliation (W537.7, W99)
- IT asset management for PIN pad firmware and compliance tracking (W537.8, W265)
- Integration with W5B (card tender during checkout), W5F (EOD close), W517 (shift-end batch close), W99 (settlement reconciliation), W265 (hardware maintenance), W48 (helpdesk), W5E (system-down fallback)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Batch close failure during shift handover** — delays cashier checkout, creates queue; mitigated by automatic retry and terminal memory buffer
- **Settlement discrepancy** — acquirer MDR fee timing differences cause reconciliation variances; mitigated by next-day settlement file matching per W99
- **PCI-DSS compliance across 600 terminals** — firmware updates and compliance certification must be centrally managed; mitigated by quarterly audit and automated firmware push
- **Card decline customer friction** — ~2–3% decline rate in Philippines causes queue delays; mitigated by express redirect to next terminal
- **EMV fallback to manual entry** — increased fraud risk and higher MDR; restricted to manager authorization only

### Time Estimate
- Per-transaction card processing: 5–15 sec (no incremental time vs. cash; absorbed in checkout)
- Shift-end batch close: 2–5 min/terminal × 3 terminals = 6–15 min per shift per store
- Daily batch verification: 5 min per store
- Settlement exception: 10–30 min per occurrence (estimated 2–3 per store per month)
- **Total per store per day**: ~20–30 min absorbed in existing shift handover and EOD procedures

### Staffing Implication
- No incremental headcount — card terminal operations absorbed by existing cashiers and Store Manager
- IT Operations: ~2 hours/quarter for PIN pad firmware audit (absorbed in existing W265 maintenance schedule)
- Finance: settlement reconciliation absorbed in existing W99 daily reconciliation process
