## W538. POS Real-Time Loss Prevention Exception Monitoring & Alert Response

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | POS system detects anomalous transaction pattern in real-time; LP analyst monitors exception dashboard |
| **Frequency** | Continuous — ~500–800 exception alerts/day across 200 stores during operating hours |
| **Volume** | Peak: bi-monthly sales events generate 2–3× normal exception volume |
| **Owner** | Loss Prevention Manager (enterprise); Store Manager (store-level response) |
| **Participants** | LP Analyst (monitoring), Store Manager (response), Cashier (involved in flagged transaction), Regional LP Manager (escalation) |

### Background

BuildRight processes 2.8M POS transactions/month across 600 terminals. While W37 (Loss Prevention & Exception Reporting) provides retrospective analysis and W522 (Daily Transaction Review) covers next-day cashier auditing, there is no workflow for real-time detection and immediate response to POS anomalies. Common retail fraud patterns — sweethearting (cashier deliberately under-rings items), pass-through fraud (scanning低价 items for高价 ones), rapid void sequences, excessive no-sale drawer opens, coupon abuse, and serial returners — require real-time detection and intervention to prevent ongoing losses. Industry benchmarks suggest that real-time LP monitoring can reduce shrinkage by 15–25% compared to retrospective-only approaches.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Real-time exception detection**: LP analytics engine continuously monitors POS event stream (W533) for anomaly patterns: (a) high void rate per cashier (> 5% of transactions in 1-hour window), (b) excessive no-sale drawer opens (> 3 per hour per cashier), (c) rapid consecutive voids (> 2 voids within 5 minutes by same cashier), (d) price override frequency exceeding threshold, (e) sweethearting detection (repeated low-value scans followed by high-value manual entry), (f) pass-through detection (weight mismatch between scanned barcode and expected item weight at SCO), (g) refund/return frequency exceeding threshold per cashier, (h) manual card entry (non-EMV) above baseline, (i) loyalty points gaming patterns | System | — | Real-time (< 30 sec from event) |
| 2 | **Alert classification and routing**: system classifies each detected anomaly by severity: (a) **Critical** (active fraud suspected — immediate response required): sweethearting, pass-through fraud, serial void-refund pattern; (b) **High** (suspicious pattern — same-shift response): excessive voids, frequent overrides, coupon stacking abuse; (c) **Medium** (anomalous but explainable — next-day review): slightly elevated void rate, higher-than-average discount usage; (d) **Low** (statistical noise — logged for trend analysis); Critical and High alerts pushed to LP Analyst dashboard and Store Manager mobile app in real-time; Medium alerts queued for W522 daily review | System | — | Automated |
| 3 | **Critical alert triage**: LP Analyst reviews critical alert on dashboard; assesses context (cashier history, transaction details, CCTV snapshot if available, recent performance metrics from W522); determines if active intervention is warranted | LP Analyst | LP Manager | 3–5 min per alert |
| 4 | **Store-level response for Critical alerts**: LP Analyst contacts Store Manager (or on-duty supervisor) via secure channel with alert details; Store Manager observes cashier covertly; if fraud confirmed, Store Manager intervenes per W37 (LP investigation protocol); if false positive, LP Analyst dismisses alert and notes reason for ML model improvement | LP Analyst / Store Manager | LP Manager | 5–15 min per response |
| 5 | **High alert same-shift review**: at next natural break (shift handover W517 or mid-shift), Store Manager reviews High alerts for their cashiers; reviews transaction-level detail; coaches cashier if pattern is procedural error; escalates to LP if pattern appears deliberate | Store Manager | — | 5–10 min per alert |
| 6 | **Medium alert daily review**: Medium alerts consolidated into W522 daily transaction review; Store Manager addresses during next-day cashier performance audit | Store Manager | — | Per W522 |
| 7 | **Pattern analytics & model tuning**: LP Analyst reviews weekly exception summary: alert volume by store, severity distribution, false-positive rate, confirmed fraud cases; adjusts detection thresholds; feeds confirmed cases back to ML model for continuous improvement; monthly report to LP Manager | LP Analyst | LP Manager | ~2 hours/week |
| 8 | **Cross-store serial fraud detection**: system detects same customer (loyalty ID, payment card token, or phone number) triggering exceptions across multiple stores within defined period; LP Analyst investigates cross-store pattern; coordinates with affected Store Managers | LP Analyst | LP Manager | Per case |

### Exception Detection Rules (Configurable)

| Pattern | Detection Method | Default Threshold | Severity |
|---|---|---|---|
| Excessive voids | Void count / total transactions per cashier per hour | > 5% void rate | Medium → High if sustained |
| No-sale drawer opens | Count per cashier per hour | > 3/hour | Medium |
| Sweethearting | Item price vs. expected basket value ratio | < 0.6 ratio + weight mismatch | Critical |
| Pass-through fraud | Weight mismatch at SCO or barcode anomaly | > 20% weight deviation | Critical |
| Rapid void sequence | Consecutive voids within time window | > 2 voids in 5 min | High |
| Coupon abuse | Coupon value vs. basket ratio | > 50% coupon-to-sale ratio | Medium → High if repeated |
| Serial returner | Return frequency by customer identifier | > 3 returns in 7 days | Medium → High if across stores |
| Manual card entry | Non-EMV transaction frequency | > 2% of card transactions per cashier | Medium |

### System Touchpoints
- Real-time POS event stream consumption (W533) for anomaly detection engine (W538.1)
- LP exception dashboard with alert classification and routing (W538.2)
- CCTV snapshot integration for alert context (W538.3)
- Store Manager mobile alert notification (W538.4)
- Integration with W37 (LP investigation — confirmed cases), W522 (daily review — medium alerts), W281 (SCO exceptions — pass-through detection), W248 (inventory variance — shrinkage correlation), W272 (cashier over/short — drawer anomaly context)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Alert fatigue** — too many false positives cause LP Analysts to ignore real fraud; mitigated by severity classification and ML model tuning with confirmed-case feedback
- **Privacy concerns** — real-time cashier monitoring may be perceived as surveillance; mitigated by clear LP policy disclosure in cashier onboarding (W518) and union coordination (W493)
- **CCTV integration latency** — snapshot retrieval must be near-real-time to be useful for Critical alerts; mitigated by edge CCTV processing or direct POS-to-camera timestamp correlation
- **Cross-store data latency** — real-time event stream (W533) must aggregate across stores for serial fraud detection; mitigated by centralized LP analytics engine consuming all store events

### Time Estimate
- System detection and alert: automated (real-time, < 30 sec from event)
- Critical alert triage: 3–5 min per alert (estimated 5–10 Critical alerts/day chain-wide)
- Store-level Critical response: 5–15 min per response
- High alert review: 5–10 min per alert (estimated 20–30 High alerts/day chain-wide, distributed across stores)
- Weekly analytics and tuning: ~2 hours/week for LP Analyst
- **Total LP Analyst time**: ~3–4 hours/day across enterprise (2–3 analysts)

### Staffing Implication
- **2–3 LP Analysts** (enterprise-level, at HQ or regional offices) for real-time dashboard monitoring and alert triage; may be absorbed into existing LP team if current headcount permits
- Store Managers: ~10 min/day reviewing High alerts (absorbed into existing LP responsibilities per W37)
- No incremental IT headcount — LP analytics engine maintained by existing IT Operations

---

## W539. POS Promotional Coupon, Voucher & Manufacturer Coupon Processing

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer presents paper coupon, digital voucher, or manufacturer coupon at POS checkout |
| **Frequency** | ~140,000 coupon/voucher redemptions/month (~5% of POS transactions); spikes during sale events to ~15% |
| **Volume** | Peak: bi-monthly sales events can generate 40,000+ coupon redemptions in a single weekend |
| **Owner** | Marketing Manager (coupon design and issuance); Store Manager (redemption compliance) |
| **Participants** | Cashier (scan/validate), Customer, Store Manager (override for manual coupons), Marketing (reconciliation), Finance (manufacturer coupon reimbursement) |

### Background

BuildRight issues and accepts several types of coupons and vouchers: (a) store-issued paper coupons (flyers, newspaper inserts, direct mail), (b) digital vouchers (mobile app, email, SMS with barcode/QR), (c) manufacturer coupons (vendor-funded discounts on specific brands), (d) store credit vouchers (issued by CSR for complaints or loyalty rewards), and (e) partner/bank coupons (credit card BIN-triggered discounts). Currently no workflow covers how these are validated, applied, redeemed, and reconciled at POS. With ~140,000 monthly coupon redemptions, this represents a significant discount liability and vendor reimbursement process that requires operational controls.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Coupon scan at checkout**: cashier scans coupon barcode/QR code at POS; for paper coupons, cashier scans barcode; for digital vouchers, customer presents phone screen for scanning; POS validates coupon against coupon master data: (a) coupon is active and within valid date range, (b) coupon applies to items in current basket, (c) coupon usage limit not exceeded (per-coupon, per-customer, per-transaction), (d) coupon is not a duplicate (unique coupon code check), (e) coupon stacking rules allow this combination | Cashier / System | — | 5–10 sec per coupon |
| 2 | **Manual coupon entry**: for coupons without scannable barcode (rare), cashier enters coupon code manually; system validates same rules; manager override required for manual entry of coupons > PHP 500 value | Cashier / Store Manager | Store Manager | 1–2 min |
| 3 | **Coupon application**: system applies coupon discount to qualifying basket items; discount shown on customer display and receipt; coupon redemption logged with unique transaction ID, cashier ID, and timestamp; coupon marked as redeemed in coupon master (prevents reuse); if coupon is manufacturer coupon, line item flagged for vendor reimbursement (FIN-019) | System | — | Automated |
| 4 | **Stacking and combination rules**: system enforces configurable stacking rules: (a) store coupon + manufacturer coupon = allowed on same item, (b) two store coupons on same item = blocked, (c) loyalty discount + coupon = lower of two applies (no double-dipping), (d) employee discount + coupon = employee discount only, (e) clearance items excluded from coupon use per W532 | System | — | Automated |
| 5 | **Digital voucher delivery verification**: for digital vouchers presented via mobile app, system validates against voucher master and confirms voucher belongs to presenting customer (loyalty ID match); prevents screenshot fraud via single-use QR with time-limited validity window | System | — | Automated |
| 6 | **Coupon exception handling**: if coupon fails validation (expired, wrong item, usage exceeded, duplicate), POS displays rejection reason; cashier communicates to customer; customer may proceed without coupon or request manager review; manager may override rejection for goodwill with reason code and documentation | Cashier / Store Manager | Store Manager | 2–5 min per exception |
| 7 | **End-of-day coupon reconciliation**: at store close (W5F), system generates coupon redemption summary: total coupons redeemed by type, total discount value, manufacturer coupon count and value for reimbursement; Store Manager reviews and confirms; summary transmitted to Finance and Marketing via event stream (W533) | System / Store Manager | Store Manager | 5 min |
| 8 | **Manufacturer coupon reimbursement**: Finance processes manufacturer coupon reimbursement per FIN-019 (vendor rebate management); manufacturer coupons accumulated and submitted to vendor monthly with proof-of-redemption; vendor credits BuildRight account | Finance | Finance Manager | Monthly batch |

### Coupon Type Matrix

| Coupon Type | Source | Validation | GL Impact | Reimbursement |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Store paper coupon | Marketing | Barcode scan + master data | Promo discount account | N/A (store cost) |
| Digital voucher (app/email) | Marketing/CRM | QR/barcode + loyalty ID + time validity | Promo discount account | N/A (store cost) |
| Manufacturer coupon | Vendor | Barcode scan + item eligibility | Vendor receivable | Monthly vendor claim |
| Store credit voucher | Customer Service | Barcode scan + master data | Store credit liability | N/A (already accrued) |
| Partner/bank coupon | Bank/partner | BIN-triggered + barcode | Co-op marketing share | Per partnership agreement |

### System Touchpoints
- Coupon master data management with active/inactive status, date ranges, usage limits, and item eligibility (W539.1)
- Barcode/QR scanning for paper and digital coupons (W539.1, W539.5)
- POS discount application with stacking rules engine (W539.3–4)
- Coupon redemption logging linked to transaction, cashier, and customer (W539.3)
- EOD coupon redemption summary (W539.7) via event stream (W533)
- Manufacturer coupon reimbursement integration with AP (W539.8, FIN-019)
- Integration with W5B (checkout — coupon application), W523 (promotional setup — coupon configuration), W13 (promotions — coupon master source), W5F (EOD — coupon summary), W538 (LP monitoring — coupon abuse detection), W40 (pricing — coupon pricing rules)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Coupon fraud (photocopies, screenshots)** — digital voucher single-use QR with time-limited window mitigates screenshot fraud; paper coupon unique serial numbers prevent photocopying
- **Stacking rule complexity** — customers and cashiers confused by which coupons combine; mitigated by system-enforced rules with clear rejection messages
- **Manufacturer coupon reimbursement delays** — vendors may dispute coupon validity or delay reimbursement; mitigated by clear documentation and photo capture at POS
- **Expired coupon customer frustration** — customer presents coupon that expired yesterday; mitigated by 3-day grace period configurable per promotion

### Time Estimate
- Per-coupon scan and validation: 5–10 sec (absorbed in checkout time)
- Manual coupon entry: 1–2 min per occurrence (~5% of coupons)
- Exception handling: 2–5 min per occurrence (~3% of coupons = ~4,200/month)
- EOD coupon reconciliation: 5 min/store/day
- **Total incremental checkout time**: ~10 sec per coupon transaction (negligible per transaction, ~23 hours/day across chain)

### Staffing Implication
- No incremental headcount — coupon processing absorbed by existing cashiers
- Marketing: coupon master data management absorbed into promotional planning (W13)
- Finance: manufacturer coupon reimbursement absorbed into existing AP process (FIN-019)

---

## W540. POS BIR Invoice Reprint, Adjustment & Credit Note Issuance

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer requests receipt/invoice reprint; cashier discovers error requiring invoice adjustment; return/refund requires BIR credit note |
| **Frequency** | ~14,000 reprints/month (~0.5% of transactions); ~56,000 credit notes/month (~2% return rate) |
| **Volume** | Peak: post-sale events when customers submit receipts for reimbursement |
| **Owner** | Store Manager (reprint authorization); Finance (credit note compliance) |
| **Participants** | Cashier, Customer, Store Manager, Finance (credit note review) |

### Background

The Bureau of Internal Revenue (BIR) has strict requirements for registered invoices and receipts issued from CAS-registered systems (per W54A). Original receipts cannot be reprinted as exact duplicates; BIR requires specific procedures for reprints, credit notes (for returns/refunds), and debit notes (for price corrections). All must maintain sequential numbering, reference the original transaction, and be retained for 7 years. This workflow covers the POS-side procedures for BIR-compliant invoice reprints, adjustments, and credit note issuance.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Receipt reprint request**: customer requests copy of receipt; cashier looks up original transaction by date, receipt number, or loyalty ID; system verifies original transaction exists and belongs to requesting customer (loyalty ID or payment card match); cashier requests reprint authorization from Store Manager | Cashier | Store Manager | 2–3 min |
| 2 | **BIR-compliant reprint**: system prints reprint with "REPRINT" watermark, original receipt number, original date/time, and reprint date/time; reprint does NOT generate a new sequential invoice number; system logs reprint with cashier ID, reason, and timestamp for audit trail; customer signs reprint acknowledgment log (paper or digital) | System / Cashier | Store Manager | 1 min |
| 3 | **Credit note for return/refund**: when processing return or refund (W12, W529), system automatically generates BIR-compliant credit note: (a) new sequential credit note number from CAS-registered range, (b) references original sales invoice number and date, (c) lists returned items with original prices, (d) shows VAT amount being reversed, (e) shows total credit amount, (f) customer name and TIN if available, (g) BIR CAS permit number; credit note printed in duplicate (customer copy + file copy) | System | Store Manager | Automated (part of return processing) |
| 4 | **Debit note for price correction**: when a post-sale price correction is required (pricing error discovered after transaction completion), Store Manager initiates debit note: (a) references original sales invoice, (b) shows price difference, (c) shows additional VAT, (d) customer signs debit note; debit note requires Finance Manager approval for amounts > PHP 5,000 | Store Manager / Finance | Finance Manager | 5–10 min |
| 5 | **Voided transaction documentation**: when a transaction is voided per W529 (void authorization), system prints void document referencing original invoice number with "VOIDED" watermark, void reason, authorizer ID, and timestamp; voided invoice number preserved in sequence (not reused) per BIR requirements; void document filed for BIR audit | System / Cashier | Store Manager | Automated (part of void processing) |
| 6 | **BIR audit trail maintenance**: all reprints, credit notes, debit notes, and void documents automatically indexed and retained in POS transaction archive; 7-year retention per BIR requirements (NFR-006); monthly summary generated for Finance review; supporting documentation for BIR audit per W77 (BIR audit response) | System | Finance | Automated + 30 min/month review |

### System Touchpoints
- BIR CAS-registered sequential numbering engine for credit notes and debit notes (W540.3–4)
- Original transaction lookup by receipt number, date, loyalty ID, or payment card (W540.1)
- Reprint watermark and logging system (W540.2)
- Credit note auto-generation integrated with return/refund processing (W540.3, W12, W529, FIN-027)
- Void document generation integrated with void authorization (W540.5, W529)
- 7-year digital archive integration (W540.6, DOC-002, NFR-006)
- Integration with W5B (checkout — original transaction source), W12/W12A (returns — credit note trigger), W529 (void/refund authorization — void document trigger), W54A (CAS registration — numbering authority), W77 (BIR audit — document retrieval), W528 (digital receipt — e-receipt reprint), W473 (BIR EIS — credit note transmission)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Credit note sequential gap** — if credit note generation fails (printer error), the CAS-registered sequence has a gap, which BIR auditors scrutinize; mitigated by system-enforced sequential integrity with void-if-error fallback
- **Customer without original receipt requesting credit note** — BIR allows credit notes without original receipt for certain transaction types; system provides lookup alternative (payment card, loyalty ID); manual override with manager approval
- **Reprint abuse for expense fraud** — customers may request reprints to submit duplicate expense claims; mitigated by "REPRINT" watermark and log of all reprints
- **Debit note approval bottleneck** — Finance Manager approval for amounts > PHP 5,000 may delay customer; mitigated by Store Manager provisional approval with next-day Finance confirmation

### Time Estimate
- Receipt reprint (lookup + print + log): 3–4 min per occurrence
- Credit note (automated as part of return): no incremental time beyond W12 processing
- Debit note (manual initiation + approval): 5–10 min per occurrence (~200/month chain-wide)
- Void document (automated): no incremental time beyond W529 processing
- Monthly audit trail review: 30 min/month by Finance
- **Total**: ~560 hours/month chain-wide for reprints + ~33 hours/month for debit notes

### Staffing Implication
- No incremental headcount — reprint and credit note operations absorbed by existing cashiers and Store Managers
- Finance: monthly audit trail review (~30 min/month) and debit note approval (~2 hours/month) absorbed into existing duties

---

## W541. POS Cash Office Operations & Bank Deposit Preparation

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | End of cashier shift (W517) or end-of-day store close (W5F) when cash drawers are submitted to cash office |
| **Frequency** | Daily — 2 shifts × 200 stores = 400 cash office processing cycles/day |
| **Volume** | ~PHP 2.1B in monthly cash sales (42% of PHP 5.04B); ~PHP 350,000/day/average store in cash |
| **Owner** | Store Manager (cash office operations); Finance (bank deposit verification) |
| **Participants** | Cash Office Clerk / designated Store Manager on-duty, Cashier (drawer handover), CIT Guard (cash pickup per W212), Bank (deposit processing) |

### Background

While W5F covers end-of-day store close procedures and W517 covers cashier shift handover, neither details the cash office back-office operations that occur after cashiers submit their drawers. The cash office is the secure back-room area where drawer counts are verified, variances documented, bank deposits prepared, and cash secured. For BuildRight with ~PHP 350,000/day in cash per store, the cash office process is critical for cash security, accurate financial reporting, and loss prevention. Stores with Smart Safe (W212) have partially automated this process, but all stores require a structured cash office workflow.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Drawer receipt from cashier**: at shift end (W517.7) or EOD (W5F.2), cashier brings sealed cash drawer or tamper-evident cash bag to cash office; Cash Office Clerk verifies tamper-evident seal is intact; records receipt time and cashier name | Cash Office Clerk | Store Manager | 2–3 min per drawer |
| 2 | **Drawer count and verification**: Cash Office Clerk opens drawer/bag in cash office (secure room with CCTV); counts bills by denomination; counts coins by denomination; records actual cash amount per cashier; system compares actual count to POS system expected amount (opening float + cash sales − cash refunds − cash payouts − voids) | Cash Office Clerk | — | 5–8 min per drawer |
| 3 | **Variance documentation**: if variance between actual and expected > PHP 50 (configurable threshold): (a) system flags variance for investigation per W272 (cashier over/short), (b) Cash Office Clerk documents variance with cashier name, amount, date, shift, (c) Store Manager notified, (d) cashier notified and signs variance acknowledgment; variance < PHP 50 recorded but not escalated | Cash Office Clerk / Store Manager | Store Manager | 3–5 min per variance |
| 4 | **Non-cash tender verification**: for stores without Smart Safe, Cash Office Clerk also verifies non-cash tender totals: (a) card batch totals vs. POS card tender report (per W537), (b) e-wallet settlement totals vs. POS e-wallet tender report, (c) trade account charges vs. POS trade account report; discrepancies flagged per W537 or W261 | Cash Office Clerk | — | 5 min |
| 5 | **Bank deposit preparation**: Cash Office Clerk prepares bank deposit: (a) selects bills and coins for deposit (keeping working float for next day), (b) fills bank deposit slip with cash breakdown by denomination, (c) includes any check payments received, (d) seals deposit in tamper-evident bank bag with deposit slip, (e) records deposit amount in cash office log; for Smart Safe stores (W212): Smart Safe counts and verifies cash automatically; Cash Office Clerk confirms Smart Safe count and initiates deposit report | Cash Office Clerk | Store Manager | 10–15 min |
| 6 | **Cash securing**: remaining working float (next day's opening float per W5A) placed in store safe; bank deposit bag secured in safe pending CIT pickup (W212) or manager bank run; cash office log updated with safe contents (working float + deposit bag); safe locked and time-lock set | Cash Office Clerk | Store Manager | 5 min |
| 7 | **Cash office close report**: system generates cash office close report: (a) per-cashier actual vs. expected with variances, (b) total cash received, (c) bank deposit amount, (d) working float retained, (e) non-cash tender summary, (f) net cash position; Store Manager reviews and digitally signs; report transmitted to Finance via event stream (W533) | System / Cash Office Clerk | Store Manager | 5 min |
| 8 | **CIT pickup or bank deposit execution**: for stores with CIT service (W212, FIN-025): CIT guard arrives per schedule; two-person custody transfer (Cash Office Clerk + CIT guard); both sign custody transfer log; CIT guard transports deposit to bank; for stores without CIT: Store Manager or designated supervisor makes bank run before close; bank deposit receipt obtained and filed | Cash Office Clerk / CIT Guard | Store Manager | 10–15 min |

### System Touchpoints
- POS system expected drawer amounts per cashier per shift (W541.2, from W5B transaction data)
- Cash office variance flagging and escalation to W272 (cashier over/short) (W541.3)
- Card and e-wallet tender verification per W537 and W261 (W541.4)
- Smart Safe integration for automated cash counting (W212) (W541.5)
- Cash office close report generation and transmission via W533 (W541.7)
- CIT custody transfer documentation integration with FIN-025 (W541.8)
- Integration with W5A (opening float — next-day float source), W5F (EOD close — triggers cash office), W517 (shift handover — drawer submission), W212 (Smart Safe — automated counting), W272 (over/short — variance investigation), W278 (mid-day skim — mid-day deposit), FIN-025 (CIT — armored car), W89 (bank reconciliation — deposit matching), W48 (helpdesk — safe/office issues)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Cash counting error** — manual counting of ~PHP 350,000/store/day is error-prone; mitigated by denomination-level count recording and dual-verification for variances > PHP 200
- **Robbery risk during bank run** — stores without CIT service are vulnerable during cash transport; mitigated by CIT service for high-volume stores, varying bank run times for others
- **Cash office bottleneck during peak** — multiple cashiers submitting drawers simultaneously at shift change; mitigated by staggered shift handover schedule and Smart Safe automation (W212)
- **Counterfeit detection** — manual counterfeit detection unreliable for high-volume cash; mitigated by UV counterfeit detection pens in cash office and Smart Safe counterfeit detection sensors
- **Cash float planning** — insufficient float for next day disrupts store opening; mitigated by minimum float threshold alerts and automatic CIT cash delivery scheduling

### Time Estimate
- Drawer receipt and count: 7–11 min per cashier (× 3–6 cashiers per shift per store = 21–66 min/shift)
- Variance documentation: 3–5 min per variance (~20% of drawers = 1–2 variances/shift)
- Bank deposit preparation: 10–15 min
- Cash securing: 5 min
- Report review and sign: 5 min
- CIT pickup: 10–15 min
- **Total per store per day**: ~90–120 min (split between 2 shifts = 45–60 min/shift)
- **Total chain-wide per day**: ~300–400 hours

### Staffing Implication
- **Cash Office Clerk**: 1 per store (part-time, 4–6 hours/day covering both shifts); currently implied by existing roles but not explicitly staffed; role may be combined with back-office/admin responsibilities
- For Smart Safe stores (W212): Cash Office Clerk time reduced by ~30–40% (automated counting)
- CIT guard cost: per FIN-025 contract; varies by location

## W542. POS Quotation & Estimate Generation with Sales Conversion

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer (walk-in or trade account) requests price quotation for large or project purchase at POS or trade counter/pro desk |
| **Frequency** | ~8,000 quotations/month (~0.3% of POS transactions); trade counter generates ~60% of quotations |
| **Volume** | Peak: construction season (Q1, Q4) generates 2× quotation volume |
| **Owner** | Sales Associate / Trade Counter Associate (quotation creation); Store Manager (discount authorization) |
| **Participants** | Customer, Sales Associate, Store Manager (discount approval), Trade Account Manager (for B2B quotes) |

### Background

BuildRight's trade and project customers (contractors, builders, institutional buyers) frequently request quotations before purchasing large quantities of materials. CRM-013 (Trade Counter/Pro Desk) mentions quick quote generation, but no detailed workflow exists for the full quotation lifecycle: creation, pricing, discount authorization, validity management, and conversion to sales order. With an average quotation value of ~PHP 35,000 (20× average basket), and a conversion rate of ~55%, quotations represent ~PHP 154M/month in potential revenue requiring a structured POS workflow.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Quotation request**: customer approaches POS or trade counter with list of items for quotation; Sales Associate creates new quotation in POS system; quotation captures: customer details (walk-in or account lookup), item list with quantities, requested delivery date (if applicable) | Sales Associate | — | 5–10 min |
| 2 | **Pricing and availability check**: system auto-populates standard pricing from price master (W289); system checks real-time ATP for each item across store + DC inventory (W536.2); for items with quantity break pricing (POS-010), system shows best applicable tier; for trade accounts, system applies contracted pricing (CRM-003); out-of-stock items flagged with expected replenishment date or alternative suggestions | System / Sales Associate | — | 2–3 min |
| 3 | **Discount application (if requested)**: if customer requests discount beyond standard pricing: Sales Associate applies discretionary discount per authorization matrix: (a) up to 5% — Sales Associate, (b) 5–10% — Department Supervisor, (c) 10–15% — Store Manager, (d) > 15% — Regional Manager; discount reason code required (volume, competitor match, loyalty, project bid); system calculates margin impact for approver | Sales Associate / Store Manager | Per authorization tier | 2–5 min |
| 4 | **Quotation document generation**: system generates BIR-compliant quotation document (not an invoice — clearly labeled "QUOTATION"); includes: (a) unique quotation number, (b) date and validity period (configurable, default 30 days), (c) customer details, (d) item list with unit prices, quantities, and line totals, (e) applicable discounts shown separately, (f) VAT calculation, (g) total amount, (h) payment terms for trade accounts, (i) delivery terms if applicable, (j) POS terminal and associate ID; quotation printed or emailed per customer preference (per W528 e-receipt delivery) | System | — | 1 min |
| 5 | **Quotation validity tracking**: system tracks quotation status: Open (within validity period), Expired (past validity date), Converted (sale completed), Cancelled (customer declined); daily batch flags quotations expiring within 3 days; Sales Associate proactively contacts customer for expiring quotations | System / Sales Associate | — | Automated + 5 min per follow-up |
| 6 | **Quotation conversion to sale**: customer returns to convert quotation to sale; Sales Associate recalls quotation by quotation number; system verifies: (a) quotation is within validity period, (b) pricing still current (alerts if price changed since quotation), (c) ATP still available (alerts if stock depleted); customer confirms or adjusts quantities; system converts quotation to sales transaction (W5B) with full POS checkout | Sales Associate / System | — | 5–10 min |
| 7 | **Quotation analytics**: monthly quotation performance report: (a) quotation count by store, (b) conversion rate by store and associate, (c) average quotation value, (d) average discount depth, (e) expired quotation analysis, (f) margin impact of quotation discounts; report feeds into W67 (store performance review) and trade sales pipeline (CRM-012) | System / Store Manager | — | Monthly automated + 30 min review |

### System Touchpoints
- Quotation creation module in POS system (W542.1)
- Real-time ATP and pricing integration (W542.2, W536.2, W289)
- Discount authorization matrix with margin impact calculation (W542.3)
- BIR-compliant quotation document generation (W542.4)
- Quotation lifecycle tracking with status management (W542.5)
- Quotation-to-sale conversion with price and stock revalidation (W542.6, W5B)
- Integration with W5B (checkout — sale conversion), W528 (e-receipt — quotation email), W289 (pricing master — standard prices), CRM-003 (trade account — contracted pricing), CRM-013 (trade counter — quotation source), W536 (order management — ATP check), W67 (store performance — quotation KPIs), POS-006 (price override — discount authorization), POS-010 (quantity break pricing)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Stale pricing on converted quotations** — prices may change between quotation date and conversion date; mitigated by price-change alert at conversion with customer confirmation
- **Quotation-to-order stockout** — ATP available at quotation time may be sold before conversion; mitigated by optional stock reservation with deposit (W545)
- **Discount margin erosion** — excessive discounting on quotations erodes profitability; mitigated by authorization matrix and monthly margin analysis
- **Low conversion rate** — 45% of quotations expire without conversion; mitigated by proactive follow-up on expiring quotations and post-expiration analysis

### Time Estimate
- Quotation creation: 7–13 min per quotation
- Conversion to sale: 5–10 min per conversion
- Follow-up on expiring quotations: 5 min per quotation
- Monthly analytics review: 30 min/store
- **Total**: ~2,400 hours/month chain-wide (quotation creation + conversion + follow-up)

### Staffing Implication
- No incremental headcount — quotation processing absorbed by existing Sales Associates and Trade Counter staff
- Trade Counter/Pro Desk Associates (per CRM-013) spend ~30% of their time on quotation-related activities

## W543. POS Consignment Sell-Through Transaction Processing & Vendor Settlement Trigger

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer purchases a consignment item at POS; consignment sell-through period settlement |
| **Frequency** | ~42,000 consignment POS transactions/month (~1.5% of POS transactions) |
| **Volume** | ~PHP 378M/month in consignment sales (~7.5% of in-store revenue) |
| **Owner** | Merchandising Manager (consignment terms); Store Manager (POS processing compliance) |
| **Participants** | Cashier (scanning), System (ownership transfer detection), Finance (vendor settlement), Vendor (consignment partner) |

### Background

BuildRight carries consignment inventory from select vendors (large appliance brands, seasonal garden items, specialty tools). Per INV-009/INV-017, consignment items are vendor-owned until sold, at which point ownership transfers to BuildRight and immediately to the customer (sell-through). At POS, the system must distinguish consignment items from owned inventory, apply appropriate pricing, trigger ownership transfer, and accumulate sell-through data for vendor settlement (FIN-018). No existing workflow covers the POS terminal-level processing of consignment transactions.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Consignment item scan at checkout**: cashier scans consignment item barcode; POS system identifies item as consignment from item master flag (MDM — consignment indicator); system displays consignment indicator on cashier screen (discrete — not shown to customer); standard pricing and promotions applied per normal POS rules (W5B) | Cashier / System | — | No incremental time vs. standard scan |
| 2 | **Consignment ownership transfer**: at transaction completion (payment tender accepted), system executes ownership transfer for each consignment line item: (a) vendor-owned consignment inventory reduced by quantity sold, (b) standard owned inventory not affected (no owned stock existed), (c) ownership transfer event published via W533 with consignment flag, (d) COGS entry posted using consignment cost (not standard cost), (e) revenue posted per normal sales transaction | System | — | Automated |
| 3 | **Consignment sell-through data accumulation**: system accumulates daily consignment sell-through data per vendor: (a) item-level sales quantities and values, (b) date and store of sale, (c) transaction reference numbers; data published to vendor settlement accumulation table for FIN-018 processing | System | — | Automated |
| 4 | **Consignment return processing**: when a consignment item is returned (W12), system reverses ownership transfer: (a) inventory returned to vendor-owned consignment stock (not BuildRight owned stock), (b) COGS reversed, (c) revenue reversed per standard return processing, (d) sell-through accumulation adjusted; if item is defective, system flags for vendor RTV per consignment terms rather than standard RTV | System / Cashier | Store Manager | Per W12 |
| 5 | **Consignment pricing exception**: if vendor requests a temporary price change on consignment items (promotional pricing, markdown), pricing update flows through standard price push mechanism (POS-047) with consignment-specific GL posting (vendor-funded discount); Store Manager does not need to distinguish at POS — system handles automatically | System | — | Automated |
| 6 | **Monthly vendor settlement trigger**: at month-end, Finance runs consignment settlement per FIN-018; POS sell-through data accumulated in W543.3 provides the settlement basis; settlement report shared with vendor for reconciliation; AP voucher created for vendor payment (consignment cost × quantity sold − vendor-funded discounts − defectives) | Finance / System | Finance Manager | Per FIN-018 |

### System Touchpoints
- Item master consignment flag (MDM) detection at POS scan (W543.1)
- Ownership transfer event with consignment-specific GL posting (W543.2, W533)
- Consignment sell-through accumulation table (W543.3)
- Consignment return reversal to vendor-owned stock (W543.4, W12)
- Price push integration with consignment GL override (W543.5, POS-047)
- Monthly settlement integration with FIN-018 (W543.6)
- Integration with W5B (checkout — consignment scan), W12 (returns — consignment return), W533 (event streaming — ownership transfer event), INV-009/INV-017 (consignment inventory), FIN-018 (consignment settlement), W252 (item master — consignment flag), W289 (pricing master — consignment pricing), W525 (nightly reconciliation — consignment inventory reconciliation)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Consignment inventory inaccuracy** — if consignment flag is incorrect in item master, ownership transfer posts to wrong GL; mitigated by item master governance (W252) with consignment flag validation at SKU creation
- **Consignment stockout not visible to cashier** — consignment ATP shown same as owned stock; if vendor recalls consignment stock, POS may sell unavailable items; mitigated by real-time consignment inventory deduction and vendor recall processing per W29
- **Settlement dispute** — vendor may dispute sell-through quantities; mitigated by transaction-level sell-through data (W543.3) as evidence
- **Consignment vs. owned confusion at return** — cashier may not distinguish consignment returns; mitigated by system-enforced return routing based on item master flag

### Time Estimate
- Consignment scan at POS: no incremental time vs. standard scan
- Ownership transfer: automated (no cashier action)
- Consignment return: no incremental time vs. standard return (system auto-detects)
- Monthly settlement data preparation: automated per FIN-018
- **Total incremental POS time**: zero — consignment processing is entirely system-driven at POS level

### Staffing Implication
- No incremental headcount at store level — consignment processing is transparent to cashier
- Finance: monthly consignment settlement processing absorbed into existing FIN-018 workflow
- Merchandising: consignment terms management absorbed into existing vendor management

## W544. POS Service Work Order Creation & Scheduling

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer purchases a service (installation, assembly, custom work) at POS alongside a product |
| **Frequency** | ~14,000 service work orders/month (~0.5% of POS transactions) |
| **Volume** | Peak: construction season and holiday gift assembly (Q4) generates 2× service volume |
| **Owner** | Services Manager (scheduling and execution); Store Manager (POS-side coordination) |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate (service sale), Customer, Service Technician (execution), Services Coordinator (scheduling) |

### Background

BuildRight offers value-added services (per SRV-001/WF-services.md) including installation (appliances, fixtures, ceiling fans), assembly (furniture, outdoor equipment), custom work (cut-to-order, paint mixing), and project consultation. When a customer purchases a service at POS, the system must create a service work order, schedule the service with a qualified technician, link the work order to the sales transaction, and track the work order through completion. No existing workflow covers the POS-initiated service work order creation and scheduling process.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Service item selection at POS**: customer selects service alongside product purchase (e.g., ceiling fan + installation); Sales Associate scans service SKU from service catalog (non-stock item per MDM-015); system identifies service SKU type (installation, assembly, custom work, consultation); system displays service description, estimated duration, and price | Sales Associate | — | 2–3 min |
| 2 | **Service scheduling**: system checks technician availability calendar for the store's service zone; presents available date/time slots to customer; customer selects preferred slot; system assigns work order to available qualified technician (skill-matched: electrical for wiring, plumbing for fixtures, etc.); for in-store services (assembly, cutting): scheduled for same-day or next available; for on-site services (installation): scheduled per customer site address and travel zone | Sales Associate / System | — | 3–5 min |
| 3 | **Work order creation**: system generates service work order linked to POS sales transaction: (a) work order number, (b) linked sales transaction and receipt number, (c) product items requiring service, (d) service type and description, (e) customer details and site address (for on-site), (f) scheduled date/time, (g) assigned technician, (h) special instructions or customer notes; work order visible in technician's mobile app and store services dashboard | System | — | Automated |
| 4 | **Payment processing**: service fee collected at POS as part of the sales transaction (prepaid service); for large installation jobs, deposit may be collected with balance on completion per W546 (progress payment); system posts service revenue to deferred revenue (service not yet delivered); on work order completion, revenue recognized per PFRS 15 (FIN-039) | Cashier / System | — | Part of W5B checkout |
| 5 | **Work order confirmation to customer**: system sends work order confirmation to customer via SMS/email: (a) work order number, (b) scheduled date/time, (c) assigned technician name, (d) service description, (e) store contact for rescheduling; customer can reschedule up to 24 hours before scheduled slot | System | — | Automated |
| 6 | **Technician notification**: assigned technician receives work order notification in mobile app: (a) work order details, (b) product details (what to install/assemble), (c) customer site address and contact, (d) estimated duration, (e) special tools or parts needed; technician accepts or flags scheduling conflict | System / Technician | Services Coordinator | Automated + 5 min if conflict |
| 7 | **Work order execution and completion**: technician executes service per standard procedure; on completion, technician marks work order complete in mobile app with: (a) completion timestamp, (b) before/after photos (for installation), (c) customer signature on work order completion form, (d) notes on any issues or additional work needed; system triggers deferred revenue recognition for the service | Technician / System | Services Coordinator | Per service type |
| 8 | **Post-service customer satisfaction**: system sends post-service satisfaction survey via SMS/email (NPS); survey response linked to work order and sales transaction; low scores escalated to Services Manager per CRM-011 (feedback-to-action) | System / Customer | Services Coordinator | Automated |

### System Touchpoints
- Service SKU catalog (non-stock items per MDM-015) at POS scan (W544.1)
- Technician availability calendar with skill matching (W544.2)
- Work order creation linked to POS sales transaction (W544.3)
- Deferred revenue posting for prepaid services with PFRS 15 recognition (W544.4, FIN-039)
- Customer notification system (W544.5, W528 delivery infrastructure)
- Technician mobile app integration (W544.6–7)
- Post-service NPS survey (W544.8, CRM-011)
- Integration with W5B (checkout — service item scan), W546 (progress payment — large service deposits), W112 (trade counter — trade service orders), SRV-001 (service lifecycle), W292 (employee master — technician skills), FIN-039 (revenue recognition — deferred service revenue), CRM-011 (feedback — post-service survey), MDM-015 (non-stock item master — service SKU)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Technician no-show** — technician fails to arrive for scheduled service; mitigated by automated reminder 24 hours before, backup technician assignment, and customer rescheduling
- **Service scope dispute** — customer expects more than what was sold (e.g., installation fee covers mounting only, customer expects wiring); mitigated by clear service description on quotation and receipt
- **Product-service mismatch** — customer buys wrong service for product (e.g., installation for non-compatible model); mitigated by system validation of product-service compatibility at scan
- **Deferred revenue tracking complexity** — services paid but not yet delivered create deferred revenue liability; mitigated by automated PFRS 15 recognition on work order completion

### Time Estimate
- Service selection and scheduling at POS: 5–8 min per transaction
- Work order creation: automated
- Technician notification and conflict resolution: 5 min per conflict (~10% of work orders)
- Post-service processing: automated
- Services Coordinator scheduling oversight: ~1–2 hours/day per store
- **Total per store per day**: ~2–3 hours for service-related activities

### Staffing Implication
- **Services Coordinator**: 1 per high-volume store (~50 stores), 1 per 2–3 stores for lower-volume locations; may be combined with existing Trade Counter Associate role
- Service Technicians: per SRV-001 staffing plan; not incremental to this workflow

## W545. POS Special Order & Customer Order Processing

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer at POS requests an item that is out of stock at current store but available in the supply chain (DC, another store, or upcoming replenishment) |
| **Frequency** | ~28,000 special orders/month (~1% of POS transactions) |
| **Volume** | Peak: during promotional stockouts, special order volume can triple |
| **Owner** | Store Manager (special order compliance); Order Fulfillment (supply chain side) |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate (order creation), Customer, Stock Associate (receiving and staging), Store Manager (exception handling) |

### Background

When a customer at POS wants an item that is out of stock at the current store, several options exist: (a) endless aisle vendor direct-ship (W273), (b) BOPIS from another store (W22 inter-store transfer), or (c) a special order — the item is ordered from DC replenishment or upcoming purchase order for delivery to the store, with the customer notified upon arrival. This third option (special order) is different from W273 (endless aisle is vendor-direct to customer) and W75 (layaway is for in-stock items held for payment). Special orders require deposit collection, order tracking, customer notification, and deposit-to-sale conversion upon item arrival.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Special order identification**: during checkout or inquiry, customer requests item not available at store; Sales Associate checks ATP across supply chain (W536.2): (a) available at another store → offer BOPIS from that store, (b) available at DC → offer special order with DC delivery, (c) not available anywhere → check upcoming PO expected delivery date; if customer selects special order, proceed to step 2 | Sales Associate / System | — | 3–5 min |
| 2 | **Special order creation**: Sales Associate creates special order at POS: (a) customer details (name, phone, email — required for notification), (b) item(s) with quantities, (c) expected availability date (from ATP or PO ETA), (d) preferred pickup store (current store or other), (e) deposit amount (configurable: 0%, 20%, 50%, or 100% — default 20% for items ≤ PHP 10,000, 50% for items > PHP 10,000) | Sales Associate | — | 5–8 min |
| 3 | **Deposit collection**: system processes deposit payment via standard POS tender (W5B.7); deposit receipt printed with "SPECIAL ORDER — DEPOSIT" label, expected availability date, and remaining balance; deposit posted to customer deposit liability (not revenue); inventory allocated to this special order (excluded from ATP for other customers) | Cashier / System | — | 3–5 min |
| 4 | **Supply chain fulfillment**: special order transmitted to DC or procurement via W533 event stream; DC picks and ships to designated store per standard replenishment (W4); if item requires procurement, PO created per PUR requirements with expedited flag; customer-visible tracking status updated in real-time | System / DC Staff | Order Fulfillment | Per W4 or PUR cycle |
| 5 | **Customer notification**: upon item arrival at store (W109 store receiving), system automatically sends customer notification: (a) SMS/email with item ready for pickup, (b) store address and operating hours, (c) remaining balance amount, (d) pickup deadline (configurable, default 14 days from notification); if customer enrolled in loyalty, notification via app as well | System | — | Automated |
| 6 | **Customer pickup and balance collection**: customer arrives at store; Sales Associate recalls special order by order number or customer phone; system verifies customer identity; Stock Associate retrieves item from special order staging area; system processes balance payment (remaining amount − deposit); full sales transaction completed (W5B) with deposit applied as tender; ownership transfers, revenue recognized | Sales Associate / Cashier | — | 5–10 min |
| 7 | **Pickup deadline and cancellation**: if customer does not pick up within deadline (default 14 days): (a) system sends reminder at day 7 and day 12, (b) at day 14, order auto-cancelled, (c) deposit refunded per cancellation policy (configurable: full refund, less processing fee, or forfeited), (d) inventory released back to saleable ATP, (e) cancellation notification sent to customer | System / Store Manager | Store Manager | Automated + 5 min per cancellation |
| 8 | **Special order analytics**: monthly special order report: (a) order count and fill rate by store, (b) average fulfillment time, (c) cancellation rate and reason, (d) deposit forfeiture revenue, (e) items most frequently special-ordered (input for assortment review W1) | System / Store Manager | — | Monthly automated + 15 min review |

### System Touchpoints
- Multi-origin ATP check at POS (W545.1, W536.2)
- Special order creation with deposit management (W545.2–3)
- Customer deposit liability GL posting (W545.3)
- Inventory allocation and ATP exclusion for special orders (W545.3, INV-019)
- Supply chain fulfillment via event stream (W545.4, W533, W4)
- Automated customer notification on item arrival (W545.5)
- Deposit-to-sale conversion at pickup (W545.6, W5B)
- Pickup deadline management with auto-cancellation (W545.7)
- Integration with W5B (checkout — deposit and balance payment), W536 (order management — ATP and fulfillment), W273 (endless aisle — alternative for vendor-direct items), W75 (layaway — related but different payment model), W22 (inter-store transfer — BOPIS alternative), W4 (replenishment — DC fulfillment), W109 (store receiving — item arrival trigger), W533 (event stream — order transmission), FIN-039 (revenue recognition — deposit accounting)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Fulfillment delay** — customer expects item by estimated date but supply chain delays occur; mitigated by realistic ETA communication and proactive delay notification
- **Customer no-show for pickup** — item occupies staging space and inventory is locked; mitigated by 14-day deadline with auto-release and deposit policy
- **Deposit accounting complexity** — deposits held as liability until pickup; monthly aging review required; mitigated by automated deposit aging report
- **Special order item damaged in transit** — item arrives damaged from DC; mitigated by per W500 (damage claim) and customer communication for re-order or refund

### Time Estimate
- Special order creation at POS: 8–13 min per order
- Customer pickup and balance collection: 5–10 min per pickup
- Cancellation processing: 5 min per cancellation (~10% of orders)
- Monthly analytics review: 15 min/store
- **Total per store per day**: ~30–45 min (based on ~140 special orders/month/store ≈ 5/day)

### Staffing Implication
- No incremental headcount — special order processing absorbed by existing Sales Associates
- Stock Associate: staging and retrieval adds ~15 min/day absorbed into existing duties
- Order Fulfillment team: special order volume (~28,000/month) is a small fraction of total DC throughput

---

## W546. POS Deposit & Progress Payment Collection for Project Orders

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | B2B/project customer places a large order at POS or trade counter with payment in installments tied to delivery milestones |
| **Frequency** | ~2,000 project orders/month requiring progress payments (~0.07% of POS transactions) |
| **Volume** | Average project order value: ~PHP 150,000; total ~PHP 300M/month in project order revenue |
| **Owner** | Trade Account Manager (project setup); Store Manager (POS deposit collection) |
| **Participants** | Trade Account Manager, Customer (contractor/builder), Cashier (deposit processing), Finance (payment tracking) |

### Background

BuildRight's B2B and project customers (contractors, developers, institutional buyers per CRM-003/CRM-004) frequently place large material orders (PHP 50,000–2,000,000) that are delivered in phases corresponding to construction milestones. Payment is collected in progress installments: an initial deposit (30–50%), intermediate payments on delivery of each phase, and a final payment on completion. This is distinct from layaway (W75 — consumer installment for in-stock items) and special orders (W545 — single delivery with single deposit). Progress payment collection at POS requires project order setup, milestone tracking, deposit allocation, and balance monitoring.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Project order setup**: Trade Account Manager creates project order in ERP (or at trade counter POS) with: (a) project name and reference, (b) customer trade account, (c) total order value, (d) delivery phases with item lists and quantities per phase, (e) payment milestones (deposit %, interim payment %, final payment %), (f) delivery addresses and dates per phase; system generates project order number | Trade Account Manager | Store Manager | 15–30 min |
| 2 | **Deposit collection at POS**: customer pays initial deposit at POS or trade counter; cashier recalls project order by project order number; system displays deposit amount due; cashier processes deposit via standard POS tender (W5B.7); receipt prints "PROJECT ORDER — DEPOSIT" with project order number, deposit amount, total order value, and remaining balance; deposit posted to customer deposit liability GL | Cashier / System | Store Manager | 5–10 min |
| 3 | **Phase delivery and interim payment**: as each delivery phase is executed (W19 home delivery or W109 store pickup), system generates interim payment due notification; customer pays interim amount at POS (or via bank transfer for trade accounts); system links payment to project order and specific delivery phase; inventory released per delivery phase; system updates project order balance | Cashier / System | — | 5–10 min per payment |
| 4 | **Final payment and project close**: upon final delivery phase completion, system generates final payment due notification; customer pays remaining balance; system completes project order: (a) full revenue recognized, (b) all inventory fully transferred, (c) project order status set to "Completed", (d) final invoice generated per BIR requirements, (e) project order archived for warranty reference (W33) | Cashier / System | Store Manager / Finance | 5–10 min |
| 5 | **Balance monitoring and aging**: Finance monitors project order balances: (a) overdue progress payments flagged, (b) payment aging report per project order, (c) collection escalation per FIN-028 for overdue payments, (d) project order holds on future deliveries if payment overdue > 30 days | Finance / System | Finance Manager | Weekly review ~2 hours |
| 6 | **Project order modification**: if project scope changes (additional items, quantity changes, delivery date changes): Trade Account Manager modifies project order; system recalculates payment milestones; additional deposit collected if order value increases; Store Manager approval required for modifications > 20% of original order value | Trade Account Manager / Store Manager | Store Manager | 10–20 min per modification |

### System Touchpoints
- Project order creation with milestone-based payment schedule (W546.1)
- POS deposit collection linked to project order (W546.2, W5B)
- Interim payment processing linked to delivery phases (W546.3, W19, W109)
- Final payment and project close with full revenue recognition (W546.4, FIN-039)
- Balance monitoring with aging and collection escalation (W546.5, FIN-028)
- Integration with W5B (checkout — payment processing), W19 (home delivery — phase delivery), W109 (store receiving — phase pickup), W542 (quotation — project order may originate from quotation), W545 (special order — single-delivery variant), W75 (layaway — consumer installment variant), CRM-003 (trade account — B2B customer), CRM-013 (trade counter — pro desk), FIN-028 (collection — overdue escalation), FIN-039 (revenue recognition — milestone-based)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Payment default on large project** — customer fails to pay interim or final payment on PHP 500K+ order; mitigated by deposit requirements, delivery holds for overdue payments, and trade credit insurance
- **Scope change pricing disputes** — project modifications may lead to pricing disagreements; mitigated by documented change orders with customer sign-off
- **Revenue recognition timing** — PFRS 15 requires performance obligation analysis for milestone-based payments; mitigated by automated recognition tied to delivery completion
- **Inventory commitment** — project order reserves inventory that could sell to walk-in customers; mitigated by ATP allocation rules (INV-019) with project order reservation capped at configurable % of store inventory

### Time Estimate
- Project order setup: 15–30 min per order
- Each deposit/progress payment at POS: 5–10 min
- Balance monitoring: 2 hours/week for Finance
- **Total per store per month**: ~5–8 hours (based on ~10 project orders/month/store)

### Staffing Implication
- Trade Account Manager: project order setup absorbed into existing B2B account management
- Cashier: deposit/progress payment collection absorbed into existing checkout operations
- Finance: balance monitoring absorbed into existing FIN-028 collection process

## W547. POS Scan & Go / Mobile Self-Scan Checkout

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer initiates scan-and-go session in BuildRight mobile app upon store entry |
| **Frequency** | ~42,000 scan-and-go transactions/month (~1.5% of POS transactions); expected to grow to 5% within 2 years |
| **Volume** | Peak: weekends and sale events generate 3× scan-and-go volume |
| **Owner** | Digital Commerce Manager (feature owner); Store Manager (in-store compliance) |
| **Participants** | Customer (self-scanning), Scan-and-Go Attendant (verification), Store Manager (exception handling) |

### Background

Scan & Go allows customers to use the BuildRight mobile app to scan item barcodes as they shop, building a virtual basket in real-time. When ready to pay, the customer proceeds to a dedicated Scan & Go quick-pay terminal (or pays directly in the app for a completely contactless experience). This is distinct from SCO (W516 — self-checkout station where customer scans at the terminal) and mPOS (W206 — staff-operated mobile POS for queue busting). Scan & Go reduces checkout time to under 60 seconds, decreases queue length, and improves customer experience. Common in modern retail globally and increasingly adopted in Philippine retail.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Session initiation**: customer opens BuildRight app in-store; app detects store location via GPS/beacon; customer taps "Scan & Go" to start session; system creates scan-and-go session linked to customer loyalty ID and store; customer grants camera access for barcode scanning | Customer / System | — | 30 sec |
| 2 | **Item scanning**: customer scans item barcodes using phone camera as they shop; system validates each scan: (a) item exists in active SKU catalog, (b) item is available for sale (not recalled, not discontinued), (c) price displayed from local data store (POS-043); for catch-weight items (POS-016): app prompts customer to weigh item on store scale and enter weight; for age-restricted items (POS-029): app flags item for attendant verification at checkout | Customer / System | — | 5–10 sec per item |
| 3 | **Basket management**: customer can view basket in app at any time: item list, quantities, running total, applied promotions (auto-applied per POS-014); customer can remove items (returns to shelf), change quantities, or scan additional items; real-time inventory deduction reserved (not finalized) | Customer / System | — | Continuous |
| 4 | **Quick-pay at dedicated terminal**: customer proceeds to Scan & Go quick-pay terminal (dedicated lane, no traditional checkout); customer scans app QR code at terminal; terminal loads customer's virtual basket; system performs final validation: (a) all items still in stock (real-time ATP check), (b) pricing still current, (c) promotions still valid; customer selects payment method (card, e-wallet, loyalty points W550); payment processed; receipt printed or e-receipt delivered (W528) | Customer / System | — | 30–60 sec |
| 5 | **In-app payment (contactless)**: alternatively, customer pays directly in app (no terminal needed); payment processed via stored payment method; digital receipt delivered to app; system generates exit QR code; customer presents exit QR to attendant at store exit for verification | Customer / System | — | 30 sec |
| 6 | **Attendant verification**: for transactions containing age-restricted items or exceeding random audit threshold (~10% of transactions): attendant scans exit QR or receipt barcode; system displays items flagged for verification; attendant verifies age-restricted items (ID check per POS-029) or performs random basket check; attendant confirms verification in app | Attendant | — | 1–3 min |
| 7 | **Skip-scan and weight verification**: system cross-references scanned items with expected weight ranges; if basket weight (measured at exit scale or estimated from item weights) deviates > 15% from expected: system flags for attendant verification; LP exception alert triggered per W538 | System / Attendant | Store Manager | Per alert |
| 8 | **End-of-day reconciliation**: at store close (W5F), system generates Scan & Go session reconciliation: (a) total sessions initiated vs. completed vs. abandoned, (b) average basket size and value (compared to standard POS), (c) payment method distribution, (d) exception rate (age-restricted, random audit, weight flag), (e) conversion rate; reconciliation report transmitted via W533 | System / Store Manager | — | Automated + 5 min review |

### System Touchpoints
- BuildRight mobile app with barcode scanning, store detection, and basket management (W547.1–3)
- Dedicated Scan & Go quick-pay terminal with QR code reader (W547.4)
- In-app payment processing with exit QR generation (W547.5)
- Attendant verification station with exit QR scanner (W547.6)
- Weight verification and skip-scan detection (W547.7, W538)
- EOD Scan & Go reconciliation (W547.8, W533, W5F)
- Integration with W516 (SCO — shared infrastructure), W206 (mPOS — shared mobile architecture), W528 (digital receipt), W538 (LP monitoring — skip-scan detection), W550 (loyalty points as tender), POS-029 (age-restricted verification), POS-016 (catch-weight item handling), POS-043 (local data store for price lookup), W533 (event stream — transaction publishing)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Skip-scan fraud** — customer intentionally fails to scan items; mitigated by weight verification, random audits, and LP monitoring (W538); industry shrinkage rate for scan-and-go: 3–5% (vs. 1.5% staffed checkout)
- **Customer technology barrier** — not all customers have smartphones or are comfortable with app-based shopping; mitigated by keeping traditional checkout lanes available
- **App reliability** — app crashes or scanning failures frustrate customers; mitigated by offline scanning capability and basket recovery on app restart
- **Age-restricted item compliance** — customer may attempt to purchase restricted items without verification; mitigated by mandatory attendant verification flag and POS-029 compliance

### Time Estimate
- Session initiation: 30 sec
- Per-item scanning: 5–10 sec (absorbed into shopping time — no incremental checkout time)
- Quick-pay or in-app payment: 30–60 sec
- Attendant verification: 1–3 min (for flagged transactions only, ~15%)
- **Total customer time**: ~1–2 min at checkout (vs. 5–8 min standard checkout)
- **Store labor saving**: ~5 min per transaction shifted from cashier to system

### Staffing Implication
- **Scan & Go Attendant**: 1 per store during operating hours (combined with SCO Attendant role); primarily verification and customer assistance
- IT/Digital: mobile app development and maintenance absorbed into existing Digital Commerce team
- Net labor reduction: ~3 min/transaction × 42,000 transactions/month = ~2,100 hours/month saved from cashier time

---

## W548. POS Third-Party On-Demand Delivery Integration

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer at POS requests same-day or on-demand delivery via third-party courier (Grab, Lalamove, Angkas, Borzo) |
| **Frequency** | ~14,000 on-demand deliveries/month (~0.5% of POS transactions) |
| **Volume** | Peak: weekends and rainy days generate 2× on-demand delivery demand |
| **Owner** | Store Manager (POS-side coordination); Logistics (3PL relationship management per W62B) |
| **Participants** | Cashier (order creation), Customer, On-Demand Courier, Stock Associate (staging) |

### Background

While W5D covers store-level customer delivery scheduling (for bulky items via BuildRight's own delivery fleet) and W273 covers endless aisle vendor-direct delivery, neither covers the growing demand for on-demand, same-hour delivery via third-party courier platforms (Grab, Lalamove, Angkas, Borzo). Customers increasingly expect the convenience of purchasing in-store and having items delivered to their home or job site within hours, especially for medium-weight items that are inconvenient to carry (bags of cement, paint gallons, tool sets). This workflow covers POS-initiated third-party on-demand delivery.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **On-demand delivery request at POS**: at checkout, customer requests delivery instead of carry-out; cashier selects "On-Demand Delivery" option in POS; system prompts for delivery address (customer input or loyalty profile address); system displays delivery fee estimate based on distance zone; customer confirms delivery | Cashier / Customer | — | 2–3 min |
| 2 | **Courier platform API call**: POS system (or store associate via integrated dispatch portal) sends delivery request to configured on-demand courier platform API: (a) pickup location (store address + contact), (b) delivery address, (c) item description and estimated weight, (d) required vehicle type (motorcycle for small items, car/SUV for medium, van/truck for bulky), (e) special instructions (fragile, heavy lifting); platform confirms driver assignment and ETA | System / Cashier | — | 1–2 min |
| 3 | **Payment processing**: customer pays for merchandise via standard POS tender (W5B.7); delivery fee added as separate line item on receipt; delivery fee collected by BuildRight and remitted to courier platform (or customer pays courier directly — configurable per platform); sale completes and inventory deducted | Cashier / System | — | Part of W5B checkout |
| 4 | **Item staging and handoff**: Stock Associate stages items at designated pickup area; courier arrives per platform ETA; Stock Associate verifies courier identity (platform-provided name, vehicle, and OTP from customer); Stock Associate hands items to courier; system logs handoff timestamp and courier details | Stock Associate / Courier | Store Manager | 5–10 min |
| 5 | **Delivery tracking**: customer receives real-time delivery tracking via platform app; BuildRight system receives delivery status updates via platform API webhook: (a) courier en route, (b) courier arrived at customer, (c) delivery completed with proof-of-delivery photo; delivery status visible in customer's BuildRight order history | System / Courier | — | Automated |
| 6 | **Delivery exception handling**: if delivery fails (customer not available, wrong address, item damaged in transit): (a) platform attempts redelivery or returns items to store, (b) Stock Associate receives returned items and restocks, (c) delivery fee refund processed per platform policy, (d) merchandise refund per standard return policy (W12) if customer requests; Store Manager reviews exception | Store Manager / Stock Associate | Store Manager | 10–15 min per exception |
| 7 | **Delivery settlement reconciliation**: weekly, Finance reconciles on-demand delivery fees: (a) delivery fees collected from customers vs. fees charged by courier platforms, (b) platform commission reconciliation, (c) failed delivery refunds, (d) GL posting for delivery fee revenue and courier expense; settlement report generated per platform | Finance / System | Finance Manager | Weekly batch ~2 hours |

### System Touchpoints
- On-demand courier platform API integration (Grab, Lalamove, Angkas, Borzo) (W548.2)
- POS delivery option with address input and fee calculation (W548.1)
- Delivery fee as POS line item with GL posting (W548.3)
- Platform webhook integration for delivery status (W548.5)
- Delivery fee settlement reconciliation (W548.7, W99)
- Integration with W5B (checkout — delivery option), W5D (delivery scheduling — BuildRight fleet alternative), W273 (endless aisle — vendor-direct alternative), W62B (3PL management — platform relationship), W438 (yard loading — carry-out alternative), W109 (store receiving — returned item restocking), W99 (payment settlement — fee reconciliation), W254 (location master — store address), W309 (banking partner master — platform payment configuration)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Courier reliability** — third-party courier availability varies by location and time; mitigated by multi-platform integration (Grab + Lalamove + Angkas) for redundancy
- **Item damage in transit** — courier mishandles items; mitigated by proper packaging at staging, delivery insurance via platform, and damage claim process
- **Delivery fee margin** — courier fees may exceed delivery fee charged to customer; mitigated by dynamic fee calculation based on distance and weight
- **Rural store limitation** — on-demand courier platforms may not serve all 200 store locations, especially in rural Mindanao and Visayas; mitigated by BuildRight own-fleet delivery (W5D) for unserved areas

### Time Estimate
- Delivery request at POS: 2–3 min per transaction
- Courier dispatch: 1–2 min (automated API)
- Item staging and handoff: 5–10 min per delivery
- Exception handling: 10–15 min per exception (~5% of deliveries)
- Weekly settlement reconciliation: ~2 hours
- **Total per store per day**: ~30–45 min (based on ~70 deliveries/month/store ≈ 2–3/day)

### Staffing Implication
- No incremental headcount — on-demand delivery processing absorbed by existing cashiers and Stock Associates
- Finance: weekly reconciliation (~2 hours) absorbed into existing payment settlement duties
- IT: courier platform API integration and maintenance absorbed into existing integration team
