## W549. POS Damaged & Open-Box Item Discount Processing

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer brings a damaged, dented, or open-box item to checkout and requests a discount; or Store Manager proactively marks damaged items for discounted sale |
| **Frequency** | ~8,400 damaged-item transactions/month (~0.3% of POS transactions) |
| **Volume** | Higher in stores with high foot traffic and self-serve format (customers damage items on shelf) |
| **Owner** | Store Manager (discount authorization and condition assessment) |
| **Participants** | Cashier (processing), Customer, Store Manager (assessment and approval), Stock Associate (removal and replacement) |

### Background

In a self-serve hardware retail environment, items frequently get damaged on the shelf: dented paint cans, torn bags of cement, scratched laminates, opened boxes with missing parts, chipped tiles. These items are distinct from demo/display units (W524 — floor models intentionally placed for demonstration) and clearance items (W532 — end-of-life or excess inventory marked for final sale). Damaged/open-box items are still functionally usable but cosmetically impaired, and customers often request a discount at checkout. A structured workflow ensures consistent discounting, proper customer disclosure, and inventory tracking of shrinkage from damage.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Damage identification at checkout**: customer brings damaged item to cashier; or cashier notices damage during scan; cashier alerts Store Manager for condition assessment | Cashier | — | 1 min |
| 2 | **Condition assessment**: Store Manager inspects item and classifies condition: (a) **Minor cosmetic** (dent, scratch, scuff — fully functional): 10–20% discount, (b) **Moderate damage** (torn packaging, missing non-essential parts): 20–35% discount, (c) **Significant damage** (missing essential parts, heavily dented, but repairable): 35–50% discount, (d) **Unsellable** (safety hazard, non-functional): refused for sale, removed to damage inventory per W91 | Store Manager | — | 2–3 min |
| 3 | **Discount application**: Store Manager authorizes damage discount per condition tier; cashier applies discount with reason code "DAMAGED" in POS; system requires manager authorization (biometric or badge scan); discount shown as separate line on receipt; customer verbal confirmation that item is sold "as-is" with disclosed condition | Cashier / Store Manager | Store Manager | 1–2 min |
| 4 | **Modified warranty disclosure**: system prints modified warranty terms on receipt: (a) damage-discounted items carry reduced warranty (default: 30 days functional warranty vs. standard warranty), (b) receipt prints "AS-IS / DAMAGED ITEM — REDUCED WARRANTY", (c) specific damage noted on receipt (e.g., "Dented can — cosmetic only"), (d) standard return policy may be restricted per damage type; customer acknowledgment captured (digital signature or verbal confirmation logged) | System / Cashier | Store Manager | 1 min |
| 5 | **Shrinkage and damage tracking**: system logs damage-discount transaction with: (a) original item cost, (b) discount amount (shrinkage), (c) condition tier, (d) damage cause (if known: customer-caused, shelf-damage, transit-damage), (e) Store Manager authorization; damage shrinkage posted to shrinkage GL account; data feeds into monthly damage report and W248 (inventory variance investigation) | System | — | Automated |
| 6 | **Shelf replacement trigger**: if item removed from shelf due to damage, system triggers replenishment alert: (a) if identical item in backstock, Stock Associate retrieves replacement, (b) if no backstock, replenishment request sent to DC per W4; shelf gap tracked until replacement placed | System / Stock Associate | — | Per W109 |

### Damage Discount Matrix (Configurable)

| Condition Tier | Discount Range | Warranty | Return Policy | Authorization |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Minor cosmetic | 10–20% | Standard warranty | Standard return | Dept Supervisor |
| Moderate damage | 20–35% | 90-day functional | Exchange only | Store Manager |
| Significant damage | 35–50% | 30-day functional | No returns | Store Manager |
| Unsellable | N/A (refused) | N/A | N/A | Removed per W91 |

### System Touchpoints
- Damage discount reason code with condition tier selection (W549.2–3)
- Manager authorization required for damage discounts (W549.3, POS-006)
- Modified warranty and return restriction on receipt (W549.4)
- Shrinkage GL posting with damage categorization (W549.5)
- Replenishment trigger for shelf replacement (W549.6, W4)
- Integration with W5B (checkout — damage discount scan), W524 (demo/display — similar but planned discount), W532 (clearance — similar but different trigger), W91 (damaged goods — unsellable removal), W248 (inventory variance — shrinkage tracking), W33 (warranty — modified terms), W12 (returns — restricted return processing), W4 (replenishment — shelf replacement), POS-006 (price override — discount authorization)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Discount abuse** — cashiers or customers may deliberately damage items to get discounts; mitigated by manager authorization, condition documentation, and LP monitoring (W538)
- **Customer dissatisfaction with restricted returns** — customer buys damaged item, later wants full return; mitigated by clear receipt disclosure and verbal confirmation at sale
- **Shrinkage under-reporting** — minor damage discounts may not be logged consistently; mitigated by system-enforced reason code and manager authorization
- **Safety liability** — selling damaged electrical or structural items creates safety risk; mitigated by safety-critical item exclusion from damage discounting (configurable by SKU category)

### Time Estimate
- Damage assessment and discount: 4–6 min per occurrence
- Modified warranty disclosure: 1 min
- Shelf replacement: per W109
- **Total per store per day**: ~15–25 min (based on ~42 damaged items/month/store ≈ 1–2/day)

### Staffing Implication
- No incremental headcount — absorbed by existing Store Manager and cashier roles
- Monthly damage report review: 15 min/store absorbed into existing W67 store performance review

---

## W550. POS Loyalty Points as Payment Tender

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer at POS chooses to redeem loyalty points as partial or full payment |
| **Frequency** | ~28,000 points-as-tender transactions/month (~1% of POS transactions) |
| **Volume** | Average points redemption: ~PHP 450 per transaction; total ~PHP 12.6M/month in points tender |
| **Owner** | CRM/Loyalty Manager (program rules); Store Manager (POS compliance) |
| **Participants** | Cashier (processing), Customer, System (validation and redemption) |

### Background

While POS-005 and W5B.5 cover loyalty integration (points earning at POS), and W17 covers loyalty program operations, no workflow details the process of using loyalty points as a payment method at checkout. BuildRight's loyalty program (CRM-001, CRM-005) allows members to accumulate points and redeem them as currency at POS. With ~480,000 active loyalty members generating ~28,000 points-as-tender transactions/month, this requires a structured POS workflow for validation, conversion, partial redemption, and deferred revenue recognition.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Customer identifies loyalty membership**: customer presents loyalty card, mobile app QR, or provides registered phone number; cashier scans or enters identifier; system retrieves loyalty account: points balance, tier status, eligible points for redemption (minimum threshold, e.g., 100 points = PHP 10) | Cashier / Customer | — | 15–30 sec |
| 2 | **Points-to-currency conversion display**: system displays available points balance and equivalent currency value on cashier and customer-facing screens; conversion rate per program rules (configurable, default: 10 points = PHP 1); system shows maximum amount payable with points | System | — | Automated |
| 3 | **Customer selects points redemption amount**: customer chooses to redeem: (a) full balance (up to transaction total), (b) specific amount, or (c) specific points quantity; system validates: (a) minimum redemption threshold met, (b) sufficient points available, (c) points not frozen (under investigation per W496 loyalty fraud), (d) within single-transaction redemption cap (configurable, default: PHP 5,000/transaction) | Customer / Cashier / System | — | 30 sec |
| 4 | **Points tender processing**: system applies points redemption as tender line: (a) points deducted from loyalty account balance in real-time, (b) points redemption event published via W533, (c) receipt shows points redeemed, equivalent currency value, and remaining points balance, (d) remaining transaction balance (if any) payable via other tender methods (cash, card, e-wallet — standard multi-tender per POS-004) | System / Cashier | — | 10–15 sec |
| 5 | **Deferred revenue recognition**: system posts points redemption to deferred revenue reversal: (a) loyalty points were accrued as deferred revenue liability when earned (FIN-039, PFRS 15), (b) at redemption, deferred revenue reversed and recognized as revenue reduction, (c) GL posting: DR Loyalty Deferred Revenue / CR Sales Revenue; loyalty liability reduced on balance sheet | System | — | Automated |
| 6 | **Offline points redemption**: if POS is offline (W535): system allows points redemption from cached loyalty balance (per offline capability matrix — degraded mode with floor limit, default PHP 1,000); points deducted locally and queued for sync; on reconnection, points reconciliation per W5G resolves any conflicts (e.g., points spent at another store during offline period) | System | — | Per W535 |
| 7 | **Points redemption reversal on return**: if transaction containing points redemption is returned (W12): system reverses points redemption — points restored to customer's account; deferred revenue re-accrued; if partial return, proportional points restored | System | — | Per W12 |

### System Touchpoints
- Loyalty account lookup via card scan, QR scan, or phone number (W550.1, W17)
- Points-to-currency conversion engine with configurable rate (W550.2, CRM-001)
- Points redemption as POS tender line (W550.4, POS-004 multi-tender)
- Deferred revenue recognition integration (W550.5, FIN-039, W104)
- Offline points redemption with floor limit (W550.6, W535)
- Return reversal with points restoration (W550.7, W12)
- Integration with W5B (checkout — points tender), W17 (loyalty operations), W533 (event stream — points redemption event), W535 (offline — cached balance), W12 (returns — points reversal), W104 (loyalty financial governance), CRM-001 (loyalty engine), CRM-005 (tiered loyalty), FIN-039 (revenue recognition), W496 (loyalty fraud — frozen points), POS-004 (multi-tender — points as one tender)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Points balance inaccuracy during offline** — customer may overspend cached points; mitigated by floor limit and reconciliation per W5G
- **Deferred revenue tracking complexity** — each points redemption triggers deferred revenue reversal; high volume requires automated posting; mitigated by real-time event-driven processing
- **Customer confusion on points value** — customers may not understand conversion rate; mitigated by clear display on customer-facing screen and receipt
- **Fraud risk** — unauthorized account access for points redemption; mitigated by PIN/biometric verification for redemptions > PHP 1,000 and W496 fraud monitoring

### Time Estimate
- Loyalty identification: 15–30 sec
- Points selection and redemption: 30–45 sec
- **Total incremental time per transaction**: ~1 min (vs. ~5–8 min standard checkout = 12–20% longer for points transactions)
- No incremental time for deferred revenue posting (automated)

### Staffing Implication
- No incremental headcount — points-as-tender processing absorbed by existing cashiers
- CRM/Loyalty team: points conversion rate management and deferred revenue monitoring absorbed into existing CRM operations

---

## W551. POS Customer On-the-Spot Loyalty Enrollment & Account Lookup

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Unidentified customer at POS wants to join loyalty program, check points balance, or update account details during checkout |
| **Frequency** | ~42,000 on-the-spot enrollments/month (~1.5% of POS transactions); ~84,000 account lookups/month (~3%) |
| **Volume** | Peak: during loyalty double-points events, enrollment spikes 5× |
| **Owner** | CRM/Loyalty Manager (program enrollment); Store Manager (POS compliance) |
| **Participants** | Cashier (enrollment/lookup), Customer, System (enrollment processing) |

### Background

CRM-016 covers the loyalty enrollment journey across channels, but the POS-specific quick enrollment workflow is not detailed. At checkout, unenrolled customers may decide to join when they see the loyalty benefits displayed, or existing members may need to check their balance, update phone number, or retrieve a forgotten card. A fast, non-disruptive enrollment/lookup process at POS is critical — if enrollment takes more than 60 seconds, it delays the queue and both cashier and customer abandon the attempt. This workflow covers the POS-lane quick enrollment and account lookup process optimized for speed.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Enrollment trigger**: during checkout, cashier asks "Are you a BuildRight Rewards member?"; if customer says no but wants to join, cashier taps "Quick Enroll" on POS; if customer is a member but forgot card, cashier taps "Account Lookup" | Cashier | — | 10 sec |
| 2 | **Quick enrollment — minimum data capture**: system prompts for minimum required data: (a) mobile number (primary identifier — validated via OTP sent to phone), (b) first name, (c) last name, (d) consent checkbox (RA 10173 Data Privacy Act per NFR-010); customer enters on customer-facing display (PIN pad or tablet) for privacy; no cashier data entry needed — customer self-enters | Customer / System | — | 30–45 sec |
| 3 | **OTP verification**: system sends 6-digit OTP to customer's mobile number; customer enters OTP on customer-facing display; system verifies; if OTP fails after 3 attempts, enrollment deferred to Customer Service counter (not blocking POS lane) | Customer / System | — | 15–30 sec |
| 4 | **Account creation and digital card issuance**: system creates loyalty account: (a) generates member ID, (b) issues digital loyalty card (visible in app or SMS with barcode), (c) registers at Bronze tier (CRM-005), (d) applies welcome bonus points (if current promotion), (e) immediately applies to current transaction — points earned on this purchase; customer receives SMS confirmation with member ID and app download link | System | — | Automated |
| 5 | **Account lookup (existing member)**: if customer is existing member but forgot card/phone: cashier enters customer name or alternate identifier; system searches: (a) by registered mobile number, (b) by name + ZIP code, (c) by transaction history (if customer used same credit card previously); system displays matching accounts (masked for privacy); customer confirms identity on customer-facing display; account linked to current transaction | Cashier / Customer / System | — | 30–60 sec |
| 6 | **Quick account update**: if customer needs to update information (new phone number, email for e-receipt): customer enters update on customer-facing display; system validates and updates with audit trail; update applied to current transaction | Customer / System | — | 30 sec |
| 7 | **Enrollment analytics**: daily enrollment count by store and cashier; enrollment conversion rate (enrollment offers vs. acceptances); new member first-purchase value vs. average; 30-day new member retention rate; data feeds into W263 (enrollment journey analytics) | System | — | Automated |

### System Touchpoints
- POS quick-enrollment module with customer-facing display input (W551.2)
- OTP verification via SMS gateway (W551.3)
- Loyalty account creation with digital card issuance (W551.4, W17, CRM-001)
- Account lookup with privacy-masked search (W551.5, W253 customer dedup)
- Customer-facing display for self-service data entry (W551.2–3, POS-011)
- Integration with W5B (checkout — enrollment trigger), W17 (loyalty operations — account creation), W263 (enrollment journey — channel tracking), CRM-001 (loyalty engine — account setup), CRM-005 (tiered loyalty — initial tier), CRM-016 (enrollment journey — POS channel), W528 (e-receipt — email collection), NFR-010 (data privacy — RA 10173 consent), POS-011 (customer display — self-service input), W253 (customer master governance — dedup check)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Queue delay** — enrollment process adds 45–90 sec to checkout; mitigated by customer self-entry on customer-facing display and strict time cap (auto-defer to Customer Service if > 90 sec)
- **Duplicate account creation** — customer may already have account under different phone number; mitigated by dedup check (W253) at enrollment using name + phone matching
- **OTP delivery failure** — SMS OTP may not arrive due to network issues; mitigated by 3-retry and fallback to Customer Service counter
- **Data privacy compliance** — capturing customer data at POS must comply with RA 10173; mitigated by mandatory consent checkbox and minimal data collection

### Time Estimate
- Quick enrollment: 45–75 sec total (data entry + OTP + account creation)
- Account lookup: 30–60 sec
- Quick update: 30 sec
- **Target**: enrollment adds < 90 sec to checkout; if exceeded, deferred to Customer Service
- **Total incremental checkout time**: ~1,575 hours/month chain-wide (45,000 lookups + enrollments × ~1.75 min avg)

### Staffing Implication
- No incremental headcount — enrollment and lookup absorbed by existing cashiers
- CRM team: enrollment analytics review absorbed into existing W263 reporting
- Customer Service counter: handles deferred enrollments (~5% of attempts = ~2,100/month ≈ 70/day across chain ≈ ~20 min/day absorbed)

## W552. POS Donation & Charity Round-Up Processing

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | POS system prompts for donation round-up after tender selection; customer opts in |
| **Frequency** | ~140,000 donation transactions/month (~5% of POS transactions opt in) |
| **Volume** | Average donation: ~PHP 7 (round-up from PHP 1,793 to PHP 1,800); total ~PHP 980K/month |
| **Owner** | Corporate Communications (charity partnerships); Store Manager (POS compliance) |
| **Participants** | Cashier (prompting), Customer, Charity Partner, Finance (donation remittance) |

### Background

Philippine retail commonly offers customers the option to round up their transaction total or add a fixed donation amount at checkout for charitable causes. BuildRight partners with selected charities (e.g., Habitat for Humanity Philippines, Philippine Red Cross, local community rebuilding efforts post-typhoon) for seasonal and ongoing donation campaigns. This is a corporate social responsibility initiative (aligned with GOV-008 ESG governance) that requires POS-level processing: prompt display, donation capture, receipt documentation, fund segregation, and charity remittance.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Donation prompt at checkout**: after tender is processed (W5B.7) and change is calculated, system displays donation prompt on customer-facing screen: "Round up to PHP [next even amount] for [Charity Name]? Your donation: PHP [round-up amount]"; customer taps "Yes" or "No Thanks" on customer-facing display; if customer-facing display not available, cashier verbally prompts | System / Customer | — | 5–10 sec |
| 2 | **Donation amount options**: customer selects from: (a) round-up (default — rounds to nearest PHP 10 or PHP 100), (b) fixed amount buttons (PHP 10, PHP 25, PHP 50, PHP 100), (c) custom amount; selected donation added as separate line item on receipt labeled "Donation — [Charity Name]" | Customer / System | — | 5 sec |
| 3 | **Donation receipt and documentation**: receipt prints donation line item with: (a) charity name and registration number, (b) donation amount, (c) BuildRight corporate donation reference; donation is NOT tax-deductible to customer (no BIR tax credit); for customer records, receipt serves as proof of donation; system does not charge VAT on donation amount | System | — | Part of receipt printing |
| 4 | **Fund segregation**: system posts donation amount to segregated charity liability account (not revenue); donations accumulated per charity partner per store per period; donations excluded from store revenue and sales metrics (W67 store performance) to prevent donation-inflated revenue reporting | System | — | Automated |
| 5 | **Charity remittance**: monthly (or per campaign end), Finance remits accumulated donations to charity partner: (a) total donation amount per charity, (b) store-level breakdown, (c) transaction count, (d) official donation receipt from charity for BuildRight records; remittance per BIR donation documentation requirements (corporate donor) | Finance | Finance Manager | Monthly batch ~4 hours |
| 6 | **Campaign management**: Corporate Communications configures donation campaigns: (a) active charity partner(s), (b) campaign start/end dates, (c) round-up rules, (d) fixed amount options, (e) campaign messaging on customer display; multiple campaigns can be active simultaneously (one per region or cause); campaign pushed to POS terminals via W533 price/content push mechanism | Corp Communications / System | — | Per campaign setup |
| 7 | **Donation analytics**: weekly donation dashboard: (a) total donations by charity and store, (b) opt-in rate (% of transactions with donation), (c) average donation amount, (d) campaign performance vs. target; monthly donation report for ESG reporting (GOV-008) and corporate communications | System | — | Automated |

### System Touchpoints
- Donation prompt on customer-facing display (W552.1, POS-011)
- Donation line item on receipt with charity reference (W552.3, POS-012)
- Segregated charity liability GL posting (W552.4)
- Campaign configuration and POS push (W552.6, W533)
- Charity remittance with BIR documentation (W552.5, FIN)
- ESG reporting integration (W552.7, GOV-008)
- Integration with W5B (checkout — donation prompt after tender), W528 (e-receipt — donation in digital receipt), W533 (event stream — donation event), W5F (EOD — donation summary), W67 (store performance — donation exclusion), GOV-008 (ESG — charitable giving reporting), POS-011 (customer display — prompt interface), POS-012 (receipt printing — donation line)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Customer annoyance** — frequent donation prompts may annoy some customers; mitigated by prompt only once per transaction, easy "No Thanks" option, and configurable prompt frequency (e.g., skip every other transaction for same customer)
- **Opt-in rate variability** — some stores/cashiers may have much lower opt-in rates; mitigated by cashier training (W518) and analytics visibility
- **Charity fund segregation error** — donations incorrectly posted as revenue; mitigated by system-enforced GL routing for donation line items
- **BIR documentation for corporate donations** — BuildRight needs official charity receipts for its own tax deduction; mitigated by formal charity partnership agreements and monthly remittance documentation

### Time Estimate
- Donation prompt and selection: 5–15 sec per transaction (only for opted-in customers)
- No incremental time for non-opting customers
- Monthly charity remittance: ~4 hours
- **Total incremental per store per day**: ~15–20 min (5% of ~467 transactions = ~23 donation transactions × ~10 sec = ~4 min; cashier verbal prompting adds ~15 min)
- **Total chain-wide per month**: ~2,500 hours

### Staffing Implication
- No incremental headcount — donation processing absorbed by existing cashiers
- Corporate Communications: campaign management absorbed into existing CSR activities
- Finance: monthly remittance (~4 hours) absorbed into existing AP duties

## W553. POS Pricing Error Detection & Immediate Correction at Checkout

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer or cashier detects discrepancy between displayed/advertised price and POS-scanned price at checkout |
| **Frequency** | ~8,400 price discrepancy events/month (~0.3% of POS transactions) |
| **Volume** | Higher during promotional changeover periods (price file updates) and after planogram resets |
| **Owner** | Store Manager (immediate resolution); Pricing Manager (root cause correction) |
| **Participants** | Cashier (detection), Customer, Store Manager (authorization), Stock Associate (shelf label correction) |

### Background

Price discrepancies between shelf labels, promotional signage, and POS-scanned prices occur regularly in high-SKU retail environments. With 35,000 active SKUs and frequent promotional changeovers, BuildRight experiences price discrepancies from: (a) shelf labels not updated after price changes (W40), (b) promotional pricing not yet propagated to POS (sync delay), (c) planogram reset moving items to wrong shelf with wrong label (W86), (d) DTI price freeze activation lag (W468). While W69 covers periodic price compliance audits and W5B.11 mentions price dispute handling, no workflow details the immediate correction process at checkout — verifying the correct price, adjusting for the customer, flagging the root cause, and correcting the shelf label. Prompt resolution is critical for customer satisfaction and DTI consumer protection compliance (RA 7394).

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Price discrepancy detection**: at scan, system displays POS price; customer or cashier notices discrepancy with shelf label or advertised price; cashier pauses transaction and calls Store Manager | Cashier | — | 30 sec |
| 2 | **Price verification**: Store Manager verifies correct price by checking: (a) POS system price (from price master W289 — source of truth), (b) shelf label price, (c) promotional signage if applicable, (d) current promotional calendar (W13), (e) DTI price freeze status (W468) if in calamity-declared area; Store Manager determines which price is correct per policy: (a) if POS price is correct and shelf label is wrong → charge POS price, fix label, (b) if shelf label shows lower price (customer was mislead) → honor shelf price per DTI consumer protection (RA 7394), (c) if promotional price should be active but not yet applied → apply promo price immediately and flag price file sync issue | Store Manager | — | 2–3 min |
| 3 | **Immediate price adjustment**: Store Manager authorizes price override per POS-006 (price override with authorization); cashier applies corrected price with reason code "PRICE-DISCREPANCY"; system logs: (a) original POS price, (b) corrected price, (c) difference, (d) reason code, (e) authorizer, (f) shelf label or promotional signage reference; transaction continues with corrected price | Cashier / Store Manager | Store Manager | 1 min |
| 4 | **Shelf label flagging**: system automatically generates shelf label correction task: (a) item SKU, (b) store location, (c) correct price, (d) discrepancy type (wrong label, missing label, promo not reflected); task assigned to Stock Associate for immediate correction; high-priority items (front-page promo, endcap display) flagged for immediate correction; all corrections completed before next peak shopping period | System / Stock Associate | Store Manager | Stock Associate: 3–5 min per label |
| 5 | **Root cause logging**: Store Manager classifies root cause: (a) label not updated after price change (process gap in W40), (b) promotional pricing sync delay (W533 latency), (c) item placed on wrong shelf (W86 planogram issue), (d) DTI price freeze not yet propagated (W468), (e) pricing master error (W289), (f) other; root cause data feeds into weekly price compliance dashboard for W69 | Store Manager / System | — | 30 sec |
| 6 | **Batch price verification trigger**: if same SKU has > 3 discrepancy events across stores within 24 hours: system triggers enterprise-wide price verification for that SKU; Pricing Manager notified; price file integrity check initiated; if systemic, corrected price pushed to all terminals via POS-047 within 60 seconds | System / Pricing Manager | Pricing Manager | Automated alert + 15–30 min investigation |

### System Touchpoints
- POS price display at scan with customer-facing screen comparison (W553.1, POS-011)
- Price verification via price master lookup (W553.2, W289)
- Price override with reason code and logging (W553.3, POS-006)
- Automated shelf label correction task generation (W553.4)
- Root cause classification and W69 dashboard feed (W553.5)
- Enterprise-wide price verification trigger (W553.6, POS-047)
- Integration with W5B (checkout — price display), W40 (price changes — label update gap), W13 (promotions — promo pricing source), W69 (price compliance audit — root cause data), W86 (planogram — shelf placement), W181 (shelf label printing — correction label), W289 (pricing master — source of truth), W468 (DTI price freeze — freeze price source), POS-006 (price override — authorization), POS-047 (price push — enterprise correction), W533 (event stream — discrepancy event)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Customer wait during verification** — 2–3 min verification delays checkout; mitigated by allowing customer to continue shopping and return, or moving to customer service counter
- **DTI consumer liability** — under RA 7394, selling at higher price than advertised/shelf price is a violation; mitigated by honoring lower price and immediate shelf correction
- **Price override abuse** — cashiers may claim "price discrepancy" to apply unauthorized discounts; mitigated by mandatory manager authorization and root cause logging with analytics
- **Systemic pricing errors** — if price file has incorrect prices, all stores affected; mitigated by enterprise-wide trigger at > 3 same-SKU discrepancies (W553.6)

### Time Estimate
- Price discrepancy detection and cashier pause: 30 sec
- Store Manager verification: 2–3 min
- Price adjustment and logging: 1 min
- Root cause logging: 30 sec
- Shelf label correction: 3–5 min per label (Stock Associate)
- **Total per occurrence**: 4–5 min at checkout + 3–5 min shelf correction = 7–10 min
- **Total per store per day**: ~10–15 min (based on ~42 discrepancies/month/store ≈ 1–2/day)

### Staffing Implication
- No incremental headcount — absorbed by existing Store Manager, cashier, and Stock Associate roles
- Pricing Manager: enterprise-wide trigger investigation (~15–30 min per trigger, estimated 2–3 triggers/week)
- W69 price compliance audit: root cause data from this workflow feeds into and improves existing price compliance process

## W554. Store-Level Daily Shelf Replenishment & Restocking

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Store opening / daily restocking schedule generated overnight by ERP |
| **Frequency** | Daily at all 200 stores |
| **Volume** | ~2,000–3,000 shelf replenishment tasks per store per day across 8 departments (lumber, plumbing, electrical, tiles, paint, tools, hardware, garden) |
| **Owner** | Stock Associate / Department Lead |
| **Participants** | Stock Associate (R), Department Supervisor (A), Store Manager (I), System (task generation) |

### Background

W4 covers DC-to-store replenishment calculation and transfer execution. W109 covers store-level receiving and backroom putaway. W86 covers monthly planogram compliance audits. However, there is no workflow covering the daily operational execution of moving products from the backroom/stockroom to the sales floor shelves. This is the most frequent store-level activity — every morning, Stock Associates systematically restock shelves based on overnight sales data, receiving activity, and planogram guidance.

For a big-box hardware retailer with 35,000 SKUs and 8,000–15,000 sqm selling areas, efficient shelf replenishment is critical to on-shelf availability and customer satisfaction. Hardware retail presents unique challenges: heavy and bulky items (cement bags, lumber, plywood sheets) require two-person handling and specialized equipment (pallet jacks, forklifts for yard areas); catch-weight bulk items (nails, screws by kilo) require gravity bin refills from bulk containers; and seasonal items (garden supplies, Christmas decor) create capacity overflow that exceeds standard shelf allocations.

Without a structured daily replenishment workflow, out-of-shelf conditions persist undetected, customer satisfaction declines, and the gap between perpetual inventory (backroom) and shelf availability widens — leading to phantom inventory where the system shows stock but the shelf is empty.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **System generates replenishment task list**: overnight ERP calculates replenishment priorities from: (a) POS real-time sales deductions (POS-013) since last replenishment, (b) yesterday's receiving putaway (W109) creating new backroom stock, (c) min-shelf-quantity rules per SKU per planogram position, (d) known out-of-shelf conditions flagged by prior day's walk (W573); system ranks tasks by department, aisle sequence, and priority (OOS = critical, below min = high, below target = standard); task list pushed to Stock Associate handheld devices before store opening | System | — | Automated (overnight batch) |
| 2 | **Stock Associate reviews prioritized task list on handheld device**: at store opening, Stock Associate logs into handheld, reviews assigned department task list sorted by priority; confirms task acceptance and estimated completion time; flags any tasks requiring two-person handling (cement bags, plywood, large tiles) for coordination with second Stock Associate | Stock Associate | Department Supervisor | 5–10 min |
| 3 | **Pull pallets/cases from backroom staging area**: Stock Associate proceeds to backroom; locates staged pallets/cases per task list (organized by department from W109 putaway); scans bin location barcode to confirm correct product; verifies product condition (no damage, no water exposure for yard-accessible backroom areas); loads pallet jack or push cart with task-assigned quantities | Stock Associate | — | 15–20 min per trip |
| 4 | **Transport to department floor location**: Stock Associate transports loaded pallet jack/cart from backroom through stockroom door to assigned department aisle; navigates around customer traffic (store is open or opening); for heavy items requiring forklift (yard area lumber/plywood): only certified operators per W171 safety protocol; secures pallet/cart in designated staging spot in aisle (not blocking customer path) | Stock Associate | — | 5–10 min per trip |
| 5 | **Shelf replenishment per planogram position**: Stock Associate places product on shelf per planogram: (a) check planogram image on handheld for correct position, (b) forward-fill existing stock to front of shelf (facing), (c) place new stock behind existing (FIFO — first in, first out for most items), (d) for FEFO items (paint, chemicals, adhesives with expiry): rotate stock to place earliest expiry at front, (e) for heavy items (cement, tiles): stack safely within weight limits per shelf rating label; verify shelf capacity not exceeded | Stock Associate | — | 2–5 min per SKU position |
| 6 | **Update bin/shelf status on handheld**: after each SKU position is replenished, Stock Associate confirms completion on handheld: (a) scans shelf barcode / enters position ID, (b) confirms quantity placed, (c) system updates perpetual inventory (INV-001) from backroom to floor location, (d) system marks task as complete; remaining backroom quantity displayed for next replenishment cycle | Stock Associate | — | 30–60 sec per SKU |
| 7 | **Flag out-of-shelf conditions for Department Supervisor**: if Stock Associate finds an out-of-shelf condition with no backroom stock: (a) flags "OOS — No Backroom Stock" on handheld, (b) system checks nearby stores for inter-store transfer availability, (c) system checks incoming DC shipment schedule, (d) if no replenishment source within 48 hours: system suggests product substitute facing per W279 to avoid empty shelf gap | Stock Associate | Department Supervisor | 1–2 min per flag |
| 8 | **Handle catch-weight/bulk items — refill gravity bins**: for bulk hardware items (nails, screws, bolts, nuts by kilo): (a) retrieve bulk container from backroom stock, (b) weigh out quantity per gravity bin capacity, (c) refill gravity bin display, (d) update catch-weight inventory (W463) on handheld — record weight added, (e) verify bin label matches product and correct price per kilo; for paint mixing station supplies: restock tint cans, mixing cups, stir sticks per W463 catch-weight protocol | Stock Associate | — | 10–15 min per bulk bin refill |
| 9 | **Yard items — restack and organize outdoor displays**: for outdoor yard items (lumber, cement, hollow blocks, steel bars, PVC pipes): (a) restack displaced items from customer handling, (b) reorganize display stacks to stable configuration, (c) cover exposed items with tarps if rain expected, (d) sweep and clear debris from yard aisles, (e) verify yard bin location labels are visible and intact; coordinate with Yard Loader (W438) for heavy repositioning | Stock Associate | — | 20–30 min per yard section |
| 10 | **Report damaged shelf labels for reprint**: during replenishment, Stock Associate identifies damaged, missing, or illegible shelf labels: (a) scan product barcode, (b) tap "Damaged Label" on handheld, (c) system generates label reprint task routed to W181 shelf label printing queue; priority given to promotional items and front-aisle positions | Stock Associate | — | 30 sec per label |
| 11 | **End-of-replenishment cycle confirmation**: Stock Associate completes all assigned tasks on handheld; system displays summary: (a) tasks completed vs. assigned, (b) unfilled items (OOS with no backroom stock), (c) time spent; Stock Associate confirms cycle completion; unfilled items auto-flagged for: (a) DC replenishment order (W4), (b) inter-store transfer request, or (c) backorder status with estimated availability date | Stock Associate | Department Supervisor | 5 min |
| 12 | **Department Supervisor spot-checks replenishment quality**: Department Supervisor walks 3–5 randomly selected aisle sections; verifies: (a) product correctly placed per planogram, (b) FIFO/FEFO rotation compliance, (c) facing quality (items pulled to shelf edge), (d) shelf not overloaded beyond weight rating, (e) no damaged products placed on shelf; documents exceptions on handheld; exceptions feed into W86 monthly planogram audit and W573 daily shelf walk | Department Supervisor | — | 15–20 min |

### System Touchpoints
- POS-013 (real-time inventory deduction feeds replenishment tasks)
- POS-066 (new requirement for shelf replenishment operations)
- INV-001 (perpetual inventory — backroom-to-floor location transfer)
- MDM-023 (planogram template master — shelf position and min-quantity rules)
- W4 (DC-to-store replenishment — replenishment order trigger for OOS items)
- W86 (planogram compliance — monthly audit partner)
- W109 (store receiving — inbound feed for new backroom stock)
- W181 (shelf label printing — damaged label triggers)
- W279 (product substitution — substitute facing guidance for extended OOS)
- W463 (catch-weight processing — bulk bin refill tracking)
- W438 (yard dispatch — heavy item yard coordination)
- W573 (daily planogram execution — replenishment quality feed)

### Time Estimate
- Per Stock Associate per morning: 2–3 hours (task review 10 min + 3–4 replenishment trips × 30 min + bulk/yard handling 30 min + cycle confirmation 5 min)
- Department Supervisor spot-check: 15–20 min
- **Total per store per day**: 4 Stock Associates × 2.5 hours = 10 person-hours + Supervisor 20 min = ~10.3 person-hours
- **Total chain-wide daily**: 200 stores × 10.3 hours = ~2,067 person-hours daily
- **Total chain-wide monthly**: ~2,067 × 30 = ~62,000 person-hours/month

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Seasonal SKU overflow exceeds shelf capacity** — during peak seasons (holiday decor, garden supplies), allocated shelf space is insufficient for seasonal assortment; mitigated by overflow display pallets in power aisles and temporary endcap allocations approved by Store Manager
- **Heavy/bulky items require two-person handling** — cement bags (40 kg), plywood sheets (4×8 ft), large tile boxes require two Stock Associates or mechanical assist; mitigated by scheduling two-person tasks in first hour before store opening
- **Outdoor yard items exposed to rain require daily re-stacking** — tropical climate means frequent rainfall; yard items (cement, hollow blocks, lumber) absorb water or become unstable; mitigated by tarp protocol and covered yard sections where feasible
- **Paint mixing station supplies need specialized handling** — tint cans, base paints, and mixing chemicals have FEFO and hazardous material handling requirements; mitigated by dedicated paint Stock Associate with HAZMAT training
- **Catch-weight bulk bin refills are time-intensive** — nails, screws, bolts require weighing, bin refilling, and inventory adjustment per unit; mitigated by pre-weighed bulk bags from DC where possible

### Staffing Implication
- Covered by existing 4 Stock Associates per store; no incremental headcount needed
- During peak seasons (Q4 holiday, summer garden), temporary Stock Associate augmentation may be needed (absorbed by existing seasonal staffing model per W16)

---

## W559. Store-Level Non-Emergency Incident & Hazard Reporting

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer or employee reports non-emergency incident (minor slip, product spill, tripping hazard, property damage, broken fixture, parking lot issue) |
| **Frequency** | ~400–600 incidents per month across 200 stores (~2–3 per store per month) |
| **Volume** | Higher during rainy season (June–August, slip-and-fall), holiday peak (November–December, crowd incidents) |
| **Owner** | Store Manager / Safety Officer (Department Supervisor as delegate) |
| **Participants** | Reporting Person (R), Department Supervisor (R), Store Manager (A), Safety Officer (C), LP Officer (I), HR (I for employee incidents) |

### Background

W140 covers occupational health and safety (OHS) incidents that are DOLE-reportable and employee-focused. W330 covers emergency response for life-threatening situations (fire, earthquake, bomb threat). W285 covers public liability claims management. W501 covers first aid and medical emergencies. However, there is no workflow covering the daily non-emergency incident and hazard reporting that happens routinely across 200 stores — minor customer slip-and-fall without injury, product spill on aisle, tripping hazard from loose tiles, parking lot pothole, broken display fixture, water leak from ceiling.

These incidents are not emergencies but must be documented for liability protection, pattern analysis, and preventive maintenance. Philippine Consumer Act (RA 7394) and civil liability under the Civil Code require documentation of premises-related incidents. Without structured documentation, repeat hazards go unaddressed, minor incidents escalate into liability claims, and patterns (e.g., same store has 5 slip-and-fall events in rainy season) are not detected for corrective action.

For a hardware retailer with heavy merchandise, construction materials in yard areas, and high customer foot traffic including children accompanying parents, the risk of non-emergency incidents is elevated compared to typical retail. Loose nails, spilled cement dust, wet floors from garden hose area overspray, and tripping hazards from pallet jacks in aisles are daily occurrences that need systematic capture and remediation.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Incident/hazard observed or reported**: customer reports incident to nearest employee (e.g., "I slipped on a wet floor in aisle 3"); employee reports hazard during routine walk (e.g., Stock Associate notices water leak from ceiling); or LP Officer observes during daily routine (W562); reporting person notifies Department Supervisor immediately via radio or in person | Reporting Person / Employee | — | 1–2 min |
| 2 | **Department Supervisor secures the area**: Supervisor immediately proceeds to location; places safety cone/barrier or "Wet Floor" sign to prevent further incidents; for spills: uses absorbent material from spill kit; for falling hazards (loose ceiling tile, unstable display): cordons off area with caution tape; assigns employee to stand guard if area cannot be physically blocked; ensures no customers or staff enter hazard zone | Department Supervisor | — | 2–3 min |
| 3 | **Supervisor documents incident on mobile form**: Supervisor opens Incident Report form on handheld/tablet: (a) date/time (auto-populated), (b) store ID and location within store (aisle, department, yard, parking lot — selected from dropdown), (c) nature of incident (dropdown: slip/trip/fall, product spill, falling object, equipment malfunction, property damage, safety hazard, near-miss, other), (d) persons involved (name, contact info, customer vs. employee), (e) witness names and contact info, (f) narrative description (free text), (g) photo capture (minimum 2 photos: overview of scene + close-up of hazard/damage); form auto-saves as draft | Department Supervisor | — | 5–10 min |
| 4 | **For customer incidents — offer assistance and document**: if incident involves a customer: (a) offer immediate assistance ("Are you okay? Can I get you water/a chair?"), (b) if customer appears injured — activate W501 first aid protocol (do NOT attempt to provide first aid beyond basic comfort unless certified first-aider), (c) if customer declines medical attention: document customer statement ("Customer states they are not injured and decline medical attention"), (d) provide incident report number for customer's records, (e) offer to contact family member if needed, (f) do NOT admit liability or say "it's our fault" — use neutral language ("We're sorry this happened; let me help you") | Department Supervisor | Store Manager | 5–10 min |
| 5 | **For hazards — assess severity and remediate or schedule maintenance**: Supervisor assesses hazard severity: (a) Immediate risk to safety (e.g., exposed electrical wiring, structural damage) → remediate immediately or close area; (b) Moderate risk (e.g., loose tile, water leak, broken shelf) → schedule maintenance work order via W47 with target completion within 24 hours; (c) Low risk (e.g., minor cosmetic damage, faded signage) → schedule maintenance per normal W47 queue; for all hazard levels: hazard remains secured/barriered until remediation complete | Department Supervisor | Store Manager | 3–5 min (assessment) |
| 6 | **For property damage — document and determine liability**: if incident involves property damage (customer dropped and broke merchandise, forklift damaged shelf unit, vehicle hit parking lot barrier): (a) photograph damage from multiple angles, (b) estimate replacement/repair cost using system price lookup, (c) determine liability: customer-caused (customer offered option to pay for damage), store-caused (standard maintenance), third-party (vendor, contractor — refer to vendor agreement), (d) document liability determination in incident report | Department Supervisor | Store Manager | 5–10 min |
| 7 | **Submit incident report in ERP**: Supervisor completes and submits incident report form; system auto-classifies into category: (a) Customer Incident — Injury, (b) Customer Incident — No Injury, (c) Employee Incident, (d) Property Damage, (e) Safety Hazard, (f) Near-Miss, (g) Parking Lot / Exterior; system assigns unique incident ID; incident record stored in store-level incident database; notification pushed to Store Manager for review | Department Supervisor / System | Store Manager | 2–3 min |
| 8 | **Store Manager reviews and classifies severity**: Store Manager reviews submitted incident report; classifies severity: (a) Minor — no injury, minor property damage < PHP 5,000, hazard easily remediated; (b) Moderate — minor injury treated on-site (W501), property damage PHP 5,000–50,000, hazard requiring maintenance work order; (c) Major — injury requiring off-site medical attention, property damage > PHP 50,000, structural hazard, repeat incident at same location; Store Manager adds review notes and severity classification | Store Manager | — | 3–5 min |
| 9 | **Moderate/Major incidents forwarded to Regional LP and Safety Officer**: for Moderate and Major classifications: (a) system auto-notifies Regional LP Officer and Regional Safety Officer via email and push notification, (b) incident report with photos available in their dashboard, (c) Regional Safety Officer reviews within 24 hours for Major and 72 hours for Moderate, (d) if incident involves employee injury: HR notified automatically for DOLE reporting assessment (per W140 threshold), (e) if incident may result in liability claim: LP Officer flags for W285 public liability claims pre-registration | System / Store Manager | Safety Officer / LP Officer | Notification: automated; Review: 15–30 min per incident |
| 10 | **System generates corrective action task if maintenance needed**: if incident requires facility maintenance: (a) system auto-generates work order in W47 facility maintenance module, (b) work order linked to incident ID for traceability, (c) priority set based on incident severity (Major = emergency maintenance, Moderate = 24-hour SLA, Minor = normal queue), (d) maintenance completion updates incident record status; for recurring incidents at same location: system flags pattern for Root Cause Analysis assignment | System | — | Automated |
| 11 | **Monthly incident summary report by store and category**: system generates monthly summary: (a) total incidents by store, (b) breakdown by category and severity, (c) top 10 stores by incident count, (d) repeat incident locations (same aisle/area flagged 2+ times), (e) average time-to-remediation for hazard-related incidents, (f) comparison to prior month and same month prior year; report distributed to Store Manager, Regional Manager, Safety Officer, and LP team | System | — | Automated (monthly batch report) |
| 12 | **Trend analysis feeding into preventive programs**: incident data feeds into: (a) W503 pest control and sanitation — spill pattern analysis for sanitation frequency adjustment, (b) W47 facility maintenance — recurring maintenance issues for capital improvement planning, (c) W140 OHS — employee incident trend for safety program improvement, (d) W285 liability claims — incident-to-claim conversion rate for insurance assessment, (e) Store performance scorecard (W67) — incident count as safety KPI; quarterly trend review by Regional Safety Officer | System / Safety Officer | Regional Safety Officer | Quarterly review: 2–3 hours |

### System Touchpoints
- POS-067 (new requirement for store incident reporting — mobile incident form, photo capture, classification engine)
- GOV-004 (public liability insurance — incident data for claims and premium assessment)
- W47 (facility maintenance — work order generation for hazard remediation)
- W140 (OHS incidents — escalation path for employee injuries and DOLE reporting)
- W330 (emergency response — escalation for incident upgrade to emergency)
- W285 (public liability claims — data source for claim filing)
- W501 (first aid — medical response partner for injury incidents)
- W503 (pest control/sanitation — spill pattern analysis)
- W67 (store performance review — safety KPI from incident data)
- W562 (LP daily routine — hazard observation during LP walkthrough)

### Time Estimate
- Per incident: 15–30 min (area securing 3 min + documentation 10 min + submission 3 min + Store Manager review 5 min)
- Major incidents: additional 30–60 min for Regional review and follow-up
- **Total per store per month**: ~2.5 incidents × 20 min average = ~50 min/month
- **Total chain-wide per month**: ~500 incidents × 20 min average = ~167 person-hours/month
- **Monthly reporting and trend analysis**: ~2 hours/month (Safety Officer)
- **Quarterly trend review**: ~3 hours/quarter (Regional Safety Officer)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Underreporting of minor incidents due to paperwork burden** — Department Supervisors may skip documentation for "small" hazards to avoid 15-minute form completion; mitigated by streamlined mobile form with photo-first design and dropdown-heavy (minimal typing) interface
- **Delayed hazard remediation leads to repeat incidents** — maintenance work orders (W47) may not be completed within SLA, leaving hazard barriers in place for extended periods and increasing repeat incident probability; mitigated by SLA tracking and escalation for overdue work orders
- **Inconsistent classification across stores** — different Store Managers may classify similar incidents at different severity levels; mitigated by classification guidelines with examples distributed quarterly and calibration sessions during Regional Manager meetings
- **Photo evidence quality varies** — poorly lit backroom/yard photos, blurry images from shaking hands in stressful situations; mitigated by handheld camera auto-focus requirement and minimum 2-photo rule (overview + close-up)
- **Language barriers with documentation** — incident narratives may be written in local language (Cebuano, Ilonggo, Bisaya) and not understood by National Safety Officer; mitigated by standardized dropdown categories and bilingual form templates

### Staffing Implication
- Covered by existing Store Manager and Department Supervisors; no incremental headcount needed
- Regional Safety Officer: incident review absorbed into existing safety responsibilities (~2–3 hours/month)
- LP Officer: incident observation during W562 daily routine absorbed into existing duties

## W562. Store-Level Loss Prevention Daily Routine

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Store opening (LP daily checklist initiation) |
| **Frequency** | Daily at all 200 stores |
| **Volume** | 200 daily checklists completed |
| **Owner** | LP Officer / Store Manager (stores without dedicated LP Officer) |
| **Participants** | LP Officer (R), Store Manager (A), Cashiers (C), Stock Associates (C) |

### Background

W37 covers loss prevention exception reporting and investigation. W248 covers store inventory variance and LP investigation. W466 covers the comprehensive Loss Prevention & Asset Protection (LPAP) program. W538 covers real-time POS exception monitoring and alert response. However, there is no workflow covering the daily operational LP routine — the morning checklist, EAS gate testing, blind-spot monitoring, fitting area checks, high-value product verification, till spot-checks, and end-of-day LP summary.

For a 200-store chain with a shrinkage target of less than 1.5%, consistent daily LP execution is critical. The Philippine retail environment faces unique challenges: organized retail crime (ORC) rings targeting hardware tools and premium items, internal theft by staff (especially in stores with low LP coverage), and vendor fraud during DSD receiving. Without a standardized daily LP checklist, LP activities become ad hoc — some stores have diligent LP Officers while others (without dedicated LP staff) neglect daily LP tasks entirely.

The rotational LP model (20 Regional LP Officers covering 200 stores, each officer visiting ~10 stores on rotation) means that most stores are without a dedicated LP Officer on any given day. On non-visit days, the Store Manager serves as LP proxy. A standardized daily checklist ensures consistent LP execution regardless of whether an LP Officer is physically present.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **LP Officer opens LP dashboard on tablet at store opening**: LP Officer (or Store Manager on non-visit days) logs into LP module on tablet; dashboard displays: (a) today's checklist items, (b) overnight exception alerts from W538 (voids, no-sales, refund patterns, sweet-hearting indicators), (c) pending investigation items from W248, (d) previous day's checklist completion status; Officer acknowledges dashboard and begins daily routine | LP Officer / Store Manager | Store Manager | 5 min |
| 2 | **EAS gate power-on test and functionality verification**: at each store entrance/exit with EAS (Electronic Article Surveillance) gates: (a) power on EAS system, (b) test each gate with test tag (walk through with tagged item — verify alarm triggers), (c) test deactivation pad at POS (scan tagged item through — verify alarm does NOT trigger), (d) record pass/fail per gate in checklist; if gate fails: log maintenance request via W47, implement manual bag-check procedure for affected entrance until repaired | LP Officer | — | 5–10 min |
| 3 | **High-value product cage/locked display integrity check**: verify security of high-value merchandise: (a) power tool cage — verify lock engaged, key accounted for, inventory count matches expected (spot-check 5 items against system), (b) locked display cases (premium appliances, high-end power tools, specialty hardware) — verify locks engaged, glass intact, item count correct, (c) key log review — verify all high-value key sign-outs from prior day are returned and logged; discrepancies flagged for immediate investigation per W248 | LP Officer | — | 10–15 min |
| 4 | **Review overnight exception alerts from W538**: review POS exception alerts generated overnight and from previous evening: (a) high-value voids (void > PHP 5,000 after 8 PM), (b) excessive no-sale drawer opens (> 3 per cashier per shift), (c) refund patterns (same cashier processing multiple refunds), (d) sweet-hearting indicators (repeated small-quantity scans of expensive items), (e) employee discount abuse patterns; prioritize alerts for follow-up investigation; flag top 3 alerts for Cashier performance discussion (W522) | LP Officer | Store Manager | 10–15 min |
| 5 | **Check CCTV camera coverage — verify all cameras online**: access CCTV system (W71); verify all cameras showing live feed: (a) entrance/exit cameras, (b) POS lane cameras, (c) backroom/receiving door cameras, (d) high-value product cage camera, (e) parking lot cameras; for any offline cameras: log IT helpdesk ticket per W48 with priority "Security System"; document offline camera locations and estimated blind-spot coverage gap; adjust physical patrol frequency for areas with offline cameras | LP Officer | — | 5–10 min |
| 6 | **Blind-spot walkthrough — areas not covered by CCTV**: walk areas with known CCTV blind spots: (a) restrooms and vestibule areas, (b) fitting/trial areas for workwear/PPE, (c) lumber yard corners and behind rack structures, (d) garden center rear sections, (e) receiving dock when not in active DSD receiving; look for: concealed merchandise, discarded packaging (evidence of in-store consumption or tag-switching), unauthorized access points; document observations in checklist | LP Officer | — | 10–15 min |
| 7 | **Fitting area / trial room check**: verify fitting area procedures: (a) item count sign-in/sign-out board compliance — are associates counting items in and out?, (b) check trial rooms for concealed merchandise or discarded security tags, (c) verify EAS-detag only happens at POS (not in fitting area), (d) assess whether fitting area associate is actively monitoring; document compliance level | LP Officer | — | 5 min |
| 8 | **DSD receiving observation — spot-check vendor deliveries**: if DSD receiving is occurring during visit: observe 1–2 deliveries per W18 for: (a) vendor identification verification, (b) item count vs. delivery receipt match, (c) quality inspection compliance, (d) short-shipment indicators (seal intact but count low, substitution without authorization), (e) vendor access restricted to receiving area only; if irregularities observed: escalate per W248 investigation protocol | LP Officer | — | 10–15 min (if DSD occurring) |
| 9 | **Mid-day till spot-check on 1 cashier**: select 1 cashier for unannounced till spot-check: (a) pause cashier's current transaction, (b) perform cash count of drawer, (c) compare physical cash to system expected amount per POS cash tracking, (d) variance > PHP 200 (over or short): document per W272 cashier over/short protocol, (e) variance within tolerance: record as "Pass" in checklist; rotate cashier selection daily to cover all cashiers within 2-week cycle | LP Officer / Store Manager | Store Manager | 5–10 min |
| 10 | **Afternoon high-value shelf count spot-check**: select 5–10 high-value SKUs from tools/hardware department: (a) physically count shelf and backroom quantity, (b) compare to system perpetual inventory (INV-001), (c) variance > 2 units: flag for W248 inventory variance investigation, (d) look for evidence of tag-switching (wrong barcode on high-value item), (e) verify EAS tags present on all items requiring tagging; SKU selection varied daily to cover top 100 high-value SKUs within monthly cycle | LP Officer | — | 10–15 min |
| 11 | **Review SCO exception log**: access SCO (self-checkout) exception log per W281: (a) review today's SCO weight-verification exceptions, (b) review SCO attendant interventions, (c) identify patterns (same customer repeatedly triggering weight exceptions, scan-and-pass behavior), (d) review SCO void rate compared to staffed lanes; flag anomalies for investigation | LP Officer | — | 5–10 min |
| 12 | **End-of-day LP daily log submission**: LP Officer completes daily checklist in LP module: (a) all checklist items marked pass/fail/NA with notes, (b) summary of findings (exceptions found, investigations opened, maintenance requests), (c) escalation items for follow-up (Regional LP Officer, Store Manager, HR), (d) overall LP risk assessment for the day (Green/Yellow/Red); submit log; system stores daily log and feeds data into: (a) W37 monthly LP dashboard, (b) W466 quarterly LPAP review, (c) W67 store performance scorecard LP metrics | LP Officer / Store Manager | Store Manager | 10 min |

### System Touchpoints
- POS-050 (real-time LP exception monitoring — overnight alert source)
- POS-068 (new requirement for store LP daily routine — checklist module, dashboard, daily log)
- W37 (LP exception reporting — escalation for investigation)
- W248 (inventory variance investigation — trigger from spot-checks)
- W466 (LPAP program — framework for LP activities and reporting)
- W538 (POS real-time LP monitoring — alert source for overnight exceptions)
- W71 (CCTV audit — camera status verification)
- W272 (cashier over/short — till spot-check protocol)
- W281 (SCO exception — exception log review)
- W18 (DSD receiving — vendor fraud observation)
- W47 (facility maintenance — EAS gate repair, CCTV repair)
- W48 (IT helpdesk — security system tickets)
- W522 (cashier performance — exception alert follow-up)

### Time Estimate
- Per LP Officer per store per visit day: 1.5–2 hours (dashboard 5 min + EAS test 10 min + high-value check 15 min + exception review 15 min + CCTV check 10 min + blind-spot walk 15 min + fitting area 5 min + DSD observation 15 min + till spot-check 10 min + shelf count 15 min + SCO review 10 min + daily log 10 min = ~2 hours 25 min; adjusted for non-visit days with abbreviated checklist: ~1.5 hours)
- **Total chain-wide daily**: 200 stores × 1.75 hours average = ~350 person-hours daily
- **Regional LP Officer visit days**: 20 officers × 1 store per day × 2.5 hours = 50 person-hours
- **Non-visit days (Store Manager as proxy)**: 180 stores × 1.5 hours = 270 person-hours (abbreviated checklist)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **200 stores cannot all have dedicated LP Officers** — only ~20 Regional LP Officers for the chain, meaning each store gets a dedicated visit only once every 2 weeks; mitigated by Store Manager serving as LP proxy with abbreviated daily checklist on non-visit days, and automated W538 exception monitoring filling the gap
- **High-value items in outdoor lumber yard are inherently difficult to secure** — lumber, steel bars, and cement in open yard areas cannot be EAS-tagged or caged; mitigated by yard gate control (W438), customer receipt verification at exit, and CCTV coverage with regular inventory counts
- **EAS tagging compliance for small hardware items is impractical** — screws, bolts, drill bits, and small accessories are too small or too low-value for EAS tags; mitigated by peg hook count reconciliation and gravity bin weight verification
- **ORC rings are sophisticated and target multiple stores in sequence** — organized retail crime groups may hit 3–5 stores in a region within a week, exploiting patterns (e.g., visiting during shift change, distracting staff with questions while accomplices steal); mitigated by regional ORC alert network (W466) and inter-store communication via W571 daily communication

### Staffing Implication
- 20 Regional LP Officers rotate across stores (~10 stores each); existing headcount sufficient with rotational coverage model
- Store Managers serve as LP proxy on non-visit days; LP daily checklist absorbed into existing Store Manager morning routine (~1.5 hours on non-visit days)
- No incremental headcount needed; LP coverage gap mitigated by W538 automated POS exception monitoring
