## W571. Store-Level Daily Communication & Memo Acknowledgment

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | HQ issues daily communication, policy update, task assignment, or urgent memo via ERP communication module |
| **Frequency** | ~10–20 communications per day from HQ to stores; Store Manager daily review |
| **Volume** | Higher during promotional changeover (bi-monthly), policy changes, seasonal transitions, price freeze events |
| **Owner** | Store Operations Director (HQ-side); Store Manager (store-side) |
| **Participants** | Store Operations Coordinator (R), Store Manager (R/A), Department Supervisors (R), IT (system support) |

### Background

No existing workflow covers the HQ-to-store daily communication cascade. For a 200-store chain spanning Mindanao, Visayas, and Luzon, consistent communication execution is critical for: promotional price changes (W40), new policy compliance (W186), recall notices (W29), operational directives, safety alerts, seasonal preparation instructions, HR announcements, and IT maintenance windows.

The communication challenge is amplified by geographic dispersion — stores in different regions may have different LGU regulations, typhoon exposure levels, and market conditions. A typhoon warning for Eastern Visayas requires urgent communication to 15 affected stores but is irrelevant to Mindanao locations. A DTI price freeze on construction materials in a calamity-declared province (W468) requires immediate action by stores in that province only. Without targeted communication, stores receive irrelevant messages, leading to communication fatigue and desensitization to genuinely urgent directives.

Without a structured communication workflow, messages get lost, acknowledged without reading, or executed inconsistently across the chain. Store Managers in a 200-store network process 10–20 HQ communications per day — without a tracking system, there is no visibility into which stores have read and acted on critical communications.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **HQ department creates communication item in ERP**: authorized HQ department (Merchandising, HR, IT, Compliance, Store Operations, Finance, Safety) creates communication item in ERP communication module: (a) subject line (clear, action-oriented), (b) body content (markdown supported with images, tables, file attachments), (c) priority level (Urgent — red, Standard — yellow, Informational — green), (d) target audience (all stores, regional [select region], specific stores [multi-select], by store class [e.g., stores with garden center]), (e) category (promotional, pricing, policy, recall, safety, HR, IT, operations, seasonal), (f) acknowledgment deadline (Urgent: 4 hours, Standard: 24 hours, Informational: 72 hours), (g) execution confirmation required (yes/no — if yes, Store Manager must confirm task completion with optional photo evidence); submit for distribution | HQ Department Lead | Store Operations Director | 10–20 min per communication |
| 2 | **Communication module pushes to targeted Store Managers**: system distributes communication to targeted Store Managers: (a) item appears on Store Manager's ERP dashboard under "Communications" tab, (b) unread count badge displayed on dashboard, (c) communication categorized and color-coded by priority, (d) full content and attachments accessible via dashboard and mobile app | System | — | Automated (within 60 seconds of creation) |
| 3 | **Urgent items trigger push notification and SMS**: for Urgent priority communications: (a) push notification sent to Store Manager's mobile app immediately, (b) SMS sent to Store Manager's registered mobile number with subject line and link (for managers who may not have app open), (c) reminder push notification every 2 hours until acknowledged, (d) if not acknowledged within 4 hours: escalate to Regional Manager (Step 11); for Standard items: appear in daily communication digest email sent at 7:00 AM; for Informational items: appear in weekly digest | System | — | Automated |
| 4 | **Store Manager opens communication during morning briefing**: Store Manager reviews communications during morning huddle (W5A store opening): (a) opens dashboard on tablet or office PC, (b) reviews new communications sorted by priority (Urgent first), (c) reads full content of each communication, (d) identifies action items requiring cascade to Department Supervisors | Store Manager | — | 10–15 min |
| 5 | **For task-type communications — assign to Department Supervisor**: if communication requires execution (e.g., "Set up promotional endcap for Power Tool Festival by Friday"): (a) Store Manager assigns task to specific Department Supervisor via communication module, (b) sets due date and expected completion evidence (photo, checklist, system confirmation), (c) task appears on Department Supervisor's handheld dashboard, (d) Store Manager tracks task status from communication detail screen | Store Manager | — | 3–5 min per task-type communication |
| 6 | **For policy-type communications — review with supervisors during daily huddle**: if communication is a policy update or operational directive: (a) Store Manager reviews key points with Department Supervisors during morning huddle, (b) supervisors acknowledge understanding verbally and sign huddle attendance log, (c) Store Manager records huddle completion in communication module as documentation of cascade | Store Manager | — | 5–10 min per policy communication |
| 7 | **Store Manager acknowledges receipt and confirms understanding**: Store Manager clicks "Acknowledge" button on each communication: (a) system records timestamp, Store Manager ID, and store ID, (b) acknowledgment status changes to "Read & Acknowledged" with timestamp, (c) communication moves from "Unread" to "Acknowledged" queue but remains accessible; checkbox with text "I have read and understood this communication" — prevents accidental acknowledgment without reading | Store Manager | — | 30 sec per communication |
| 8 | **For execution-required items — confirm completion with evidence**: for communications marked as "execution confirmation required": (a) after Store Manager or Department Supervisor completes the task (e.g., promotional display set up per W523, recall items pulled from shelf per W29), (b) Store Manager opens the communication and clicks "Confirm Completion", (c) uploads required evidence: photo of completed display, screenshot of system confirmation, or checklist sign-off, (d) system records completion timestamp and evidence; Regional Manager can review completion evidence for quality spot-check | Store Manager / Department Supervisor | Store Manager | 5–10 min per execution confirmation |
| 9 | **System tracks acknowledgment rate and completion status per store**: system maintains real-time tracking dashboard: (a) per communication: acknowledgment rate (X of 200 stores acknowledged), completion rate (for execution-required items), average time-to-acknowledgment, stores not yet acknowledged, (b) per store: acknowledgment compliance rate (last 30 days), overdue items count, average response time, (c) per priority level: Urgent SLA compliance (4-hour target); dashboard accessible to Store Operations Director and Regional Managers | System | — | Automated (real-time tracking) |
| 10 | **Regional Manager reviews non-acknowledging stores and follows up**: Regional Manager reviews communication compliance dashboard: (a) identifies stores with overdue acknowledgments, (b) calls or messages Store Manager directly for follow-up, (c) documents reason for delayed acknowledgment (system issue, Store Manager absence, connectivity problem), (d) if systemic issue (e.g., ISP outage in region): escalates to IT (W48) for resolution and extends acknowledgment deadline for affected stores | Regional Manager | — | 15–30 min/day |
| 11 | **Unacknowledged urgent items after 4 hours escalate to Regional Manager**: for Urgent communications not acknowledged within 4-hour SLA: (a) system auto-escalates to Regional Manager with store name and communication subject, (b) Regional Manager contacts Store Manager via phone call (mandatory — not just message), (c) Regional Manager can acknowledge on behalf of Store Manager with note explaining circumstance (e.g., "Store Manager on emergency leave — I confirmed with Assistant Manager over phone"), (d) escalation logged in audit trail | System / Regional Manager | Regional Manager | 5–10 min per escalation |
| 12 | **Monthly communication compliance dashboard**: system generates monthly compliance report: (a) overall chain-wide acknowledgment rate (target: > 95% within SLA), (b) completion rate for execution-required items, (c) average response time by priority level, (d) bottom 10 stores by compliance rate (flagged for Store Manager coaching), (e) top 5 most impactful communications (highest execution-required volume), (f) communication volume trend (per department, per category); report feeds into W67 store performance review as "Communication Compliance" KPI | System | Store Operations Director | Automated (monthly report generation) |

### System Touchpoints
- POS-069 (new requirement for store communication management — communication module, dashboard, acknowledgment tracking, escalation engine)
- W67 (store performance review — communication compliance KPI)
- W186 (SOP governance — policy update distribution)
- W523 (promotional terminal setup — promo setup task communication)
- W40 (price changes — price update communications)
- W29 (product recall — recall notification distribution)
- W468 (DTI price freeze — targeted regional communication)
- W48 (IT helpdesk — connectivity issue resolution for communication delivery)
- W5A (store opening — morning huddle communication review)

### Time Estimate
- Store Manager daily review and acknowledgment: 15–30 min (10–20 communications × 1–2 min each)
- Task assignment to Department Supervisors: 3–5 min per task-type communication (~2–3 per day)
- Execution confirmation: 5–10 min per item (~1–2 per day)
- **Total per store per day**: ~20–40 min for Store Manager; ~10–15 min for Department Supervisors
- **Total chain-wide daily**: 200 stores × 20 min Store Manager + 200 stores × 12 min Supervisors = ~107 person-hours daily
- **Regional Manager follow-up**: 15–30 min/day
- **Monthly compliance reporting**: automated (no incremental time)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Communication fatigue from excessive HQ messages** — Store Managers receiving 10–20 messages daily may skim without reading, especially Informational items; mitigated by strict categorization (only genuinely urgent items marked Urgent), mandatory reading confirmation checkbox, and HQ communication guidelines limiting daily volume
- **Store Managers may acknowledge without cascading to supervisors** — clicking "Acknowledge" is easy but actually briefing Department Supervisors during huddle is harder; mitigated by periodic spot-checks by Regional Manager and supervisor cascade documentation requirement for policy and task-type communications
- **Language barriers for detailed policy communications** — communications written in English may not be fully understood by all Store Managers (especially in Visayas and Mindanao where primary languages are Cebuano/Bisaya); mitigated by bilingual communication templates (English + Filipino) and regional translation support from Regional Manager office
- **Urgent communications during offline POS events may be delayed** — if store is in offline mode (W535) due to connectivity loss, push notifications and dashboard updates are delayed; mitigated by SMS fallback for Urgent items (SMS does not require ERP connectivity) and regional IT priority for store connectivity restoration

### Staffing Implication
- Covered by existing Store Manager and Store Operations team; no incremental headcount needed
- Store Operations Coordinator (HQ-side): communication creation and distribution absorbed into existing role (~1–2 hours/day for 10–20 communications)
- Regional Managers: compliance follow-up absorbed into existing store visit cadence

---

## W573. Store-Level Daily Planogram Execution & Shelf Compliance Check

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Store opening (daily shelf walk); planogram update distribution from HQ |
| **Frequency** | Daily shelf walk at all 200 stores; planogram updates distributed as needed (typically weekly/bi-weekly) |
| **Volume** | ~200 daily compliance checks; focused checks on recently reset sections or promotional displays |
| **Owner** | Department Supervisor / Visual Merchandising Associate |
| **Participants** | Department Supervisor (R/A), Stock Associate (R), Store Manager (I), Visual Merchandising Associate (R for resets) |

### Background

W86 covers monthly planogram compliance audits conducted by the Store Manager or Regional Manager. W314 covers planogram template and space planning master governance. W262 covers store promotional setup and visual merchandising execution. However, no workflow covers the daily planogram execution — the morning shelf walk, product facing, label alignment, display maintenance, and quick compliance checks that Department Supervisors perform every day.

In a big-box hardware environment with 35,000 SKUs and heavy, bulky merchandise, daily shelf maintenance is physically demanding and operationally critical. Customers in hardware retail expect to find specific products in specific locations; misaligned planograms lead to frustration, especially for trade professionals (contractors, electricians, plumbers) who navigate stores quickly and know exactly where their regular items should be. A contractor looking for 10mm electrical conduit who finds the shelf empty or replaced with a different item will not browse — they will go to a competitor.

Daily planogram execution bridges the gap between the replenishment activities of W554 (which places product on shelves) and the monthly audit of W86 (which measures compliance). Without daily execution, planogram drift accumulates — products migrate to wrong positions, labels fall off or get mismatched, promotional displays remain past their end date, and facing quality degrades. By the time the monthly audit occurs, corrective action is a major reset rather than minor adjustments.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Department Supervisor starts daily shelf walk using department planogram on handheld device**: at store opening (after initial replenishment by Stock Associates per W554), Department Supervisor logs into handheld and opens "Daily Shelf Walk" module; system loads assigned department planogram (4 departments per store: lumber/building, plumbing/electrical, tiles/flooring, tools/hardware); planogram displays aisle-by-aisle view with shelf positions; Supervisor selects starting aisle and begins walk | Department Supervisor | — | 2–3 min |
| 2 | **Walk first aisle of assigned department**: Supervisor walks first aisle systematically: (a) start from aisle entrance end, (b) walk one side of aisle at eye-level, then return on opposite side, (c) scan 3–5 shelf positions per bay for visual compliance, (d) compare physical shelf layout to planogram image on handheld; for each aisle section, assess: facing quality, label accuracy, product placement correctness, shelf cleanliness, fixture condition | Department Supervisor | — | 5–8 min per aisle |
| 3 | **Check product facing — pull forward items to shelf edge**: for each SKU position: (a) verify at least one unit is pulled forward to the shelf edge lip (facing), (b) if product is pushed back beyond shelf edge: pull forward, (c) if facing quantity is less than planogram minimum front-facing count: flag for replenishment (links to W554 task list), (d) for heavy items (tile sample boards, paint cans, tool sets): ensure facing is stable and not at risk of falling; note: in hardware retail, "facing" is physically demanding — 20 kg paint cans and tile boxes cannot be easily repositioned one-handed | Department Supervisor | — | 2–3 min per bay |
| 4 | **Verify shelf label matches product — flag mismatches for reprint**: for each SKU position: (a) verify shelf label product description matches physical product, (b) verify barcode on label matches product barcode (quick scan if uncertain), (c) verify price on label matches current system price (especially for promotional items), (d) if mismatch found: tap "Label Mismatch" on handheld — system generates label reprint task routed to W181 queue with priority; common mismatches: product placed in wrong position during replenishment, label from previous product not removed during reset, promotional price label not updated after promo ended | Department Supervisor | — | 1–2 min per bay |
| 5 | **Check promotional display positions against current planogram**: for promotional displays (endcaps, power aisle stacks, freestanding displays): (a) verify display matches current promotional planogram from W262, (b) verify promotional signage matches current promo (W13 promo calendar) — check promo dates have not expired, (c) verify promotional pricing is correct per W40 price file, (d) if promo has ended: flag for immediate teardown and restock to standard planogram, (e) if promo display is depleted: flag for replenishment or substitute facing | Department Supervisor | — | 3–5 min per display |
| 6 | **Verify endcap and power aisle displays match HQ planogram template**: for permanent endcap positions and power aisle (main aisle) displays: (a) compare physical display to planogram template (MDM-023) on handheld, (b) verify correct products in correct positions per template, (c) verify display height and stack configuration matches template (safety consideration — unstable stacks of cement or tiles are a falling hazard), (d) take comparison photo for recently reset endcaps (within last 7 days) for Regional Manager review | Department Supervisor | — | 3–5 min per endcap |
| 7 | **Report shelf damage or fixture issues to W47**: during shelf walk, Supervisor identifies: (a) broken shelf brackets or sagging shelves (weight overload), (b) damaged peg hooks (bent, missing), (c) broken display fixtures, (d) damaged signage holders, (e) water damage to shelving units (especially in garden center and near loading dock); tap "Report Damage" on handheld: system auto-generates W47 facility maintenance work order with photo and location; priority based on safety risk (sagging shelf = high priority, cosmetic damage = normal queue) | Department Supervisor | — | 1–2 min per issue |
| 8 | **For newly reset sections — detailed compliance check against updated planogram**: for sections that were recently reset (planogram update applied within last 7 days): (a) perform detailed position-by-position compliance check against updated planogram, (b) photo documentation: take overview photo of full section + close-up photos of each shelf position, (c) compare each product position to planogram: correct product, correct position, correct facing quantity, correct label, (d) calculate compliance percentage for the reset section, (e) if compliance < 90%: flag for corrective action within 24 hours by Stock Associate | Department Supervisor | Store Manager | 10–15 min per reset section |
| 9 | **Flag gaps where products are out-of-stock for extended periods**: for SKU positions that are empty or have been at minimum facing for 3+ consecutive days: (a) check system for replenishment status (on order from DC, inter-store transfer pending, or discontinued), (b) if product is active but no replenishment in pipeline: escalate to Store Manager for manual order or substitute facing, (c) for substitute facing: apply W279 product substitution guidance — place a visually similar or related product in the gap with temporary label, (d) if product is discontinued: flag for planogram update request to HQ (MDM-023 change request) | Department Supervisor | Store Manager | 2–3 min per gap |
| 10 | **Check price tag accuracy on promotional items**: for items on current promotion (identified by promotional shelf tag or W13 promo calendar): (a) verify promotional price tag is displayed, (b) cross-reference promotional price with W40 price file on handheld, (c) verify promo dates on tag are current (not expired), (d) verify "was/now" pricing is accurate per DTI consumer protection requirements, (e) if discrepancy found: flag as price error per W553 pricing error workflow for immediate correction | Department Supervisor | — | 2–3 min per promotional section |
| 11 | **Department Supervisor signs off daily shelf walk completion**: after completing assigned department walk: (a) Supervisor reviews summary on handheld — total positions checked, exceptions found (label mismatches, facing issues, fixture damage, OOS gaps, promo discrepancies), (b) Supervisor adds notes on any unusual conditions, (c) signs off with electronic signature and timestamp, (d) system records completion and exception count per department per store per day | Department Supervisor | — | 3–5 min |
| 12 | **Weekly exception summary feeds into W86 monthly audit**: system compiles daily shelf walk data into weekly summary: (a) total exceptions by category (labels, facing, fixtures, OOS, promo), (b) trend by department (improving or deteriorating), (c) exception resolution rate (what % of flagged items were resolved within 24 hours), (d) repeat exception locations (same position flagged 3+ days in a row); weekly summary feeds into W86 monthly planogram compliance audit as: (a) pre-audit focus areas (departments with highest exception rates get priority audit attention), (b) historical compliance trend data, (c) exception resolution effectiveness metric | System / Department Supervisor | Store Manager | Automated (weekly summary generation) |

### System Touchpoints
- POS-070 (new requirement for daily planogram execution — shelf walk module, planogram viewer, exception logging)
- POS-066 (shelf replenishment — execution partner for OOS flags)
- MDM-023 (planogram template master — planogram source data and image library)
- W86 (planogram compliance — monthly audit partner receiving weekly exception data)
- W181 (shelf label printing — label reprint task generation)
- W262 (promotional setup — promo display planogram reference)
- W279 (product substitution — substitute facing guidance for extended OOS)
- W40 (price changes — price accuracy cross-reference)
- W420 (AI shelf monitoring — automated gap detection feeding daily walk priorities)
- W47 (facility maintenance — fixture and shelf damage work orders)
- W553 (pricing error — price discrepancy flag for promotional items)
- W554 (shelf replenishment — replenishment task source for facing quantity issues)

### Time Estimate
- Per Department Supervisor per day: 30–45 min (walk preparation 3 min + 8–10 aisles × 5 min + promo checks 10 min + sign-off 5 min)
- Additional time for reset section compliance checks: 10–15 min per recently reset section (occurs 1–2 times per week)
- **Total per store per day**: 4 Department Supervisors × 40 min = ~160 min = ~2.7 person-hours
- **Total chain-wide daily**: 200 stores × 2.7 hours = ~533 person-hours daily
- **Weekly exception summary review by Store Manager**: ~30 min/week

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Heavy hardware items are difficult to face and reposition daily** — cement bags (40 kg), tile boxes (20–25 kg), lumber sections cannot be casually pulled forward like grocery items; mitigated by Stock Associate (W554) handling heavy item facing during replenishment and Supervisor focusing on visual compliance check rather than physical repositioning
- **Outdoor yard sections cannot follow traditional planograms** — lumber, cement, and steel bar yard displays are bulk-stacked rather than planogrammed; mitigated by separate yard display guidelines (visual placement standards rather than fixed planogram positions) and W554 Step 9 yard restacking protocol
- **Seasonal displays require frequent re-merchandising** — holiday decor, garden seasonal, and promotional power displays change every 2–4 weeks, requiring Supervisor to verify against frequently updated planogram templates; mitigated by HQ distributing updated planograms 3 days before effective date via W571 communication
- **35,000 SKUs across 8 departments means no single person can check everything daily** — each Supervisor covers 1–2 departments per day on rotation; full store coverage requires 2-day cycle; mitigated by prioritizing high-traffic aisles (power tools, electrical, plumbing) daily and secondary aisles on alternating days
- **Promo display setup quality varies by store** — promotional endcap execution depends on Store Manager and Supervisor visual merchandising skill; mitigated by photo evidence requirement (Step 8) and Regional Manager spot-check during monthly W86 audit

### Staffing Implication
- Covered by existing 4 Department Supervisors per store; no incremental headcount needed
- Visual Merchandising Associate (where present in larger stores): supports reset execution per W262; absorbed into existing headcount
- Store Manager: weekly exception summary review (~30 min/week) absorbed into existing management routine

## W574. Store-Level Daily Closing Procedure

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Store operating hours end (typically 8:00 PM, or 9:00 PM during extended hours) |
| **Frequency** | Daily per store |
| **Volume** | 200 stores × 365 days = 73,000 closings/year |
| **Owner** | Store Manager (or Assistant Manager) |
| **Participants** | Store Manager, Lead Cashier, Security Guard, Stock Associate |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Announce last call 15 minutes before closing; PA system announcement in English and Filipino | Lead Cashier | Store Manager | 2 min |
| 2 | Escort remaining customers to checkout and then to exit; no new transactions after posted closing time | Sales Associate | Store Manager | 5–10 min |
| 3 | Run Z-report (end-of-day reading) on all POS terminals; verify all terminals report successfully | Lead Cashier | Store Manager | 10 min |
| 4 | Count cash per drawer against system Z-report totals; flag any variances exceeding PHP 100 per drawer | Lead Cashier | Store Manager | 15 min |
| 5 | Resolve cashier over/short variances per W272; document unresolved variances for next-day follow-up | Store Manager | Store Manager | 5–10 min |
| 6 | Prepare bank deposit per W541 (cash, coin, checks separated; deposit slip completed; smart safe or deposit bag sealed) | Lead Cashier | Store Manager | 10 min |
| 7 | Store security walkthrough: yard/lumber area, receiving dock, paint room, garden center, sales floor aisles, break room, restrooms — check for: remaining customers, safety hazards, open doors/windows, water leaks | Store Manager | Store Manager | 15 min |
| 8 | Verify all equipment secured: paint mixer covered and powered off, lumber/panel saw blade guards on and power disconnected, forklifts parked and keys removed to lockbox, pallet jacks returned to designated area, ladders secured | Stock Associate | Store Manager | 5 min |
| 9 | Verify all refrigerated/temperature-sensitive items (adhesives, certain chemicals) are within acceptable range per W587 temperature protocol if applicable | Stock Associate | Store Manager | 3 min |
| 10 | Set alarm system; verify all entry points locked (front doors, receiving dock, emergency exits, yard gate, office) | Security Guard | Store Manager | 5 min |
| 11 | Submit daily sales summary to Regional Manager via system dashboard: total sales vs. target, transaction count, top 5 SKUs, cash over/short total, any incidents noted | Store Manager | — | 5 min |

**Total time per closing**: ~75–90 minutes per store per day

### System Touchpoints (W574 — Store-Level Daily Closing Procedure)
- POS Z-report generation per terminal (POS-009)
- Cash drawer count reconciliation vs. system (POS-008, POS-009)
- W272 (cashier over/short dispute resolution for unresolved variances)
- W541 (cash office operations & bank deposit preparation)
- W71 (physical security & access control — alarm system, lock verification)
- W579 (equipment safety check — next-morning counterpart)
- Daily sales summary dashboard for Regional Manager
- BIR daily sales report retention per NFR-006 (7-year retention)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Cash count discrepancies delay closing** — if a drawer is significantly over/short (> PHP 500), the Store Manager must investigate before allowing the cashier to leave, extending closing time by 15–30 min; mitigated by mid-day cash sweeps per W278 reducing drawer cash levels
- **Customers refusing to leave at closing** — some customers, especially trade professionals, may resist leaving; mitigated by last-call announcement 15 min before close and polite escort by Sales Associate
- **Overnight security risk if closing procedure rushed** — skipping the walkthrough to leave earlier may miss unlocked doors or safety hazards; mitigated by Store Manager sign-off requirement on digital closing checklist
- **Equipment not properly secured creates DOLE liability** — unsecured saws or forklift keys accessible to unauthorized persons; mitigated by lockbox protocol and morning verification per W579

### Staffing Implication
- 1 Store Manager + 1 Lead Cashier + 1 Security Guard per closing; absorbed into existing shift duties
- ~73,000 store closings/year across 200 stores = ~100,000 staff-hours/year for closing procedures
- No incremental headcount needed

---

## W575. Store-Level Weekly Sales & Operations Review

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Every Monday morning (or first working day of the week) |
| **Frequency** | Weekly per store; Regional Manager reviews bottom-quartile stores |
| **Volume** | 200 stores × 52 weeks = 10,400 reviews/year |
| **Owner** | Store Manager |
| **Participants** | Store Manager, Department Supervisors, Regional Manager (for flagged stores) |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Store Manager reviews system-generated weekly scorecard: sales vs. budget by department, average transaction value (ATV), transaction count trend, gross margin by department | Store Manager | — | 10 min |
| 2 | Review promotional sell-through vs. plan for any promotions active during the week | Store Manager | — | 5 min |
| 3 | Review customer satisfaction scores and top 3 complaint categories per W597 | Store Manager | — | 5 min |
| 4 | Review shrinkage and LP incidents; compare vs. prior week and vs. regional average | Store Manager | — | 5 min |
| 5 | Review staffing effectiveness: labor cost % of revenue, overtime hours, absenteeism rate per W594 | Store Manager | — | 5 min |
| 6 | Review facility and equipment issues: open maintenance work orders per W47, equipment safety check failures per W579 | Store Manager | — | 3 min |
| 7 | Identify top 3 action items for the coming week with owners, deadlines, and expected outcomes | Store Manager | Department Supervisors | 10 min |
| 8 | Brief Department Supervisors on key findings and action items during Monday morning huddle | Store Manager | Department Supervisors | 10 min |
| 9 | Submit weekly report to Regional Manager via system (auto-populated from scorecard with Store Manager commentary) | Store Manager | — | 5 min |
| 10 | Regional Manager reviews bottom-quartile stores by sales performance and takes action (call, visit, or escalation to Store Ops Director) | Regional Manager | Store Ops Director | 30 min/week across portfolio |

**Total time**: ~45 min for Store Manager; ~30 min/week for Regional Manager across portfolio

### System Touchpoints (W575 — Weekly Sales & Operations Review)
- System-generated weekly scorecard (sales, ATV, transactions, margin by department)
- W67 (monthly store performance review — monthly deep-dive complement)
- W522 (daily transaction review — daily data feeding weekly scorecard)
- W35 (management reporting rhythm — consolidated reporting)
- W597 (complaint escalation — CSAT and complaint data feed)
- W594 (attendance verification — absenteeism and overtime data)
- W562 (LP daily routine — shrinkage data)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Scorecard review becomes perfunctory** — Store Managers may treat the weekly review as a checkbox exercise; mitigated by Regional Manager follow-up on bottom-quartile stores and requiring Store Manager commentary on variance explanations
- **Action items not tracked to completion** — weekly action items created but not followed through; mitigated by carrying forward incomplete items to next week's review and tracking in system
- **Regional Manager bandwidth for 40–50 stores** — reviewing all stores weekly is not feasible; mitigated by focusing on bottom quartile and exception-flagged stores only

### Staffing Implication
- Covered by existing Store Manager and Regional Manager roles; no incremental headcount
- ~200 stores × 45 min/week = ~7,800 person-hours/year for Store Manager weekly reviews
- Regional Manager weekly review absorbed into existing management cadence

## W576. Store-Level Typhoon & Severe Weather Preparedness Protocol

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | PAGASA issues tropical cyclone advisory (TCWS Signal 1 or higher) for any store location |
| **Frequency** | ~20–25 typhoon events/year affecting Philippine archipelago; individual store impact varies |
| **Volume** | During typhoon season (June–November), average 2–3 stores affected per event; major events (Signal 4–5) may affect 30–50 stores simultaneously |
| **Owner** | Store Operations Director (HQ activation); Store Manager (store-level execution) |
| **Participants** | Store Ops Director, Regional Managers, Store Managers, Safety Officer, Security, LP, Insurance, Facilities |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Pre-season preparation (April–May)**: HQ distributes typhoon preparedness kits to all 200 stores (sandbags, tarps, emergency lighting, battery radio, first aid restock); Store Manager verifies kit completeness; generators tested per W111 | Store Ops Coordinator | Store Ops Director | 2 weeks (annual) |
| 2 | **PAGASA monitoring**: HQ monitors PAGASA tropical cyclone bulletins every 6 hours during active weather disturbances; automated alerts pushed to Regional Managers and affected Store Managers when TCWS raised for any store province | Store Ops Coordinator | Store Ops Director | Continuous during events |
| 3 | **Signal 1 (Alert Mode)**: affected stores execute: (a) secure all outdoor merchandise and display fixtures (yard, garden center), (b) test backup generator and verify fuel level, (c) activate staff contact tree — confirm all employees have emergency contact info, (d) increase cash holdings for potential multi-day offline operations per W580, (e) verify POS offline capability per W535 (full SKU catalog + customer cache current) | Store Manager | Regional Manager | 2–3 hours |
| 4 | **Signal 2 (Reduced Operations)**: (a) Regional Manager authorizes early closure if conditions warrant, (b) expedite remaining customer transactions, (c) pre-position emergency supplies (flashlights, water, first aid), (d) cash safe reinforcement — all excess cash deposited per W541 before closure, (e) staff release plan communicated (essential staff only to remain for securing facility) | Store Manager | Regional Manager | 2–3 hours |
| 5 | **Signal 3+ (Full Closure)**: (a) complete customer evacuation per W330, (b) lock down facility — all doors, windows, dock doors secured, (c) sandbag entry points if flood risk, (d) activate alarm system despite closure, (e) all staff released except minimum security presence (1 guard with emergency communication), (f) Store Manager submits closure notification to HQ dashboard | Store Manager | Store Ops Director | 1–2 hours |
| 6 | **During typhoon**: HQ maintains real-time store status dashboard; affected Store Managers send hourly status updates (if communication available); Regional Manager monitors portfolio | Store Ops Coordinator | Store Ops Director | Continuous |
| 7 | **Post-typhoon damage assessment (within 24 hours of all-clear)**: Store Manager conducts damage assessment using standardized checklist: (a) structural damage (roof, walls, doors, windows), (b) flooding/water damage to inventory, (c) equipment damage, (d) electrical system status, (e) inventory loss estimate; photographs all damage; submits assessment to HQ | Store Manager | Regional Manager | 2–4 hours |
| 8 | **Insurance claim initiation**: for stores with damage exceeding PHP 50K, HQ Insurance Coordinator initiates claim with insurer per W59; provides Store Manager's damage assessment, photos, and inventory loss details | Insurance Coordinator | CFO | 1–2 hours per claim |
| 9 | **Reopening checklist**: (a) structural safety confirmed (no visible damage or damage repaired), (b) power restored or generator operational, (c) water supply confirmed, (d) POS connectivity verified (online sync with HQ per W533), (e) damaged inventory segregated and documented per W91, (f) staff safety confirmed (no employee injuries), (g) Store Manager submits reopening notification to HQ dashboard | Store Manager | Regional Manager | 2–3 hours |

**Total time per event**: ~8–15 hours of active management time per affected store; HQ coordination adds ~20–40 hours per major event

### System Touchpoints (W576 — Typhoon & Severe Weather Preparedness)
- PAGASA weather alert integration with store location master (MDM-006)
- Store status dashboard (open/closed/damaged/reopening) for HQ and Regional Managers
- W330 (in-store emergency response protocol — evacuation procedures)
- W49 (natural disaster business continuity — enterprise-level BC activation)
- W428 (community disaster relief — post-typhoon community support)
- W111 (energy & utility management — generator testing and fuel)
- W535 (offline capability — POS offline readiness)
- W580 (emergency manual operations — if extended power failure)
- W91 (damaged goods disposition — inventory damage documentation)
- W59 (insurance policy lifecycle — claim initiation)
- W571 (daily communication — typhoon advisories to stores)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Communication blackout during severe typhoons** — cell towers and internet may be down for 24–48 hours in badly hit areas; Store Managers cannot submit status updates; mitigated by satellite phone at each DC (fallback communication point) and pre-arranged SMS check-in protocol via backup carrier
- **Inventory damage from flooding** — stores in flood-prone areas (Metro Manila, coastal cities) may lose significant inventory; mitigated by pre-positioning inventory above floor level during Signal 1, and flood insurance riders
- **Staff safety during commute to secure store** — asking staff to travel during Signal 2–3 to secure the store is dangerous; mitigated by Signal 1 preparation completing all securing activities so minimal travel needed at higher signal levels
- **Reopening pressure vs. safety** — commercial pressure to reopen quickly vs. structural safety risk; mitigated by mandatory damage assessment checklist sign-off before reopening authorization from Regional Manager

### Staffing Implication
- Pre-season preparation: 200 Store Managers × 2 hours = 400 hours (annual, absorbed into existing duties)
- Per-event active management: ~8–15 hours per affected store, absorbed by Store Manager and Regional Manager
- HQ coordination: 1 Store Ops Coordinator (existing role) manages dashboard and communication during events
- No incremental headcount needed

## W577. Store-Level Holiday Season (Ber Months) Operational Ramp-Up

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Annual seasonal planning calendar — T-8 weeks before September 1 |
| **Frequency** | Annual (September–December), with mid-season checkpoints |
| **Volume** | All 200 stores; "Ber months" represent ~35–40% of annual revenue |
| **Owner** | Store Operations Director |
| **Participants** | Store Ops Director, Regional Managers, Store Managers, HR, Merchandising, LP, IT, Marketing |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **T-8 weeks (early July)**: HR prepares seasonal staffing plan per W555 — identify temp headcount needs per store (10–15% increase = ~580–870 seasonal workers across 200 stores); initiate recruitment | HR Manager | Store Ops Director | 2 weeks |
| 2 | **T-6 weeks (mid July)**: Merchandising and Supply Chain coordinate inventory build-up with DCs — seasonal SKUs pre-positioned, DC capacity allocated for peak outbound volumes | Supply Planning Manager | VP Merchandising | 2 weeks |
| 3 | **T-4 weeks (early August)**: Store Managers prepare fixture/display expansion plan; identify seasonal display locations; submit extended operating hours request to LGU per regulatory requirements | Store Manager | Regional Manager | 1 week |
| 4 | **T-2 weeks (mid August)**: LP adds additional shifts and increases CCTV coverage review frequency; IT pre-positions spare POS terminals and networking equipment for rapid deployment; promotional calendar finalized per W13 | LP Director / IT Manager | Store Ops Director | 1 week |
| 5 | **T-1 week (late August)**: dress rehearsal — Store Manager runs through peak-day simulation with full staffing; all promotional displays installed per W262; POS promo prices verified per W523 | Store Manager | Regional Manager | 4 hours/store |
| 6 | **In-season (Sept–Dec)**: daily flash sales report reviewed by Store Manager and Regional Manager; staffing flexed up/down based on daily traffic; bi-monthly sale events executed per promotional calendar | Store Manager | Regional Manager | Daily |
| 7 | **Post-season (early January)**: seasonal close-out review — sales vs. plan, staffing costs, LP incidents, inventory remaining; lessons learned documented for next year | Store Manager | Store Ops Director | 2 hours/store |

**Total time**: ~40–60 hours of planning per store over the preparation period; absorbed by existing management

### System Touchpoints (W577 — Holiday Season Operational Ramp-Up)
- W555 (seasonal & temporary staffing process — recruitment and onboarding)
- W13 (promotions & pricing execution — promotional calendar)
- W262 (store promotional setup & visual merchandising — display installation)
- W34 (shift scheduling — extended hours and additional shifts)
- W562 (LP daily routine — heightened security)
- W578 (payday weekend readiness — peak day operations)
- W577 feeds into W67 (monthly store performance review — seasonal performance KPIs)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Seasonal workers require intensive training** — 580–870 temp workers across 200 stores needing POS training, safety orientation, and product knowledge in compressed timeframe; mitigated by standardized 2-day onboarding program per W555 and W518
- **DC capacity bottleneck during inventory build-up** — 4 DCs must handle both regular replenishment and seasonal pre-positioning simultaneously; mitigated by advance scheduling and cross-dock flow for fast-moving seasonal items per W221
- **Extended hours fatigue** — employees working extended shifts during peak season leads to higher error rates and safety incidents; mitigated by mandatory rest day per DOLE Labor Code and overtime caps per HR-010

### Staffing Implication
- Seasonal workers: 580–870 FTEs (temporary, ~4 months); cost budgeted as seasonal labor
- Existing Store Managers absorb planning (~10 additional hours/week during preparation)
- Regional Manager portfolio reviews increase from monthly to weekly during Ber months
- No incremental permanent headcount

---

## W578. Store-Level Payday Weekend & Peak Day Operational Readiness

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Predictable high-traffic days: paydays (15th, 30th/31st), holidays, sale events, month-end |
| **Frequency** | ~4–6 predictable peak days per month per store |
| **Volume** | Payday weekends show 30–50% sales uplift vs. normal weekend; contractor trade customers spike on 15th/30th |
| **Owner** | Store Manager |
| **Participants** | Store Manager, Lead Cashier, Stock Associates, LP |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **3 days before**: system analyzes historical sales patterns by store for upcoming peak day — predicts transaction volume, ATV, peak hours, and top-selling categories | System | Store Manager | Automated |
| 2 | **2 days before**: increase cash floats per forecast (typical payday float: 1.5× normal); coordinate with CIT service per W174 for additional cash delivery | Lead Cashier | Store Manager | 15 min |
| 3 | **2 days before**: inventory top-up for fast-moving SKUs identified by forecast — coordinate with backroom Stock Associates for shelf stocking per W554 | Stock Associate | Department Supervisor | 2 hours |
| 4 | **1 day before**: confirm additional cashier deployment per W34 (flex up to all terminals); activate backup POS terminal if normally idle; verify POS system connectivity | Store Manager | — | 15 min |
| 5 | **1 day before**: queue management preparation — set up express lane, stage mPOS tablets per W206, activate scan & go per W547 | Lead Cashier | Store Manager | 15 min |
| 6 | **Day of — morning**: all-hands briefing: expected traffic, key promotions active, express lane assignments, LP heightened awareness | Store Manager | — | 10 min |
| 7 | **Day of — during peak**: Store Manager monitors queue length and checkout wait times; activates queue-busting (mPOS, additional cashier) if wait exceeds 5 minutes; LP patrols high-theft departments | Store Manager | — | Continuous |
| 8 | **Day after**: post-peak flash report — actual vs. predicted sales, checkout wait times, stockouts, LP incidents | Store Manager | — | 10 min |

**Total time**: ~45 min preparation + active monitoring during peak + 10 min post-report

### System Touchpoints (W578 — Payday Weekend & Peak Day Operational Readiness)
- Historical sales pattern analysis engine (POS transaction data)
- W34 (shift scheduling — additional cashier deployment)
- W174 (CIT & armored car — cash float increase)
- W212 (smart safe integration — cash management)
- W206 (mobile POS — queue-busting)
- W547 (scan & go — self-scan checkout)
- W554 (shelf replenishment — inventory top-up)
- W562 (LP daily routine — heightened awareness)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Forecast underestimation** — if actual traffic exceeds prediction, cash shortages and stockouts occur; mitigated by conservative 1.5× cash float rule and maintaining safety stock for top 100 SKUs
- **All terminals needed simultaneously** — normally 3 of 5 terminals active; payday requires all 5; if one terminal is down per W265, checkout capacity is reduced during highest-traffic period; mitigated by pre-peak terminal verification (Step 4) and spare terminal pre-positioning during Ber months
- **Contractor rush at opening** — trade professionals arrive at store opening on paydays to buy materials before heading to job sites; this creates an early-morning surge that can overwhelm standard opening staffing; mitigated by opening with full cashier deployment on payday mornings (all 5 terminals from 8:00 AM)

### Staffing Implication
- Covered by existing store staff; peak-day flex is absorbed by shift overlap scheduling per W34
- ~200 stores × 45 min preparation × 6 peak days/month = ~900 staff-hours/month
- No incremental headcount needed

---

## W579. Store-Level Daily Equipment & Specialized Fixture Safety Check

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Store opening (daily, after alarm disarm and before customer entry) |
| **Frequency** | Daily per store; each equipment item checked before first use |
| **Volume** | 200 stores × ~15 equipment items per store = 3,000 daily checks |
| **Owner** | Store Manager |
| **Participants** | Department Supervisors, Stock Associates |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Forklift pre-use inspection: fluid levels (oil, hydraulic, coolant), tire condition, horn, lights, backup alarm, seat belt, forks condition; enter results in equipment safety app | Stock Associate | Department Supervisor | 5 min |
| 2 | Pallet jack inspection: wheel condition, hydraulic pump operation, handle function, fork tips not bent | Stock Associate | Department Supervisor | 2 min |
| 3 | Paint mixing machine: calibration check (run test mix with water), colorant level verification for all tinting stations, mixing blade condition, guard in place | Paint Dept Supervisor | Store Manager | 5 min |
| 4 | Lumber/panel saw: blade guard verified and functional, blade condition (no chipped teeth), emergency stop button tested, power cord insulation intact | Lumber Dept Supervisor | Store Manager | 5 min |
| 5 | Ladder stability check: all ladders in store (step ladders, extension ladders) — feet intact, rungs secure, no visible damage | Department Supervisors | Store Manager | 3 min |
| 6 | Electrical tool inspection (if demo tools available): insulation check on power cords, ground pin intact, no visible damage | Department Supervisor | Store Manager | 3 min |
| 7 | Log all results in equipment safety app: item ID, check type, pass/fail, inspector name, timestamp | Department Supervisors | Store Manager | 2 min |
| 8 | If any item fails: (a) tag out-of-service with red tag (lock-out/tag-out), (b) notify Store Manager immediately, (c) system auto-generates maintenance work order per W47, (d) Store Manager determines if safe alternative exists (e.g., hand saw instead of panel saw), (e) equipment not returned to service until maintenance clears | Department Supervisor | Store Manager | 5 min per failure |
| 9 | Weekly: Store Manager reviews equipment safety log for recurring failures; schedules preventive maintenance for items showing degradation patterns | Store Manager | — | 15 min/week |

**Total time per store per day**: ~25 min for all checks; ~15 min additional per failed item

### System Touchpoints (W579 — Daily Equipment Safety Check)
- Equipment safety checklist app (mobile) integrated with maintenance module
- W47 (store facility maintenance — work order auto-generation for failed checks)
- W139 (tool rental — rental equipment inspection reference)
- W168 (custom paint mixing — machine-specific maintenance)
- W169 (lumber cutting — saw-specific safety protocols)
- W501 (first aid — injury reporting if equipment-related incident occurs)
- DOLE OHS documentation per W436 (annual statutory reporting)
- Equipment master data per MDM-027 (EAM master governance)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Checks skipped during rushed openings** — Department Supervisors under time pressure may skip non-critical checks; mitigated by Store Manager sign-off requirement on completed checklist before store opens
- **Paint mixing calibration drift** — colorant dispensing accuracy degrades over time; incorrect color matching leads to customer complaints and remixed product waste; mitigated by daily calibration check and monthly professional calibration service
- **Lumber saw blade failure is catastrophic** — a blade failure during customer cutting operation is a severe safety risk; mitigated by daily blade condition check and mandatory blade replacement at configured interval (regardless of visual condition)

### Staffing Implication
- Covered by existing Department Supervisors and Stock Associates; no incremental headcount
- ~200 stores × 25 min/day = ~3,050 person-hours/day chain-wide = ~1,113,000 person-hours/year
- Weekly Store Manager review: 15 min × 52 weeks = ~13 hours/year per Store Manager

---

## W580. Store-Level Emergency Manual Operations Protocol (Total System/Power Failure)

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Total POS/ERP system failure or extended power outage exceeding UPS battery life (typically 30–60 min) at store level |
| **Frequency** | Rare — ~2–4 total system failure events per store per year (mostly power-related in provincial areas); ~10–20 chain-wide per month during typhoon season |
| **Volume** | When activated: all transactions at affected store(s) processed manually until restoration |
| **Owner** | Store Manager |
| **Participants** | All store staff on duty during failure |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Determine scope: single store (local power/ISP issue) or area outage; check POS connectivity status on all terminals; if all terminals offline and WAN indicator red — declare manual operations | Store Manager | — | 2 min |
| 2 | Activate manual operations kit (pre-positioned in store office): (a) pre-printed receipt books (BIR-compliant format with sequential numbering), (b) backup price book (printed monthly, laminated — contains top 5,000 SKUs by velocity with SRP and trade prices), (c) calculator, (d) manual transaction log forms, (e) cash drawer count form, (f) emergency contact list | Lead Cashier | Store Manager | 3 min |
| 3 | Manual cash handling: (a) assign one manual cash drawer per operational checkout point, (b) count starting float and log, (c) all transactions are cash-only (no card, e-wallet, or loyalty) — post signage at entrance "CASH ONLY — system temporarily unavailable" | Lead Cashier | Store Manager | 5 min |
| 4 | Manual transaction processing: for each customer transaction — (a) look up item price in backup price book or use shelf tag price, (b) manually calculate totals on calculator, (c) compute VAT (12% inclusive — prices in book are VAT-inclusive), (d) write receipt in pre-printed BIR receipt book (duplicate: customer copy + store copy), (e) record transaction in manual log: date, time, receipt number, item(s), quantity, total amount, tender, change, customer name (if willing) | Cashier | Store Manager | 5–10 min per transaction |
| 5 | Manual loyalty recording: if customer presents loyalty card, record loyalty card number and transaction amount in separate loyalty log for retroactive points posting upon system restoration | Cashier | — | 30 sec |
| 6 | Safe drop every 2 hours: Lead Cashier counts cash accumulated, completes safe drop slip, and secures in safe to limit cash exposure | Lead Cashier | Store Manager | 5 min per drop |
| 7 | If failure exceeds 4 hours: Store Manager declares mandatory closure — (a) process remaining customers, (b) secure all cash in safe, (c) post closure signage, (d) notify Regional Manager | Store Manager | Regional Manager | 15 min |
| 8 | Upon system restoration: (a) enter all manual transactions into POS with audit flag "MANUAL-RECOVERY" and corresponding receipt number, (b) verify cash on hand matches total of system entries + starting float, (c) post loyalty points for recorded loyalty card transactions, (d) Store Manager reconciles and signs off | Store Manager | — | 1–2 hours (depending on transaction volume) |
| 9 | Post-incident report: Store Manager submits incident report to IT and Regional Manager — duration of outage, number of manual transactions, total manual revenue, any discrepancies, root cause if known | Store Manager | — | 15 min |

**Total time**: ~20 min activation + 5–10 min per manual transaction + 1–2 hours reconciliation

### System Touchpoints (W580 — Emergency Manual Operations Protocol)
- W535 (POS offline capability — first-line offline mode before manual fallback)
- W541 (cash office operations — manual cash handling and safe drop procedures)
- W525 (POS nightly batch synchronization — post-recovery data entry)
- W574 (daily closing — closing procedure during extended failure)
- BIR compliance: manual receipt books must be BIR-registered per NFR-022a (CAS registration)
- Manual transaction log retained for 7 years per NFR-006

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Manual transactions are 3–5× slower than POS** — customer throughput drops dramatically, causing long queues and abandoned purchases; mitigated by express-item-only policy during manual mode (limit to top 500 SKUs in price book)
- **Pricing errors in manual calculations** — cashiers may miscalculate totals or use wrong prices; mitigated by VAT-inclusive price book and calculator requirement; Store Manager spot-checks every 10th transaction
- **No inventory deduction during manual mode** — POS system does not deduct inventory for manual sales; system inventory becomes inaccurate until post-recovery entry; mitigated by post-recovery reconciliation and manual log cross-reference
- **Theft risk increases** — without POS transaction logging, the audit trail relies entirely on manual receipt books; mitigated by mandatory receipt for every transaction and Store Manager safe-drop verification

### Staffing Implication
- All store staff on duty participate during manual operations; no incremental headcount
- Post-recovery reconciliation (1–2 hours) may require Store Manager to stay late or come in early; absorbed as overtime if needed
- Manual operations kit: ~PHP 2,000 per store (one-time, refreshed annually)

## W581. Store-Level Vendor Representative Access & Activity Management

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Vendor representative (promodizer, merchandiser, product trainer, brand ambassador) arrives at store |
| **Frequency** | ~5–15 vendor reps per store per day; ~1,000–3,000 visits chain-wide daily |
| **Volume** | ~150–200 active vendor representatives across 200 stores |
| **Owner** | Store Manager |
| **Participants** | Department Supervisors, Security Guard, Vendor Representative |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Vendor rep arrives at store entrance; presents government-issued ID to Security Guard; Guard verifies vendor rep is on approved vendor representative list (maintained by Store Manager and updated monthly per vendor coordination) | Security Guard | Store Manager | 2 min |
| 2 | Security Guard issues temporary visitor badge with: vendor name, company, date, time-in; badge is time-limited (max 8 hours); vendor rep signs visitor log | Security Guard | — | 1 min |
| 3 | Vendor rep directed to relevant Department Supervisor for activity assignment; Supervisor reviews planned activities (vendor visit schedule provided by Buyer/Category Manager in advance via system) | Department Supervisor | Store Manager | 3 min |
| 4 | Department Supervisor assigns specific tasks: (a) shelf stocking/replenishment for vendor products, (b) product demonstration for customers, (c) inventory audit of vendor SKUs, (d) product knowledge training for store staff, (e) promotional display setup per W262, (f) price tag update per W63 | Department Supervisor | — | 2 min |
| 5 | Vendor rep executes assigned activities under Department Supervisor oversight; time tracked via badge scan (check-in/check-out at department zones) | Vendor Representative | Department Supervisor | 2–6 hours |
| 6 | Activity output verification: Department Supervisor reviews completed work — shelf stocking completed, demo materials cleaned up, inventory counts reconciled, training attendance documented | Department Supervisor | — | 5–10 min |
| 7 | Incident reporting: if vendor rep causes any issue (customer complaint, safety violation, merchandise damage, unauthorized area access), Department Supervisor documents incident and reports to Store Manager | Department Supervisor | Store Manager | 5 min per incident |
| 8 | Vendor rep checks out at security desk: badge returned, time-out recorded; Security Guard collects badge and signs out visitor log | Security Guard | — | 1 min |
| 9 | Weekly: Store Manager compiles vendor rep activity summary (visits, hours, activities completed, incidents); shared with Buyer/Category Manager for vendor coordination per W269 | Store Manager | — | 15 min/week |

**Total time per visit**: ~5 min store overhead + 2–6 hours vendor activity + 5–10 min verification

### System Touchpoints (W581 — Vendor Representative Access & Activity Management)
- Visitor management system (badge issuance, time tracking)
- W71 (store physical security — access control and visitor management)
- W269 (vendor promodizer management — vendor coordination)
- W449 (promodizer labor compliance — DOLE D.O. 174 compliance)
- W63 (shelf label & price tag — vendor-assisted price updates)
- W262 (store promotional setup — vendor-assisted display build)
- Vendor representative master list maintained in vendor master per W287

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Unscheduled vendor visits disrupt operations** — vendor reps arriving without prior coordination may not have planned activities and take Department Supervisor time away from customer service; mitigated by advance scheduling requirement (vendor must coordinate visit 24 hours in advance via Buyer)
- **Vendor reps accessing unauthorized areas** — vendor reps may wander into stockroom, office, or receiving areas beyond their authorized zone; mitigated by zone-specific badge access and Security Guard monitoring
- **Vendor reps giving incorrect product information** — vendor reps may provide biased or inaccurate product information favoring their brand; mitigated by requiring Department Supervisor presence during customer demos and BuildRight product knowledge training materials as authoritative source
- **Labor compliance risk per DOLE D.O. 174** — vendor promodizers working in stores may be deemed BuildRight employees under Philippine labor law if not properly documented; mitigated by W449 compliance protocol (vendor-employed, vendor-paid, clear contractor status documentation)

### Staffing Implication
- Security Guard visitor processing: ~2 min × 10 visits/day = ~20 min/day (absorbed)
- Department Supervisor oversight: ~10 min per visit × 10 visits/day = ~100 min/day (absorbed)
- Store Manager weekly summary: ~15 min/week (absorbed)
- No incremental headcount needed

---

## W582. Store-Level Fire Drill Execution & Documentation

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Scheduled semi-annual fire drill; or unscheduled drill required by BFP after incident or inspection |
| **Frequency** | Semi-annual per store (once every 6 months); alternating morning/afternoon shifts |
| **Volume** | 200 stores × 2 drills/year = 400 drills/year; each drill involves all staff on duty (~29 employees per store) |
| **Owner** | Store Manager |
| **Participants** | All store staff on duty, any customers present, Safety Officer (HQ advisory) |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Drill scheduling** (T-1 week): Store Manager schedules drill date with Regional Manager approval; select off-peak hours (typically 2:00–3:00 PM weekday or 9:00–10:00 AM weekend); alternate morning/afternoon between drills; notify BFP local fire station if required by local ordinance | Store Manager | Regional Manager | 15 min |
| 2 | **Staff briefing** (T-1 day): brief all staff on evacuation routes, assembly point location, fire wardens per zone, and drill procedures; do NOT announce exact drill time to maintain realistic response | Store Manager | — | 15 min |
| 3 | **Drill day — activation**: designated fire warden activates fire alarm; timer starts | Fire Warden | Store Manager | 1 min |
| 4 | **Evacuation execution**: (a) Sales Associates guide customers to nearest exit; priority: customers near fire hazards (paint, chemicals, lumber), (b) Cashiers secure cash drawers (close and lock), (c) Stock Associates in backroom/receiving dock evacuate via designated rear exits, (d) Fire wardens sweep assigned zones to verify complete evacuation (check restrooms, break room, fitting areas), (e) Store Manager or designee checks for anyone requiring assistance (PWD, elderly) | All staff | Store Manager | 3–5 min (target) |
| 5 | **Assembly point accountability**: all staff report to designated assembly point (outside building, minimum 50m from structure); Department Supervisors conduct biometric/roll call verification against staff on-duty roster; note any missing persons | Department Supervisors | Store Manager | 3–5 min |
| 6 | **All-clear signal**: Store Manager confirms all persons accounted for; announces all-clear and directs orderly building re-entry | Store Manager | — | 2 min |
| 7 | **Post-drill debrief** (within 30 min of drill): (a) evacuation time vs. target (< 5 minutes), (b) issues identified (blocked exits, confused staff, missing persons, customer resistance), (c) corrective actions with owners and deadlines, (d) staff questions and feedback | Store Manager | Safety Officer | 15 min |
| 8 | **BFP-compliant documentation**: complete fire drill report form: (a) drill date and time, (b) number of participants (staff + estimated customers), (c) evacuation time, (d) issues and corrective actions, (e) fire warden names, (f) assembly point confirmation, (g) Store Manager signature; file in store records and submit copy to Safety Officer (HQ) for BFP FSIC compliance per W476 | Store Manager | Safety Officer | 20 min |
| 9 | **Corrective action tracking**: any issues identified during drill are entered as corrective actions in system; tracked to completion before next drill; unresolved corrective actions escalated to Regional Manager | Store Manager | Regional Manager | 5 min |

**Total time per drill**: ~60–75 min (15 min briefing [day before] + 15 min evacuation + 15 min debrief + 20 min documentation + 10 min overhead)

### System Touchpoints (W582 — Fire Drill Execution & Documentation)
- Fire drill documentation template (BFP-compliant format)
- W476 (BFP fire safety inspection certificate — drill compliance as FSIC evidence)
- W141 (workplace safety inspection — annual safety audit includes drill review)
- W330 (in-store emergency response — actual emergency protocol)
- W436 (annual OHS statutory reporting — drill completion as OHS evidence)
- W579 (equipment safety — equipment power-down during evacuation)
- Fire Code of the Philippines (RA 9514) compliance documentation

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Customer resistance to evacuation** — some customers may refuse to leave their shopping carts or queue position during drill; mitigated by clear PA announcement "This is a fire drill — please follow staff to exits" and staff training to firmly but politely escort customers
- **Evacuation of lumber yard and outdoor areas** — these areas have different evacuation challenges (open space, heavy materials, forklift traffic); mitigated by separate yard evacuation procedure and designated outdoor assembly point away from vehicle traffic
- **Drill documentation quality varies by Store Manager** — some Store Managers produce minimal documentation that may not satisfy BFP during FSIC inspection; mitigated by standardized BFP-compliant template and HQ Safety Officer review of all drill reports
- **Drill frequency may be insufficient** — semi-annual drills mean staff may not remember procedures after 6 months; mitigated by quarterly tabletop exercises (15 min discussion-based) in between full drills

### Staffing Implication
- Covered by existing store staff; no incremental headcount
- ~200 stores × 75 min × 2 drills/year = ~500 staff-hours/year chain-wide
- Safety Officer (HQ) reviews 400 drill reports/year = ~40 hours/year (absorbed)

---

## W583. Store-Level Seasonal Promotional Transition & Display Reset

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Promotional transition brief received from HQ (typically T-3 days before new promotion start) |
| **Frequency** | Bi-monthly major sales + monthly hot deals + 4 seasonal transitions = ~20–24 transitions/year per store |
| **Volume** | 200 stores × 22 transitions/year = 4,400 transition events/year |
| **Owner** | Store Manager |
| **Participants** | Department Supervisors, Stock Associates, Visual Merchandising Associate (larger stores) |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Receive transition brief** (T-3 days): HQ distributes promotional transition brief via W571 communication module — contains: old promo teardown instructions, new promo planograms, new signage, new shelf tags, POS configuration changes, display build instructions, stock pick list for promotional items | Store Manager | Store Ops Director | 10 min (review) |
| 2 | **Old promo teardown** (T-1 day or after store close on last day of old promo): (a) remove old promotional signage and shelf talkers, (b) dismantle old promotional displays (endcaps, power wings, feature tables), (c) remove old promotional shelf tags per W63, (d) return unsold promotional stock to regular shelf locations or backroom | Department Supervisors | Store Manager | 60–90 min |
| 3 | **New planogram execution** for promotional displays: (a) execute new planogram per W86 for endcaps, feature tables, and promotional bays, (b) install new display fixtures if provided (risers, bins, signage holders), (c) position promotional stock from backroom pick list | Stock Associates | Department Supervisor | 60–120 min |
| 4 | **Signage and shelf tag installation**: (a) install new promotional signage (hanging signs, shelf talkers, window posters, department banners), (b) print and install new shelf tags per W181 for all promotional SKUs, (c) update digital signage per W504 if applicable | Stock Associates | Department Supervisor | 30–45 min |
| 5 | **POS configuration verification**: verify all promotional prices are loaded in POS system per W523 — scan sample promotional items to confirm correct promotional price, validate quantity break pricing, verify bundle/combo pricing | Lead Cashier | Store Manager | 15 min |
| 6 | **Endcap and feature display build**: construct feature displays for high-impact promotional items (e.g., power tool festival, paint sale, tile showcase) — includes display fixture assembly, product placement, price card insertion, and signage | Department Supervisors | Store Manager | 30–60 min per display |
| 7 | **Promotional stock placement and fronting**: place all promotional stock on designated shelves and displays; front and face all items; verify stock levels match allocation | Stock Associates | Department Supervisor | 30–45 min |
| 8 | **Pre-opening walkthrough verification**: Store Manager walks all promotional displays and zones — verify: (a) correct planogram execution, (b) all signage in place, (c) correct pricing on shelf tags, (d) POS prices match shelf tags, (e) displays are visually appealing and safe, (f) no old promotional material remaining | Store Manager | — | 15–20 min |
| 9 | **Photographic documentation**: Department Supervisor photographs each promotional display and endcap; uploads to system for HQ compliance audit; photos time-stamped and geo-tagged | Department Supervisor | Store Manager | 10 min |

**Total time per transition**: ~4–7 hours per store (spread across 2–3 days around transition date)

### System Touchpoints (W583 — Seasonal Promotional Transition & Display Reset)
- W13 (promotions & pricing execution — promotional calendar and pricing)
- W262 (store promotional setup & visual merchandising — setup instructions)
- W523 (POS promotional terminal setup — POS price verification)
- W554 (shelf replenishment — stock placement and fronting)
- W86 (planogram compliance — planogram execution for displays)
- W63 (shelf label & price tag — tag removal and installation)
- W181 (price tag printing — new promotional tag printing)
- W504 (digital signage — electronic signage update)
- W571 (daily communication — transition brief distribution)
- Photo evidence upload system for HQ compliance audit

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Insufficient promotional stock for display build** — allocated stock may not arrive in time or may be insufficient for all 200 stores; mitigated by pre-positioning per W57 and substitution protocol (display with available stock, backorder sign for remaining)
- **Planogram execution quality varies by store** — smaller stores with fewer staff may not execute complex planograms correctly; mitigated by photographic documentation requirement and Regional Manager spot-check during first week of new promo
- **POS pricing errors on first day of new promo** — if promotional prices are not correctly loaded in POS, customer disputes occur; mitigated by mandatory Step 5 POS verification and W553 pricing error detection
- **Old promotional stock not returned to shelf** — unsold promo stock left in backroom instead of returned to regular shelf location creates phantom inventory; mitigated by teardown checklist including stock disposition step and backroom audit on T+1

### Staffing Implication
- Covered by existing Department Supervisors and Stock Associates; no incremental headcount
- ~200 stores × 5.5 hours average × 22 transitions/year = ~24,200 person-hours/year
- Store Manager walkthrough: ~20 min × 22 transitions/year = ~7 hours/year per Store Manager
- Photo documentation: absorbed by Department Supervisor duties
