## W601. Store-Level Daily HR Operations & People Management

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Start of store business day; ongoing throughout operating hours |
| **Frequency** | Daily per store |
| **Volume** | ~29 employees per store; 200 stores |
| **Owner** | Store Manager |
| **Participants** | Store Manager, Department Supervisors, employees, HR Coordinator (HQ) |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Store Manager reviews daily attendance report from biometric/RFID system; identifies absences, late arrivals, and missing punches | Store Manager | — | 10 min |
| 2 | For unplanned absences: Department Supervisor contacts employee to determine reason (sick leave, emergency, AWOL); system auto-flags "absent without notification" if no contact by 1 hour after shift start | Department Supervisor | Store Manager | 5 min |
| 3 | Leave approval for same-day requests: Store Manager approves/denies based on department coverage; emergency leave auto-approved per policy; system updates attendance record and payroll input | Store Manager | — | 3 min/request |
| 4 | Overtime authorization: Department Supervisor submits overtime request with business justification (unexpected customer traffic, delivery backlog, inventory count); Store Manager approves/denies; system tracks overtime budget utilization vs. monthly allocation per W602 | Department Supervisor | Store Manager | 5 min |
| 5 | Shift swap requests: Employee A initiates swap with Employee B (same skill set); both confirm; Department Supervisor verifies coverage; Store Manager approves; system updates schedule and attendance rules | Employee | Store Manager | 5 min |
| 6 | Employee relations quick-check: Store Manager conducts brief check-in with any employee flagged for attendance issues, performance concerns (per W72), or personal matters affecting work; documents outcome | Store Manager | — | 5 min/employee |
| 7 | System generates daily HR summary: attendance rate, overtime hours, leave usage, open HR action items; auto-submits to Regional Manager HR dashboard | System | — | Automated |
| 8 | Weekly (Fridays): Store Manager reviews upcoming week schedule for coverage gaps; submits schedule change requests to HR Coordinator; confirms weekend staffing adequacy | Store Manager | — | 15 min |

### System Touchpoints
- ERP HR module: attendance integration, leave management, overtime tracking, shift scheduling
- Biometric/RFID system: real-time attendance feed
- Mobile app: Store Manager HR dashboard, employee self-service for leave/swap requests
- Payroll module: daily payroll input accumulation

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Attendance system downtime**: biometric failure requires manual punch entry with supervisor confirmation per W561; mitigated by backup RFID badge scanning
- **Overtime budget overruns**: store-level overtime exceeding monthly allocation triggers Regional Manager review; mitigated by real-time overtime budget tracking in Step 4
- **Inconsistent leave approval**: Store Managers may apply leave policies inconsistently across 200 stores; mitigated by system-enforced policy rules and Regional Manager audit
- **Employee relations sensitivity**: Store Managers may lack HR training for employee relations issues; mitigated by HR Coordinator hotline and escalation protocol to HR Business Partner

### Staffing Implication
- **Store Manager**: ~30 min/day on HR operations (attendance, leave, overtime) + ~15 min/week on schedule review = ~3.5 hours/week. Absorbed within existing role.
- **Department Supervisors**: ~5 min/day on overtime requests and attendance follow-up = ~30 min/week per supervisor. Absorbed within existing role.
- **HR Coordinator (HQ)**: ~2 hours/week reviewing store-level HR reports and handling escalations from 200 stores. Absorbed within existing role.

---

## W602. Store-Level Labor Cost Monitoring & Overtime Budget Control

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Start of pay period (semi-monthly); weekly review trigger |
| **Frequency** | Daily monitoring; weekly review; semi-monthly reconciliation |
| **Volume** | 200 stores; ~5,800 store employees; ~PHP 180M/month store payroll |
| **Owner** | Store Manager |
| **Participants** | Store Manager, Department Supervisors, Regional Manager, HR Coordinator, Finance Analyst |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | System calculates daily labor cost flash: total regular hours × hourly rate + overtime hours × premium rate + holiday premium (if applicable) + night differential; compares to daily budget allocation | System | — | Automated |
| 2 | Store Manager reviews daily labor cost as % of daily revenue on morning dashboard; target: labor cost ≤ 12% of store revenue; amber alert at > 14%; red alert at > 16% | Store Manager | — | 5 min |
| 3 | If amber/red alert: Store Manager reviews overtime authorization log (per W601 Step 4) and identifies departments exceeding allocation; implements corrective action (reduce remaining week overtime, adjust schedules) | Store Manager | Regional Manager | 10 min |
| 4 | Weekly (Monday): Store Manager reviews prior week labor cost report with breakdown by department, overtime category (customer-driven vs. operational), and trend vs. prior 4 weeks | Store Manager | — | 15 min |
| 5 | Department Supervisors submit overtime forecast for upcoming week with business justification; Store Manager approves aggregate overtime budget allocation per department | Department Supervisor | Store Manager | 10 min |
| 6 | Semi-monthly: Store Manager reconciles actual payroll (from W10) vs. store labor budget; investigates variances > 5%; submits variance explanation to Regional Manager and Finance Analyst | Store Manager | Regional Manager | 30 min |
| 7 | Finance Analyst consolidates 200-store labor cost flash report for CFO review; identifies top/bottom 10 stores by labor cost %; flags chronic over-budget stores for remediation | Finance Analyst | CFO | 2 hours |
| 8 | Quarterly: Finance and HR review store-level labor budgets against actual; adjust allocation formulas based on store revenue, format size, and seasonal patterns | Finance Analyst | CFO | 4 hours/quarter |

### System Touchpoints
- ERP HR/Payroll module: hours worked, overtime, premium calculations, budget allocation
- ERP Finance module: labor cost GL posting, budget vs. actual reporting
- BI dashboard: store-level labor cost flash, trend analysis, ranking

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Revenue volatility distorts labor cost %**: stores with revenue dips (weather, local events) show elevated labor cost % even with controlled hours; mitigated by tracking absolute hours vs. budget in addition to percentage
- **Philippine holiday complexity**: with 12 regular holidays and 9 special non-working days, holiday premium calculations are complex and error-prone; mitigated by automated holiday calendar in payroll module per HR-011
- **Delayed overtime data**: if overtime approvals are not entered same-day, labor cost flash is inaccurate; mitigated by system requirement for same-day overtime entry in W601

### Staffing Implication
- **Store Manager**: ~20 min/day on labor cost review + ~15 min/week on weekly review + ~30 min semi-monthly reconciliation = ~3 hours/week. Absorbed within existing role.
- **Finance Analyst**: ~2 hours semi-monthly on consolidation + ~4 hours/quarter on budget review. Absorbed within existing FP&A role.

---

## W603. Store-Level Employee Disciplinary Process & DOLE Due Process Compliance

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Employee policy violation, attendance infraction, performance issue, misconduct, or customer complaint against employee |
| **Frequency** | As needed; estimated ~80-120 incidents/month across 200 stores |
| **Volume** | ~0.4-0.6 incidents per store per month |
| **Owner** | Store Manager |
| **Participants** | Store Manager, Department Supervisor, employee, HR Coordinator (HQ), HR Business Partner, witness(es) |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Incident occurs or is reported: Department Supervisor or Store Manager documents initial observation with date, time, location, nature of incident, witnesses; system creates disciplinary case record | Dept. Supervisor / Store Manager | Store Manager | 10 min |
| 2 | Store Manager conducts preliminary fact-finding: interviews employee, witnesses, reviews CCTV footage (per W207), reviews system transaction logs if POS-related | Store Manager | — | 30 min |
| 3 | Store Manager classifies offense severity per policy matrix: Minor (tardiness, dress code, minor policy violation), Moderate (insubordination, repeated minor offenses, customer service failure), Severe (theft, fraud, violence, safety violation, gross misconduct) | Store Manager | HR Coordinator | 5 min |
| 4 | **For Minor offenses — Verbal Warning**: Store Manager conducts private counseling with employee; documents verbal warning in system with date, nature, and improvement expectation; system tracks verbal warning count (3 verbal warnings = 1 written warning) | Store Manager | — | 15 min |
| 5 | **For Moderate offenses or repeated Minor — Written Warning**: Store Manager drafts written notice of explanation (NTE) per DOLE two-notice rule; employee given 5 business days to submit written explanation; NTE references specific company policy violated and incident details | Store Manager | HR Coordinator | 30 min |
| 6 | Employee submits written explanation or waives right to respond; Store Manager and HR Coordinator evaluate explanation; if explanation is unsatisfactory, proceed to Step 7 | Employee / HR Coordinator | Store Manager | 15 min |
| 7 | **Notice of Decision (NOD)**: Store Manager issues written decision with disciplinary action: written warning, suspension (1-30 days per severity), or termination recommendation for severe offenses; HR Coordinator reviews for DOLE compliance before issuance | Store Manager | HR Business Partner | 30 min |
| 8 | **For Severe offenses — Preventive Suspension**: If investigation requires more than 5 days, employee placed on preventive suspension (max 30 days per Labor Code) with pay; Store Manager coordinates with HR Business Partner for investigation | Store Manager | HR Business Partner | 15 min |
| 9 | **For termination cases**: HR Business Partner conducts independent review; ensures two-notice rule compliance, just cause per Article 297 Labor Code, and due process documentation; Legal Counsel reviews if employee is regular status; final termination decision by VP Legal & Compliance | HR Business Partner | VP Legal | 60 min |
| 10 | All disciplinary actions recorded in employee master (W292) with immutable audit trail; system flags employees with active disciplinary actions for promotion/transfer eligibility check | System | — | Automated |
| 11 | Monthly: HR Coordinator generates store-level disciplinary summary report; Regional Manager reviews for pattern analysis (specific stores with high incident rates may indicate management or training issues) | HR Coordinator | Regional Manager | 2 hours |

### System Touchpoints
- ERP HR module: disciplinary case management, employee master data, warning tracking
- Document management: NTE/NOD template generation and storage (W255)
- CCTV system: footage retrieval for investigation (per W207)
- POS transaction logs: evidence for POS-related incidents (per W522)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **DOLE due process non-compliance**: failure to follow two-notice rule (NTE → NOD) or denial of opportunity to explain results in illegal dismissal finding; mitigated by system-enforced workflow with mandatory NTE issuance before NOD
- **Inconsistent discipline across stores**: Store Managers may apply different standards; mitigated by policy matrix with offense severity classification and HR Coordinator review in Steps 5 and 7
- **Retaliation claims**: employees may claim disciplinary action was retaliatory for union activity or whistleblower reports (W79); mitigated by HR Business Partner independent review for termination cases
- **Witness cooperation**: witnesses may be reluctant to provide statements against coworkers; mitigated by confidential reporting option and anti-retaliation policy

### Staffing Implication
- **Store Manager**: ~30 min per incident (investigation + documentation); ~10-15 hours/month across all incidents. Absorbed within existing role.
- **HR Coordinator**: ~30 min per incident for compliance review; ~5 hours/month. Absorbed within existing role.
- **HR Business Partner**: ~1 hour per termination case; ~4-6 hours/month for severe offense reviews. Absorbed within existing role.

---

## W604. Store-Level Customer Experience Standards & Daily Service Operations

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Store operating hours begin (part of W5A store opening) |
| **Frequency** | Daily per store |
| **Volume** | ~14,000 customer transactions per store per month; ~467 per day |
| **Owner** | Store Manager |
| **Participants** | Store Manager, Department Supervisors, all customer-facing staff, Customer Service Counter staff |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Store Manager or Assistant Store Manager conducts 5-minute CX briefing during morning huddle: highlights today's CX focus (greeting standard, wait time, service recovery from yesterday's issues); shares one CX success story | Store Manager | — | 5 min |
| 2 | Department Supervisors verify CX touchpoints are ready: checkout areas clean and organized, customer service counter staffed, returns area clear, BOPIS pickup zone staged, promotional signage accurate | Dept. Supervisor | Store Manager | 10 min |
| 3 | Throughout the day: customer-facing staff execute CX standards — greeting within 10 seconds of customer entering department, proactive offer of assistance, product knowledge support, and farewell at checkout | All Staff | Dept. Supervisor | Ongoing |
| 4 | Customer Service Counter: staff handle walk-in inquiries, returns (W12), BOPIS pickups (W11), special orders (W545), warranty claims (W33), and complaints (W41) per SLA targets; system tracks queue wait times | CS Counter Staff | Store Manager | Ongoing |
| 5 | **Service recovery**: when a customer expresses dissatisfaction (complaint, long wait, stock-out): (a) frontline staff acknowledges immediately, (b) offers immediate resolution per empowerment guidelines (10% discount, free delivery, expedited order), (c) if unresolved, escalates to Department Supervisor then Store Manager, (d) system logs service recovery action | Frontline Staff | Store Manager | 5-15 min |
| 6 | Mid-day: Store Manager walks sales floor for 15-minute CX observation — staff greeting compliance, department appearance, customer queue lengths, bathroom cleanliness, parking lot order | Store Manager | — | 15 min |
| 7 | End of day: system generates CX daily dashboard — average queue wait time, service recovery count, customer feedback scores (if collected via W608), BOPIS pickup SLA compliance, complaint count | System | — | Automated |
| 8 | Weekly: Store Manager reviews CX metrics in weekly operations review (W575); identifies departments needing CX improvement; assigns CX coaching targets | Store Manager | — | 15 min |

### System Touchpoints
- ERP customer module: complaint tracking, service recovery logging, BOPIS SLA
- POS system: queue wait time analytics, checkout speed metrics
- BI dashboard: store-level CX daily dashboard, weekly CX scorecard
- Mobile app: Store Manager CX observation checklist

### Pain Points / Risks
- **CX inconsistency across 200 stores**: without daily enforcement, CX standards drift; mitigated by morning briefing, mid-day walkthrough, and mystery shopping program (W566)
- **Service recovery empowerment limits**: frontline staff unsure how much they can offer without approval; mitigated by clear empowerment matrix and system-enforced authorization tiers
- **Customer feedback fatigue**: daily feedback collection may annoy customers; mitigated by rotating feedback methods (SMS survey, QR code, in-app) and limiting to random sample

### Staffing Implication
- **Store Manager**: ~20 min/day on CX operations (briefing + walkthrough) + ~15 min/week on CX review = ~2.5 hours/week. Absorbed within existing role.
- **Department Supervisors**: ~10 min/day on CX touchpoint verification. Absorbed within existing role.
- **No incremental headcount**: CX standards are embedded in daily operations of existing staff.

## W605. Return Fraud Detection & Serial Returner Management

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Return transaction processed at POS (W12) or system generates return pattern alert |
| **Frequency** | Continuous; ~56,000 returns/month across 200 stores (~2% return rate) |
| **Volume** | Fraudulent returns estimated at 3-5% of total returns = ~1,700-2,800/month |
| **Owner** | Loss Prevention Officer (LPO) |
| **Participants** | LPO, Store Manager, POS Cashier, LP Analyst (HQ), Customer Service Counter staff |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | System continuously monitors return transactions for fraud indicators: (a) same customer > 3 returns in 30 days, (b) returns without receipt > 2 in 30 days, (c) high-value returns (> PHP 10,000), (d) return of commonly wardrobed items (power tools, appliances), (e) return at different store than purchase (cross-store returns), (f) receipt appears altered or duplicated | System | — | Automated |
| 2 | When fraud indicator triggered: system generates alert to LPO dashboard and Store Manager mobile app with alert classification: Yellow (monitoring), Orange (likely fraud), Red (confirmed pattern) | System | — | Automated |
| 3 | **Yellow alerts**: LPO adds customer to watch list; reviews transaction history; no customer-facing action | LPO | — | 5 min |
| 4 | **Orange alerts**: LPO investigates customer's full return history across all 200 stores; reviews CCTV footage of return transaction; checks loyalty account for anomalies; prepares case summary | LPO | LP Analyst | 20 min |
| 5 | **Red alerts (confirmed pattern)**: LPO coordinates with LP Analyst for cross-store pattern analysis; if fraud confirmed: (a) customer flagged in system with return restriction (receipt-only returns, manager override required), (b) if employee collusion suspected, escalates per W248, (c) if organized retail crime, escalates per W490 | LPO | LP Analyst | 30 min |
| 6 | Store-level return policy enforcement: for flagged customers, Cashier receives system prompt requiring manager authorization for return; Customer Service Counter staff trained on receipt verification, product condition assessment, and time-since-purchase validation | Cashier / CS Staff | Store Manager | 5 min/return |
| 7 | Weekly: LP Analyst generates return fraud analytics report — top serial returners, fraud rate by store, fraud rate by category, common fraud methods, recovery value from fraud prevention | LP Analyst | LP Manager | 2 hours |
| 8 | Monthly: LP Manager reviews return policy effectiveness; recommends threshold adjustments to system fraud detection rules; coordinates with Merchandising on high-return-rate SKUs for root cause analysis (quality issues vs. fraud) | LP Manager | VP Merchandising | 1 hour |

### System Touchpoints
- ERP POS module: return transaction logging, receipt validation, customer return history
- LP analytics module: fraud detection rules engine, alert management, case tracking
- CCTV system: footage retrieval for return transaction review
- Customer master: return flag, watch list status, restriction enforcement
- BI module: return fraud analytics dashboard, trend analysis

### Pain Points / Risks
- **False positives**: legitimate frequent returners (contractors with project overages) may be flagged; mitigated by trade account exemption from standard return frequency thresholds and human review before restrictions
- **Cross-store fraud**: customer returns items at multiple stores to avoid per-store thresholds; mitigated by enterprise-wide return history query in real-time
- **Receipt fraud**: counterfeit or duplicated receipts used for returns; mitigated by BIR-compliant sequential receipt numbering (W540) and barcode-based receipt validation at POS
- **Customer relations impact**: denying returns to flagged customers may cause confrontations; mitigated by discrete system-enforced restrictions and Store Manager authority to override with documentation

### Staffing Implication
- **Loss Prevention Officer (per store)**: ~15 min/day reviewing alerts = ~1.5 hours/week. Absorbed within LP daily routine (W562).
- **LP Analyst (HQ)**: ~2 hours/week on return fraud analytics. Absorbed within existing LP analytics role.
- **LP Manager**: ~1 hour/month on policy review. Absorbed within existing role.

## W606. Store-Level Water & Utility Conservation Operations

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Start of billing cycle; daily monitoring |
| **Frequency** | Daily monitoring; monthly review per utility bill cycle |
| **Volume** | 200 stores; ~PHP 80,000-150,000/month utility cost per store (electricity, water, gas for forklift) |
| **Owner** | Store Manager |
| **Participants** | Store Manager, Department Supervisors, Maintenance Staff, Finance Analyst, Facilities Coordinator |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | System auto-captures utility meter readings via IoT sensors (electricity, water) where installed; manual meter readings captured by Maintenance Staff daily at stores without IoT | Maintenance Staff | Store Manager | 5 min |
| 2 | System calculates daily utility consumption vs. budget allocation; displays consumption per sqm benchmark on Store Manager dashboard; alerts on > 15% daily variance from 30-day average | System | — | Automated |
| 3 | Store Manager conducts daily conservation walkthrough: (a) verify air conditioning set to 24°C policy, (b) check for water leaks in garden center and restrooms, (c) verify non-selling area lights off during daytime, (d) check forklift charging schedule (off-peak hours), (e) verify lumber yard sprinkler timer operation | Store Manager | — | 10 min |
| 4 | If leak or waste detected: Store Manager creates maintenance work order (per W47) with urgency classification; for water emergencies (burst pipe), activates immediate shutoff and emergency repair protocol | Store Manager | Facilities Coordinator | 5 min |
| 5 | Monthly: Finance Analyst receives utility bills per store; system auto-compares bill to meter reading data; flags discrepancies > 10% for investigation (billing error, meter malfunction, theft) | Finance Analyst | Controller | 30 min/store |
| 6 | Monthly: Store Manager reviews utility cost as % of store operating budget (W489); identifies top 3 utility cost drivers; implements conservation action items for next month | Store Manager | Regional Manager | 20 min |
| 7 | Quarterly: Facilities Coordinator conducts utility benchmark analysis across 200 stores — identifies bottom 10% performers (highest utility cost per sqm); coordinates energy audit for worst-performing stores; shares best practices from top 10% performers | Facilities Coordinator | COO | 4 hours |
| 8 | Annually: Facilities Coordinator evaluates utility cost reduction projects (LED retrofit, solar panel installation per W173, water recycling for garden centers, inverter AC upgrade); presents business case to CFO for capital approval (per W21) | Facilities Coordinator | CFO | 8 hours |

### System Touchpoints
- ERP Facilities module: meter reading capture, utility consumption tracking, benchmark analytics
- ERP Finance module: utility bill processing, cost allocation to store P&L (W489)
- IoT platform: real-time utility monitoring and alerting (where installed)
- BI dashboard: store-level utility cost trends, benchmarking, conservation tracking

### Pain Points / Risks
- **IoT sensor gaps**: not all 200 stores have IoT metering; manual readings are inconsistent and may be skipped; mitigated by daily reading checklist with compliance tracking
- **Utility billing errors**: Philippine utility providers (MERALCO, Davao Light, VECO) occasionally issue incorrect bills; mitigated by automated bill-to-meter comparison in Step 5
- **Tenant-shared utilities**: some stores share utility infrastructure with other tenants in commercial complexes; mitigated by lease-level utility allocation agreements tracked in W117

### Staffing Implication
- **Store Manager**: ~10 min/day on conservation walkthrough + ~20 min/month on utility review = ~5 hours/month. Absorbed within existing role.
- **Maintenance Staff**: ~5 min/day on meter readings = ~2.5 hours/month. Absorbed within existing role.
- **Finance Analyst**: ~30 min/store/month × 200 stores = ~100 hours/month; distributed across FP&A team (3-4 analysts) = ~25 hours each. Absorbed within existing roles.

## W607. Store-Level Product Demo & Trial Station Daily Operations

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Store operating hours begin; part of daily store operations |
| **Frequency** | Daily per store |
| **Volume** | ~2-4 demo stations per store (power tool testing, paint mixing, tile/lumber cutting, appliance testing) |
| **Owner** | Department Supervisor |
| **Participants** | Department Supervisor, Sales Associate, Stock Associate, Maintenance Staff |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Sales Associate powers on demo stations during store opening (W5A); verifies equipment operational — power tool testing station (drill, sander, circular saw with safety guards), paint mixing station (colorant levels, tinting machine calibration), appliance display (plugged in, functioning) | Sales Associate | Dept. Supervisor | 10 min |
| 2 | Sales Associate verifies demo station supplies: safety goggles and ear protection available, test material stock (wood scraps for cutting station, paint sample cards, tile samples for display), product information brochures stocked | Sales Associate | — | 5 min |
| 3 | Throughout the day: Sales Associate conducts product demonstrations for interested customers; demonstrates power tool features, explains paint mixing options (W168), shows appliance features; logs demonstration count in system for vendor reporting | Sales Associate | — | 5-15 min/demo |
| 4 | Customer trial: Sales Associate supervises customer hands-on trial with mandatory safety briefing (eye protection, proper grip, emergency shutoff location); customer signs trial waiver for power tools | Sales Associate | Dept. Supervisor | 10 min |
| 5 | Mid-day: Department Supervisor spot-checks demo stations for cleanliness, safety compliance, and supply levels; replenishes test materials as needed | Dept. Supervisor | — | 10 min |
| 6 | End of day: Sales Associate powers down demo equipment; cleans station; secures power tools in locked cabinet; records demo station usage count and any equipment issues in system | Sales Associate | Dept. Supervisor | 10 min |
| 7 | Weekly: Department Supervisor reviews demo station usage analytics and sales conversion rate (demos conducted → products sold); identifies underperforming stations for repositioning or removal; reports to Store Manager | Dept. Supervisor | Store Manager | 15 min |
| 8 | Monthly: Merchandising team reviews demo station effectiveness across 200 stores; updates demo product selection based on new arrivals (W564) and seasonal trends; coordinates with vendor for demo unit replacement per W524 | Category Manager | VP Merchandising | 2 hours |

### System Touchpoints
- ERP POS module: demo unit sales tracking, demo-to-sale conversion analytics
- ERP Maintenance module: equipment issue logging, preventive maintenance scheduling
- Mobile app: demo count logging, supply checklist
- BI dashboard: store-level demo station usage, conversion rates, equipment uptime

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Safety liability**: power tool demo stations carry injury risk; mitigated by mandatory safety briefing, safety equipment provision, and customer trial waiver
- **Equipment wear and tear**: demo units experience heavy use and degrade; mitigated by scheduled preventive maintenance and quarterly demo unit replacement program
- **Low demo-to-sale conversion**: poorly positioned or maintained demo stations may not generate sales; mitigated by weekly usage analytics and station optimization
- **Vendor cost recovery**: some demo units are vendor-supplied; tracking vendor-provided vs. company-purchased demo inventory requires separate SKU treatment per W97

### Staffing Implication
- **Sales Associate**: ~20 min/day on demo station operations (opening + closing + demonstrations). Absorbed within existing role.
- **Department Supervisor**: ~10 min/day spot-check + ~15 min/week review. Absorbed within existing role.
- **No incremental headcount**: demo operations are part of daily selling activities.

## W608. Store-Level Customer Feedback Collection & Daily CX Pulse Monitoring

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer completes transaction at POS or visits store |
| **Frequency** | Daily per store |
| **Volume** | ~467 transactions/store/day; target ~5-10 feedback responses/store/day via random sampling |
| **Owner** | Store Manager |
| **Participants** | Store Manager, Customer Service Counter staff, CX Analyst (HQ) |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | System randomly selects ~2% of daily transactions for feedback invitation; customer receives SMS/email survey link 2 hours after transaction (configurable); survey is 3-question micro-survey: overall satisfaction (1-5), recommend likelihood (NPS 0-10), free-text comment | System | — | Automated |
| 2 | In-store: Customer Service Counter displays QR code for instant feedback; BOPIS pickup customers receive feedback prompt at counter | CS Counter Staff | — | 1 min |
| 3 | System aggregates daily feedback into CX Pulse dashboard: NPS score, satisfaction rating, sentiment analysis of free-text comments, trending keywords (positive and negative) | System | — | Automated |
| 4 | Store Manager reviews CX Pulse dashboard as part of morning routine; focuses on: (a) previous day's NPS change, (b) negative feedback requiring follow-up, (c) recurring themes by department | Store Manager | — | 5 min |
| 5 | For negative feedback (rating ≤ 2 or NPS detractor): system auto-creates follow-up task assigned to Store Manager; Store Manager reviews specific transaction details and determines appropriate service recovery action per W604 Step 5 | Store Manager | — | 10 min |
| 6 | Weekly: Store Manager reviews CX Pulse trend in weekly operations review (W575); identifies top 3 customer pain points for the week; assigns corrective actions to Department Supervisors | Store Manager | — | 10 min |
| 7 | Monthly: CX Analyst generates store CX benchmark report — NPS by store, satisfaction by department, feedback volume, response rate, improvement actions completed; identifies top/bottom 10 stores; distributes to Regional Managers | CX Analyst | CMO | 4 hours |
| 8 | Quarterly: CX team reviews feedback pattern analysis feeding into systemic improvement workflow (W507); adjusts survey questions based on current CX priorities; reviews survey fatigue metrics and adjusts sampling rate | CX Analyst | CMO | 2 hours |

### System Touchpoints
- ERP CRM module: customer feedback collection, NPS calculation, sentiment analysis
- POS system: transaction-triggered survey invitation
- SMS/email gateway: automated survey delivery
- BI dashboard: CX Pulse daily scorecard, trend analysis, benchmarking

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Low response rate**: typical retail SMS survey response rate is 5-15%; mitigated by loyalty points incentive for survey completion and in-store QR code alternative
- **Survey fatigue**: customers who shop frequently may receive multiple survey invitations; mitigated by 30-day cooldown period per customer and rotating survey methods
- **Negative feedback overwhelm**: high-volume stores may generate many negative responses requiring follow-up; mitigated by automated triage (only responses mentioning specific issues trigger follow-up tasks)
- **Action closure gap**: collecting feedback without acting on it erodes customer trust; mitigated by weekly corrective action assignment and monthly action completion tracking

### Staffing Implication
- **Store Manager**: ~15 min/day on CX Pulse review and follow-up = ~1.5 hours/week. Absorbed within existing role.
- **CX Analyst (HQ)**: ~4 hours/month on reporting + ~2 hours/quarter on review = ~4.5 hours/month. Absorbed within existing CX team role.

## W609. Store-Level New Employee Buddy System & First-Week Onboarding

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | New hire first day at store (following HQ onboarding per W15) |
| **Frequency** | As needed; ~600-800 new hires/year across 200 stores (~3-4 per store per year) |
| **Volume** | 1 new hire per event; ~60-70 active buddy assignments at any time |
| **Owner** | Store Manager |
| **Participants** | New employee, assigned Buddy (experienced Sales Associate), Department Supervisor, Store Manager |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Prior to new hire arrival: Store Manager assigns Buddy from same department — selection criteria: ≥ 6 months tenure, performance rating ≥ satisfactory, completed Buddy training module; system checks Buddy eligibility and current assignment load (max 1 buddy at a time) | Store Manager | — | 10 min |
| 2 | **Day 1**: Buddy meets new hire at store entrance; provides store tour (selling floor, backroom, break room, emergency exits, first aid station, restrooms); introduces to all team members by name and role | Buddy | Dept. Supervisor | 60 min |
| 3 | Buddy guides new hire through department-specific orientation: product locations, top 20 selling SKUs, shelf layout, price tag reading, safety hazards specific to department (heavy items in lumber, chemicals in paint, sharp tools in hardware) | Buddy | Dept. Supervisor | 2 hours |
| 4 | **Days 2-3**: Buddy shadows and is shadowed — new hire observes Buddy handling customer interactions, POS transactions (if cashier), and department tasks; Buddy explains reasoning and shares tips; new hire attempts first customer interactions with Buddy present | Buddy | Dept. Supervisor | Full shift |
| 5 | **Days 4-5**: New hire handles customer interactions independently with Buddy available for questions; Department Supervisor conducts 15-minute check-in to assess comfort level and identify knowledge gaps | New Hire / Dept. Supervisor | Store Manager | 15 min |
| 6 | **End of Week 1**: Buddy completes first-week assessment form: new hire's product knowledge (top 20 SKUs), customer interaction confidence, POS familiarity, safety awareness, areas needing improvement; Department Supervisor reviews and adds comments | Buddy | Dept. Supervisor | 15 min |
| 7 | Store Manager reviews first-week assessment; schedules additional training for identified gaps; if performance is below expectations, extends buddy period by 1 week with specific improvement targets | Store Manager | — | 10 min |
| 8 | **Weeks 2-4**: Buddy check-ins reduce to twice weekly; Department Supervisor conducts weekly progress assessment; by Week 4, new hire should meet basic competency thresholds per W518 (if cashier) or department standards | Buddy / Dept. Supervisor | Store Manager | 10 min |
| 9 | **End of Month 1**: Store Manager conducts formal 30-day review; if competency thresholds met, buddy assignment formally ends; if not met, remediation plan created with HR Coordinator; system updates employee record with onboarding completion status | Store Manager | HR Coordinator | 20 min |
| 10 | System tracks buddy program metrics per store: average onboarding duration, 30-day competency pass rate, 90-day retention rate for buddy-supported hires vs. non-buddy; monthly report to HR Coordinator | System | — | Automated |

### System Touchpoints
- ERP HR module: buddy assignment tracking, assessment forms, onboarding milestone completion
- Employee master (W292): buddy eligibility flag, current assignment status, onboarding status
- Mobile app: buddy assessment form, check-in reminders, progress dashboard
- BI module: buddy program effectiveness analytics, retention correlation

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Buddy quality inconsistency**: not all experienced employees make good trainers; mitigated by mandatory Buddy training module and Store Manager selection oversight
- **Buddy absenteeism**: if Buddy is absent during first week, new hire loses critical orientation time; mitigated by assigning backup Buddy and cross-training multiple eligible buddies per department
- **First-week overwhelm**: too much information in first 3 days leads to poor retention; mitigated by structured Day 1-5 progression with specific daily objectives
- **High early turnover**: new hires who leave within 90 days waste buddy investment; mitigated by realistic job preview during recruitment (W15) and first-week engagement check

### Staffing Implication
- **Buddy (Sales Associate)**: ~8 hours Week 1 (full shift shadowing) + ~2 hours/week Weeks 2-4 = ~16 hours total per new hire. Absorbed within existing role with schedule accommodation.
- **Department Supervisor**: ~30 min Week 1 + ~15 min/week Weeks 2-4 = ~1.5 hours per new hire. Absorbed within existing role.
- **Store Manager**: ~20 min Week 1 setup + ~10 min end-of-week review + ~20 min 30-day review = ~50 min per new hire. Absorbed within existing role.

## W665. Store-Level KPI Dashboard & Daily Performance Monitoring

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Daily system-generated KPI refresh at 06:00 |
| **Frequency** | Daily dashboard review; weekly Store Manager review; monthly Regional Manager consolidation |
| **Volume** | 200 stores generating daily KPI snapshots |
| **Owner** | Store Manager (daily); Regional Manager (weekly consolidation) |
| **Participants** | Department Supervisor, Stock Associate, Cashier Lead, Regional Manager |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | System auto-generates overnight KPI snapshot per store from POS, inventory, and workforce data: sales (revenue vs. budget, ATV, transaction count, units per transaction), labor (cost % of revenue, overtime hours, absenteeism rate), inventory (out-of-stock count on top 100 SKUs, shrinkage incidents, receiving backlog), customer (NPS pulse, complaint count, queue wait time from W526), fulfillment (BOPIS SLA compliance, delivery orders pending) | System | BI Analyst | Automated |
| 2 | Store Manager reviews dashboard by 08:00 on tablet; system highlights Red metrics (> 10% below target) requiring immediate action | Store Manager | Store Manager | 15-20 min |
| 3 | Store Manager assigns corrective actions for Red metrics: sales shortfall → promotional push with trade counter, labor cost overrun → adjust next-day schedule per W34, OOS items → expedite replenishment per W596 | Store Manager | Store Manager | 10-15 min |
| 4 | Department Supervisors review department-level KPIs during morning huddle (sales by department, shelf gaps, customer feedback) | Department Supervisor | Store Manager | 15-20 min |
| 5 | System generates weekly store scorecard with peer comparison (quartile ranking within region) | System | BI Analyst | Automated |
| 6 | Regional Manager reviews bottom-quartile stores weekly; top-quartile stores flagged for best-practice documentation | Regional Manager | VP Store Operations | 3-4 hours |
| 7 | Monthly: system generates 200-store consolidated dashboard for VP Store Operations with trend analysis, outlier flagging, and regional heat map | System | BI Analyst | Automated |

### System Touchpoints
- BI dashboard module (daily KPI snapshot, weekly scorecard, monthly consolidated dashboard)
- POS transaction data (sales revenue, ATV, transaction count, units per transaction)
- Inventory module (OOS count, shrinkage incidents, receiving backlog)
- HR attendance data (overtime hours, absenteeism rate)
- Customer feedback module (NPS pulse, complaint count, queue wait time from W526)
- Order management module (BOPIS SLA compliance, delivery orders pending)

### Time Estimate
- Store Manager daily: 15-20 min; weekly review: 1 hour
- Regional Manager weekly: 3-4 hours
- Monthly consolidation: automated

### Pain Points / Risks
- Dashboard fatigue from too many metrics — store teams may ignore alerts if over-alerted
- Data latency for real-time operational decisions; overnight batch may not capture late-evening POS adjustments
- Regional comparison unfairness across urban vs. provincial stores with different traffic patterns and competitive landscapes
- Gaming of metrics (e.g., suppressing overtime by understaffing, inflating ATV by splitting transactions)
- Inconsistent department-level data entry across 200 stores affecting KPI accuracy

### Staffing Implication
- Store Manager: ~20 min/day + 1 hour/week. Absorbed within existing role.
- Regional Manager: ~4 hours/week. Absorbed within existing role.
- No incremental headcount required.

## W666. Store-Level Inventory Receiving Quality Control

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Each inbound delivery at store receiving dock |
| **Frequency** | Per delivery; 2-3 deliveries per store per week (~500-600 store receipts/week network-wide) |
| **Volume** | ~15-25 pallets/cases per delivery; ~200-300 SKUs per delivery |
| **Owner** | Receiving Clerk |
| **Participants** | Department Supervisor, Stock Associate, Store Manager (for exceptions) |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Delivery truck arrives; Receiving Clerk verifies delivery appointment against dock schedule and matches driver identification with carrier manifest | Receiving Clerk | Receiving Clerk | 5-10 min |
| 2 | Receiving Clerk unloads goods to staging area; performs visual inspection for transit damage: crushed boxes, wet packaging, broken seals, dented containers; segregates damaged items | Receiving Clerk | Department Supervisor | 15-20 min |
| 3 | Receiving Clerk scans delivery receipt against transfer order or PO in ERP using handheld device; system displays expected items with quantities | Receiving Clerk | Receiving Clerk | 5-10 min |
| 4 | Receiving Clerk performs count verification: scans barcode on each case/pallet; for catch-weight items (lumber, wire, nails bulk), weighs or measures and records actual quantity; flags quantity variances > 2% for Supervisor review | Receiving Clerk | Department Supervisor | 15-25 min |
| 5 | For designated AQL sampling items (per PUR-016), Receiving Clerk performs quality inspection per sampling plan: visual quality, correct labeling and barcode readability, shelf-life/expiration date check for paint and chemicals | Receiving Clerk | Department Supervisor | 10-15 min |
| 6 | Receiving Clerk identifies discrepancies: quantity shortage, quality defects, wrong items, missing labels; logs discrepancy with photo evidence in ERP | Receiving Clerk | Department Supervisor | 5-10 min |
| 7 | Department Supervisor reviews flagged items; approves or rejects delivery line items; for material discrepancies (>₱5,000 or > 10% shortage), escalates to Store Manager | Department Supervisor | Store Manager | 5-10 min |
| 8 | Receiving Clerk completes goods receipt in ERP; accepted items automatically update store inventory; rejected items flagged for carrier claim or return-to-DC per W22B | Receiving Clerk | Receiving Clerk | 5-10 min |
| 9 | Stock Associate moves received goods to selling floor or backroom per planogram location; system updates bin-level inventory | Stock Associate | Department Supervisor | 15-20 min |

### System Touchpoints
- WMS/ERP receiving module (goods receipt processing, transfer order/PO matching)
- RF handheld scanner (barcode scanning, count verification, photo capture)
- Inventory update module (real-time store-level and bin-level inventory)
- Carrier claim module (damage and shortage logging)
- Planogram/shelf management module (bin assignment and put-away confirmation)

### Time Estimate
- Per delivery: 45-90 min depending on size and complexity (small DC transfer: 45 min; large PO delivery with catch-weight items: 90 min)

### Pain Points / Risks
- Receiving during peak selling hours causing dock congestion and customer-facing aisle obstructions
- Catch-weight item measurement accuracy for lumber, wire, and bulk nails — inconsistent scales across stores
- Vendor DSD deliveries bypassing quality checks when Receiving Clerk is unavailable
- Seasonal surge (e.g., construction season, typhoon prep) overwhelming receiving capacity at high-volume stores
- Photo evidence upload delays due to weak WiFi at receiving dock area
- Discrepancy resolution delays when Department Supervisor is on the selling floor

### Staffing Implication
- Receiving Clerk (2 per store): ~3-4 deliveries/week × 90 min = ~6 hours/week. Absorbed within existing role (2 receiving clerks per store in staffing model).
- No incremental headcount required; seasonal peaks may require temporary receiving support.

## W667. Store-Level Price Verification & Daily Compliance Operations

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Daily morning opening routine; system price change alerts |
| **Frequency** | Daily spot-check (morning); weekly comprehensive shelf walk |
| **Volume** | 200 stores × ~35,000 active SKUs; daily price changes affecting ~50-200 SKUs/store |
| **Owner** | Department Supervisor (daily); Store Manager (weekly audit) |
| **Participants** | Stock Associate, Cashier Lead |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | System pushes overnight price changes to POS terminals per W533 and POS-047; Stock Associate receives daily price change notification on handheld listing affected SKUs with old and new prices | System / Stock Associate | Department Supervisor | 5-10 min |
| 2 | Stock Associate prints and replaces shelf labels/price tags for all changed SKUs per W181; verifies label matches new system price; flags items where shelf label cannot be located (potential out-of-shelf) | Stock Associate | Department Supervisor | 20-25 min |
| 3 | Department Supervisor performs morning spot-check: scans 20-30 random SKUs across departments with handheld price verification tool; system compares scanned barcode price vs. POS system price vs. shelf label | Department Supervisor | Store Manager | 10-15 min |
| 4 | For any mismatches: Stock Associate immediately corrects shelf label and verifies POS price; if POS price is incorrect, Department Supervisor escalates to Store Manager for manual price override per W553 | Stock Associate / Department Supervisor | Store Manager | 5-10 min |
| 5 | Weekly: Store Manager conducts comprehensive shelf walk in one department (rotating weekly across 4 departments); verifies price accuracy, promotional display pricing per W523, and DTI price freeze compliance per POS-024 | Store Manager | Regional Manager | 2 hours |
| 6 | Store Manager logs price verification results in compliance module; system calculates store-level price accuracy rate (target ≥ 99%) | Store Manager | Regional Manager | 15-20 min |
| 7 | Monthly: system generates price compliance report for Regional Manager with store ranking; bottom-quartile stores receive corrective action plan | System | Regional Manager | Automated |
| 8 | Price verification data feeds into W69 (price compliance audit) and W553 (pricing error detection) | System | Compliance Team | Automated |

### System Touchpoints
- POS price master (central price repository, promotional pricing)
- Shelf label printing module (label generation, batch printing)
- Handheld price verification tool (barcode scan, price comparison)
- Compliance tracking module (accuracy rate calculation, audit trail)
- DTI price freeze compliance module per POS-024

### Time Estimate
- Daily spot-check: 30 min (label replacement + random scan)
- Weekly comprehensive shelf walk: 2 hours
- Monthly reporting: automated

### Pain Points / Risks
- High volume of price changes during promotional transitions (monthly promo calendar, seasonal events) — up to 200 SKUs/store/day during peak
- Shelf label damage obscuring prices in high-traffic aisles (construction materials, garden center)
- Catch-weight items priced per unit vs. per kg causing customer and staff confusion
- DTI price freeze requiring immediate manual intervention across 200 stores with limited grace period
- Inconsistent handheld scanner battery life affecting morning spot-check completion
- Promotional display pricing mismatches when marketing materials arrive before POS price updates

### Staffing Implication
- Stock Associate: ~30 min/day label replacement + 30 min/week comprehensive walk. Absorbed within existing role.
- Department Supervisor: ~15 min/day spot-check. Absorbed within existing role.
- Store Manager: ~2 hours/week shelf walk. Absorbed within existing role.
- No incremental headcount required.

---

## W668. Store-Level Home Delivery & Third-Party Logistics Coordination

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer home delivery order placed at POS or ecommerce order routed to store for fulfillment |
| **Frequency** | Daily; varies by store location (urban stores: 5-10/day, provincial: 1-3/day) |
| **Volume** | ~17,200 home delivery orders/month network-wide; ~86/store/month |
| **Owner** | Stock Associate (pick/pack); Store Manager (coordination) |
| **Participants** | Receiving Clerk, Customer Service Rep, Third-Party Courier |

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | System routes home delivery order to originating store with customer details, item list, delivery address, and preferred delivery window | System | Store Manager | Automated |
| 2 | Stock Associate picks items from selling floor/backroom per pick list on handheld; verifies item, quantity, and condition; flags items out-of-stock for substitute suggestion per W279 or backorder per W56 | Stock Associate | Store Manager | 10-15 min |
| 3 | Stock Associate packs items in delivery-appropriate packaging: fragile items (tiles, electrical fixtures) in protective wrap, heavy items (cement, lumber) in reinforced packaging, hazardous items (paint, chemicals) per W237 hazmat labeling requirements | Stock Associate | Store Manager | 5-10 min |
| 4 | Customer Service Rep contacts customer for delivery confirmation and address verification; provides delivery tracking information | Customer Service Rep | Store Manager | 5 min |
| 5 | System auto-dispatches to third-party courier via API integration (Lalamove, Transportify, own fleet per W548); selects vehicle type based on order weight/volume (motorcycle for small, van for bulky/lumber) | System | Store Manager | Automated |
| 6 | Courier arrives at store; Stock Associate verifies courier identity (OTP from platform); hands over packaged order with delivery manifest; captures handover timestamp | Stock Associate | Store Manager | 5 min |
| 7 | System tracks delivery in real-time via courier API webhook; customer receives tracking link | System | Store Manager | Automated |
| 8 | Upon delivery: courier captures proof-of-delivery (photo + e-signature); system updates order status; triggers revenue recognition | System / Courier | Store Manager | Automated |
| 9 | For failed deliveries: system auto-reschedules within 48 hours; perishable/sensitive items returned to store inventory | System | Store Manager | Automated |
| 10 | Weekly: Store Manager reviews delivery SLA compliance (on-time %, damage rate, customer satisfaction per delivery CSAT survey); escalates underperforming couriers to HQ logistics team per W242 | Store Manager | Regional Manager | 30 min |

### System Touchpoints
- Order management system (order routing, status tracking, revenue recognition trigger)
- Pick list on handheld (item location, barcode verification, substitute suggestion)
- Courier API integration (Lalamove, Transportify, own fleet dispatch per W548)
- Delivery tracking module (real-time status, webhook updates, proof-of-delivery capture)
- Customer notification module (order confirmation, tracking link, delivery ETA)
- Hazmat labeling module per W237 (compliance check for paint, chemicals, adhesives)

### Time Estimate
- Per order: 20-30 min pick/pack + 5 min handoff
- Daily coordination: 30 min (Store Manager)
- Weekly SLA review: 30 min

### Pain Points / Risks
- Bulky/lumber items exceeding courier vehicle capacity — requires multi-vehicle or flatbed dispatch
- Delivery address accuracy in provincial areas (informal settlements, missing street numbers)
- Delivery damage claims for fragile items (tiles, electrical fixtures, glass) — high claim rate
- Courier availability during peak hours (10:00-14:00) causing dispatch delays
- Hazmat shipping restrictions for paint, solvents, and chemicals — limited courier options for dangerous goods
- Returns and reverse logistics for failed deliveries — restocking and re-shelving overhead
- Customer expectations for same-day delivery in Metro Manila vs. next-day in provincial areas

### Staffing Implication
- Stock Associate: ~3-5 orders/day × 30 min = 1.5-2.5 hours/day. Absorbed within existing role.
- Customer Service Rep: ~30 min/day delivery coordination. Absorbed within existing role.
- Store Manager: ~30 min/day operational coordination + 30 min/week SLA review. Absorbed within existing role.
- No incremental headcount required at current volume.

---

## W702. New Store Opening Project Management & Go-Live Execution

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Board-approved new store location identified; lease signed per W635; construction/renovation nearing completion per W96/W223 |
| **Frequency** | ~5-10 new store openings/year (growth plan) |
| **Volume** | Each new store opening is a 3-6 month project involving 10+ departments and 150+ individual tasks |
| **Owner** | VP Store Operations (project sponsor); Store Opening Project Manager |
| **Participants** | Real Estate Manager, Construction Manager, IT Director, HR Manager, Merchandising Manager, Procurement Manager, Marketing Manager, Finance Controller, Compliance Manager, Supply Chain Manager, Store Manager (appointed) |

### Background

W16 covers the high-level new store opening process. W623 covers the cross-functional readiness review. W223-W227 cover engineering and construction. W96 covers store renovation. However, no workflow provides comprehensive project management for the full new store opening lifecycle — from lease signing through construction, fit-out, staffing, merchandising, system setup, training, soft launch, and grand opening. Each new store represents a PHP 20-50M investment and requires precise coordination across departments to open on time, on budget, and fully operational.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Project initiation**: VP Store Operations appoints Store Opening Project Manager: (a) define project scope: location, target opening date, store format (standard, compact, express), estimated budget; (b) establish project governance: steering committee (VP Store Ops, CFO, CIO), weekly status reporting, milestone review gates; (c) create project plan in PPM tool: 150+ tasks across 10 workstreams with dependencies, durations, and owners; (d) major milestones: lease signed, construction start, construction complete, systems installed, inventory delivered, staff hired and trained, soft launch, grand opening | Project Manager | VP Store Operations | 1 week |
| 2 | **Construction & fit-out**: Construction Manager manages build-out per W223: (a) obtain LGU building permits and fire safety permit per W476; (b) construction execution per W224; (c) POS counter, racking, display fixtures, paint mixing station, lumber cutting area installation per W225; (d) final inspection and commissioning per W227; (e) obtain Fire Safety Inspection Certificate (FSIC) from BFP; (f) obtain LGU Certificate of Occupancy; (g) project manager monitors construction milestones and budget | Construction Manager | VP Store Operations | 3-4 months |
| 3 | **Utilities & IT infrastructure**: Facilities Manager and IT coordinate: (a) electricity connection and transformer installation per W701 (3-6 month lead time — initiated during construction); (b) water connection per W701; (c) internet/telecom connection per W701; (d) POS terminals installed and tested (3 terminals per store per model-company profile); (e) network infrastructure: WiFi, CCTV, security alarm system per W71; (f) ERP system configuration: new location created in location master per W508, POS terminal mapping, printer setup, scanner configuration; (g) backoffice computers and handheld devices provisioned | Facilities Manager / IT Director | CIO | Overlaps with construction |
| 4 | **Regulatory & compliance setup**: Compliance Manager coordinates: (a) BIR branch registration (apply for BIR Certificate of Registration for new branch — TIN, registered address, books of accounts); (b) LGU business permit for new location; (c) BIR-printed or BIR-registered POS invoices for new branch; (d) DTI price board display compliance; (e) fire safety permit per W476; (f) BFP fire extinguisher inspection; (g) DENR permit if applicable (garden center with water discharge); (h) coordinate with W54 regulatory permits workflow | Compliance Manager | VP Legal | 2-3 months |
| 5 | **Staffing & training**: HR Manager coordinates: (a) recruit Store Manager (2-3 months before opening); (b) Store Manager recruits team: 2 Department Supervisors, 4-6 Cashiers, 6-10 Stock Associates, 1 Admin, 2 Security guards per store profile (~25-30 FTE per store); (c) all staff complete onboarding per W15 and safety training per W655; (d) cashiers complete POS training per W518; (e) stock associates complete receiving and inventory training per W109; (f) Store Manager and Supervisors complete management training; (g) all certifications current per W655 before opening day | HR Manager / Store Manager | CHRO | 2-3 months before opening |
| 6 | **Merchandising & inventory**: Merchandising Manager and Supply Chain Manager coordinate: (a) planogram design for new store format per W86; (b) initial assortment selection based on store format and local market per W679; (c) initial stock order placed 4-6 weeks before opening per W2C; (d) initial inventory received and putaway per W109 (typically over 2-3 days); (e) shelf stocking and display setup per W573; (f) price tags and shelf labels printed and placed per W667; (g) promotional displays for grand opening per W583 | Merchandising Manager / Supply Chain Manager | VP Merchandising | 4-6 weeks before opening |
| 7 | **Soft launch & readiness verification**: 1-2 weeks before grand opening: (a) conduct soft launch: open store to employees, friends, and family for 2-3 days; (b) test all systems under load: POS transactions, loyalty enrollment, card payments, ecommerce BOPIS routing; (c) verify inventory accuracy: spot-check 100 SKUs across all departments; (d) verify regulatory compliance: BIR invoice printing, senior citizen/PWD discount per W170, price display per W667; (e) Store Manager conducts final walk-through: cleanliness, signage, safety, merchandising standards; (f) fix any issues identified during soft launch | Project Manager / Store Manager | VP Store Operations | 1-2 weeks |
| 8 | **Grand opening**: (a) Marketing coordinates grand opening event per W142: local advertising, social media, PR, community engagement; (b) VP Store Operations or Regional Manager attends grand opening; (c) Store Manager manages opening day operations with full staffing; (d) first-day performance tracked: sales, transactions, customer count, loyalty signups; (e) IT and Helpdesk on standby for system issues per W48 | Marketing / Store Manager | VP Store Operations | 1 day |
| 9 | **Post-opening stabilization**: 30 days after opening: (a) Project Manager conducts post-opening review: sales vs. plan, customer feedback, operational issues, staffing adequacy; (b) Store Manager receives intensive coaching from Regional Manager during first 90 days; (c) merchandising adjustments based on actual sales mix vs. plan; (d) project close-out: final budget reconciliation, lessons learned documentation, project file archived per W255; (e) handover from Project Manager to Regional Manager for ongoing management | Project Manager / Regional Manager | VP Store Operations | 30 days post-opening |

### System Touchpoints

- Project portfolio management (PPM) tool for task tracking and milestone management
- ERP location master per W508 (new store setup)
- POS configuration and terminal management per POS-001
- BIR registration module per FIN-010
- HR recruitment and onboarding per W15, W518
- Merchandising and planogram per W86
- Inventory and replenishment per W2C, W109
- Utility connection management per W701
- Regulatory permits per W54
- Marketing campaign per W142
- Capex tracking per W184

### Pain Points / Risks

- **Construction delays**: LGU building permit delays, contractor performance issues, and material supply disruptions (especially in provincial areas) can push opening date by months
- **Cross-departmental coordination failure**: 10+ departments must deliver on parallel timelines; any single department delay (IT installation, regulatory permits, initial inventory) can delay the entire opening
- **New store underperformance**: initial sales may not meet projections; Merchandising must be ready to quickly adjust assortment and pricing based on actual customer demand
- **Staff quality at provincial locations**: recruiting qualified Store Managers and experienced staff for provincial locations is challenging; training may need to be more intensive
- **Utility connection delays**: electricity and internet connections for new locations (especially in provincial areas) can take 3-6 months and are often on the critical path

### Staffing Implication

- **Store Opening Project Manager** (1 FTE or dedicated role per opening): full-time during 3-6 month opening project. Can manage 2-3 concurrent openings.
- **Construction Manager**: dedicated during construction phase per W223.
- **Department resources**: ~10-20% of each department head's time during opening project. Absorbed within existing roles.
- **New store staff**: ~25-30 FTE per new store, hired per W15.

### Time Estimate

- Full project duration: 3-6 months from lease signing to grand opening
- Project initiation: 1 week
- Construction: 3-4 months
- Staffing & training: 2-3 months
- Merchandising setup: 4-6 weeks
- Soft launch: 1-2 weeks
- Post-opening stabilization: 30 days
