## W703. Store Closure, Consolidation & Asset Recovery

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Board-approved store closure/consolidation decision; lease expiry with non-renewal; sustained underperformance; strategic market exit; force majeure (disaster damage beyond repair) |
| **Frequency** | Ad-hoc; estimated 2-5 closures/consolidations per year based on portfolio optimization |
| **Volume** | Each closure is a 2-4 month project; store inventory value typically PHP 15-30M per location |
| **Owner** | VP Store Operations (project sponsor) |
| **Participants** | Real Estate Manager, Store Manager, HR Manager, Finance Controller, IT Director, Merchandising Manager, Supply Chain Manager, Legal Counsel, Facilities Manager |

### Background

W45 covers the high-level store closure/relocation process. However, a store closure is a complex multi-departmental project requiring careful management of inventory liquidation, asset recovery, employee separation, lease termination, regulatory filings, and customer communication. Mishandled closures result in inventory write-offs, lease penalties, employee lawsuits (illegal dismissal per Philippine labor law), and brand damage in the local community.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Closure decision & announcement**: (a) Board approves closure with specific rationale: underperformance, lease expiry, strategic consolidation, or force majeure; (b) VP Store Operations appoints Closure Project Manager; (c) Legal reviews lease termination provisions: notice period required (typically 60-90 days), early termination penalties, security deposit recovery, restoration obligations; (d) communicate closure timeline to Store Manager (confidential until public announcement); (e) public announcement: VP Store Operations and Marketing coordinate community and media communication; (f) customer notification per W708: notify loyalty members in store's trade area of closure and nearest alternative location | VP Store Operations / Legal | CEO | 1-2 weeks |
| 2 | **Employee separation**: HR Manager coordinates: (a) DOLE-compliant separation process per Philippine Labor Code: 30-day written notice to affected employees, report to DOLE Regional Office per Article 298 (formerly 283) for closures affecting 50+ employees; (b) separation pay computation: 1 month pay per year of service for closures due to redundancy or retrenchment per W643 final pay framework; (c) offer relocation to nearby BuildRight stores for employees willing to transfer (especially Store Managers and Supervisors); (d) outplacement support: job fair with local employers, reference letters, DOLE employment assistance referral; (e) ensure all final pay, 13th month, unused leave, and tax documents (BIR 2316) are prepared per W643 | HR Manager | CHRO | 30-60 days |
| 3 | **Inventory liquidation**: Merchandising Manager coordinates: (a) plan liquidation timeline: 4-6 weeks of clearance sales before final closure; (b) progressive markdown schedule: week 1-2: 20-30% off, week 3-4: 40-50% off, final week: 60-80% off; (c) advertise clearance sale per W142 marketing campaigns; (d) items not sold during clearance: transfer to nearest BuildRight store or DC for redistribution; (e) unsaleable items (damaged, expired): write off per W91/W92; (f) final inventory count: physical count on last operating day for financial reconciliation | Merchandising Manager / Store Manager | VP Merchandising | 4-6 weeks |
| 4 | **Asset recovery**: Facilities Manager coordinates: (a) catalog all BuildRight-owned assets: POS terminals, racking, display fixtures, refrigeration units, paint mixing station, lumber cutting equipment, security cameras, IT equipment; (b) assess asset condition and recovery value: reusable (transfer to other locations or new store openings), sellable (sell to used equipment buyers), scrap (dispose per W443); (c) coordinate physical removal and logistics; (d) update fixed asset register per W184 and compute gain/loss on asset disposal; (e) ensure all assets removed before lease handover | Facilities Manager / Finance | VP Operations | 2-4 weeks |
| 5 | **System & IT decommissioning**: IT Director coordinates: (a) deactivate POS terminals and unregister from BIR per FIN-010; (b) decommission ERP location master per W508 (mark as inactive, not deleted for historical data retention); (c) deactivate user accounts for departing staff; (d) cancel internet/telecom service per W701; (e) retrieve IT equipment (terminals, handhelds, routers, printers); (f) ensure all data backed up per W256 retention policy; (g) wipe all devices of BuildRight data per W647 data privacy requirements | IT Director | CIO | 1-2 weeks |
| 6 | **Regulatory closure filings**: Compliance Manager coordinates: (a) BIR branch closure registration: file closure notice with BIR Revenue District Office, surrender unused BIR-registered invoices, obtain Certificate of Registration cancellation; (b) LGU business permit cancellation; (c) DOLE closure report (if 50+ employees affected); (d) cancel DENR permits if applicable; (e) cancel local insurance policies; (f) notify PhilHealth, Pag-IBIG, SSS of employee separations | Compliance Manager | VP Legal | 1-2 months |
| 7 | **Lease handover**: Real Estate Manager coordinates: (a) restore premises to lease-required condition (remove BuildRight fixtures, repair walls, repaint if required by lease); (b) schedule landlord walk-through inspection; (c) negotiate security deposit return (typically 2-3 months rent); (d) settle any outstanding rent or utility balances; (e) formal lease termination agreement signed by both parties per W688; (f) utility disconnection and final billing per W701 | Real Estate Manager / Legal | VP Operations | 2-4 weeks |
| 8 | **Financial close-out**: Finance Controller: (a) final P&L for closed store: revenue through closure date, all closure costs (employee separation, lease penalties, inventory write-off, asset disposal loss, removal costs); (b) compare closure costs to Board-approved budget; (c) capital gain/loss on leasehold improvements (if any); (d) update intercompany positions if store was in different entity per W442; (e) archive store financial records per W256 retention policy; (f) final closure report to CFO and Board | Finance Controller | CFO | 2-4 weeks |
| 9 | **Post-closure review**: VP Store Operations conducts post-closure review: (a) document lessons learned for future closures; (b) assess impact on customers in trade area: are they patronizing alternative BuildRight stores or switching to competitors?; (c) assess employee outcomes: how many transferred vs. separated?; (d) compare actual closure costs vs. budget; (e) update store portfolio strategy if closure was part of broader optimization | VP Store Operations | CEO | 2 weeks post-closure |

### System Touchpoints

- Project portfolio management (PPM) tool for closure task tracking
- ERP location master deactivation per W508
- Fixed asset register update per W184
- POS terminal de-registration per FIN-010
- HR separation processing per W643
- BIR closure filing per FIN-010
- Inventory management per W91/W92
- Customer communication per W708
- Lease management per W688
- Utility disconnection per W701
- Document retention per W256

### Pain Points / Risks

- **Employee litigation risk**: Philippine labor law provides strong employee protections; improper separation process (insufficient notice, below-minimum separation pay, failure to report to DOLE) can result in illegal dismissal claims with back wages and moral damages
- **Inventory write-off magnitude**: if clearance sales do not move sufficient volume, remaining inventory must be transferred (logistics cost) or written off (financial impact); timing markdowns correctly is critical
- **Lease restoration costs**: landlords may demand extensive premises restoration beyond BuildRight's lease obligations; Legal must negotiate to limit restoration scope per lease terms
- **Community and brand impact**: store closures in provincial communities generate negative publicity and may affect BuildRight's reputation and employee morale at remaining locations
- **Customer migration failure**: customers of closed store may not migrate to alternative BuildRight locations, especially if competitor is closer; marketing must actively communicate alternative store locations per W708

### Staffing Implication

- **Closure Project Manager**: full-time during 2-4 month closure project. Can be the Store Opening Project Manager in dual role (rotating between openings and closures).
- **Store Manager**: full-time through closure (typically the last employee to leave).
- **Department resources**: ~5-10% of each department head's time during closure. Absorbed within existing roles.
- **No incremental headcount** (project-managed closure).

### Time Estimate

- Full closure project: 2-4 months from Board decision to lease handover
- Employee separation: 30-60 days
- Inventory liquidation: 4-6 weeks
- Asset recovery: 2-4 weeks
- System decommissioning: 1-2 weeks
- Regulatory filings: 1-2 months
- Lease handover: 2-4 weeks
- Financial close-out: 2-4 weeks
- Post-closure review: 2 weeks

---

## W720. Store-Level Daily Safety Briefing & Toolbox Talk

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Start of each store operating day |
| **Frequency** | Daily (all 200 stores) |
| **Volume** | 200 daily safety briefings (~5-10 min each) |
| **Owner** | Store Manager |
| **Participants** | All on-shift store staff (~15-20 per shift), Department Supervisors |

### Background

Daily safety briefings (toolbox talks) are a retail industry best practice and a DOLE-recommended safety communication method. For BuildRight Depot, where employees handle hazardous materials (paint, chemicals per W237), heavy materials (cement, lumber), power tools (for cutting services per W169), and operate in a customer-facing environment with slip/trip/fall hazards, a daily 5-10 minute safety briefing reinforces awareness, communicates store-specific hazards, and builds a safety-first culture. This workflow complements W579 (daily equipment safety check) and W512 (health & safety committee) by providing the daily team-level communication touchpoint.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Topic selection**: system auto-selects daily safety topic from rotating 30-day calendar: (a) topics rotate through: hazmat handling per W237, ladder safety, manual lifting techniques, forklift/yard safety per W438, fire safety per W582, electrical safety, slip/trip/fall prevention, customer area safety, PPE compliance per W172, emergency procedures per W330, heat stress (summer), typhoon preparedness per W576, cash handling safety, robbery response per W717, first aid awareness per W501; (b) Store Manager can override with store-specific topic if recent incident warrants; (c) system provides 1-page briefing card with key talking points | System / Store Manager | Store Manager | 2 min |
| 2 | **Team gathering**: at start of shift (or shift changeover), Department Supervisors gather their teams: (a) all on-shift employees in each department assemble; (b) Store Manager rotates between departments for daily briefing or holds single all-staff briefing before store opening per W5A; (c) briefing conducted at department workstation or break area | Department Supervisor | Store Manager | 1 min |
| 3 | **Briefing delivery**: Store Manager or Department Supervisor delivers briefing: (a) present today's safety topic (2-3 key points from briefing card); (b) highlight any store-specific hazards observed: wet floor in lumber yard, damaged racking in backroom, broken exterior light; (c) review any incidents from previous day (without naming individuals): what happened, what was the corrective action, what to watch for; (d) remind staff of any active safety alerts: product recall per W29, construction near store entrance, seasonal weather warning per W576; (e) ask if anyone has safety concerns to report per W559 | Store Manager / Dept Supervisor | Store Manager | 5-7 min |
| 4 | **Attendance and documentation**: (a) Department Supervisor records attendance (verbal roll call or badge scan); (b) system logs: date, time, department, topic, number of attendees, Store Manager name; (c) employees who missed briefing (late arrival, day off) receive topic via mobile app notification; (d) system tracks completion rate: target 95% of on-shift employees attend daily | Department Supervisor | Store Manager | 2 min |
| 5 | **Monthly effectiveness review**: Store Manager reviews monthly safety briefing metrics: (a) attendance rate by department and day; (b) topic coverage: all 30 topics covered in rotation?; (c) correlation with incident trends: did briefing on a topic precede reduction in related incidents?; (d) employee feedback: any topics requested for addition?; (e) report included in monthly store performance review per W575 | Store Manager | Regional Manager | 30 min/month |

### System Touchpoints

- Safety topic calendar with automated rotation and briefing card library
- Employee attendance system integration per W561
- Incident tracking integration per W140 for yesterday's incident reference
- Mobile app notification for missed briefing employees
- Store performance dashboard per W665 for attendance tracking

### Pain Points / Risks

- **Briefing fatigue**: daily repetition can lead to disengagement; mitigated by rotating 30 topics, incorporating real store incidents, and varying delivery format (discussion, demonstration, video)
- **Time pressure during opening**: store opening per W5A has many competing tasks; Store Manager may skip or rush the safety briefing under time pressure
- **Part-time and shifting staff coverage**: employees on different shift schedules may consistently miss briefings; mitigated by mobile app topic delivery and requiring missed-topic acknowledgment

### Staffing Implication

5-10 min/day across 200 stores = ~17-33 hours/day chain-wide. Absorbed within existing Store Manager and Department Supervisor duties. No incremental headcount.

### Time Estimate

**Total**: 5-10 min per store per day. Topic selection: 2 min. Briefing: 5-7 min. Documentation: 2 min. Monthly review: 30 min.

---

## W721. Store-Level Vendor Promodizer Floor Activity Coordination & Compliance

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Vendor promodizer arrives at store for scheduled or unscheduled floor activity; or daily promodizer schedule review |
| **Frequency** | Daily (multiple promodizers per store) |
| **Volume** | ~2-5 promodizers active per store at any time; ~400-1,000 promodizer visits/month chain-wide |
| **Owner** | Department Supervisor |
| **Participants** | Vendor Promodizer, Department Supervisor, Store Manager, HR (promodizer compliance per W269) |

### Background

Vendor promodizers (merchandiser demonstrators, product promoters, and brand ambassadors deployed by vendors to BuildRight stores) are a common feature of Philippine big-box retail. With ~800-1,000 vendors, many deploy promodizers to maintain shelf presence, conduct product demonstrations, and influence purchase decisions. W269 covers enterprise-level promodizer management and W449 covers DOLE 174 labor compliance. This workflow addresses the daily store-level coordination challenge: managing multiple promodizers from different vendors operating simultaneously on the sales floor, ensuring they follow BuildRight rules, and preventing conflicts between promodizer activities and store operations.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Daily promodizer schedule review**: Store Manager or Department Supervisor reviews daily promodizer schedule: (a) system displays scheduled promodizer visits: vendor name, promodizer name, assigned department, approved activity (demonstration, shelf stocking, product sampling, inventory check), approved time window; (b) identify conflicts: multiple promodizers in same department, overlapping demo stations, promodizer scheduled during peak traffic when floor space is limited; (c) resolve conflicts by adjusting time slots or relocating activity areas | Store Manager / Dept Supervisor | Store Manager | 10-15 min/day |
| 2 | **Promodizer check-in**: upon arrival: (a) promodizer reports to customer service counter or department supervisor; (b) Department Supervisor verifies: (i) promodizer has valid BuildRight-issued ID/visitor badge per W581 vendor access management; (ii) promodizer is on the approved schedule; (iii) activity type matches approved activity for today; (c) if unscheduled visit: Store Manager decides to allow (if space available and activity is pre-approved type) or decline (redirect to vendor's BuildRight account manager for scheduling); (d) issue floor access badge with time-limited validity | Department Supervisor | Store Manager | 5 min per promodizer |
| 3 | **Floor activity monitoring**: during promodizer activity: (a) Department Supervisor periodically checks promodizer activity (every 1-2 hours): (i) promodizer is in assigned area only; (ii) not interfering with customer shopping flow; (iii) not disparaging competing products; (iv) following BuildRight store rules: no loud music, no blocking aisles, no unauthorized promotional materials; (v) wearing required PPE if in paint/chemical department per W237; (b) if violations observed: (i) first offense: verbal warning and correction; (ii) repeated offense: ask promodizer to leave and report to vendor via W269; (iii) serious violation (customer harassment, theft, safety hazard): immediate removal and incident report per W471 | Department Supervisor | Store Manager | 5-10 min per check |
| 4 | **Product demonstration coordination**: for promodizers conducting live product demonstrations: (a) Department Supervisor assigns demonstration area: not blocking main aisles, near relevant product section, with access to power outlet if needed; (b) verify demonstration materials are safe: no open flames, no hazardous chemicals without PPE, no food samples without sanitary compliance; (c) ensure demonstration does not create excessive noise or waste; (d) coordinate with cashier team if promodizer is offering special pricing or coupons that need POS setup per W523 | Department Supervisor | Store Manager | 10 min per demo setup |
| 5 | **Promodizer check-out**: at end of activity: (a) promodizer returns floor access badge; (b) Department Supervisor conducts brief debrief: (i) activity completed as planned?; (ii) any incidents or customer complaints?; (iii) any store property damage?; (iv) any inventory discrepancies noted?; (c) Department Supervisor logs visit completion in system with activity notes; (d) feedback sent to vendor via W269 promodizer management for performance tracking | Department Supervisor | — | 5 min per promodizer |

### System Touchpoints

- Promodizer scheduling module integrated with W269 vendor promodizer management
- Visitor badge/floor access system linked to W581 vendor representative access
- Activity logging and compliance tracking
- Incident reporting integration per W471
- Vendor communication module per W269 for feedback routing
- POS promotional setup per W523 for demo-related pricing

### Pain Points / Risks

- **Unscheduled promodizer visits**: vendors frequently send promodizers without prior scheduling, disrupting store operations and creating coverage conflicts; mitigated by strict check-in process and requirement for advance scheduling
- **Promodizer boundary violations**: promodizers may overstep their authorized area, approaching customers in departments they are not assigned to, or making unauthorized claims about competing products
- **DOLE 174 compliance risk**: if BuildRight is deemed to be directing promodizer work activities (rather than the vendor), DOLE may classify promodizers as BuildRight employees, triggering benefits obligations; mitigated by clear vendor control of promodizer tasks and W449 compliance monitoring
- **Customer disturbance**: aggressive promodizers who follow customers, use loud speakers, or create congestion can degrade the shopping experience and generate customer complaints per W41

### Staffing Implication

Department Supervisors: ~20-30 min/day per store on promodizer coordination (10-15 min schedule review + 5 min × 2-5 check-ins + 5 min × 2-5 check-outs). Absorbed within existing Department Supervisor duties. No incremental headcount.

### Time Estimate

**Total per store per day**: 20-30 min (schedule review 10-15 min + check-in 5 min/promodizer + monitoring 5-10 min/check + check-out 5 min/promodizer). Chain-wide: ~67-100 hours/day.

---

## W722. Store-Level Exterior Display & Garden Center Daily Operations

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Store opening per W5A; or weather event affecting exterior displays |
| **Frequency** | Daily (all stores with exterior/garden areas) |
| **Volume** | ~180 stores with outdoor/garden center areas |
| **Owner** | Department Supervisor (Garden/Outdoor) |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate, Stock Associate, Store Manager |

### Background

BuildRight Depot's Garden & Outdoor category (3% of active SKUs, ~1,050 items) includes live plants, potted items, garden furniture, outdoor lighting, hoses, and sprinklers. Additionally, many stores use exterior areas for lumber display per W3B, seasonal promotions, and building material showcases. Exterior operations face unique challenges: weather exposure (Philippine tropical climate with intense rain and heat), product degradation, security risks (theft from outdoor areas), and customer safety on wet/uneven surfaces. This workflow manages the daily operations specific to exterior and garden center areas, complementing W554 (daily shelf replenishment) which focuses on indoor operations.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Morning exterior inspection**: before store opening per W5A, Stock Associate or Department Supervisor inspects exterior area: (a) check for overnight damage: vandalism, theft evidence, storm damage, animal disturbance; (b) check plant health: wilting, pest infestation, sun damage; water plants as needed; (c) check display stability: outdoor fixtures secured against wind, lumber stacks stable per W3B; (d) check safety hazards: standing water after rain, slippery surfaces, blocked drainage, loose pavers; (e) check signage: exterior promotional signage intact and current, price tags weather-resistant; (f) report issues to Store Manager for resolution | Stock Associate / Dept Supervisor | Store Manager | 15-20 min |
| 2 | **Display setup and merchandising**: Stock Associate sets up exterior displays: (a) position seasonal promotional displays per W583 merchandising plan; (b) arrange garden plants for maximum visual appeal and accessibility; (c) set up any vendor demonstration areas per W721 if applicable; (d) ensure all exterior items have weather-resistant price tags or signage per W181; (e) verify outdoor lighting is functional for evening display visibility | Stock Associate | Dept Supervisor | 20-30 min |
| 3 | **Weather-responsive operations**: throughout the day, Department Supervisor monitors weather conditions: (a) if rain approaching: (i) move vulnerable items (electrical, metal prone to rust, cardboard-packaged goods) under cover; (ii) secure lightweight items against wind; (iii) deploy non-slip mats at entry points; (iv) ensure drainage channels are clear; (b) if extreme heat: (i) increase plant watering frequency; (ii) move heat-sensitive items to shade; (iii) ensure outdoor staff take hydration breaks per W720 safety briefing; (c) if typhoon warning per W576: activate full exterior protection protocol | Dept Supervisor | Store Manager | 10-15 min per weather event |
| 4 | **Customer assistance in exterior area**: Sales Associate provides customer service in exterior/garden area: (a) assist customers with plant selection and care advice; (b) help customers load bulky exterior items (pavers, outdoor furniture, lumber) per W438 yard loading operations; (c) guide customers on quantity calculations for garden and outdoor projects; (d) for live plants: provide care instructions and guarantee terms; (e) alert Stock Associate for replenishment when exterior stock runs low | Sales Associate | Dept Supervisor | Ongoing |
| 5 | **End-of-day exterior close-down**: before store closing per W574: (a) secure exterior area: lock gates, bring high-value portable items inside; (b) final plant watering if needed; (c) cover vulnerable displays with weather protection if overnight rain forecast; (d) turn off exterior display lighting; (e) check for any items that need markdown due to weather damage or plant decline per W93; (f) Department Supervisor logs exterior area status in daily closing report | Stock Associate / Dept Supervisor | Store Manager | 15-20 min |

### System Touchpoints

- Weather integration (PAGASA API) for automated weather alerts
- Exterior inventory tracking per INV-001 separate from indoor inventory
- Markdown processing per W93 for weather-damaged items
- Daily task checklist integrated with store opening W5A and closing W574
- Plant care scheduling (watering, maintenance) with task tracking
- POS for exterior area sales linked to yard operations per W438

### Pain Points / Risks

- **Product shrinkage in exterior areas**: outdoor displays are vulnerable to theft, especially during peak traffic when staff attention is divided; higher shrinkage rate than indoor areas
- **Plant inventory waste**: live plants have limited shelf life and are sensitive to Philippine tropical weather; estimated 15-20% shrinkage from plant death/decline, significantly higher than non-perishable categories
- **Weather damage costs**: sudden tropical downpours can damage cardboard-packaged goods, metal items, and electrical products in exterior displays within minutes
- **Customer safety liability**: wet exterior surfaces from rain create slip-and-fall risk; BuildRight faces customer injury claims per W285 public liability if adequate precautions are not taken
- **Lumber yard safety**: exterior lumber storage per W3B creates trip hazards, splinter risk, and heavy-lifting injury potential for customers and staff

### Staffing Implication

~15-20 min morning + 20-30 min setup + 15-20 min close-down = ~50-70 min/day of dedicated exterior operations time per store. Absorbed by existing Stock Associates and Department Supervisors at stores with exterior areas. No incremental headcount.

### Time Estimate

**Total per store per day**: ~60-90 min (15-20 min inspection + 20-30 min setup + 10-15 min weather response + 15-20 min close-down). Weather events add 10-15 min each.

---

## W723. Store-Level Loading Bay Traffic & Truck Queue Management

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Scheduled delivery truck arrival; or DSD vendor truck arrival per W18B |
| **Frequency** | 2-3 trucks per store per day (replenishment) + DSD trucks (~1-2/day) |
| **Volume** | ~600-1,000 truck arrivals/day chain-wide |
| **Owner** | Receiving Clerk |
| **Participants** | Receiving Clerk, Stock Associate, Truck Driver, Security Guard |

### Background

Each BuildRight store receives 2-3 replenishment trucks per week from DCs and 1-2 DSD deliveries per day from vendors. With 200 stores, that translates to ~600-1,000 truck movements per day across the chain. Many stores have limited loading bay capacity (1-2 bays), creating queuing challenges when multiple trucks arrive simultaneously. The loading bay also serves as the customer yard loading area per W438, creating potential conflicts between commercial trucks and customer vehicles. This workflow manages the daily loading bay traffic flow, complementing W585 (DC dock scheduling) which handles the DC side, and W109 (store-level receiving) which handles the goods receipt process.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Daily delivery schedule review**: at start of day, Receiving Clerk reviews delivery schedule: (a) system displays expected deliveries: DC replenishment trucks (from W4 transfer orders), DSD vendor trucks (from W18B scheduled deliveries), inter-store transfers (from W22), return pickups (from W22B); (b) note expected arrival time windows and number of pallets/cases per truck; (c) identify potential conflicts: multiple trucks scheduled in same time window; (d) adjust receiving sequence if needed: prioritize perishable/temperature-sensitive items, time-critical promotional stock per W57; (e) communicate adjusted schedule to Security Guard for gate coordination | Receiving Clerk | Store Manager | 10-15 min |
| 2 | **Truck arrival and queuing**: when truck arrives: (a) Security Guard directs truck to loading bay or queue area: (i) if loading bay available: direct to bay; (ii) if loading bay occupied: direct to queue area and assign queue position; (iii) if customer vehicles in yard loading area per W438: prioritize customer safety, hold truck until yard is clear; (b) Security Guard verifies truck identity: driver matches scheduled delivery, license plate matches expected vehicle; (c) for unscheduled/unexpected trucks: Receiving Clerk verifies against open POs or transfer orders before accepting; refuse delivery if no matching order | Security Guard / Receiving Clerk | Store Manager | 5 min per truck |
| 3 | **Bay allocation and turnaround management**: Receiving Clerk manages bay turnaround: (a) target turnaround time: DC replenishment trucks = 45-60 min; DSD trucks = 20-30 min; inter-store transfers = 30-45 min; (b) monitor receiving progress: if truck exceeding target turnaround, investigate bottleneck (missing PO, product discrepancy, short-staffed); (c) if queue is building (2+ trucks waiting): (i) request additional Stock Associate support; (ii) expedite current truck completion; (iii) communicate wait time to queued drivers; (d) for DSD vendors: enforce W18B delivery time window compliance; late arrivals may be rescheduled | Receiving Clerk | Store Manager | Ongoing |
| 4 | **Customer yard conflict management**: loading bay area also serves customer yard loading per W438: (a) separate customer loading zone from commercial truck bay with clear signage and barriers; (b) if customer loading blocks truck access: Security Guard assists with customer vehicle relocation; (c) if commercial truck blocks customer access: expedite truck departure or redirect to alternate bay; (d) ensure pedestrian safety: customers walking through yard area are separated from forklift and truck traffic by barriers and signage | Security Guard / Receiving Clerk | Store Manager | Ongoing |
| 5 | **Daily bay utilization reporting**: at end of day: (a) system generates daily loading bay utilization report: number of trucks received, average turnaround time, peak hour utilization, queue wait times, customer vs. truck conflict incidents; (b) Receiving Clerk reviews for anomalies; (c) report feeds into weekly store operations review per W575; (d) persistent queuing issues escalated to Regional Manager for DC delivery schedule adjustment or facility improvement | Receiving Clerk | Store Manager | 5-10 min |

### System Touchpoints

- Delivery scheduling module with real-time truck tracking from DC
- Transfer order system per W4 for expected DC deliveries
- DSD scheduling per W18B for vendor truck arrivals
- Loading bay availability tracking with queue management
- W109 store-level receiving integration for goods receipt
- W438 yard loading coordination for customer vehicle management
- Daily utilization analytics for bay efficiency tracking

### Pain Points / Risks

- **Loading bay capacity constraints**: most BuildRight stores have only 1-2 loading bays; during peak receiving periods (Monday after weekend sales, pre-promotion stock buildup), trucks queue on the street, creating traffic issues and neighbor complaints
- **Customer-truck conflict**: shared yard area creates safety risk when customers load bulky purchases while trucks maneuver in the same space; mitigated by physical separation and dedicated customer loading zone
- **DSD vendor non-compliance with time windows**: vendors frequently arrive outside scheduled time windows, disrupting the planned receiving sequence and creating conflicts with DC trucks
- **Driver waiting time disputes**: truck drivers (especially 3PL carriers) may complain about extended wait times, claiming demurrage charges; Receiving Clerk must balance thorough receiving per W109 with turnaround efficiency
- **Security risk during peak receiving**: Security Guard attention is divided between truck management and store perimeter security during peak receiving periods

### Staffing Implication

Receiving Clerk: absorbed within existing receiving duties (~10-15 min/day on scheduling + ongoing bay management). Security Guard: ~15-30 min/day on truck direction and yard management. Stock Associates: called for surge support during multi-truck periods. No incremental headcount.

### Time Estimate

**Total per store per day**: ~30-45 min active management (10-15 min schedule review + 5 min/truck × 3-5 trucks + 5-10 min reporting). Ongoing monitoring absorbed in receiving duties.

---

## W739. Store-Level Daily Huddle & Morning Team Briefing

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Store opening preparation (scheduled daily, 15 minutes before store opens) |
| **Frequency** | Daily per store |
| **Volume** | 200 stores × 1 huddle/day |
| **Owner** | Store Manager |
| **Participants** | All on-shift store associates, Department Supervisors, Store Manager |

### Background

Each BuildRight Depot store conducts a 10–15 minute morning team briefing before store opening. This daily alignment ritual ensures all associates are informed of daily sales targets, active promotions, staffing assignments, safety reminders, and policy updates. For a 200-store chain, consistent daily communication is critical for operational execution, promotional compliance, and employee engagement.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Pre-huddle preparation — Store Manager reviews overnight system alerts (low-stock alerts from W554, LP overnight alerts from W562, memo inbox from W571), pulls daily targets from W665 KPI dashboard, and identifies key talking points | System / Store Manager | Store Manager | 5 min |
| 2 | Team assembly — All on-shift associates gather at designated briefing area; Store Manager confirms attendance against shift roster (W34) | Store Manager | Store Manager | 2 min |
| 3 | Yesterday's performance flash — Store Manager shares prior-day sales vs. target, top-performing departments, conversion rate, and any customer service highlights or issues from W608 | Store Manager | Store Manager | 2 min |
| 4 | Today's priorities — Daily sales target, active promotions (W13), planogram changes (W573), incoming deliveries (W18/W18B), scheduled services (W544), and special events (W147) | Store Manager | Store Manager | 3 min |
| 5 | Safety minute — Quick safety reminder (topic rotates weekly: forklift safety, ladder safety, hazmat handling, fire safety, customer slip prevention) per W655 training schedule | Store Manager | Store Manager | 1 min |
| 6 | Policy update & memo acknowledgment — Any new policy directives, product recalls (W29), price changes (W40), or urgent communications from W716 | Store Manager | Store Manager | 2 min |
| 7 | Staffing & assignment confirmation — Confirm department coverage, break schedules per DOLE requirements (W753), special assignments (inventory count zones, promo setup, vendor demos W745) | Store Manager | Store Manager | 2 min |
| 8 | Team dispersal to stations — Associates proceed to assigned stations; opening procedures per W5A begin | All associates | Store Manager | 3 min |

### System Touchpoints

- W665 store KPI dashboard — daily target and prior-day performance data
- W34 shift scheduling module — attendance verification against roster
- W13 promotional calendar — active promos for the day
- W571 communication module — overnight memos and directives
- W554 replenishment task list — incoming stock priorities
- W544 service order queue — scheduled installations/visits

### Pain Points / Risks

- Inconsistent briefing quality across 200 stores — without a structured template, some Store Managers may skip critical elements, leading to uneven execution
- Time pressure before store opening may cause rushed or skipped huddles, especially during peak seasons (Ber months)
- Part-time or late-shift associates miss morning huddle entirely, creating information gaps for afternoon/evening operations
- No mechanism to verify briefing compliance and content coverage across the chain

### Time Estimate

10–15 minutes per store per day. Store Manager preparation: 5 minutes. Total daily time investment per store: 15–20 minutes.

### Staffing Implication

No incremental headcount. The huddle is conducted by the Store Manager as part of opening duties. 200 stores × 15 min/day = ~50 staff-hours/day across the chain, absorbed within existing Store Manager responsibilities.

---

## W740. Store-Level Customer Complaint On-the-Spot Resolution & Floor Staff Empowerment

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer expresses dissatisfaction to any floor associate or department supervisor |
| **Frequency** | Daily across 200 stores; estimated 200–400 on-the-spot resolutions/day chain-wide |
| **Volume** | ~15–20 per store per day (80%+ of all customer issues resolved at floor level before formal W41 escalation) |
| **Owner** | Department Supervisor |
| **Participants** | Floor Associate, Department Supervisor, Store Manager (escalation), Customer |

### Background

Not all customer issues should escalate to the formal complaint process (W41). BuildRight Depot empowers floor associates and department supervisors with resolution authority to address common issues immediately — product availability, pricing discrepancies, minor defects, slow service, and product knowledge gaps. Resolving issues on the spot improves customer satisfaction and reduces the burden on formal complaint handling.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Issue identification — Floor associate observes or is informed of customer dissatisfaction; determines issue category (pricing, availability, quality, service, policy) | Floor Associate | Department Supervisor | 2 min |
| 2 | Empathy & acknowledgment — Associate acknowledges the concern using trained empathy phrases; assures customer the issue will be addressed immediately | Floor Associate | — | 1 min |
| 3 | Issue assessment — Associate determines if within their resolution authority: pricing discrepancy ≤ PHP 500, product exchange of same value, rain check for out-of-stock, fetching item from backroom, calling department expert | Floor Associate | Department Supervisor | 2 min |
| 4 | Resolution execution — If within authority: apply resolution immediately (price adjustment via W5B, exchange via W12A, rain check issuance, expedited retrieval); if outside authority, escalate to Step 5 | Floor Associate | Department Supervisor | 5 min |
| 5 | Supervisor escalation — For issues exceeding associate authority: pricing discrepancy > PHP 500, return without receipt, defective high-value item, policy exception request — Department Supervisor applies resolution using extended authority matrix | Department Supervisor | Department Supervisor | 5 min |
| 6 | Store Manager escalation — For issues exceeding supervisor authority (refund > PHP 5,000, safety concern, discrimination complaint, threat of DTI escalation) — escalate to Store Manager | Store Manager | Store Manager | 10 min |
| 7 | Resolution logging — Regardless of resolution level, associate logs the issue in the system: category, resolution type, time to resolve, customer satisfaction indicator (satisfied/neutral/dissatisfied) | Floor Associate | Department Supervisor | 2 min |
| 8 | Pattern flagging — System aggregates daily on-the-spot resolutions; flags recurring issues (same SKU pricing error, same department service complaints) for root cause action via W507 | System / Department Supervisor | Department Supervisor | Automated |

### System Touchpoints

- Resolution authority matrix configured per role in ERP (associate vs. supervisor vs. manager thresholds)
- Quick price adjustment function at POS linked to W5B with authorization tracking
- Rain check issuance module with auto-restock notification to W596
- On-the-spot resolution logging form (mobile-friendly, < 60 seconds to complete)
- W507 complaint root cause analysis — pattern data feed from on-the-spot resolutions
- W608 customer feedback pulse — daily satisfaction data feed

### Pain Points / Risks

- Over-empowerment risk — associates granting excessive discounts or unauthorized exceptions erode margin
- Under-empowerment risk — associates refusing to resolve minor issues lead to formal complaints and DTI escalations that cost 10× more to resolve
- Resolution logging compliance — busy associates may skip logging, creating blind spots in W507 pattern analysis
- Inconsistent resolution quality across 200 stores without standardized training and authority matrices

### Time Estimate

2–15 minutes per resolution depending on escalation level. Most resolved at Step 4 in under 5 minutes.

### Staffing Implication

No incremental headcount. Resolutions are absorbed within existing floor associate and supervisor duties. 200 stores × 15–20 resolutions/day × 5 min avg = ~250–330 staff-hours/day, distributed across existing floor staff. Training investment required for resolution authority and empathy training (1 day per associate, refreshed annually per W518).

---

## W741. Store-Level Fuel Inventory & Backup Generator Management

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Daily fuel level check (scheduled); generator start test (weekly); or power outage event (W470) |
| **Frequency** | Daily fuel check; weekly generator test |
| **Volume** | ~150 stores with backup generators (stores in typhoon-prone and brownout-prone areas) |
| **Owner** | Store Maintenance Associate |
| **Participants** | Store Manager, Maintenance Associate, Fuel Supplier, Finance (fuel expense) |

### Background

With the Philippines' frequent power outages (rotational brownouts, typhoon-related outages, grid instability), approximately 150 BuildRight Depot stores are equipped with backup generators. Managing diesel fuel inventory, generator testing, and maintenance ensures stores can continue operating during extended outages per W470 and W580 protocols.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Daily fuel level check — Maintenance Associate records diesel tank level via dipstick or digital gauge at start of shift; enters reading in system | Maintenance Associate | Store Manager | 5 min |
| 2 | Reorder trigger — If fuel level below 40% capacity, system auto-generates fuel replenishment request to contracted supplier; if below 20%, system flags as urgent | System / Maintenance Associate | Store Manager | Automated + 5 min |
| 3 | Fuel delivery receipt — Fuel supplier delivers diesel; Maintenance Associate verifies quantity delivered vs. invoice; system records delivery and updates tank level | Maintenance Associate | Store Manager | 15 min |
| 4 | Weekly generator start test — Every Monday, Maintenance Associate starts generator and runs for 15 minutes under load; records start time, voltage output, unusual sounds/vibrations | Maintenance Associate | Store Manager | 20 min |
| 5 | Monthly load bank test — Monthly full-load test (1 hour); records voltage stability, oil pressure, coolant temperature | Maintenance Associate | — | 1 hour |
| 6 | Maintenance scheduling — System tracks generator runtime hours; auto-generates PM work orders per manufacturer schedule (oil change every 250 hours, filter replacement every 500 hours) | System / Maintenance Associate | — | Automated |
| 7 | Outage event execution — When power outage occurs, Maintenance Associate starts generator within 3 minutes (per W470); records start time and fuel level; monitors throughout outage; records end time and fuel consumed | Maintenance Associate | Store Manager | Duration of outage |
| 8 | Fuel expense reporting — System accumulates fuel costs per store; monthly report to Finance for W489 store-level budget tracking | System / Finance | Finance | Automated |

### System Touchpoints

- Fuel inventory tracking module with tank level monitoring and auto-reorder triggers
- Generator runtime hour meter integration for PM scheduling
- PM work order integration with W47 facility maintenance
- Fuel expense accumulation for W489 store-level budget
- W470 brownout protocol integration — outage event logging

### Pain Points / Risks

- Fuel theft or pilferage risk — diesel fuel is a high-value commodity; without accurate tank monitoring, losses may go undetected
- Generator failure during actual outage due to infrequent testing — weekly tests may not catch issues that only appear under sustained load
- Fuel supplier delivery delays in disaster-affected areas when fuel is most needed
- Environmental compliance — fuel storage tanks require DENR permits and spill containment; leaks create regulatory liability

### Time Estimate

Daily check: 5 min. Weekly test: 20 min. Monthly load test: 1 hour. Fuel delivery receipt: 15 min. Total monthly per store: ~3 hours.

### Staffing Implication

Maintenance Associate at each store absorbs generator management within existing duties. 150 stores × 3 hours/month = 450 staff-hours/month, distributed across existing maintenance staff. No incremental headcount.

---

## W742. Store-Level Key, Access Card & Secure Area Daily Management

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Employee onboarding (W15), separation (W43), shift start/end, lost key/card report |
| **Frequency** | Daily access transactions; periodic audits |
| **Volume** | 200 stores × ~29 employees × average 2 access credentials = ~11,600 active credentials |
| **Owner** | Store Manager |
| **Participants** | Store Manager, Floor Associates, IT (W152), Security Guard, HR |

### Background

Each BuildRight Depot store manages physical keys, electronic access cards, alarm codes, and safe combinations for multiple secure areas: cash office, stockroom, IT/server room, pharmacy/chemical storage (per W236), and management office. With 200 stores and ~11,600 active credentials, systematic credential management is essential for security compliance and audit trail integrity.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Credential issuance — At onboarding (W15), Store Manager issues access card and/or physical keys based on role; system records issuance with employee ID, credential type, and access level | Store Manager | Store Manager | 10 min |
| 2 | Access level configuration — IT configures access card permissions per role: floor associate (sales floor, break room), stock associate (+ stockroom), cashier (+ cash office during shift), department supervisor (+ chemical storage), store manager (all areas) | IT | Store Manager | 15 min |
| 3 | Shift-based access activation — At shift start, system activates access card for authorized areas; at shift end, system deactivates non-common areas | System | — | Automated |
| 4 | Lost/stolen credential reporting — Employee reports lost key/card immediately; Store Manager deactivates access card in system and reissues; physical key loss triggers lock rekey for affected area | Employee / Store Manager | Store Manager | 30 min |
| 5 | Quarterly combination change — Safe combination and alarm code changed quarterly; Store Manager updates system record and distributes new codes to authorized personnel via sealed envelope | Store Manager | Store Manager | 30 min |
| 6 | Separation credential collection — At offboarding (W43), Store Manager collects all physical keys and access cards; IT deactivates electronic access same day | Store Manager / IT | — | 10 min |
| 7 | Monthly credential audit — Store Manager reconciles active credentials against current employee roster; flags unreturned credentials from separated employees | Store Manager | Store Manager | 1 hour |
| 8 | Security incident escalation — If unauthorized access detected (door forced, access at unusual hours), system triggers alert to Store Manager and LP (W37) | System / LP | LP | Automated |

### System Touchpoints

- Electronic access control system integration with employee master (W292)
- Role-based access level configuration linked to position/department
- Shift-based access activation/deactivation linked to W34 scheduling
- Credential audit report — active credentials vs. active employees
- LP alert integration (W37) for unauthorized access attempts
- IT provisioning integration (W152) for access card lifecycle

### Pain Points / Risks

- Unreturned credentials from separated employees — the #1 physical security gap in retail; monthly audit (Step 7) may not catch recently separated employees quickly enough
- Key duplication by employees — physical keys cannot be tracked like electronic cards; high-security areas should use electronic-only access
- Alarm code sharing among employees defeats individual accountability; biometric access for cash office and IT room mitigates this
- Seasonal workers (W555) create credential churn — 200 stores × seasonal hiring = significant issuance/collection volume during peak seasons

### Time Estimate

Issuance: 10 min. Deactivation: 10 min. Lost credential: 30 min. Quarterly combination change: 30 min. Monthly audit: 1 hour.

### Staffing Implication

Store Manager absorbs credential management within existing duties. ~11,600 credentials across 200 stores requires systematic tracking. No incremental headcount, but access control system investment required for electronic access management.

---

## W743. Store-Level Daily Cleaning & Sanitation Checklist Execution

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Store opening preparation (W5A) and store closing (W5F) |
| **Frequency** | Twice daily (pre-opening and post-closing) |
| **Volume** | 200 stores × 2 checklist executions/day |
| **Owner** | Department Supervisor |
| **Participants** | Floor Associates, Maintenance Associate, Janitorial Staff |

### Background

Cleanliness directly impacts customer experience, LGU sanitary permit compliance (W448), DOLE OHS standards (W436), and food safety (where concessionaires operate per W177). A structured daily cleaning checklist ensures consistent store presentation across 200 locations and provides documented compliance evidence for regulatory inspections.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Pre-opening cleaning verification — Before store opens (W5A), Department Supervisors verify overnight cleaning completion per zone: sales floor swept/mopped, restrooms cleaned and stocked, entrance glass cleaned, checkout counters sanitized, trash bins emptied | Department Supervisor | Store Manager | 15 min |
| 2 | Zone-by-zone checklist completion — Each Department Supervisor completes mobile checklist for their zone (10–15 items per zone: floor condition, shelf dust, display cleanliness, signage condition, lighting cleanliness, spill kit availability) | Department Supervisor | — | 10 min |
| 3 | Failed item remediation — Any failed checklist items trigger immediate remediation task; assign to janitorial staff or floor associate | Department Supervisor | Department Supervisor | 5–15 min |
| 4 | Mid-day spot check — Store Manager or Duty Manager conducts random spot check of 3–5 zones; logs observations | Store Manager | Store Manager | 10 min |
| 5 | Post-closing deep clean — After store closes (W5F), janitorial staff executes deep clean per zone: floor mopping/polishing, restroom sanitization, break room cleaning, stockroom sweeping, outdoor area cleanup | Janitorial Staff | Department Supervisor | 1–2 hours |
| 6 | Chemical inventory management — Track cleaning chemical usage and inventory; reorder trigger when below 2-week supply; ensure MSDS (W698) available for all chemicals | Maintenance Associate | Store Manager | 15 min/week |
| 7 | Monthly sanitation compliance report — System compiles daily checklist completion rates and failed items per store; Regional Manager reviews for chronic issues | System / Regional Manager | Regional Manager | Automated + 1 hour review |

### System Touchpoints

- Mobile cleaning checklist application with zone-based task assignment and photo evidence
- Failed-item remediation task tracking with SLA (resolve within 1 hour of store opening)
- Cleaning chemical inventory module with auto-reorder (W136 indirect procurement)
- W448 LGU sanitary permit compliance documentation — checklist archive
- W503 pest control integration — sanitation findings feed pest control prioritization
- Monthly compliance dashboard for Regional Manager review

### Pain Points / Risks

- Cleaning quality inconsistency across 200 stores — subjective pass/fail criteria without photo evidence
- Janitorial staff shortages during peak seasons (Ber months, payday weekends) when stores are at highest foot traffic and need more frequent cleaning
- Cleaning chemical MSDS compliance — LGU inspectors may request MSDS for all chemicals on-site during sanitary permit inspection
- Restroom cleanliness — the #1 driver of negative customer reviews in retail; requires more frequent checks than twice daily

### Time Estimate

Pre-opening verification: 15 min. Zone checklist: 10 min per zone × 5 zones = 50 min. Mid-day spot check: 10 min. Post-closing deep clean: 1–2 hours. Total daily per store: ~2.5–3.5 hours.

### Staffing Implication

Janitorial staff: typically 1–2 per store (outsourced or in-house). 200 stores × 2 janitors = ~400 janitorial staff across the chain. Department Supervisors absorb checklist duties within existing roles. No incremental headcount beyond contracted janitorial services.

---

## W744. Store-Level Special Order Follow-Up & Proactive Customer Notification

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Special order (W545) status change (item shipped, item received at store, item ready for pickup, order overdue) |
| **Frequency** | Daily; triggered by open special orders |
| **Volume** | ~50–100 active special orders per store at any time; ~1,000 status changes/day chain-wide |
| **Owner** | Department Supervisor |
| **Participants** | CSR, Stock Associate, Customer, Supply Chain (W4/W22) |

### Background

Special orders (non-stock items, custom orders, items from other stores) represent a significant revenue stream for a hardware retailer. Unlike routine replenishment, special orders have longer lead times and higher customer anxiety. Proactive follow-up and notification ensures customer satisfaction, reduces abandoned orders, and prevents the "I forgot about my order" problem that leads to unclaimed merchandise and revenue loss.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Daily open order review — Department Supervisor reviews all open special orders in their department sorted by age; system highlights orders approaching or past promised delivery date | Department Supervisor | Store Manager | 15 min |
| 2 | Supply chain status check — For orders with pending fulfillment, Supervisor checks shipment status via W250 control tower or W4 replenishment tracker; contacts DC or supplying store if no update | Department Supervisor | — | 10 min |
| 3 | Customer proactive notification — For significant status changes (item shipped, item received, ready for pickup, delay notification), system sends automated SMS/email to customer; Supervisor calls for high-value orders (> PHP 10,000) | System / Department Supervisor | — | Automated + 5 min per high-value |
| 4 | Received item inspection — When special order arrives at store (W109), Stock Associate inspects item condition, verifies against order specifications (correct model, color, size, quantity) | Stock Associate | Department Supervisor | 10 min |
| 5 | Customer pickup scheduling — Supervisor contacts customer to confirm pickup date/time; for bulky items, coordinates carry-out assistance (W438) or home delivery scheduling (W5D) | Department Supervisor | — | 5 min |
| 6 | Overdue order escalation — Orders past promised date by > 5 business days auto-escalate to Store Manager; Store Manager contacts customer with revised estimate and service recovery offer | Store Manager | Store Manager | 10 min |
| 7 | Unclaimed order management — Orders not picked up within 14 days of notification: system sends final notice; after 21 days, order cancellation initiated per W545 cancellation policy; deposit retained per terms | System / CSR | Store Manager | Automated + 5 min |
| 8 | Monthly special order analytics — System generates report: fill rate, average lead time, on-time pickup rate, cancellation rate, top categories; Store Manager reviews with team during W575 weekly review | System / Store Manager | Store Manager | Automated + 15 min review |

### System Touchpoints

- Special order tracking module with real-time status and age-based prioritization
- W250 supply chain control tower — shipment status integration
- Automated customer notification engine (SMS/email) triggered by status changes
- W109 store receiving — arrival trigger for special orders
- W545 special order — parent workflow for order creation
- W5D / W438 — delivery and carry-out coordination
- Deposit management linked to W754 customer deposit aging

### Pain Points / Risks

- Customer frustration from lack of proactive communication — the #1 complaint for special orders in retail
- Items arriving damaged or incorrect after long wait — the damage/return process (W91/W12) is especially frustrating for customers who waited weeks
- Unclaimed orders consuming valuable backroom storage — 14/21-day unclaimed policy must be enforced consistently
- Special order visibility gap — Department Supervisors managing 50–100 open orders need system prioritization to prevent overdue orders from falling through cracks

### Time Estimate

Daily review: 15 min. Status checks: 10 min. Customer notifications: 5 min per high-value × ~5 per day = 25 min. Received item inspection: 10 min × ~3–5 per day = 30–50 min. Total daily per store: ~60–90 minutes.

### Staffing Implication

Department Supervisors absorb follow-up duties within existing roles. 200 stores × 60–90 min/day = ~200–300 staff-hours/day across the chain. High-volume stores (near construction sites, with large trade customer base) may need dedicated CSR for special order management.

## W745. Store-Level Receiving Dock Scheduling & DSD Vendor Delivery Window Management

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | DSD vendor delivery schedule; inter-store transfer arrival; customer pickup of bulky items |
| **Frequency** | Daily; ~5–15 receiving events per store per day |
| **Volume** | 200 stores × ~10 receiving events/day = ~2,000 receiving events/day chain-wide |
| **Owner** | Stock Associate / Department Supervisor |
| **Participants** | Stock Associate, Department Supervisor, Vendor Driver, Delivery Carrier, LP (W37) |

### Background

BuildRight Depot stores receive merchandise from multiple sources: DC replenishment (W4), DSD vendor deliveries (W18/W18B), inter-store transfers (W22), and special order receipts (W545). Without structured receiving dock scheduling, multiple deliveries arriving simultaneously create congestion, delays, and receiving errors. This workflow manages the store-side receiving dock as a scheduled resource.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Dock schedule generation — System compiles next-day delivery schedule from: confirmed DC delivery windows (W4), DSD vendor appointments (W18B), inter-store transfer ETAs (W22), and special order shipment notifications | System | — | Automated |
| 2 | Time slot allocation — System assigns dock time slots based on delivery priority (perishable/temperature-sensitive first, then high-priority replenishment, then routine), dock door availability (1–2 dock doors per store), and staffing availability (W34) | System / Stock Associate | Department Supervisor | 5 min |
| 3 | Vendor notification — System sends dock time slot confirmation to vendor/carrier via SMS/email; includes dock door assignment, expected unload duration, and store contact number | System | — | Automated |
| 4 | Day-of gate check-in — Vendor/carrier arrives; Stock Associate verifies delivery appointment, checks against schedule, and assigns to designated dock door or staging area | Stock Associate | Department Supervisor | 5 min |
| 5 | Receiving execution — Stock Associate executes receiving per W18 (DSD) or W109 (DC/transfer); dock time slot enforces expected completion time | Stock Associate | — | 15–60 min |
| 6 | Dock congestion management — If delivery arrives outside scheduled window or previous delivery overruns, Stock Associate redirects to overflow staging area; system reschedules to next available slot | Stock Associate | Department Supervisor | 5 min |
| 7 | No-show management — Vendor no-show for scheduled delivery: system logs no-show; feeds to vendor scorecard (W44); auto-triggers follow-up to vendor and DC for alternate supply plan | System / Department Supervisor | Department Supervisor | Automated |
| 8 | Weekly receiving efficiency report — System compiles: on-time delivery rate, average unload time, dock utilization rate, no-show rate; Store Manager reviews in W575 weekly review | System / Store Manager | Store Manager | Automated + 10 min |

### System Touchpoints

- Store receiving dock scheduling module with calendar view and time slot management
- W18B DSD scheduling integration — vendor appointment feed
- W4 replenishment integration — DC delivery window feed
- W22 transfer integration — inter-store transfer ETA feed
- W44 vendor scorecard — no-show and late delivery data feed
- W34 shift scheduling — staffing availability for receiving windows
- Mobile notification for vendor/carrier time slot confirmation

### Pain Points / Risks

- Small stores with single dock door — any delay cascades to all subsequent deliveries
- DSD vendors ignoring scheduled windows — common in PH where vendor logistics are less structured; enforcement requires vendor scorecard consequences
- Peak season congestion — during Ber months, receiving volume doubles; stores need overflow staging areas
- Customer vehicle conflict — customer pickup/loading (W438) uses same dock area as receiving delivery; scheduling must account for both inbound and outbound dock usage

### Time Estimate

Schedule generation: automated. Time slot allocation: 5 min. Check-in: 5 min. Receiving: 15–60 min. Total daily management: ~30–60 minutes beyond actual receiving time.

### Staffing Implication

Stock Associate absorbs dock scheduling within existing receiving duties. One Stock Associate per store dedicated to receiving during morning delivery window (6 AM – 12 PM). No incremental headcount.

## W746. Store-Level Building Material Sample Management & Customer Selection Assistance

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer requests to view or compare material samples (tiles, countertops, wood, paint, fabric, flooring) |
| **Frequency** | Daily; ~10–20 sample requests per store per day |
| **Volume** | 200 stores × ~15 sample requests/day = ~3,000 sample interactions/day chain-wide |
| **Owner** | Department Supervisor (Building Materials) |
| **Participants** | Floor Associate, Department Supervisor, Customer, Merchandising (W1) |

### Background

Unlike most retail categories, building materials require tactile evaluation — customers need to see, touch, and compare tile finishes, wood grains, countertop materials, paint colors, and fabric swatches before purchasing. BuildRight Depot stores maintain sample libraries that must be curated, organized, and replenished. This workflow manages the sample library lifecycle from display to customer selection assistance to replenishment.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Sample library maintenance — Daily: Floor Associate verifies sample board displays are complete, organized, and labeled; reports missing or damaged samples for replacement | Floor Associate | Department Supervisor | 15 min |
| 2 | Customer sample request — Customer requests to view samples; Floor Associate guides customer to sample display area, provides sample boards, and offers to check inventory availability for preferred options | Floor Associate | — | 10 min |
| 3 | Sample checkout — For take-home samples (tile pieces, wood samples, paint swatches), Floor Associate checks out sample against customer loyalty account; system tracks sample borrower and due date (7-day loan period) | Floor Associate | Department Supervisor | 5 min |
| 4 | Selection consultation — Floor Associate assists with quantity calculation (square meters of tiles, linear meters of lumber, liters of paint), provides product specifications from W50 PIM, and generates quotation (W542) | Floor Associate | — | 15 min |
| 5 | Sample return tracking — System tracks overdue sample loans; sends automated reminders at 7 and 14 days; after 21 days, system flags for W605 return fraud review if patterns suggest abuse | System | — | Automated |
| 6 | Sample replenishment — When sample inventory drops below threshold, system generates replenishment request to merchandising (W1) or vendor; merchandising ships new samples via W4 | System / Department Supervisor | — | 5 min/request |
| 7 | Seasonal sample rotation — At seasonal transitions (W583), Department Supervisor coordinates with merchandising to rotate sample displays for new seasonal products | Department Supervisor | Merchandising | 1 hour |
| 8 | Sample performance analytics — System tracks which samples drive the highest conversion to sale; merchandising uses data for assortment decisions (W1) and planogram placement (W314) | System | — | Automated |

### System Touchpoints

- Sample inventory tracking module with per-SKU sample quantity and display location
- Sample checkout/loan module linked to customer loyalty account (W17)
- Overdue sample loan tracking with automated reminders
- W50 PIM integration — product specifications and imagery for consultation
- W542 quotation generation — quantity calculation and pricing
- W1 assortment review — sample performance data feed
- W314 planogram master — sample display position mapping

### Pain Points / Risks

- Sample theft and loss — small tile pieces, wood samples, and fabric swatches are easily pocketed; without checkout tracking, sample libraries deplete rapidly
- Outdated samples — vendors discontinue products but samples remain on display, leading to customer orders for unavailable items
- Quantity calculation errors — incorrect square meter or board foot calculations lead to under/over-ordering, costly for large projects
- Sample display space — building material departments require significant floor space for sample displays, competing with merchandise for selling space

### Time Estimate

Library maintenance: 15 min. Customer sample interaction: 10–20 min. Sample checkout: 5 min. Selection consultation: 15 min. Total daily: ~3–4 hours per building materials department.

### Staffing Implication

Building materials department typically staffed with 2–3 specialized associates who have product knowledge in construction materials. 200 stores × 2–3 associates = ~400–600 specialized building material associates. Sample management is absorbed within existing department duties but requires dedicated floor space.

## W747. POS Credit Card Installment Selling & 0% Interest Promotion Processing

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer selects big-ticket item and elects to pay via credit card installment (3/6/12/24 months) at POS |
| **Frequency** | Daily; ~15–25 installment transactions per store per day |
| **Volume** | 200 stores × ~20 installment transactions/day = ~4,000 installment transactions/day chain-wide (~15% of non-cash transactions by value) |
| **Owner** | Cashier |
| **Participants** | Cashier, Store Manager (override), Bank Partner (real-time), Customer |

### Background
In the Philippine retail market, credit card installment selling is a major revenue driver for big-ticket items. Banks (BDO, BPI, Citibank, Metrobank, HSBC) partner with retailers to offer 0% interest installment promos on eligible SKUs. BuildRight Depot participates in these programs for appliances, power tools, furniture, bathroom fixtures, and building material packages. This workflow manages the POS-side installment selling process including bank promo validation, term selection, and transaction completion.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Installment eligibility check**: Customer expresses interest in installment; Cashier scans item and system checks installment eligibility: (a) SKU is on bank installment-eligible list, (b) item price meets minimum threshold (typically PHP 3,000+), (c) at least one active bank promo covers this SKU | Cashier | System | 1 min |
| 2 | **Bank promo presentation**: System displays available installment options: bank name, available terms (3/6/12/24 months), monthly amortization, interest rate (0% for promo, regular rate otherwise), and promo validity period | System | — | Automated |
| 3 | **Customer term selection**: Customer selects preferred bank and installment term; Cashier confirms customer has the corresponding credit card | Customer | Cashier | 2 min |
| 4 | **Credit card authorization**: Cashier swipes/dips/taps credit card; POS sends installment transaction to bank for real-time authorization (distinct from straightcharge); bank responds with approval/decline and installment confirmation | Cashier | Bank | 2 min |
| 5 | **Installment transaction completion**: System records transaction as installment sale with: bank reference, term, monthly amortization, original amount, and interest subsidy (if 0% promo); BIR-compliant receipt printed with installment details | Cashier | System | 2 min |
| 6 | **Interest subsidy tracking**: For 0% promos, system records merchant interest subsidy amount (paid by BuildRight to bank); Finance reconciles subsidy deductions from bank settlement (W750) | System | — | Automated |
| 7 | **Customer installment disclosure**: Customer receives printed installment schedule with: total amount, monthly payment, number of months, first billing date, and cancellation policy | Cashier | — | 1 min |
| 8 | **Exception handling**: If bank declines: offer alternative bank promo, straightcharge, or layaway (W75); if SKU not eligible: suggest eligible alternative or straightcharge | Cashier | Store Manager | 3 min |

### System Touchpoints

- Bank installment promo engine: real-time bank promo lookup by SKU, configurable promo calendar, bank API integration
- POS installment transaction mode: distinct from straightcharge with installment-specific receipt format
- BIR-compliant receipt with installment details (per W540 BIR invoice requirements)
- Merchant interest subsidy tracking module for 0% promo cost accounting
- Bank settlement reconciliation feed to W750 (credit card installment reconciliation)
- W149 bank partnership management — promo terms and eligibility configuration
- W85 product costing — interest subsidy impact on margin analysis

### Pain Points / Risks

- Bank system downtime — if bank installment API is unavailable, customers cannot complete installment purchases; fallback to straightcharge loses the 0% promo appeal
- Promo calendar complexity — managing overlapping bank promos with different eligible SKU lists, term structures, and validity periods across 5+ banks is administratively burdensome
- Interest subsidy margin erosion — 0% promos require BuildRight to subsidize interest (typically 3–5% of transaction value); without proper margin analysis, promotions may be unprofitable
- Customer disputes on installment charges — bank billing discrepancies require coordination between BuildRight, the customer, and the issuing bank
- DTI compliance — installment promos require DTI sales promotion permits (W427)

### Time Estimate

Per transaction: 8–12 minutes total (1 min eligibility + 2 min selection + 2 min authorization + 2 min completion + 1 min disclosure). Cashier training: additional 2 hours on installment procedures beyond standard POS training (W518).

### Staffing Implication

No incremental headcount. Installment selling is absorbed within existing cashier duties. Additional training investment: 2 hours per cashier on installment procedures, refreshed quarterly with new bank promo updates. Finance team: ~4 hours/week for bank promo reconciliation and subsidy tracking across chain.

---

## W748. POS Customer Loyalty On-the-Spot Upselling & Cross-Selling

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer checkout in progress (W5B); system identifies upsell/cross-sell opportunity based on basket contents and loyalty profile |
| **Frequency** | Every transaction with identified loyalty member (~60% of transactions) |
| **Volume** | 200 stores × ~140 loyalty transactions/day = ~28,000 upsell opportunities/day chain-wide |
| **Owner** | Cashier |
| **Participants** | Cashier, Customer |

### Background
With 600,000 loyalty members and a rich customer data platform (W156), BuildRight Depot can leverage real-time basket analysis at POS to suggest complementary products, promotional bundles, and loyalty tier upgrade opportunities. This workflow transforms the checkout from a transactional endpoint into a revenue-generating touchpoint.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Loyalty member identification**: Customer presents loyalty card or mobile app at checkout; system loads customer profile including tier, purchase history, and active offers | Cashier | System | Instant |
| 2 | **System upsell suggestion**: Based on basket contents, system generates 1–3 targeted suggestions: (a) complementary item (paint → brushes, tiles → grout), (b) bundle completion (buy 2 more for bundle discount), (c) loyalty tier upgrade incentive (spend PHP 500 more to reach next tier) | System | — | Automated |
| 3 | **Cashier suggestion delivery**: Cashier presents suggestion naturally: "Based on your purchase, you might also need..." or "You're only PHP 500 away from Gold tier this month" | Cashier | — | 30 sec |
| 4 | **Customer acceptance**: If customer accepts: Cashier scans suggested item (fetched from nearby shelf/display by stock associate if needed); if customer declines, proceed with original transaction | Cashier | Customer | 1–2 min |
| 5 | **Cross-sell service attachment**: For eligible products (e.g., appliance purchase), system prompts service attachment: extended warranty (W33), installation service (W138), delivery scheduling (W5D) | System | Cashier | 30 sec |
| 6 | **Suggestion analytics**: System logs suggestion delivery, acceptance/rejection, and resulting incremental revenue; feeds to W156 CDP for suggestion algorithm optimization | System | — | Automated |
| 7 | **Weekly performance tracking**: Store Manager reviews upsell/cross-sell attachment rate per cashier in W522 daily transaction review; top performers recognized in W630 employee recognition program | Store Manager | — | Part of W522 |

### System Touchpoints

- W156 Customer Data Platform — real-time customer profile and purchase history
- W5B POS checkout integration — suggestion engine triggered during transaction
- W313 loyalty program configuration — tier thresholds and incentive rules
- W13 promotional rules — bundle and promotional attachment
- W522 cashier performance audit — upsell attachment rate per cashier
- W138/W33/W5D service attachment prompts

### Pain Points / Risks

- Customer annoyance — aggressive upselling at checkout can harm customer experience; must be limited to 1–3 relevant suggestions per transaction
- Cashier resistance — cashiers may skip suggestions due to time pressure or discomfort with selling; training and incentive alignment needed
- Suggestion relevance — irrelevant suggestions (e.g., suggesting paint for a plumbing customer) undermine program credibility and customer trust
- Queue impact — extended checkout time from upselling may increase queue length during peak hours (W526)

### Time Estimate

Per suggestion: 30 seconds delivery + 1–2 minutes if accepted. Average impact per transaction: +30–90 seconds. Total daily per store: ~20–30 additional minutes across all transactions.

### Staffing Implication

No incremental headcount. Upselling is integrated into cashier checkout workflow. Cashier incentive program recommended: commission or reward for upsell attachment rate above threshold. Training: 2 hours per cashier on suggestive selling techniques, refreshed annually.
