## W749. POS Heavy Equipment & Power Tool Safety Acknowledgment & Release Processing

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer purchases high-risk equipment (chainsaws, circular saws, angle grinders, jackhammers, generators, welding equipment, pressure washers) |
| **Frequency** | Daily; ~5–10 transactions per store per day |
| **Volume** | 200 stores × ~7 high-risk equipment sales/day = ~1,400 safety acknowledgment transactions/day chain-wide |
| **Owner** | Cashier / Department Supervisor |
| **Participants** | Cashier, Department Supervisor, Customer |

### Background
BuildRight Depot sells professional-grade power tools and heavy equipment that carry significant injury risk. Philippine regulations (DOLE for professional use, DTI/FPA for consumer chemicals) and corporate liability management require documented customer acknowledgment of safety warnings and proper usage guidelines before release. This workflow creates an auditable safety documentation trail.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **High-risk item detection**: System flags scanned item as high-risk based on item master safety classification (W252); POS prompts safety acknowledgment workflow | System | — | Instant |
| 2 | **Safety brief display**: Customer-facing screen displays safety brief: equipment hazards, required PPE, proper operation summary, and emergency procedures; printed safety data sheet (W698 SDS) attached to receipt | System | Cashier | 1 min |
| 3 | **Verbal safety confirmation**: Cashier verbally confirms customer has read and understood safety brief; asks if customer has questions about safe operation | Cashier | — | 2 min |
| 4 | **PPE suggestion**: System suggests required PPE for purchased equipment (safety goggles for grinder, ear protection for jackhammer, gloves for chainsaw); Cashier offers PPE add-to-basket | System | Cashier | 1 min |
| 5 | **Customer acknowledgment signature**: Customer signs digital safety acknowledgment on customer-facing screen or printed form; acknowledges receipt of safety information and assumption of personal responsibility | Customer | Cashier | 1 min |
| 6 | **ID verification for restricted items**: For items with age restrictions (FPA-regulated chemicals in equipment, cutting tools per W520): Cashier verifies customer ID and age | Cashier | — | 1 min |
| 7 | **Documentation archival**: System archives signed safety acknowledgment with transaction record; 7-year retention per W256 document retention policy; accessible for W285 public liability claims | System | — | Automated |
| 8 | **Exception handling**: If customer refuses to sign acknowledgment: Cashier explains requirement; Store Manager may override with documented reason; refusal flagged in LP system (W37) | Cashier | Store Manager | 5 min |

### System Touchpoints

- Item master safety classification flag (W252) — high-risk equipment attribute
- POS safety acknowledgment workflow triggered by SKU classification
- SDS repository integration (W698) — auto-print relevant SDS
- PPE suggestion engine — linked to equipment SKU via W252 item attributes
- Digital signature capture on customer-facing display
- Document archival system (W255) — 7-year retention per W256
- W285 public liability claims integration — archived acknowledgments for liability defense
- W520 age-restricted product verification — overlapping equipment with age restrictions

### Pain Points / Risks

- Customer resistance to signing "legal documents" at checkout — some customers perceive safety acknowledgments as liability waivers and refuse
- Queue disruption — safety acknowledgment adds 3–5 minutes to checkout for high-risk items, impacting queue during peak hours
- Inconsistent safety brief quality — verbal confirmation may be skipped or rushed by cashiers under time pressure
- Missing PPE — customer purchases dangerous equipment without required safety gear, exposing BuildRight to negligence claims if injury occurs
- Regulatory compliance — FPA-regulated equipment requires specific labeling and documentation; non-compliance risks fines and product seizure

### Time Estimate

Per transaction: 5–8 minutes additional time beyond standard checkout (1 min detection + 1 min brief + 2 min verbal + 1 min PPE + 1 min signature + 1 min ID). Cashier training: additional 4 hours on safety equipment procedures.

### Staffing Implication

No incremental headcount. Safety acknowledgment absorbed within cashier duties. Additional training: 4 hours per cashier on high-risk equipment identification, safety brief delivery, and PPE recommendations. Store Manager involvement for refusals: ~2–3 per store per week.

## W771. Store-Level BOPIS Order Aging & Abandoned Pickup Processing

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | BOPIS order exceeds 5-day hold period without customer pickup |
| **Frequency** | Daily; ~200-300 abandoned BOPIS orders per week chain-wide |
| **Volume** | ~200-300 aged BOPIS orders/week across 200 stores (~1-2 per store per week) |
| **Owner** | Customer Service Representative |
| **Participants** | Customer Service Rep, Stock Associate, Store Manager, Customer |

### Background
BuildRight Depot's BOPIS (Buy Online, Pick Up In Store) orders are fulfilled within 4 hours per W11 and held for 5 days. Approximately 3-5% of BOPIS orders (~25,700 orders/month × 4% = ~1,000/month) are never picked up, creating tied-up inventory, cluttered staging areas, and customer service overhead. Unclaimed orders must be systematically processed to return inventory to saleable stock and initiate customer refunds per policy.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Daily aging report review**: System generates BOPIS order aging report: (a) Day 3 orders — automated reminder notification to customer via W708 (SMS, email, app push), (b) Day 4 orders — CSR makes personal phone call to customer, (c) Day 5 orders — final notification and pickup deadline (store closing time) | System / CSR | Store Manager | 30 min/day |
| 2 | **Day 3 automated reminder**: System sends pickup reminder: "Your BuildRight order #[X] is ready for pickup at [Store Name]. Please pick up by [Date/Time] or your order will be cancelled and refunded." | System | — | Automated |
| 3 | **Day 4 personal outreach**: CSR calls customer: (a) confirms customer received notifications, (b) offers pickup extension (maximum 2 additional days per Store Manager approval), (c) if customer wants to cancel — process cancellation per W98 with auto-refund, (d) log outcome in order management system | CSR | — | 5 min/call |
| 4 | **Day 5 abandoned order processing**: For orders not picked up by store closing on Day 5: (a) system auto-cancels order per W536 unified order management, (b) inventory returned to saleable stock — system removes hold and updates ATP across all channels, (c) automated refund initiated per original payment method via W101 | System / CSR | Store Manager | 5 min/order |
| 5 | **Customer cancellation notification**: System sends cancellation notification: "Your order #[X] has been cancelled as it was not picked up within 5 days. A refund of PHP [X] has been processed to your [payment method]. Please allow 3-5 business days for the refund to appear." | System | — | Automated |
| 6 | **Staged inventory restock**: Stock Associate returns staged items from BOPIS staging area to selling floor: (a) verify item condition, (b) return to shelf location per planogram (W86), (c) system confirms inventory is available for sale | Stock Associate | — | 5-10 min/order |
| 7 | **Weekly BOPIS abandonment analytics**: Store Manager reviews weekly abandonment metrics: (a) abandonment rate by store, (b) customer response to Day 3/4 outreach, (c) refund processing accuracy, (d) inventory restock timeliness; feeds to W575 weekly operations review | Store Manager | — | 30 min/week |
| 8 | **Monthly chain-wide BOPIS optimization**: Monthly: ecommerce and CX teams review chain-wide BOPIS abandonment patterns: (a) stores with > 5% abandonment rate root cause analysis, (b) seasonal or product-category correlation, (c) customer feedback from abandoned orders, (d) recommendations for pickup SLA or communication improvements | CX Manager | VP Ecommerce | 2 hours/month |

### System Touchpoints

- Unified order management (W536) — BOPIS order lifecycle and cancellation
- W11 BOPIS order fulfillment — order staging and readiness confirmation
- W98 ecommerce order exception & cancellation — cancellation workflow
- W101 customer refund & credit processing — automated refund initiation
- W708 customer communication management — automated and manual notifications
- POS inventory ATP — inventory hold release and availability update
- W86 planogram compliance — restock location verification
- W575 store weekly operations review — abandonment metrics feed

### Pain Points / Risks

- Customer dissatisfaction — auto-cancellation may frustrate customers who intended to pick up but forgot; proactive Day 3/4 outreach mitigates
- Refund processing delays — payment method determines refund speed: e-wallet (1-2 days), credit card (3-5 days), GCash (instant); customer expectations must be managed
- Staging area congestion — high-value or bulky items (tiles, lumber) in BOPIS staging consume limited backroom space; expedited processing needed
- Inventory inaccuracy — if restock is delayed or incorrect location used, ATP and planogram compliance affected
- Fraud potential — customer could claim they picked up order they did not receive; BOPIS pickup requires customer ID and order confirmation per W11

### Time Estimate

Daily: aging review (30 min) + Day 4 calls (5 min/call × ~5-10 calls = 25-50 min) + Day 5 processing (5 min/order × ~2-3 = 10-15 min) + restock (5-10 min/order) = ~70-105 min/day. Weekly analytics: 30 min.

### Staffing Implication

Absorbed within existing CSR and Stock Associate duties. Daily time: ~1.5-2 hours per store. No incremental headcount.

---

## W772. Store-Level Rain Check Issuance for Out-of-Stock Promotional Items

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer requests promotional item that is out of stock during promotional period |
| **Frequency** | During promotional periods; ~5-10 rain check requests per store per promotional event |
| **Volume** | 200 stores × ~7 rain checks per promo event × 6 major promos/year = ~8,400 rain checks/year |
| **Owner** | Customer Service Representative |
| **Participants** | CSR, Department Supervisor, Customer |

### Background
Per the Philippine Consumer Act (RA 7394) and DTI regulations, retailers are expected to honor advertised promotional prices even when the item is temporarily out of stock. While not a strict legal obligation to issue rain checks in all circumstances, BuildRight Depot issues rain checks as a customer service policy and competitive practice — ensuring customers receive the promotional price when the item is restocked. This manages customer goodwill, DTI compliance risk per W731 Consumer Act monitoring, and competitive reputation.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Customer request received**: Customer approaches customer service counter requesting out-of-stock promotional item; CSR verifies: (a) item is currently in advertised promotion per W13 promotional calendar, (b) item is out of stock at this store per POS inventory check | CSR | — | 3 min |
| 2 | **Inventory check and alternative offer**: CSR checks: (a) current store inventory (confirmed OOS), (b) nearby store availability for customer pickup, (c) expected replenishment date from W596 replenishment exception management; offers alternatives: (i) rain check at this store, (ii) transfer from nearby store per W214, (iii) substitute product per W279 | CSR | — | 5 min |
| 3 | **Rain check issuance**: If customer chooses rain check: (a) CSR creates rain check in ERP: customer name, contact number, item SKU, promotional price, quantity, promotional period end date, rain check expiry (30 days from promo end), (b) system validates promotional price against W300 promotional rule master, (c) prints rain check document with unique tracking number | CSR | Department Supervisor | 5 min |
| 4 | **System inventory reservation**: System creates soft reservation: (a) flags item for priority replenishment per W596, (b) allocates next incoming stock to rain check queue before general availability, (c) maximum 10 rain checks per SKU per store to prevent excessive reservation | System | — | Automated |
| 5 | **Customer notification on restock**: When item is received and allocated to rain check queue: system triggers customer notification per W708 (SMS, email, app push): "Your rain check item [SKU] is now available at [Store Name]. Please pick up within 7 days. Your promotional price: PHP [X]." | System | — | Automated |
| 6 | **Rain check redemption**: Customer presents rain check at pickup: (a) CSR verifies rain check validity (not expired, correct item), (b) processes sale at rain check promotional price per POS override per W529, (c) system closes rain check and releases inventory reservation | CSR | — | 5 min |
| 7 | **Rain check expiry and close**: For unredeemed rain checks: (a) 7 days after restock notification — final reminder, (b) 14 days after notification — rain check expired, (c) system closes rain check, releases inventory reservation, (d) customer notified of expiration | System / CSR | — | Automated + 2 min/expired |
| 8 | **Monthly rain check analytics**: Store Manager reviews: (a) rain check issuance rate by promotion, (b) redemption rate (target > 70%), (c) average time from issuance to redemption, (d) OOS rate on promoted items feeding W596 replenishment exception analysis | Store Manager | — | 30 min/month |

### System Touchpoints

- Rain check module — issuance, tracking, reservation, and redemption lifecycle
- W13 promotions & pricing execution — promotional price validation
- W300 promotional rule master — promotional price verification
- W596 replenishment exception management — priority replenishment trigger
- W214 store-to-store expedited transfers — alternative fulfillment option
- W279 product substitution rules — substitute product suggestion engine
- W529 POS void & refund tiered authorization — promotional price override
- W708 customer communication management — restock notification
- W731 Consumer Act compliance monitoring — rain check policy compliance tracking

### Pain Points / Risks

- DTI regulatory risk — failure to offer rain checks for advertised promotional items may trigger DTI complaints per W469; Consumer Act compliance per W731 requires documented rain check process
- Inventory gaming — excessive rain check reservations reduce available-to-promise for regular customers; 10-per-SKU cap mitigates
- Promotional pricing conflict — if promotional period ends and regular price resumes, rain check customer expects promotional price; rain check must clearly state expiry
- Customer no-show — customer never returns to redeem rain check, tying up inventory reservation; 14-day post-restock expiry limits exposure
- Store-level compliance — inconsistent rain check issuance across 200 stores creates compliance risk; W731 Consumer Act monitoring must include rain check compliance

### Time Estimate

Per rain check: issuance (5 min) + inventory check (5 min) = 10 min. Redemption: 5 min. Monthly analytics: 30 min. Per promotional event: ~1-2 hours additional CSR time per store.

### Staffing Implication

Absorbed within existing CSR duties. Per promotional event: ~1-2 hours additional CSR time. No incremental headcount.

---

## W773. Store-Level Customer Hold & Will-Call Order Management

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer requests item be held for later pickup; or customer special order (W545) arrives and needs staging |
| **Frequency** | Daily; ~3-5 will-call orders per store per day |
| **Volume** | 200 stores × ~4 will-call orders/day = ~800 will-call orders/day chain-wide |
| **Owner** | Customer Service Representative |
| **Participants** | CSR, Stock Associate, Customer |

### Background
Customers frequently request that items be held at the store for pickup within a short period — a contractor rushing to a job site who wants materials guaranteed for afternoon pickup, or a homeowner who found an item online but wants to see it in person before purchasing. Unlike BOPIS (online order, pre-paid), will-call holds are typically in-store requests without pre-payment, requiring different inventory management, expiration, and customer communication processes.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Hold request received**: Customer requests hold at customer service counter: (a) CSR verifies item availability per POS inventory, (b) confirms item is in sellable condition (not display unit per W524 unless customer agrees), (c) identifies customer via loyalty account or phone number | CSR | — | 3 min |
| 2 | **Hold creation in system**: CSR creates will-call hold order: (a) customer details (name, phone, email), (b) item SKU, quantity, and location, (c) hold expiry (standard: 24 hours; extended: 48 hours with Department Supervisor approval), (d) special instructions (e.g., "customer will measure lumber to length"), (e) no payment collected — hold is a reservation, not a sale | CSR | — | 3 min |
| 3 | **Inventory reservation**: System creates soft hold on inventory: (a) removes held quantity from available-to-promise across all channels (POS, ecommerce, BOPIS), (b) physical item pulled from shelf by Stock Associate and placed in will-call staging area with hold tag showing customer name, hold number, and expiry date | Stock Associate | — | 10 min |
| 4 | **Customer notification**: System sends confirmation to customer: "Your item [SKU] has been held at [Store Name] until [Date/Time]. Please bring a valid ID for pickup. Hold reference: #[X]." | System | — | Automated |
| 5 | **Expiry monitoring**: System monitors hold expiry: (a) 4 hours before expiry — reminder notification to customer, (b) at expiry — hold auto-cancelled if no pickup, (c) item returned to shelf and ATP restored | System / CSR | — | Automated |
| 6 | **Customer pickup and sale completion**: Customer arrives for pickup: (a) CSR retrieves item from will-call staging, (b) verifies customer identity, (c) processes sale transaction at current price (not price at time of hold — holds do not guarantee pricing unless rain check per W772), (d) system closes hold order and confirms sale | CSR | — | 5 min |
| 7 | **Expired hold restock**: For expired holds: (a) Stock Associate returns item to shelf per planogram (W86), (b) system releases inventory reservation, (c) CSR attempts to contact customer (one-time courtesy call), (d) no penalty or charge to customer for expired holds | Stock Associate / CSR | — | 10 min |
| 8 | **Weekly will-call analytics**: Store Manager reviews: (a) hold-to-sale conversion rate (target > 75%), (b) average hold duration, (c) expired hold rate, (d) items most frequently held (indicates possible planogram or inventory positioning improvement) | Store Manager | — | 15 min/week |

### System Touchpoints

- Will-call/hold management module — creation, tracking, expiry, and release
- POS inventory ATP — inventory reservation and availability update across channels
- W536 unified order management — will-call integration with order lifecycle
- W545 special order processing — special order arrival staging integration
- W708 customer communication — hold confirmation and expiry reminders
- W86 planogram compliance — restock location guidance
- W772 rain check management — pricing guarantee distinction (rain check vs. hold)

### Pain Points / Risks

- Inventory distortion — excessive holds reduce visible ATP, potentially deterring ecommerce and BOPIS customers who see "out of stock" online
- Staging area space — will-call items in customer service staging area create clutter, especially for bulky items (tiles, lumber, cement bags)
- Pricing expectation mismatch — customer may expect price at time of hold, not current price; clear communication at hold creation essential
- Abandoned holds — frequent expired holds indicate process abuse or customer misunderstanding; analytics must identify patterns
- Cross-channel conflict — item held for will-call but also available online creates fulfillment conflict; system must enforce inventory reservation across all channels

### Time Estimate

Per hold: creation (3 min) + inventory check (3 min) + pull and stage (10 min) = 16 min. Pickup: 5 min. Expired restock: 10 min. Daily per store: ~1 hour for 4 holds.

### Staffing Implication

Absorbed within existing CSR and Stock Associate duties. Daily per store: ~1 hour. No incremental headcount.

---

## W774. Store-Level Parking Lot & Exterior Facility Daily Management

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Start of each store business day; and ad-hoc incident response |
| **Frequency** | Daily; continuous during business hours |
| **Volume** | 200 stores × daily exterior facility management |
| **Owner** | Maintenance / Utility Staff |
| **Participants** | Maintenance Staff, Store Manager, Security Guard |

### Background
BuildRight Depot's big-box format stores (8,000-15,000 sqm selling area) include significant exterior infrastructure: parking lots accommodating 80-150 vehicles, outdoor lumber yard areas, garden center spaces, loading bays per W723, and building exterior including signage and facade. Daily exterior facility management ensures customer safety, regulatory compliance (PWD accessibility per W497, fire lane clearance), and brand presentation. Unlike W47 which handles reactive facility maintenance work orders, this workflow addresses proactive daily exterior management.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Morning exterior walkthrough**: Maintenance Staff performs opening exterior inspection: (a) parking lot condition (potholes, debris, standing water from overnight rain), (b) signage lighting and visibility, (c) PWD parking space availability and markings per BP 344, (d) fire lane clearance per BFP requirements, (e) outdoor lumber yard security and inventory stacking, (f) garden center plant condition and watering needs | Maintenance Staff | Store Manager | 20 min |
| 2 | **Parking lot preparation**: (a) remove any overnight debris, abandoned items, or hazards, (b) verify parking striping visibility, (c) check cart return corrals are empty and positioned, (d) confirm PWD ramp accessibility unobstructed, (e) verify exterior lighting operational for early morning/evening hours | Maintenance Staff | — | 15 min |
| 3 | **Customer cart retrieval**: Throughout the day: Maintenance Staff retrieves shopping carts from parking lot at regular intervals (every 30-60 minutes during peak, every 2 hours off-peak); returns to store entrance; verifies cart condition (broken wheels, clean) | Maintenance Staff | — | 5 min/trip, ~8-12 trips/day |
| 4 | **Mid-day exterior check**: (a) parking lot capacity observation (overflow triggers notification to Store Manager for potential traffic management), (b) exterior signage still operational, (c) outdoor display areas (garden center, lumber promotional displays) maintained, (d) loading bay area clear and organized per W723 | Maintenance Staff | — | 10 min |
| 5 | **Hazard response**: For exterior hazards during business hours: (a) oil/chemical spill in parking lot — contain and clean per hazmat procedure W238, (b) broken glass — immediate cleanup, (c) flooding from heavy rain — deploy warning signs and sandbags, (d) fallen tree/branch — clear pathway and file maintenance work order per W47 | Maintenance Staff | Store Manager | 10-30 min/incident |
| 6 | **End-of-day exterior close**: (a) final cart retrieval from parking lot, (b) exterior lighting check (parking lot lights on for safety), (c) garden center items that need overnight protection moved indoors or covered, (d) outdoor lumber yard secured, (e) parking lot gate/closure (if applicable) | Maintenance Staff | — | 15 min |
| 7 | **Weekly exterior maintenance checklist**: Weekly: (a) parking lot line striping assessment, (b) exterior signage cleaning, (c) PWD ramp and accessible path condition check per W497, (d) gutter and drainage clearing (Philippine rainy season critical), (e) exterior paint and facade condition assessment | Maintenance Staff | Store Manager | 1 hour/week |

### System Touchpoints

- Store facility maintenance module — daily checklist and hazard logging
- W47 store facility maintenance & work orders — maintenance work order generation
- W497 PWD accessibility compliance — PWD parking and ramp verification
- W723 loading bay traffic management — exterior truck and customer vehicle coordination
- W238 hazmat spill response — parking lot chemical spill protocol
- W503 pest control & sanitation — exterior pest management
- W722 exterior display & garden center operations — garden center and outdoor display coordination

### Pain Points / Risks

- Customer slip-and-fall liability — wet parking lot, potholes, or debris create premises liability per W285 public liability claims; proactive maintenance is liability defense
- PWD compliance — blocked or inadequately marked PWD parking spaces violate BP 344 and RA 7277 per W497; LGU inspections per W54 may issue citations
- Philippine rainy season — June-November brings daily rainfall causing flooding, mud, and safety hazards; exterior management workload doubles during this period
- Shopping cart theft — carts taken off-premises create replacement cost (~PHP 8,000-12,000/cart); perimeter management needed
- Signage damage — typhoons damage exterior signage; prompt repair needed for brand image and safety (falling sign hazard)

### Time Estimate

Daily: morning walkthrough (20 min) + parking prep (15 min) + cart retrieval (5 min × 10 trips = 50 min) + mid-day check (10 min) + EOD close (15 min) = ~110 min/day. Weekly maintenance: +1 hour. Rainy season: +30 min/day for water management.

### Staffing Implication

Absorbed within existing Maintenance/Utility Staff (1 per store). Daily time: ~2 hours. No incremental headcount.

---

## W775. Store-Level Customer Loyalty Card Replacement & Account Recovery

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer reports lost, damaged, or defective loyalty card; or customer forgets loyalty number |
| **Frequency** | Daily; ~3-5 replacement requests per store per day |
| **Volume** | 200 stores × ~4 replacements/day = ~800 replacements/day chain-wide (~292,000/year) |
| **Owner** | Customer Service Representative |
| **Participants** | CSR, Customer |

### Background
BuildRight Depot's loyalty program has ~600,000 registered members. Physical loyalty cards are lost, damaged, or worn out through regular use. Digital cards (mobile app) may also have access issues. When customers cannot access their loyalty account, they lose the ability to earn points, receive personalized pricing, and redeem rewards — directly impacting customer satisfaction and retention. This workflow ensures rapid account recovery and card replacement at the customer service counter.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Customer identification and account lookup**: CSR looks up customer account: (a) by mobile number (primary), (b) by name + birthday, (c) by email address, (d) by scanning old card barcode if partially readable; verifies customer identity by confirming 2 of: registered phone, address, or last transaction details | CSR | — | 2 min |
| 2 | **Account status verification**: System displays account: (a) active status, (b) current points balance, (c) current tier (Bronze/Silver/Gold/Platinum per W17), (d) any account flags (frozen per W496 fraud, merged per W619); confirms account is valid and not flagged for fraud | System | CSR | Instant |
| 3 | **Old card deactivation**: If old card is being replaced: system deactivates old card barcode and links; if card was lost, system flags old card as "Lost — Do Not Honor" to prevent unauthorized use of points | System | — | Instant |
| 4 | **New card issuance**: CSR issues replacement: (a) physical card — scans new blank card barcode and links to account, (b) digital card — sends new digital card link via SMS/email for mobile wallet; system logs replacement with date, store, and CSR ID | CSR | — | 2 min |
| 5 | **Account verification**: CSR verifies: (a) new card scans correctly at POS terminal, (b) points balance displays correctly, (c) customer tier and benefits are intact, (d) customer confirms information is current (phone, email, address per RA 10173 consent) | CSR | — | 2 min |
| 6 | **Customer communication**: System sends confirmation: "Your BuildRight loyalty card has been replaced at [Store Name]. Your points balance: [X]. Your tier: [Y]. If you did not request this replacement, please contact us immediately at [hotline]." | System | — | Automated |
| 7 | **Replacement analytics**: Monthly: system reports: (a) replacement rate by store, (b) replacement reason (lost, damaged, defective), (c) time-to-resolution, (d) flagged accounts (repeated replacements may indicate fraud per W496) | System | CRM Manager | Automated |

### System Touchpoints

- Loyalty member module — account lookup, card issuance, and deactivation per W17
- W17 customer loyalty program operations — loyalty account management
- W253 customer master data governance — deduplication per W619
- W496 customer loyalty fraud detection — fraud flagging for suspicious replacement patterns
- W551 POS loyalty enrollment & account lookup — POS account verification
- W708 customer communication — replacement confirmation notification
- RA 10173 consent management — customer data verification during replacement

### Pain Points / Risks

- Account fraud — unauthorized person may attempt to claim another customer's account by providing partial information; identity verification (2 of 4 data points) mitigates but does not eliminate risk
- Points theft — if old card is not promptly deactivated, finder could redeem points before customer reports loss; real-time deactivation is critical
- Replacement frequency — customers who request multiple replacements may be attempting to exploit the system; fraud detection per W496 must flag patterns
- Customer frustration — inability to quickly access account during checkout (no card, no phone, can't remember number) leads to missed points earning; W551 on-the-spot lookup mitigates

### Time Estimate

Per replacement: identification (2 min) + verification (instant) + issuance (2 min) + verification (2 min) = ~6 minutes. Daily per store: ~24 minutes for 4 replacements.

### Staffing Implication

Absorbed within existing CSR duties. Daily per store: ~25 minutes. No incremental headcount.

## W776. Store-Level Product Recall Customer Notification Execution

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Product recall activated per W29; customer notification required |
| **Frequency** | Ad-hoc; ~2-4 product recalls per year requiring customer notification |
| **Volume** | Per recall: ~100-5,000 affected customer transactions depending on SKU velocity |
| **Owner** | Store Manager |
| **Participants** | Store Manager, CSR, LP Analyst, Customer, HQ Compliance |

### Background
When a product recall is activated per W29, BuildRight Depot must notify customers who purchased the affected product. While W29 manages the recall process at the enterprise level and W627 handles recall effectiveness verification, this workflow addresses the store-level execution of customer notification — identifying affected customers from POS transaction history, contacting them, and coordinating product return or exchange. Philippine regulations (DTI, FDA, DENR depending on product type) and corporate liability management require documented customer notification efforts.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Recall notification receipt**: Store Manager receives recall notification from HQ Compliance per W29: (a) affected SKU(s), (b) recall reason and risk level, (c) affected date range of sales, (d) required customer action (return, dispose, exchange), (e) customer notification template approved by Legal | Store Manager | — | 15 min |
| 2 | **Affected transaction identification**: System queries POS transaction history per store for affected SKU within specified date range: (a) transaction date/time, (b) customer identification (loyalty member, trade account, or anonymous cash sale), (c) quantity purchased, (d) payment method for refund reference | System | Store Manager | Automated + 15 min review |
| 3 | **Loyalty member notification**: For identified loyalty members: (a) system sends notification via W708 (SMS, email, app push) using approved template, (b) notification includes: product description and image, recall reason, risk level, required action, return instructions, refund/exchange options, store contact number, (c) system tracks notification delivery status | System | — | Automated |
| 4 | **Trade account notification**: For identified trade/B2B accounts: (a) Store Manager calls Key Account Manager for direct customer notification per W617, (b) notification logged in customer account per CRM, (c) return/exchange logistics coordinated (bulk pickup or store return) | Store Manager | Sales Manager | 1-2 hours |
| 5 | **In-store recall signage**: CSR posts recall notice at: (a) store entrance, (b) customer service counter, (c) product's former shelf location (with "RECALLED — Do Not Sell" sign), (d) store social media pages; notice includes product image, recall reason, and action instructions | CSR | Store Manager | 30 min |
| 6 | **Customer return processing**: Customers returning recalled products: (a) CSR processes return per W12 with special recall reason code, (b) no receipt required for recalled items, (c) full refund regardless of item condition (safety recall), (d) system flags return as recall-related for W627 effectiveness tracking | CSR | — | 5-10 min/return |
| 7 | **Daily recall status reporting**: Store Manager reports daily to HQ Compliance: (a) customers notified vs. total affected, (b) returns received, (c) notification delivery failures, (d) customer inquiries or complaints about the recall | Store Manager | — | 15 min/day during recall |
| 8 | **Recall close-out**: When recall period ends per W627: (a) Store Manager confirms all customer notification attempts completed, (b) removes in-store recall signage, (c) files recall documentation per W256 retention policy, (d) submits final store-level recall effectiveness report | Store Manager | HQ Compliance | 30 min |

### System Touchpoints

- W29 product recall execution — recall activation and management
- W627 product recall effectiveness verification — recall tracking and effectiveness measurement
- POS transaction history — affected customer identification by SKU and date range
- W708 customer communication management — automated and manual customer notifications
- W12 returns & exchanges — recall-specific return processing
- W540 BIR invoice management — refund documentation for recalled items
- W256 document retention — recall documentation archival
- W476 BFP fire safety / W479 FDA / W447 DTI-BPS — regulatory agency notification integration

### Pain Points / Risks

- Anonymous transaction gap — cash sales without loyalty identification (~40% of transactions) cannot be directly notified; in-store signage and media coverage are the only outreach channels
- Customer panic — overly alarming recall notifications may create disproportionate customer concern; notification language must be reviewed by Legal and balanced with safety obligation
- Return volume surge — high-velocity recalled items may generate significant return volume; store staffing may need temporary adjustment
- Recall fatigue — frequent recalls (especially for low-risk items) may desensitize customers to notifications; recall communication must be calibrated to risk level
- Regulatory documentation — DTI/FDA/DENR may require proof of customer notification efforts; detailed logging of all notification attempts essential for W627 effectiveness verification

### Time Estimate

Per recall (store-level): setup and notification (2-4 hours) + daily reporting (15 min/day × recall duration) + return processing (absorbed in W12) + close-out (30 min) = ~4-8 hours over recall period.

### Staffing Implication

Absorbed within existing Store Manager and CSR duties. Per recall: ~4-8 hours concentrated at activation. No incremental headcount.

## W823. Store-Level Customer Material Calculator & Quantity Estimation Service

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer requests material quantity calculation for a project (floor tiling, wall painting, fencing, cement work) |
| **Frequency** | Daily; ~15-25 estimation requests per store per month |
| **Volume** | 200 stores x 20 requests = ~4,000 estimations/month; ~70% converted to immediate purchase |
| **Owner** | Sales Associate |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate, Department Supervisor, Customer |

### Background

Hardware/DIY retail customers frequently need help calculating material quantities. "How many bags of cement do I need for a 10 sqm slab, 4 inches thick?" "How many tiles for a 20 sqm bathroom including wastage?" "How many liters of paint for a 4x5 meter room with 3-meter ceilings?" While W820 covers comprehensive project BOM estimation for large projects, this workflow addresses the common daily interaction where a customer needs a quick quantity calculation for a specific material — typically at the point of sale or during product selection. Incorrect calculations result in customer dissatisfaction (not enough material, too many trips) or over-purchase (wasted money, potential returns). The calculator must account for Philippine construction practices and product specifications.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Customer approaches Sales Associate with project dimensions and material question — "I'm tiling my bathroom, it's 3 meters by 4 meters, using 60x60 cm tiles" or "I need to pour a concrete slab, 5 meters by 3 meters, 4 inches thick" | Customer / Sales Associate | — | 2 min |
| 2 | Sales Associate opens material calculator on POS terminal or handheld device: (a) selects material type (tiles, paint, cement, lumber, wire, pipes, roofing), (b) enters project dimensions (area, length, volume as applicable), (c) enters application parameters (tile grout gap, paint coats, slab thickness, stud spacing) | Sales Associate | — | 2 min |
| 3 | System calculates quantities using: (a) product specifications from W252 item master (tile dimensions, paint coverage per liter, cement yield per bag), (b) wastage factors by material type (tiles: +10-15%, paint: +5-10%, cement: +5%, lumber: +15%), (c) Philippine construction standards (hollow block per sqm, rebar spacing per structural plan, concrete mix ratios — 1:2:4 for general purpose, 1:1.5:3 for structural) | System | — | Automated (< 5 sec) |
| 4 | System displays: (a) calculated quantity with wastage — "You need 42 tiles (37 exact + 5 wastage at 12%)", (b) available stock at store per POS-013 real-time inventory, (c) alternatives if preferred item out of stock per W279 substitution rules, (d) price per unit and total estimated cost, (e) complementary materials needed ("For tiling, you'll also need tile adhesive at 4-5 kg/sqm and grout — shall I add those?") | System | — | Automated |
| 5 | Customer reviews estimate: Sales Associate can adjust parameters — reduce wastage if professional installer (from 15% to 8%), change quality tier (economy vs. premium tile), modify dimensions if customer corrects measurement | Sales Associate / Customer | — | 3 min |
| 6 | If customer decides to purchase: Sales Associate adds items to transaction at POS per W5B or creates quotation per W542 if customer wants to think about it; quotation valid for 7 days with estimated quantities locked | Sales Associate | — | 3 min |
| 7 | If customer wants to compare options: Sales Associate runs alternative calculations with different products/tiers — e.g., compare 60x60 porcelain tile vs. 30x60 ceramic tile quantity and cost difference | Sales Associate | — | 2 min |
| 8 | Post-sale: system logs estimation for analytics — estimation accuracy (comparison of estimated vs. actual purchase quantity for repeat customers), conversion rate (estimate to sale), most estimated materials by store and region, popular project types by store trade area | System | Department Supervisor | Automated |

### System Touchpoints

- W252 item master — product specifications (tile dimensions, paint coverage, cement yield per bag, board foot calculations)
- POS-013 real-time inventory — stock availability check at store level during estimation
- W279 substitution rules — alternative product suggestions when preferred item is out of stock
- W5B in-store selling — add estimated items to POS transaction
- W542 quotation — create formal quotation from estimation for deferred purchase
- W298 product attributes — material specifications and application parameters
- W40 pricing — current selling price and promotional price for cost estimation
- W463 catch-weight processing — for lumber/wire sold by length or weight
- W109 receiving — stock level updates from inbound deliveries affecting availability

### Pain Points / Risks

- **Estimation accuracy depends on customer providing accurate dimensions** — customers often estimate incorrectly ("I think my room is about 4 meters" may be 3.5 or 4.5 meters); a 10% dimension error compounds into significant quantity error; recommendation: advise customer to measure before estimation or offer home measurement service per W148
- **Product specification changes** — manufacturers changing tile sizes, paint coverage rates, or cement bag weight without timely update to W252 item master; calculator gives wrong quantities based on stale specs
- **Complex projects requiring professional advice** — some estimations are beyond Sales Associate capability (multi-room tiling with different patterns, structural concrete mixes, electrical load calculations for wire sizing); must refer to licensed professionals per W827
- **Wastage factor variability** — DIY customers waste significantly more than professional installers (tiles: 15-20% for DIY vs. 5-8% for professionals); wrong wastage assumption leads to shortage or excess
- **Structural calculations requiring licensed engineer** — if customer's project involves load-bearing construction, quantity estimation alone is insufficient; Sales Associate must escalate per W827 load calculation advisory

### Time Estimate

Per estimation: 5-15 min (2 min intake + 2 min calculator entry + 3 min review + 3-8 min comparison/purchase). Daily per store: ~20-40 min (1-2 requests/day at 15-25/month). Monthly chain-wide: ~600-1,000 staff hours (4,000 estimations x 10-15 min avg).

### Staffing Implication

- Absorbed by existing Sales Associates as part of floor selling duties per W5B
- Training on material calculator tool essential — 2-hour training module per W518 store training; coverage of common Philippine construction materials and standard calculations
- No incremental headcount — estimation is a value-added service that increases conversion rate and average transaction value
- Department Supervisors provide backup for complex estimations beyond Sales Associate knowledge level

---

## W824. Store-Level Lumber Yard & Outdoor Area Daily Operations

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Store opening per W5A; daily operational cycle for outdoor/lumber yard areas |
| **Frequency** | Daily; 200 stores with lumber yards |
| **Volume** | 200 stores x daily operations; each store has 500-2,000 sqm outdoor yard area |
| **Owner** | Department Supervisor (Lumber & Building Materials) |
| **Participants** | Department Supervisor, Receiving Clerk, Stock Associate, Sales Associate, Yard Helper |

### Background

BuildRight Depot stores have dedicated outdoor/covered lumber yards and building material areas (500-2,000 sqm) per the model company profile. These areas require daily operational management distinct from indoor store operations: weather exposure protection, heavy material handling (cement bags, plywood sheets, steel bars, hollow blocks), bulk sales by board foot/meter (catch-weight per POS-016), forklift/hoist operations, and yard traffic management. W3B covers DC-level yard management and W438 covers yard dispatch/customer vehicle loading, but neither covers the daily operational management of the store-level lumber yard which has unique challenges: customer self-service in yard, weather-dependent operations (typhoons, rain), and safety risks (heavy materials, forklifts, cutting equipment).

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Morning yard inspection**: Department Supervisor conducts pre-opening walkthrough: (a) check overnight security — gate locks, CCTV per W71 physical security, fencing integrity, no signs of unauthorized entry, (b) inspect material stacks for weather damage (rain-soaked cement bags, warped lumber, rust on steel rebar), (c) verify forklift pre-operation check per W579 daily equipment safety — fluid levels, tire condition, horn/lights, (d) check safety signage and barriers in place (hard hat zone, forklift zone, pedestrian walkway), (e) verify catch-weight scale calibration per W463 — test weigh a known reference | Department Supervisor | — | 20 min |
| 2 | **Material display and restocking**: Stock Associate organizes lumber racks and building material displays: (a) replenish from backstock per W554 daily restocking — priority items: cement, hollow blocks, lumber, plywood, (b) ensure price tags displayed per W667 price verification — outdoor weatherproof tags, (c) sort lumber by species and dimension (tanguile, narra, mahogany; 2x2, 2x3, 2x4, 2x6), (d) stack cement bags properly — max 10 bags high per safety, on pallets to prevent moisture wicking from ground, (e) arrange hollow blocks for easy customer access — stable stacks, no leaning towers | Stock Associate | Department Supervisor | 30-60 min |
| 3 | **Customer service operations**: Sales Associate assists yard customers: (a) material selection and quantity estimation per W823 — "How many hollow blocks for a 10-meter perimeter wall, 2 meters high?", (b) catch-weight processing for lumber and wire per W463 — measure and weigh on yard scale, (c) cutting services for lumber and pipe per W169 — cross-cut and rip-cut to customer specifications, (d) loading coordination per W438 yard dispatch — assist customer vehicle loading safely | Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | Integrated into selling |
| 4 | **Weather monitoring and protection**: Department Supervisor monitors weather conditions throughout the day: (a) if rain approaching: cover exposed materials immediately — tarp over cement bags, plywood, MDF, insulation; roll up lumber end-grain protectors, (b) if PAGASA typhoon signal raised for store area: activate W576 severe weather protocol — secure all outdoor materials, suspend yard operations, protect equipment, (c) post-wet weather: inspect for water damage — test cement bags for hardening (lump test), check plywood for delamination, process damaged goods per W91 for markdown or disposal | Department Supervisor | Store Manager | 15-30 min as needed |
| 5 | **Yard safety management**: (a) enforce PPE requirements for yard staff per W780 — hard hats, steel-toe boots, high-visibility vests mandatory in yard, (b) manage forklift traffic — pedestrian exclusion zones clearly marked, forklift horn when turning corners, speed limit 5 kph in customer areas, (c) customer loading safety per W438 — only trained staff operate forklift/hoist for loading customer vehicles, customers must stand clear during loading, (d) daily hazard check per W559 — tripping hazards (loose lumber offcuts, stray nails), unstable stacks, loose materials, standing water after rain | Department Supervisor / Stock Associate | Store Manager | Ongoing throughout day |
| 6 | **End-of-day yard close**: (a) secure all gates and access points — chain and padlock main yard gate, verify CCTV coverage active per W71, (b) cover exposed materials — cement bags, plywood, insulation, (c) forklift parked and locked per safety protocol — key removed, parked in designated area, parking brake engaged, (d) yard inventory spot-check for high-value items — steel bars, premium lumber (narra, mahogany), expensive hardware (galvanized sheets), (e) report any losses, damages, or safety incidents to Store Manager via daily yard operations log | Department Supervisor | Store Manager | 15 min |

### System Touchpoints

- W5A store opening — yard inspection triggered as part of daily opening checklist
- W554 daily restocking — replenishment of outdoor material displays from backstock
- W463 catch-weight processing — lumber, wire, and rebar sold by weight/length measurement
- W438 yard dispatch — customer vehicle loading coordination and documentation
- W169 lumber cutting — cross-cut and rip-cut services for customer-specified dimensions
- W579 daily equipment check — forklift pre-operation safety inspection
- W71 physical security — CCTV, fencing, gate security for outdoor yard area
- W91 damaged goods — weather-damaged material processing (markdown or disposal)
- W576 severe weather protocol — typhoon and severe weather preparation and response
- W559 hazard reporting — daily safety hazard identification and remediation
- W780 PPE compliance — personal protective equipment requirements for yard staff
- W667 price verification — outdoor material price tag accuracy and weatherproof display
- W252 item master — product specifications for lumber species, cement types, steel grades

### Pain Points / Risks

- **Weather damage to outdoor inventory** — Philippine typhoons (averaging 20 per year) and monsoon rain cause significant material damage; cement bags harden when wet, plywood delaminates, lumber warps in heat and humidity; estimated PHP 2-5M annual weather damage across chain
- **Forklift safety in customer-accessible yard** — customers walking through yard while forklift operates; pedestrian-forklift collision risk; mitigated by exclusion zones but enforcement is difficult during peak hours
- **Heavy material handling injuries** — cement bags (40 kg), plywood sheets (20-30 kg), steel bars (6m length); back injuries and crush injuries are top yard safety risks; mechanical lifting aids and two-person lift rules mitigate
- **Cement bag deterioration from moisture** — even covered storage in Philippine humidity causes gradual deterioration; FIFO rotation per W554 critical; bags stored > 3 months should be tested before sale
- **Lumber warping from heat and humidity** — Philippine tropical climate causes rapid lumber warping; flat stacking with weight on top and proper sticker placement required; warped lumber leads to customer returns
- **Theft of high-value items from outdoor area** — steel rebar, premium lumber, and galvanized roofing sheets are high-value and difficult to secure in open yard; CCTV per W71 and daily spot-check mitigate
- **Customer safety in forklift zones** — customers may not understand forklift exclusion zones; clear signage in English and Filipino required; verbal warning protocol during active forklift operation

### Time Estimate

Morning inspection: 20 min. Daily restocking: 30-60 min. Customer service: integrated into selling time per W5B. Weather protection: 15-30 min as needed (more during rainy season June-November). Safety management: ongoing, ~15 min dedicated checks. End-of-day close: 15 min. Total per store: ~2-3 hours/day. Chain-wide: ~400-600 staff hours/day across 200 stores.

### Staffing Implication

- Absorbed by existing Department Supervisor, Receiving Clerk, and Stock Associates as part of daily duties
- 1 dedicated Yard Helper per store (can be part-time, 4-6 hours/day) for material handling, restocking, and loading assistance
- Forklift operator certification required per W579 — minimum 1 certified operator per store; most Stock Associates trained as backup operators
- Safety training per W518 must include yard-specific modules: heavy lifting, forklift awareness, weather emergency procedures

## W825. Store-Level Bulky Item Delivery Proof Collection & Documentation

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer purchases bulky items (lumber, cement, appliances, tiles in bulk) requiring delivery; delivery completed by store or 3PL |
| **Frequency** | Daily; ~5-10 bulky deliveries per store per week |
| **Volume** | 200 stores x 7.5 deliveries/week = ~1,500 bulky deliveries/week chain-wide; ~6,000/month |
| **Owner** | Receiving Clerk / Delivery Driver |
| **Participants** | Receiving Clerk, Delivery Driver, Customer, Store Manager (for escalations) |

### Background

Unlike ecommerce home delivery per W19 which has digital tracking per W592, store-originated bulky item deliveries (customer buys lumber, cement, tiles, appliances in-store and needs delivery) often use BuildRight's own trucks or local 3PL per W668. These deliveries require physical proof of delivery documentation: customer signature, photo of delivered items at location, and damage documentation. This is distinct from ecommerce tracking — store bulky deliveries are scheduled at POS per W5D, may include installation per W138, and often involve COD collection. Philippine practice requires signed delivery receipts for BIR compliance and customer dispute resolution.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | At POS purchase: Cashier creates delivery order per W5D — captures customer delivery address, contact number, preferred delivery date/time window (AM 8-12 or PM 1-5), items to deliver, COD amount if applicable, special instructions ("gate code: 1234", "load to second floor via service elevator") | Cashier | Store Manager | 5 min |
| 2 | Receiving Clerk prepares delivery: (a) picks items from store stock per delivery order pick list, (b) verifies items match delivery order — correct SKU, correct quantity, correct size/color for appliances, (c) loads onto delivery vehicle per W438 yard dispatch — proper securing for transport (strapping lumber bundles, wrapping appliances in protective blanket, stacking cement bags on pallets), (d) generates delivery trip sheet with all deliveries scheduled for this run | Receiving Clerk | Department Supervisor | 15 min per delivery |
| 3 | Delivery driver executes delivery: (a) follows route per delivery trip sheet, (b) navigates to customer address — Philippine addressing challenges (barangay-level directions, landmarks, informal settlements), (c) arrives at customer location, unloads items at customer-designated location (driveway, garage, ground floor, specified room if installation included per W138) | Delivery Driver | — | 30-90 min including drive |
| 4 | **Proof of delivery collection**: (a) delivery driver presents delivery receipt to customer for signature — customer confirms items received in good condition, (b) customer notes any discrepancies or damage on delivery receipt ("2 tiles chipped", "wrong color appliance"), (c) delivery driver takes photo of delivered items at customer location using mobile app — items in position, condition visible, customer location identifiable, (d) if COD: collects payment per POS multi-tender per W5B — cash or GCash/bank transfer per W99, issues receipt per W540 BIR-compliant receipt | Delivery Driver | Customer | 10 min |
| 5 | Delivery driver returns to store: (a) submits signed delivery receipt and photo evidence to Receiving Clerk, (b) turns over COD cash collections to Cash Office per W541 cash handling — counted, verified, receipted, (c) reports any delivery exceptions (customer refusal, damage in transit, wrong address) | Delivery Driver | Receiving Clerk | 10 min |
| 6 | Receiving Clerk processes proof of delivery: (a) scans signed delivery receipt into ERP per W255 document storage — linked to original delivery order, (b) matches delivery order to proof of delivery — system validates all items delivered, quantities match, customer signature present, (c) closes delivery order in system — triggers inventory deduction from store stock and revenue recognition per W487, (d) if COD: reconciles cash collected against COD amount on delivery order per W89 bank reconciliation | Receiving Clerk | Store Manager | 5 min per POD |
| 7 | **Exception handling — customer not at delivery address**: (a) driver contacts customer via phone — reschedule if customer reachable within 30 min, (b) if customer unreachable: leave delivery attempt notice per W5D with rescheduling number, (c) return items to store — note on delivery trip sheet as "failed delivery", (d) Receiving Clerk reschedules per W5D rescheduling rules (max 2 attempts, then cancellation) | Delivery Driver / Receiving Clerk | Store Manager | 15-30 min per exception |
| 8 | **Exception handling — customer refuses delivery**: (a) driver documents refusal reason on delivery receipt, (b) returns items to store, (c) Receiving Clerk initiates return per W12 returns — items restocked, delivery fee non-refundable per policy, (d) Store Manager reviews for pattern (customer repeatedly ordering and refusing) | Delivery Driver / Receiving Clerk | Store Manager | 20 min per exception |
| 9 | **Exception handling — delivery damage**: (a) driver documents damage on delivery receipt and takes photos, (b) customer signs with damage notation, (c) Receiving Clerk processes per W91 damaged goods — insurance claim per W59 if applicable, replacement delivery scheduled, customer compensation per policy | Delivery Driver / Receiving Clerk | Store Manager | 30 min per incident |

### System Touchpoints

- W5D customer delivery scheduling — delivery order creation at POS with address, items, COD, time window
- W438 yard dispatch — loading coordination and vehicle dispatch for bulky deliveries
- W5B in-store selling — POS transaction creation with delivery line item
- W541 cash office — COD collection reconciliation and cash handling
- W255 document storage — signed delivery receipt and photo evidence archival
- W487 revenue recognition — delivery completion triggers revenue posting
- W89 bank reconciliation — COD cash reconciliation
- W59 insurance claims — transit damage insurance claim processing
- W91 damaged goods — delivery damage documentation and disposition
- W12 returns — customer refusal return processing
- W19 home delivery — ecommerce delivery parallel workflow for reference
- W592 ecommerce delivery tracking — digital tracking contrast with store bulky delivery
- W668 3PL coordination — third-party logistics provider management for store deliveries

### Pain Points / Risks

- **Customer not home for delivery** — wasted driver time and fuel cost; estimated 10-15% failed first-attempt rate; Philippine context: customers may give landmark-based directions that are imprecise; mitigated by confirmation call day before per W5D
- **Difficulty finding addresses in Philippine neighborhoods** — many addresses use landmarks ("behind the municipal hall", "near Jollibee on the highway") rather than street numbers; GPS navigation unreliable in rural areas; driver experience and barangay knowledge is critical
- **Delivery damage on rough roads** — Philippine road conditions (especially provincial) cause transit damage to fragile items (tiles, appliances); proper packaging and securing per W438 mitigates but does not eliminate; estimated 2-3% damage rate
- **COD collection risk** — delivery driver carries significant cash (PHP 5,000-50,000 per delivery run); robbery risk in some areas; cash handling protocol per W541; GCash/bank transfer per W99 preferred for large COD amounts
- **Proof of delivery document loss** — signed delivery receipts can be lost before scanning; mobile app photo capture mitigates but physical signature on paper remains primary proof; daily reconciliation catches missing PODs
- **Photo quality in low-light conditions** — evening deliveries (5-7 PM) may have poor photo quality; driver training on proper photo documentation (flash, multiple angles, items clearly visible)

### Time Estimate

Per delivery cycle: preparation at store (15 min) + drive to customer (15-45 min) + delivery and documentation (10 min) + drive back (15-45 min) = 1-2 hours average. Store processing: 5 min per POD scanning and reconciliation. Exception handling: 15-30 min per exception. Weekly per store: ~7.5 deliveries x 1.5 hours = ~11 delivery hours/week.

### Staffing Implication

- Absorbed by existing Receiving Clerk for preparation and POD processing — ~2 hours/day
- Dedicated delivery driver per store (high-volume stores) or shared across 2-3 stores (lower-volume stores); BuildRight operates ~80-100 delivery vehicles chain-wide per the model company profile
- 3PL driver per W668 for overflow deliveries — contracted per trip basis
- Delivery helper (loads/unloads heavy items) — can be Stock Associate assigned to delivery duty; 1 per delivery vehicle
- Training on delivery documentation, COD handling, and customer interaction per W518

## W826. Store-Level Layaway Payment Reminder & Forfeiture Processing

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Layaway installment payment due per schedule created in W75; payment overdue > 7 days |
| **Frequency** | Weekly; ~100-200 overdue layaway accounts chain-wide at any time |
| **Volume** | ~100-200 overdue accounts/month; ~5-10% of active layaway plans reach forfeiture stage |
| **Owner** | CSR |
| **Participants** | CSR, Store Manager, Customer |

### Background

W75 covers layaway/installment sales creation but not the payment reminder and forfeiture sub-processes. BuildRight's layaway program (POS-020) allows customers to reserve high-value items (appliances, power tools, furniture) with a deposit and pay in installments over 30-90 days. When payments lapse, the store must: (a) proactively remind customers to avoid forfeiture, (b) process forfeiture per policy when payment deadlines are missed, (c) return forfeited items to selling stock, and (d) handle forfeited deposits per accounting policy (deposit forfeiture income vs. store credit refund). Philippine Consumer Act (RA 7394) requires fair forfeiture policies with adequate notice.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | System generates daily overdue layaway report — categorized by aging bucket: (a) payment due in next 3 days (pre-due reminder queue), (b) 7 days overdue (first notice queue), (c) 14 days overdue (second notice/final notice queue), (d) 30 days overdue (forfeiture eligible queue); report includes: customer name, contact number, layaway plan number, item description, total plan value, payments made, balance due, overdue installment amount, days overdue | System | — | Automated |
| 2 | **Pre-due reminder** (3 days before payment due): system sends automated reminder to customer via W708 — SMS preferred channel (Philippine mobile penetration ~95%), email backup: (a) payment amount due, (b) due date, (c) payment options — in-store at any BuildRight, GCash per W99, bank transfer to BuildRight account, (d) consequence of missed payment: "If payment is not received by [date], your layaway may be subject to cancellation. Please contact us to discuss payment arrangements." | System / CSR | — | Automated (< 1 min) |
| 3 | **First overdue notice** (7 days overdue): CSR calls customer using contact number from layaway agreement: (a) confirms customer received pre-due reminder, (b) asks about payment intention — "When can you make your installment payment?", (c) offers one-time payment extension (max 14 additional days) with Store Manager approval for customers with good payment history (2+ on-time payments), (d) documents call outcome in system: "committed to pay by [date]", "requested extension", "unable to reach customer", "customer requests cancellation" | CSR | — | 5-10 min per call |
| 4 | **Final notice** (14 days overdue): CSR sends formal notice via W708 — registered SMS or email with read receipt: (a) states current overdue amount and total remaining balance, (b) warns: "This is your final notice. Per BuildRight layaway policy, your layaway plan will be cancelled if full overdue balance is not received within 14 days from this notice date [specific date].", (c) provides store contact number for payment arrangement, (d) explains forfeiture policy — deposit disposition per customer's election at plan creation | CSR | Store Manager | 5 min |
| 5 | **Forfeiture processing** (30 days overdue, grace period expired, no response or no payment): (a) Store Manager reviews layaway account — verifies customer contact history (all reminders and notices sent, call outcomes documented), approves forfeiture, (b) system cancels layaway agreement — layaway status changes to "Forfeited", (c) reserved inventory released back to available stock per POS-020 layaway inventory reservation — items return to sellable on-hand, (d) deposit forfeiture calculation per policy: Option A — full deposit forfeited (Dr. Cash / Cr. Forfeiture Income); Option B — deposit less cancellation fee (PHP 500 or 10% of deposit, whichever is higher) returned as store credit per W28 gift card/store credit issuance, (e) system posts forfeiture accounting entries — forfeiture income recognized, layaway liability cleared, inventory reservation released, (f) customer notified of forfeiture via W708 with deposit disposition explanation and store credit reference number if applicable | CSR / Store Manager | Store Manager | 15-20 min per forfeiture |
| 6 | **Partial payment layaway forfeiture**: if customer made some installments but forfeits: (a) calculate total payments made vs. total plan value, (b) apply forfeiture policy — typically all payments converted to store credit less cancellation fee (PHP 500 or 10% of total payments, whichever is higher), (c) store credit issued per W28 — can be used for any future purchase at any BuildRight store, (d) never refund cash for forfeited layaways per policy (deters strategic layaway abuse — customer using layaway to lock sale price then canceling), (e) accounting: Dr. Layaway Liability / Cr. Store Credit Liability (for credit issued) + Cr. Forfeiture Income (for cancellation fee retained) | CSR | Store Manager | 10 min |
| 7 | Monthly layaway analytics report generated by system: (a) plan creation rate by store and by category, (b) completion rate — target >85% of plans completed without forfeiture, (c) forfeiture rate by store (flag stores >10% forfeiture for investigation), by category (flag categories >15% forfeiture for policy review), and by customer segment, (d) average plan duration (target: <60 days), (e) deposit forfeiture income — recognized monthly in GL, (f) payment reminder effectiveness — conversion rate from pre-due reminder to payment (target: >70%), first notice to payment (target: >50%), final notice to payment (target: >30%) | System | Store Manager / Finance | 2 hours/month review |

### System Touchpoints

- W75 layaway/installment sales — original plan creation with payment schedule and terms
- POS-020 layaway capability — inventory reservation, payment processing, plan lifecycle management
- W708 customer communication management — automated SMS/email reminders and formal notices
- W28 gift card/store credit — forfeiture store credit issuance for partial payment layaways
- W541 cash office — in-store layaway payment collection and reconciliation
- W99 payment settlement — GCash, bank transfer, and other digital payment methods for layaway installments
- W540 BIR receipt management — layaway payment receipts and forfeiture documentation
- W255 document storage — forfeiture approval documentation, customer communication logs
- W41 complaint resolution — customer disputes on forfeiture, deposit amount, or store credit calculation

### Pain Points / Risks

- **Customer disputes on forfeiture** — despite adequate notice (pre-due + first notice + final notice), customers may dispute forfeiture claiming they never received notices; mitigated by multi-channel notification (SMS + email + call) and system-logged contact history; W41 handles disputes
- **Deposit amount disputes at forfeiture** — customer disagrees with forfeiture calculation or cancellation fee; clear policy documentation at plan creation per W75 (signed acknowledgment of forfeiture terms) mitigates; Store Manager has authority to waive cancellation fee for goodwill on case-by-case basis
- **Strategic layaway abuse** — customer uses layaway to reserve sale-priced item (e.g., PHP 15,000 power tool on 20% off promo), then cancels when item returns to regular price or is restocked; "no cash refund on forfeiture" policy deters this behavior; system flag for repeat cancelers (>2 forfeitures in 12 months)
- **Inventory reservation impact** — items reserved for layaway are unavailable for other customers; if layaway customer forfeits after 30+ days, the item may have missed its selling window (seasonal items, promotional items); releasing reserved inventory promptly at forfeiture is critical
- **Forfeiture accounting complexity with partial payments** — when customer made 3 of 6 installments before forfeiting, the accounting requires splitting between store credit liability (for credit issued) and forfeiture income (for cancellation fee); automated system calculation per W826.6 reduces errors
- **Philippine Consumer Act compliance** — RA 7394 requires fair and transparent forfeiture practices; BuildRight policy must provide adequate notice (3 touchpoints over 30 days), reasonable cancellation fee, and documented customer acknowledgment of terms at plan creation

### Time Estimate

Per pre-due reminder: automated (< 1 min). Per first notice call: 5-10 min. Per final notice: 5 min documentation. Per forfeiture processing: 15-20 min (including Store Manager review and approval). Monthly analytics review: 2 hours. Weekly chain-wide: ~100-200 contacts x 7 min avg = ~12-23 hours/week across all stores. Per store: ~5-8 hours/week during active layaway periods.

### Staffing Implication

- Absorbed by existing CSR as part of customer service duties — layaway follow-up is a standard CSR responsibility
- Estimated ~5-8 hours/week chain-wide for layaway reminder and forfeiture processing
- Store Manager involvement: approval of extensions (5 min each), approval of forfeitures (5 min each), monthly analytics review (30 min); estimated ~1 hour/month
- Training on layaway policy, forfeiture procedures, and customer communication per W518 — 1-hour module for all CSRs
- No incremental headcount — layaway management is part of standard CSR workload
